“Dr. Balman,” I said, my voice sounding strange to my own ears, “Dr. Letheny is caught out in the storm somewhere and we have not yet been able to find him. Mr. Jackson was not to have morphine: it was not ordered and moreover at twelve thirty he was all right. He has evidently been﻿—killed﻿—so that someone could steal the radium. There will be﻿—confusion. Someone must take charge from now on﻿—and since Dr. Letheny is gone﻿—”

“Leave things to us, Miss Keate,” said Dr. Balman at once. “See to your wing as usual and Dr. Hajek and I will do what is necessary.”

“Do you intend to call the coroner?” I asked.

“Certainly. I shall telephone at once. It means police﻿—detectives﻿—all that, but this is a terrible thing. Steps must be taken immediately. A delay in such a matter﻿—”

===Chinese===
就在这时，一件怪事发生了。我们都盯着那处小小的针伤，要不然我们绝不会看见那一小点红色，正从针眼里缓缓渗出来。那远算不上一滴，仅只隐约可见，却把我们心头的老迷信勾了起来：凶手近身时，尸体会流血。我看着，一股寒意顺着脊背爬上来；巴尔曼医生哑着嗓子迸出一两个词，霍地跳起身；梅达倒抽一口气叫出声来，踉跄后退；就连那个不动声色的海耶克医生也低声咕哝了一句什么，抬手抹了抹眼睛。

我强自按捺住自己。再这么下去，我们都要变成语无伦次的傻子了，而要办的事还多得很。

“巴尔曼医生，”我说，自己的嗓音听来都陌生，“莱瑟尼医生让暴雨堵在半道上了，我们还没能找到他。杰克逊先生不该用吗啡：没有这项医嘱，况且十二点半时他还好好的。他显然是——被人害了——好叫人偷走那镭。接下来会——乱作一团。必须有个人从此刻起主持大局——既然莱瑟尼医生不在——”

“交给我们吧，基特小姐，”巴尔曼医生立刻说，“照常照管好你的翼，需要做的事，我和海耶克医生来办。”

“您打算请验尸官吗？”我问。

“当然。我马上打电话。这就意味着警察——侦探——诸如此类，都躲不掉，可这是桩可怕的事，必须立刻着手。这种事拖不得——”

===Original===
“Here I am, Miss Keate,” said Olma Flynn from the doorway. “I hurried to get dressed. What﻿—” her pale eyes travelled past me to the bed. “Why﻿—why what is it? He is﻿—*dead!*” Her voice rose. I suppose our very attitudes and gray faces told her the truth, for suddenly she began to scream. I seized her by the arm none too gently, clapped my other hand over her mouth and pulled her outside, closing the door behind me.

But it was too late. Others had heard her screams, and there was no keeping the thing secret, especially as some prowling nurse heard Dr. Balman and Dr. Hajek telephoning for the police and the coroner. The story was over the hospital in ten minutes and only the strictest measures prevented a panic. Terror-stricken nurses crowding into the halls and wing, the demands of the sick to whom the excitement seemed to have communicated itself, flaring, inadequate lamps and candles and their little flickering circles of light that made frightened faces whiter and the surrounding gloom blacker, horrified questions that no one could answer, stark fear in every pair of eyes﻿—all this made it an hour not soon forgotten.

Fortunately Maida and I found that our own patients had not suffered from our enforced absence from duty. It was a difficult matter, however, to calm some of the more nervous ones and keep the knowledge of what had happened from reaching their ears. Olma Flynn’s assistance was of the slightest as she refused to stir three feet from Maida or me, and her hands shook so that she spilled everything she touched.

===Chinese===
“我来了，基特小姐，”奥尔玛·弗琳在门口说，“我赶紧穿戴好的。怎么——”她那双浅色的眼睛越过我，落向那张床。“哎呀——哎呀，这是怎么了？他——*死了！*”她的嗓音拔高起来。想来是我们僵硬的姿态和灰败的脸色道破了真相，因为她陡然尖叫起来。我一把攥住她的胳膊——毫不客气——另一只手捂住她的嘴，把她拖出门外，随手带上了门。

可是太迟了。尖叫已经落进了别人耳朵里，这事再瞒不住，何况早有哪个爱打探的护士听见巴尔曼医生和海耶克医生在给警察和验尸官打电话。十分钟后，事情传遍了全院，只有最严厉的手段才防止了一场恐慌。吓破了胆的护士挤满大厅和病房翼；病人们仿佛也被这骚动传染，叫嚷不休；一盏盏明灭不定、聊胜于无的油灯和蜡烛，摇曳的小小光圈把受惊的脸照得愈发惨白、把四周的黑暗衬得愈发浓黑；没人答得上的惊骇追问；每一双眼睛里赤裸裸的恐惧——这一切，使那个钟头成了久久难以忘怀的一页。

所幸梅达和我发现，自己病房的病人并未因我们被迫擅离岗位而遭殃。然而要安抚住几个神经质的病人、不让事情的真相钻进他们耳朵，实在颇费周章。奥尔玛·弗琳的帮忙实在有限，她死也不肯离开梅达或我三步开外，两只手抖得端什么洒什么。

===Original===
We were very busy and I did not see the coroner and the police when they arrived and went directly to Room 18. Along about half-past three I slipped into the diet kitchen and made some very strong coffee which I shared with Maida and Olma Flynn. We felt a little better after that though still weak and sick and controlling our fears by sheer strength of will.

Somehow the weary gray hours dragged along. Dawn came through still gusty rain and wind and the cold light crept reluctantly into the sick rooms. Breakfast was late that morning owing to the cook’s not being able to find enough candles for adequate lights, but the day nurses finally came on duty, white and fear-stricken over what the night had held.

By that time, however, policemen were all over the place and I must say that their broad, blue backs gave me a welcome sense of security. Dr. Letheny had not turned up yet; at least, if he had I had not seen him.

The breakfast trays came up at last and Maida, Olma Flynn, and I washed our hands and faces and descended to the dining room in the basement. We said little. The candles on the long table flickered; the rain beat against the small windows; our uniforms were wrinkled and looked cold; our eyes were hollow and our faces drawn and gray, and already we were starting nervously at sudden sounds and were beginning to cast furtive glances over our shoulders as if to be sure there was nothing there.

===Chinese===
我们忙得不可开交，验尸官和警察什么时候到的、径直进了18号病房，我都没见着。三点半前后，我溜进配膳室，煮了些极浓的咖啡，同梅达和奥尔玛·弗琳分着喝了。喝过之后我们略略缓过一点，可仍旧虚弱欲呕，全凭一股意志强压着恐惧。

那几个疲惫的灰白时辰，总算挨挨挤挤地过去了。黎明穿过依然狂风夹雨的天空，清冷的晨光迟迟疑疑地爬进一间间病房。那天早饭开得迟了，因为厨娘找不够蜡烛来照明，可日班护士总算来接班了，一个个面无人色，为这一夜的变故吓得不轻。

不过到那时，警察已经布满全院，我得说，他们那一个个宽阔的蓝背影，给了我一种受欢迎的安全感。莱瑟尼医生还没露面；至少，就算他回来了，我也没有见到。

早饭托盘总算端上来了。梅达、奥尔玛·弗琳和我洗了手脸，下楼到地下层的餐室去。我们很少说话。长桌上烛火摇曳；雨点敲打着小窗；我们的制服皱巴巴的，看上去冷冰冰的；眼窝深陷，脸色灰败，我们已经开始被突然的声响吓得一激灵，开始不住地回头偷觑，仿佛要确认背后确实空无一物。

===Original===
But it was not until I had finished drinking some very black coffee and playing with my toast that the reason for our strained silence made itself clear to me.

Only someone connected with the hospital could have known that the radium was out of the safe and in use in Room 18. Only a doctor or a nurse would have known how to administer morphine with a hypodermic syringe.

It might be﻿—anyone! It might be one of us!

The thought threatened that remnant of courage I still maintained. I rose, pushing back my chair. It scraped along the floor and at the sound heads jerked in my direction too quickly and someone cried out nervously.

I hurried from the room, up the stairs and to my room in the nurses’ dormitory. I am not ashamed to say that I locked the door. But though I needed rest I could not sleep.

===Chinese===
可直到我灌下几口极黑的咖啡、拨弄完我的吐司，我们这份紧绷绷的沉默的缘由，才在我心里明晰起来。

只有同医院有关系的人，才可能知道镭已经出了保险柜、正用在18号病房里。只有医生或护士，才懂得怎样用皮下注射器注入吗啡。

那人可能——是任何人！可能是我们中间的一个！

这个念头，威胁着我勉强撑住的最后一点勇气。我站起身，把椅子往后一推。椅子刮着地板，那声音一起，一颗颗脑袋猛地朝我这边甩过来，快得不自然，有人神经质地叫出了声。

我匆匆出了餐室，上楼，回到护士宿舍我自己的房间。我不怕承认：我把门锁上了。可尽管亟需休息，我却怎么也睡不着。

## III Dr. Letheny Does Not Return / 莱瑟尼医生没有回来

===Original===
From sheer fatigue, however, I must have dozed for I awoke at the sound of a repeated knocking at the door. It was a frightened little student nurse wanting to know if all training classes and lectures were to be suspended.

“Suspended?” I said, the horror of the past night sweeping over me. “Suspended? I﻿—why, Dr. Letheny will tell you.”

She blinked.

“But Dr. Letheny﻿—we﻿—they﻿—nobody knows where Dr. Letheny has gone.”

“What!” I was fully awake.

“No, ma’am. They can’t find him anywhere.” Frightened though she was, she yet appeared to take a naive relish in being the first to tell me the news. “They can’t find him at all. Miss Letheny has telephoned everywhere that he might be and the police are working on it and they have been asking us all kinds of questions.”

===Chinese===
不过，纯粹是累到了极点，我必定是打了个盹，因为一阵反复的敲门声把我惊醒。门外是个吓坏了的小个子学生护士，想来问问培训课和讲座是不是全都停了。

“停了？”我说，昨夜的恐怖霎时涌上心头。“停了？我——咦，这得问莱瑟尼医生。”

她眨了眨眼。

“可是莱瑟尼医生——我们——他们——谁也不知道莱瑟尼医生去哪儿了。”

“什么！”我一下子全醒了。

“是啊，小姐。他们哪儿都找遍了。”她虽然害怕，却似乎因为头一个把这消息告诉我，还带着一种天真的得意。“压根儿找不着人。莱瑟尼小姐把他可能去的地方都打电话问了个遍，警察也在查，还问了我们各种各样的问题。”

===Original===
I reached for a fresh uniform.

“I’ll come down immediately,” I said. “About the training classes, did you speak to Dr. Balman?”

“No. Miss Dotty said to find out if you knew what was to be done.” Which was like Miss Dotty, she being amiable but not very clear-thinking.

“Dr. Balman is Dr. Letheny’s assistant. I have nothing to do with it.”

The little student nurse rustled away and ten minutes later, refreshed by a bath and a clean uniform, I followed her.

===Chinese===
我伸手去拿一套干净的制服。

“我马上就下来，”我说，“培训课的事，你问过巴尔曼医生了吗？”

“没有。多蒂小姐说来问问您，看您知不知道该怎么办。”这很像多蒂小姐的做派——人和气，脑子却不大清楚。

“巴尔曼医生是莱瑟尼医生的助手。这事与我无关。”

小个子学生护士窸窸窣窣地走了。十分钟后，我洗过澡、换上干净制服，精神一振，也跟着下了楼。

===Original===
I found the main portion of the hospital fairly shuddering with excitement. To my extreme annoyance it appeared that the moronic fraction of our nursing staff was beginning to take a melancholy satisfaction in the tumult and posing freely for the reporters who, with their flashlight affairs, were swarming over the whole place. I might say here and now that I soon stopped that and did not mince matters in so doing, though I could not prevent the headlines that had already found their way into the city newspapers.

In the main office Dr. Balman and Dr. Hajek, both looking worn and haggard, were literally surrounded by our board of directors who, it seemed, had descended in a body and were determined to hold somebody responsible for the terrible thing that had occurred. I learned later that there was some trouble in convincing them that Mr. Jackson’s death was not due to a mistake on the part of the nurses. Some policemen were in the room, too, and the chief of police, himself, a burly fellow who looked habitually as if his darkest suspicions were about to be verified.

This expression intensified itself as I entered the room, which, by the way, was the first indication of a fact that later became all too painfully evident, namely that I, Sarah Keate, occupied a prominent place in the list of suspects, for had I not been in the south wing? Had I not been in a position to administer the morphine that caused the patient’s death? Had I not been the one to find him?

===Chinese===
医院主楼里简直是一片兴奋得发颤的景象。叫我极为恼火的是，我们护理人员里那帮缺心眼的家伙，似乎已开始从这场骚乱中咂摸出一种愁惨的快意，还大大方方地任由记者们摆布拍照——那些人扛着闪光灯家伙什，正在整个医院里蜂拥穿梭。我可以在此直说：这种事我很快就刹住了，而且话说得毫不含糊，只是城里报纸已经登出去的那些头条，我是拦不住了。

总办公室里，巴尔曼医生和海耶克医生——两人都是一脸憔悴枯槁——简直被我们的董事会团团围住了。看样子，董事们是倾巢而出，铁了心要为这桩惨事找出个担责的人来。我后来才知道，费了好一番口舌才让他们相信，杰克逊先生的死并非护士们的过失。屋里还有几名警察，连警察局长也亲自在场——一个五大三粗的汉子，惯常挂着那副神情，仿佛他心底最阴暗的怀疑随时就要得到证实。

我一进屋，这副神情便愈发浓重。顺带一提，这正是某一事实的头一个苗头——那事实后来显露得分外痛切：我，萨拉·基特，在嫌疑名单上占着显眼的一席。因为，我不就在南翼当班吗？我不就有机会注射那夺去病人性命的吗啡吗？发现他的，不正是我吗？

===Original===
One or two of the board had the grace to rise as I entered, but most of them were too agitated to remember their manners.

“What is this about Dr. Letheny?” I began.

“Are you Miss Keate?” asked the chief of police.

“Yes,” I replied, none too graciously.

“We were just about to send for you,” he informed me. “Now suppose you tell us everything you know of this affair. Mind, I say *everything*.”

===Chinese===
我进来时，董事里倒有一两位还晓得起身，但大多数人都激动得顾不上礼数了。

“莱瑟尼医生的事到底怎么回事？”我开口问道。

“你是基特小姐？”警察局长问。

“是我，”我答道，语气算不得客气。

“我们正要派人去叫你，”他通知我，“现在，把你在这桩案子里知道的一切都讲出来吧。记住，我说的是‘一切’。”

===Original===
I turned to Dr. Balman.

“Hasn’t Dr. Letheny returned yet?”

He shook his head slowly.

“Come, come, Miss Keate,” said the chief.

“Doesn’t Miss Letheny know where he is?” I insisted anxiously.

===Chinese===
我转向巴尔曼医生。

“莱瑟尼医生还没回来吗？”

他缓缓摇了摇头。

“行了，行了，基特小姐，”局长说。

“莱瑟尼小姐也不知道他在哪儿？”我焦灼地追问。

===Original===
“Apparently not.” It was Dr. Hajek who answered.

“Will you answer my questions?” demanded the chief loudly.

“Another time,” I stated impatiently. Didn’t the man see what the pressing issue was! “When did Miss Letheny see him last?”

Dr. Hajek shook his head. “She has not seen him since last night about twelve thirty.”

The chief rose.

“Now, look here! We’ll have no more funny business,” he began to bluster.

===Chinese===
“显然不知道。”答话的是海耶克医生。

“你到底回不回答我的问题？”局长高声喝问。

“回头再说吧，”我不耐烦地说。这人难道看不出眼下火烧眉毛的是什么！“莱瑟尼小姐最后一次见到他，是什么时候？”

海耶克医生摇了摇头。“从昨晚十二点半左右以后，她就再没见过他。”

局长站起身来。

“喂，听着！可不许再跟我们来这套鬼把戏了，”他咋呼起来。

===Original===
“Oh, *do* be still!” I may have spoken somewhat irritably; at any rate the chief turned purple. “Don’t you see,” I explained reasonably, “don’t you see that we must find Dr. Letheny? That so much hinges upon our finding him? Why, so far as we know, *he* decided to remove the radium, perhaps he﻿—” I stuck, appalled by the literal truth of my words.

The chief was quick to pick me up.

“So you have already formed your opinion. And quite right, too. It is very clear that this Letheny fellow has got away with the radium.” The chief actually began rubbing his hands together and smiling. “Now, Miss Keate, just tell us why you suspect Dr. Letheny of this crime.”

“But I don’t!” I cried in exasperation. “I have not had time to suspect anyone yet. I have been too busy. The reason I spoke as I did of Dr. Letheny is that he is the attendant physician; he knew more of Mr. Jackson’s condition than any of us. He may have decided that the radium was﻿—er﻿—not doing any good and may have removed it for that reason. It seems to me that our hands are tied without him.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你安静点儿行不行！”我这话也许说得急躁了些；反正局长的脸涨成了猪肝色。“你难道不明白，”我据理分辩道，“你难道不明白我们必须找到莱瑟尼医生吗？多少事情都系在找到他这一环上！要知道，据我们所知，是他决定把镭取走的，说不定他——”我猛地噎住，被自己这话的字面意思吓住了。

局长立刻抓住我的话头。

“这么说，你已经自有主张了。而且说得一点不错。很清楚嘛，这个姓莱瑟尼的家伙卷着镭跑了。”局长竟真的搓起手来，脸上堆起笑。“好了，基特小姐，快说说，你凭什么怀疑莱瑟尼医生干下这桩罪案。”

“可我没有！”我气恼地喊道，“我根本还没来得及怀疑谁。我太忙了。我之所以那样说到莱瑟尼医生，是因为他是主治医师；比起我们任何人，他都更了解杰克逊先生的病情。他也许是觉得镭起不了——呃——起不了什么作用，因此才把它取走的。在我看来，找不回他，我们便束手无策。”

===Original===
“Just a moment, chief,” remarked one of the most intelligent members of the board. “Suppose we follow your suggestion and leave all investigation until this man﻿—what is his name?”

“Lance O’Leary,” supplied the chief sulkily.

“Until this Lance O’Leary gets here. You seem to have great confidence in him and﻿—”

“Him and me always work together,” interpolated the chief.

“He is out of town at present,” went on the board member, addressing me.

===Chinese===
“且慢，局长，”董事会里一位最有头脑的成员开了腔，“假如照你的提议办，把一切调查都搁到那个人来之后再说——他叫什么名字来着？”

“兰斯·奥利里。”局长没好气地接口道。

“搁到这位兰斯·奥利里到这儿之后。你好像对他信心十足，而且——”

“他跟俺向来是搭伙办案的，”局长插嘴道。

“他眼下不在城里，”那位董事接着说，转头对我。

===Original===
“Suits me,” said the chief. “I’ve wired him and he will be here on the afternoon train. We’ve got everything under guard and can leave the room just as we found it.”

“Then there is no need for us to stay any longer,” remarked a particularly well-fed board member, getting fussily to his feet and kicking a little to shake down his trousers over his fat calves. “I’ve got to get to the office. And now see here, Dr. Balman﻿—and you others﻿—of course we don’t say that this is your fault﻿—”

“Well, I should hope not!” I interrupted tartly.

“Your fault,” he repeated, eyeing me severely. “But at the same time it shouldn’t have happened. There is something wrong somewhere. Here we go and put sixty-five thousand dollars into a whole gram of radium and now look what happens!” The other members shook their fat cheeks in sympathy.

===Chinese===
“正合我意，”局长说，“我已经给他拍了电报，他会坐下午的火车到。一切都派了人看守，那间屋子可以照我们发现时的原样留着。”

“那我们就不必再待下去了，”一位养尊处优、脑满肠肥的董事说道，手忙脚乱地起身，还踢了踢腿，好让裤管顺着肥肥的小腿肚垂下来。“我还得回公司去。还有，你们听着，巴尔曼医生——还有你们各位——我们当然不是说这是你的错——”

“哼，我也希望你们不是！”我尖刻地插了一句。

“你的错，”他重复了一遍，严厉地瞪着我，“可与此同时，这种事本就不该发生。总有什么地方出了岔子。我们一掷六万五千美元，买下整整一克镭，现在你瞧瞧落得个什么下场！”其余的董事纷纷摇晃着肥嘟嘟的两颊，深表同感。

===Original===
“You seem to forget,” I remarked with some asperity, “that there was also a murder in the hospital last night, which might have been prevented had we had an emergency gas line installed. We were without lights a good share of the night.”

This was not quite true, in that the murder had been committed, I had no doubt, before the lights had gone out, but the subject of gas for emergency use had been a matter of contention between the board and the staff for some time and I was glad to note that the entire board looked distinctly uneasy as it filed fatly from the office.

“A splendid group of gentlemen,” commented the chief approvingly.

“Then we are to do nothing until this detective arrives?” I asked impatiently.

“So it seems,” said Dr. Balman, sighing wearily.

===Chinese===
“你们倒好像是忘了，”我的话头带了些刺，“昨晚医院里还出了桩命案呢。要是当初装了应急煤气管道，这案子本是可以防住的。大半个晚上，我们都是在没有灯的情况下熬过来的。”

这话不尽属实——我毫不怀疑，凶案是在停电之前便已发生了的——但应急照明用气的问题，董事会和院方之间已争执了好一阵子。看到全体董事拖着一身肥肉鱼贯挤出办公室时那一副副明显不安的面孔，我心里暗暗解气。

“一群体面得很的绅士，”局长赞许地评论道。

“那么在这位侦探到之前，我们就什么都不用做了？”我不耐烦地问。

“看来是这样，”巴尔曼医生说着，疲惫地叹了口气。

===Original===
“Yes, and nothing is enough,” said the chief, whose name, by the way, proved to be Blunt. “Once Lance O’Leary gets his teeth in anything it is as good as finished. Say﻿—I could tell you things﻿—”

“If only we could find Dr. Letheny,” I reflected. “It is so strange, his disappearing like this and at such a time.”

“Maybe it ain’t so strange as you think,” remarked Chief Blunt. “There is many a man would like to disappear with about sixty-five thousand dollars in his pocket. Say, what does that radium look like? How would you carry it anyhow? Wouldn’t it burn you?”

“It is carried in a small steel box that is especially made to protect it﻿—and you,” explained Dr. Balman. As I glanced at him I was struck by the unbelievably drawn and haggard appearance of his face, which was intensified by a bruise on one cheek bone that was turning a dark, purplish green. “It would be a ticklish thing to dispose of,” he added thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
“是啊，‘什么都不做’也就尽够了，”局长说——顺便提一句，这位局长的大名，后来得知是布伦特。“只要兰斯·奥利里咬上一口的事，就等于办完了。嘿——我肚子里可有的是故事——”

“要是能找到莱瑟尼医生就好了，”我沉吟道，“他就这样不声不响地失踪，还偏偏挑在这个节骨眼上，实在太蹊跷了。”

“兴许没你想的那么蹊跷呢，”布伦特局长说，“天底下有的是男人，巴不得揣上六万五千美元开溜哩。哎，那镭到底长什么样？真要带在身上，可怎么个拿法？不会灼人吗？”

“它装在一只特制的小钢盒里，那盒子正是专门用来保护它——也保护你——的，”巴尔曼医生解释道。我瞥了他一眼，不由得吃了一惊：他的面容枯槁憔悴得令人难以置信，一边颧骨上还有一块淤伤，正转成深青紫色，愈发衬得那张脸骇人。“要把这东西脱手，可是件棘手的事，”他又若有所思地补了一句。

===Original===
“Well, we shall have some disclosures in another night,” said the chief comfortably. “And mark my words, this Letheny has had something to do with it. A man don’t disappear like this for nothing. In the meantime we’ll guard Room 18 and keep everybody away from it. And let nobody leave or come into the hospital.”

“No visitors?” I inquired, with the first shade of approval I had felt for the chief so far.

“No visitors,” he agreed.

“And in the meantime,” said Dr. Balman, “business as usual. Eh, Miss Keate?”

===Chinese===
“哼，再过一晚，准会水落石出，”局长悠然自得地说，“记住我的话，这个莱瑟尼脱不了干系。一个男人不会平白无故就这么没了影儿。这期间我们会守着18号病房，谁也不许靠近。医院里的人，一个不许出去，一个不许进来。”

“谢绝探视？”我问。这是打从见面以来，我头一回对这位局长生出一丝赞许。

“谢绝探视，”他应道。

“这期间呢，”巴尔曼医生说，“一切照常运转。对吧，基特小姐？”

===Original===
“By all means. But Dr. Balman﻿—you don’t think that Dr. Letheny killed Mr. Jackson and got away with the radium﻿—”

“Certainly not,” said Dr. Balman. “There is nothing upon which to base such a conclusion.”

“Don’t be too sure of that,” muttered Chief Blunt from the depths of the telephone transmitter.

It took a few moments for Dr. Balman and Dr. Hajek to arrange between them to take over Dr. Letheny’s work in case, we were careful to say, Dr. Letheny did not come to St. Ann’s that day, while Chief Blunt put the telephone to such good use that at the end of a few minutes he assured us that Dr. Letheny would be found within twenty-four hours. This I thought to be a somewhat sweeping prophecy but said nothing.

===Chinese===
“那自然。可是巴尔曼医生——您总不会认为，是莱瑟尼医生杀了杰克逊先生，再卷着镭跑了吧——”

“当然不会，”巴尔曼医生说，“毫无根据下这种结论。”

“这话可别说得太满，”布伦特局长埋头对着电话话筒咕哝道。

巴尔曼医生和海耶克医生花了几分钟商量好，万一——我们都小心地措辞说——莱瑟尼医生那天不到圣安医院来，两人便分头接手他的工作；与此同时，布伦特局长把电话打得风生水起，几分钟后便向我们担保，二十四小时之内定能找到莱瑟尼医生。我觉得这预言未免下得太满，但没吭声。

===Original===
Leaving the office, I walked thoughtfully down to the south wing. It was a compliment to St. Ann’s routine that, with the exception of a certain nervousness on the part of the nurses, all was quite as it should be. Morning baths had been given, breakfasts were all over and rooms dusted, and discipline in general had been maintained. However, there is no use saying things were just as usual for they were not. It was dark and cold that morning, with one of those quick changes of temperature for which our part of the country is famous. The electric service had not yet been repaired and there were lamps at intervals along the corridor. Miss Dotty, wisely for once, had doubled the number of girls on duty, and blue-striped skirts and white aprons of training nurses, as well as the severe white of graduate nurses, glimmered everywhere.

So far we had been successful in keeping the news of the murder from the ears of the patients, but of course they were aware of some kind of disturbance during the night, and several of them were quite fussy and upset and demanded to be moved to another wing, which naturally we could not do. We kept the newspapers from them, too, but one of the minor troubles of the day was the continual telephone calls from anxious relatives, which began as soon as the morning extras were out.

Oh, yes, the newspapers got out extras with all kinds of pictures and the most absurd statements that made St. Ann’s appear to be something between a boarding school and a den of iniquity. This unfortunate impression was helped by the pictures of nurses in conjunction with the murder and radium theft.

===Chinese===
离开办公室，我若有所思地朝南翼走去。除了护士们多少有些神经过敏之外，一切都井井有条、恰到好处——这份镇定，足以为圣安医院的常规争光。晨间擦浴都做完了，早饭都开过了，病房也掸过尘，总的说来纪律未失。然而，硬说一切如常毫无意义，因为事实并非如此。那天早晨又阴又冷，我们这一带素以天气骤变出名，这正是那种骤变。电还没修好，走廊里隔一段便点着一盏油灯。多蒂小姐难得聪明了一回，把当班的人手加了一倍，学生护士的蓝条纹裙和白围裙，连同资深护士那身素净的白，在四处隐隐闪动。

到那时为止，我们一直成功地没让凶案的消息传进病人耳中，不过他们当然察觉到夜里有过什么骚动，好几位病人烦躁不安，吵着要搬到别的病区去——这我们自然办不到。报纸也给他们拦下了，可那天一桩不大不小的麻烦，便是焦虑的亲属们打来的电话，此起彼伏，早报号外一上街就开始了。

哦，没错，报纸出了号外，登着五花八门的图片和最荒唐不过的种种说法，把圣安医院说得介于寄宿女校和藏污纳垢的贼窝之间。护士的照片同凶案、镭失窃案并排登在一起，更坐实了这种倒霉的印象。

===Original===
And in spite of our efforts to carry on work the same as usual, in spite of cleaned rooms and spick-and-span corridors and careful charts, there lingered, somehow, pervading the very old walls of St. Ann’s, a certain gloom, a sense of foreboding, that centred in the south wing.

Room 18 was closed and guarded by a stalwart policeman, who sat uncompromisingly in front of the door, but that end of the corridor was shunned as if there were live smallpox there, and when one of the nurses had to go to Room 17, opposite, or to the next room, Sixteen, she quite frankly sought the company of another nurse.

Old Mr. Jackson’s lawyer had been notified immediately of the tragedy, I learned, and he, in turn, had notified the dead man’s only relatives, a cousin and a nephew, living somewhere in the East. Along in the middle of the morning a rather impersonal telegram came from them to Chief Blunt, bidding him spare no expense and keep them informed of developments.

===Chinese===
尽管我们竭力让一切照常运转，尽管病房收拾得干干净净、走廊窗明几净、病历单记录得一板一眼，可不知怎的，总有一股阴郁、一种不祥的预感，弥漫在圣安医院古老厚重的墙壁之间，盘踞在南翼不散。

18号病房闭了门，由一名魁梧的警察把守，他寸步不让地坐在门前。可走廊那一头人人避之不及，仿佛那里闹着活天花；每逢哪个护士不得不到对面的17号病房或隔壁的16号病房去，她总会拉上另一个护士作伴，毫不掩饰。

我听说，老杰克逊先生的律师已第一时间获知了这场惨剧，他又转而通知了死者仅有的亲人——一位表亲和一位侄子，都住在东部某地。上午过半，他们给布伦特局长发来一封口气冷淡、公事公办的电报，吩咐他不惜一切代价，随时向他们通报进展。

===Original===
What with one thing and another I had very little time of my own until about two o’clock in the afternoon when, after firmly getting rid of Miss Dotty, who evidenced a distressing disposition to cling and whisper in horrified italics, I sat down at the south-wing chart desk, drew a blank chart toward me, and presenting as forbidding a back against interruption as I could, I tried to think. Until that moment the whirl of events had so caught me that I had had to act and had had literally no time in which to consider the matter.

I began, logically enough, at twelve thirty, the time I had last seen Dr. Letheny. In spite of my defence of Dr. Letheny before Chief Blunt, I felt in my heart that his absence at such a time was, to say the least, rather strange.

It had been a queer night, even before its shocking development; that strange dinner at Corole’s, where everyone had seemed strung to such a singular pitch of excitement, our walk home through the suffocating heat, Maida’s preoccupation, my own disquiet, the storm﻿—And now a memory recurred to me with such force that I almost jumped﻿—that man with whom I had collided there at the corner of the porch! Who was he? What had he been doing?

===Chinese===
一桩接一桩的事，弄得我直到下午两点来钟才有点属于自己的时间。我好不容易摆脱了多蒂小姐——她那动辄黏上来咬耳朵、字字句句都念成惊惶斜体的脾气实在叫人吃不消——在南翼的病历台前坐下，拉过一张空白病历单摆在面前，竭力摆出一副生人勿近的背影，试着理一理思绪。直到那一刻为止，一连串事件把我卷得团团转，我一直只能疲于应付，压根儿没有工夫细细思量这桩案子。

我从十二点半想起，这样最顺理成章——那是我最后一次见到莱瑟尼医生的时刻。尽管我在布伦特局长面前为莱瑟尼医生辩护，可我心里明白，他偏偏在这种时候缺席，说得轻些，也够蹊跷的。

那原就是古怪的一夜，甚至在爆出那桩骇人惨事之前就是如此：科罗尔家那顿怪里怪气的晚宴，席上人人都像绷到了一种异样的兴奋顶点；我们穿过闷得人喘不过气的暑气回医院；梅达的心不在焉；我自己的忐忑；那场暴雨——此刻，一段记忆蓦地涌上心头，力道之猛，惊得我险些跳起来——我在门廊拐角撞上的那个男人！他是谁？他当时在干什么？

===Original===
And then, of course, I recalled the flat, smooth object I had found at the edge of the orchard, there below the kitchen window.

It took only a moment or two to hurry to my room and dive my hand into the pocket of my soiled uniform. Then I sank down on the edge of the bed, staring at the thing in my hand.

I recognized it at once.

It was Jim Gainsay’s cigarette case.

The engraved fraternity shield winked at me as I turned it over in my hands and snapped it open; inside were two or three cigarettes; dazedly I noted the brand﻿—Belwood’s. Jim Gainsay! It was he, then, whom I had met there at the steps of the porch. What had he been doing? What had been his business about St. Ann’s after midnight? And my breath caught and my heart began to pound as I recalled his words of the previous night: “If I can manage to lay my hands on fifty thousand dollars﻿ ﻿… can make as much money as I want﻿ ﻿… I can do it﻿ ﻿… *and I will*.”

===Chinese===
接着，我自然又想起了在果园边上、厨房窗下拾到的那个扁平光滑的东西。

不消片刻，我便赶回自己的房间，把手伸进那件脏制服的口袋。随后我一屁股坐到床沿上，盯着手里这样东西发怔。

我一眼便认出了它。

那是吉姆·盖恩赛的烟盒。

我把它翻过来、啪地打开，上面刻的兄弟会会徽冲着我直眨眼；里头还装着两三支烟；我晕晕乎乎地认出那个牌子——贝尔伍德牌。吉姆·盖恩赛！这么说，我在门廊台阶边撞见的原来是他。他当时在干什么？半夜过后，他在圣安医院周围有什么勾当？待想起他前一夜说的话，我一口气堵在嗓子眼，心口咚咚直跳：“只要我能弄到五万美元……就能要多少钱有多少钱……我办得到……而且我一定办到。”

===Original===
And he had heard our discussion of the radium. He had even heard﻿—yes, I remembered distinctly﻿—he had even heard in what room the radium was in use and that the south door was to be left unlocked. To be sure, I might have been expected to lock the door following Dr. Letheny’s visit, but there were windows and﻿—

Someone was knocking at the door and I had barely time to slip the cigarette case under the pillow. It was Miss Dotty, her eyes fairly popping with excitement.

“Where is the key to the closet in the south wing?”

“What closet? There are several﻿—”

===Chinese===
而且镭的事，他也听见我们议论了。他甚至还听到——不错，我记得清清楚楚——他甚至还听到镭正用在哪间病房、南门将不上锁。诚然，按理说莱瑟尼医生来过之后，我本该把门锁上，可还有窗户呢，而且——

有人在敲门，我刚刚来得及把烟盒塞到枕头底下。来的是多蒂小姐，两只眼睛激动得都快瞪出来了。

“南翼壁橱的钥匙在哪儿？”

“哪间壁橱？有好几间呢——”

===Original===
“I mean the closet in Room 18, of course. Do you have the key?”

“No. And I don’t know where it is. Who wants it?”

“They want it downstairs.”

“They?”

===Chinese===
“当然是18号病房里的那间壁橱。钥匙在您这儿吗？”

“不在。我也不知道搁哪儿了。谁要钥匙？”

“楼下的人要。”

“楼下的人？”

===Original===
“That little, slim detective. He has just come. And oh, Miss Keate, he is so handsome,” she rolled her rather vacant eyes upward.

“Who is handsome?” I spoke somewhat snappishly. Miss Dotty’s rhapsodies aggravate me.

“That Mr. O’Leary. Just wait till you see him. Such a way of speaking! Such clothes! And his eyes are simply wonderful!” Miss Dotty appeared to recall herself from Mr. O’Leary’s charms with difficulty. “But I must hurry. They said if we couldn’t find the key they would have to take the door off the hinges.”

“Take the hinges off, you mean. Indeed they shan’t! That lovely gumwood door! They’ll be sure to scar it. Maybe some of the student nurses locked it. Ask them. Or﻿—wait! I’ll come down myself.”

===Chinese===
“那个瘦瘦小小的侦探呀。他刚到。噢，基特小姐，他可真俊，”她那双颇有些空洞的眼睛朝上翻着。

“谁俊？”我的话冲了点儿。多蒂小姐这种如痴如醉的赞美最让我上火。

“那位奥利里先生呀。您等着见了他就知道了。那样一副谈吐！那样的衣着！还有他那双眼睛，简直没得说！”多蒂小姐这才好不容易从奥利里先生的魅力里回过神来。“可我得赶紧走了。他们说，要是找不着钥匙，就只好把门从合页上卸下来。”

“你是说把合页卸下来。他们休想！那么好的桉木门！准会给刮花的。也许是哪个学生护士锁的。去问问她们。要不——等等！我自己下去。”

===Original===
But Miss Dotty’s starched skirts were already scuttling away.

Before leaving the room, and not without a guilty feeling in my heart, I placed the cigarette case in a safe hiding place which was nothing more nor less than the bottom of my laundry bag. Almost without conscious volition on my part I had resolved to keep the matter of the cigarette case a secret and in my own possession, at least until I knew more certainly where my duty lay concerning it. It carried with it too grave an implication to act upon readily.

Then, still preoccupied, I took my way downstairs, through the main portion of St. Ann’s, past the general office, and turned into the corridor leading to the south wing. As I approached the chart desk, one of the student nurses seized upon me tearfully with a tale of Three’s hysterics, and wouldn’t I help for she had not the least idea what to do. There was nothing for it but to go to her assistance, much as I was interested in the proceedings in Room 18. And it was a good thing for me that I did! Otherwise I should have been in the room when they opened the closet door.

===Chinese===
可多蒂小姐那浆得笔挺的裙子已经窸窸窣窣地跑远了。

出门前，我怀着一种撇不清的内疚，把烟盒藏进了一个稳妥的去处——不偏不倚，正是我洗衣袋的袋底。几乎是未加思索，我已打定主意：烟盒这件事要瞒下来，东西也留在自己手里，至少等我看清自己的本分究竟在哪一边再说。它牵连的干系太重，不容轻率行事。

随后，我仍旧心事重重地下楼去，穿过圣安医院的主楼，经过总办公室，拐进通往南翼的走廊。快到病历台时，一个学生护士哭腔哭调地一把拉住我，说3号病房的病人歇斯底里犯了，她全然不知如何是好，求我一定去帮帮忙。我纵然一心惦着18号病房那边的动静，也只得随她去救场。而我这一去，倒真是万幸！否则，他们打开壁橱门的时候，我就在屋里了。

===Original===
Three’s hysterics proved to be of an unusually stubborn kind, really virulent in fact, and though I was aware of a sort of subdued confusion and tremor of excitement outside the door I could not clearly understand what it was about. I heard faintly the sound of hammering, of feet running along the corridor, of a man’s voice calling out something indistinguishable, and a hastily hushed, woman’s scream which Three promptly and wilfully echoed. Then several people hurried through the hall, and as they passed the door I heard the unmistakable little metallic rattle of the wheels of the stretcher-truck, and caught the words﻿—“Call Dr. Balman,” and something about an ambulance.

This was too much for me and I left my patient as soon as possible. No one was to be seen in the corridor, however, so I walked hurriedly down toward Room 18. Just as I reached it a policeman opened it, saw me, slid hastily through the narrow aperture and, closing the door, stood squarely before it.

“You can’t go in there, miss,” he said firmly.

“But﻿—what has happened? What is all the commotion about?”

“You can’t go in there,” he repeated stupidly. To my surprise I saw that the man was actually frightened. His eyes were staring, his weather-beaten face a sort of yellow-green, and his breath coming in gasps. “You can’t go in there. You can’t﻿—”

He seemed capable only of keeping me out of the room, so without wasting time or effort I turned about and retraced my steps. As I passed the linen-closet door I saw a group of nurses inside. One of them was lying back in a chair in a dead faint and the others were clustered around talking excitedly in low voices and nearly drowning the recumbent one with cold water.

===Chinese===
3号病房的歇斯底里一发作起来竟是异乎寻常地顽固，说凶猛也不为过。门外有一种压低了的骚动与颤巍巍的兴奋，我虽有所察觉，却闹不清究竟。我隐约听见锤子敲打的声音、走廊里奔跑的脚步声、一个男人喊着的什么听不真切的话，还有一声被慌忙捂住的女人尖叫——3号病房这一位立刻任性地跟着叫了一嗓子。接着几个人匆匆穿过大厅，打门外经过时，我听见担架车轮那声错不了的小铁轮响，还逮住几个字——“快去叫巴尔曼医生”，还有什么救护车之类的。

这下我再也按捺不住，撂下病人便尽快脱了身。走廊里却已不见人影，于是我快步朝18号病房走去。刚到门口，一名警察开了门，一见是我，慌忙从窄窄的门缝里侧身挤出去，随手带上门，端端正正堵在门前。

“您不能进去，小姐，”他语气坚决。

“可是——出什么事了？这一阵阵的骚动是怎么回事？”

“您不能进去，”他又呆呆地重复了一遍。叫我吃惊的是，这人竟是真的吓着了。他双眼发直，那张饱经风霜的脸泛着一层黄绿色，喘着粗气。“您不能进去。您不能——”

看来他只剩下把我拦在门外这一样本事，我便不再费口舌，转身原路折回。路过布草间时，我看见里面聚着一群护士。其中一个仰在椅子里，晕得不省人事；其余的围作一圈，压低嗓子激动地议论，一盆盆凉水几乎要把那躺倒的一位浇成落汤鸡。

===Original===
“What on earth?” I exclaimed and at my voice they turned; one of them was frankly sobbing and the other two were white as ghosts.

“Oh, Miss K﻿—K﻿—” began one, her teeth chattering so she could not speak, while the others just stood there with their mouths opening and closing like so many fish. Naturally it was very trying and I believe I shook her till her teeth chattered in good earnest.

“Now tell me what has happened,” I said, releasing her shoulders.

“Oh, Miss Keate, the most terrible thing has﻿—”

===Chinese===
“这到底是怎么回事？”我失声问道。听见我的声音，她们回过头来：一个索性放声哭了起来，另外两个白得像鬼。

“噢，基——基——小姐，”其中一个开了口，牙齿打颤，话都说不成句；其余的只站在那儿，嘴巴一张一合，活像一串鱼。这情形自然叫人窝火，我相信我确实摇了她好一阵，摇得她牙齿真真切切地打起战来。

“现在告诉我，出什么事了，”我松开她的肩膀说。

“噢，基特小姐，最可怕的事——”

===Original===
“Is this Miss Keate?” interrupted a clear voice from the doorway.

I whirled.

A man stood in the doorway; at the moment I was conscious only of a pair of extraordinarily lucid gray eyes; later I noted that he was slender and not very tall, that his gray business suit was well tailored, his gray socks of heavy silk and with a small scarlet thread, his scarf neatly knotted and chosen with care, his face clean-shaven, with clear rather delicately cut features, and that he wore an air of well-groomed prosperity. I knew at once that this was Lance O’Leary.

“I am Miss Keate,” I replied.

“I am Lance O’Leary,” he said (superfluously, but he did not know that). “I should like to talk to you if you have time. Will you come to the office with me, please﻿—I think we shall be undisturbed there.”

Being a woman of some strength of mind, I had intended to take a firm line with this detective whom everyone seemed to think so remarkable, but I found myself walking as meekly as any lamb at his side, and once inside the general office with the door closed, I sat as resignedly in a chair opposite him as if there were not a thousand and one things that I should be doing.

===Chinese===
“这位是基特小姐吗？”门口一个清朗的声音打断了她们。

我霍地转过身去。

门口站着一个男人；那一刻我只留意到一双异常清澈明亮的灰眼睛；过后我才看清，他身形清瘦，个子不高，灰色西服剪裁考究，灰袜子是厚丝的，织着一缕细细的猩红丝线，领带打得整整齐齐、选得用心，脸刮得干干净净，五官清晰而颇为秀气，通身一副修饰齐整、殷实体面的气派。我立刻明白，这就是兰斯·奥利里。

“我就是基特小姐，”我答道。

“我是兰斯·奥利里，”他说（纯属多此一举，他自己却不知道）。“要是您有时间，我想同您谈谈。请随我到办公室去好吗——我想在那儿不会有人打扰。”

我素来算是个有主意的女人，原打算在这位人人都称了不得的侦探面前拿一拿架子，可我发觉自己竟像羔羊一样乖顺地走在他身边；一进总办公室，门一关，我又认命似的在他对面坐下，仿佛手头并非有千头万绪的事等着去做似的。

===Original===
“You are the superintendent of the south wing?” He spoke very quietly and with what I found later to be a wholly deceptive air of detachment.

“Yes.”

“You were on duty last night between twelve and six o’clock?”

“Yes.”

“Miss Maida Day was your assistant?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“您是南翼主管？”他说话声气极轻，带着一种我后来才发现纯属假象的漠然。

“是。”

“昨夜十二点到六点，是您当班？”

“是。”

“梅达·戴小姐是您的副手？”

“是。”

===Original===
“Dr. Balman tells me that Miss Day telephoned to him about two o’clock﻿—possibly ten minutes before the hour. I judge that was only a few moments after you found that your patient was dead?”

“Yes. It must have been about that time. It was something after one thirty when the storm broke and I hurried along the corridor and closed the south door. Then I closed the window in Room 17 and went directly into Eighteen.” My voice was not quite steady at the recollection of those moments and he waited briefly, his clear eyes studying a pencil in his hands, before he went on.

“The windows in Room 18 were also open?”

“Yes. All the windows in the wing were open. It had been very hot and close before the storm began.”

He nodded.

===Chinese===
“巴尔曼医生告诉我，戴小姐在两点前后给他打了电话——大约是两点差十分。我推断，那是在您发现病人死亡后仅仅几分钟？”

“是的。想必就是那个时候。暴雨倾泻下来时刚过一点半，我赶紧沿着走廊跑去关上南门。随后我关好17号病房的窗子，径直进了18号病房。”想起那些时刻，我的嗓音有些不稳。他略等了等，清澈的眼睛端详着手里的铅笔，才继续问下去。

“18号病房的窗子也开着？”

“开着。整个病区所有的窗子都开着。暴雨来之前，天又热又闷。”

他点点头。

===Original===
“Those windows are not far from the ground. Do you think someone from outside could get into the hospital without attracting your attention?”

“Yes,” I said slowly. “It might be done but does not seem very probable. With the doors to the sickrooms open and the night so still I believe I should have heard any unusual sound. But the door to Eighteen was closed. I can’t be sure.”

“You heard no unusual sound, then?”

“Why, no﻿—except that a few moments before the storm began I heard a sort of bang﻿—as if a window had dropped to the sill. It was not very clear.”

===Chinese===
“那些窗子离地面不高。您认为外面的人能不能不惊动您就进入医院？”

“能，”我慢慢地说，“也许办得到，但看来不大像。各病房的门都开着，夜又那么静，我相信只要有一点不寻常的响动我都该听见。可18号病房的门是关着的。我就不敢说了。”

“那么，您没有听见什么异样的声响？”

“呃，没有——只是暴雨快来之前一会儿，我听见砰的一声闷响——像是有扇窗落到了窗台上。听不很真切。”

===Original===
He was looking directly into my face, his eyes as clear as water.

“You are sure it was a window? It might have been a door closing.”

“It was not the door for it was still open. I am not sure﻿—I investigated but found nothing. The south door was still open﻿—and as far as I could tell the windows were as they had been.”

“Did you look in Room 18?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
他直直地望着我的脸，那双眼睛清澈如水。

“您肯定是一扇窗？也可能是门关上的声音。”

“不是门，因为门还开着。我说不准——我查看过，什么也没发现。南门仍然开着——依我看，窗子也都还是原样。”

“您往18号病房里看了吗？”

“看了。”

===Original===
There was a slight pause. Then:

“The patient was﻿—quiet at the time?”

“The room was dark and still so I did not enter it. I just stood there for a moment holding the door half open; I was afraid if I entered the room I would wake him. He was asleep﻿—that is﻿—” I stopped abruptly as it occurred to me that he had not been asleep; that the incident had occurred not more than fifteen minutes before I found him dead.

===Chinese===
略停了停。然后：

“当时，病人——很安静？”

“屋里黑黢黢的，静悄悄的，我便没有进去。我只把门半开着，在门口站了一会儿；我怕一进去会吵醒他。他是睡着了——也就是说——”我猛地收住话头，因为我蓦地想起，他那时并没有睡着；那桩事发生在我发现他身亡之前，顶多不过一刻钟。

===Original===
O’Leary seemed to read my thoughts.

“Yes,” he said quietly. “He must have been dead﻿—then. Can you be certain of the time?”

“Yes. It was shortly after one thirty. I remember because I had just noted the time on a patient’s chart, when I heard that dull sound and went to see what it was.”

He returned to his pencil, a shabby little red thing it was, which he rolled absently between his well-kept fingers.

===Chinese===
奥利里像是看穿了我的心思。

“是的，”他轻声说，“那时候——他必定已经死了。时间您能拿得准吗？”

“拿得准。那是在一点半刚过。我记得，是因为我刚在一位病人的病历单上记下时间，就听见了那声闷响，便过去看看是什么。”

他又低下头摆弄他那支铅笔——一支寒酸的红色小铅笔——在修得齐整的指间心不在焉地捻来捻去。

===Original===
“Was this sound sharp and loud?”

“No﻿—” I hesitated, trying to recall just how it had seemed. “No﻿—it was rather dull﻿—muffled﻿—and yet heavy. It was not very distinct.”

“There were no other unusual circumstances? Nothing out of the ordinary?”

“Why, yes. There was someone﻿—a man﻿—” I broke off abruptly. That man must have been Jim Gainsay. I had no wish to involve him in the matter, at least until I became convinced that his movements should be investigated.

But Lance O’Leary’s gray eyes looked straight through to my back hair.

“Yes?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“那声音又尖又响吗？”

“不——”我迟疑着，竭力回想它听上去究竟怎样。“不——它发闷——闷闷的——却又沉重。听不十分清楚。”

“没有别的异常情况了？没有什么不对劲的地方？”

“呃，有的。有那么一个人——一个男人——”我突然打住。那人多半就是吉姆·盖恩赛。我不想把他牵扯进来，至少在我确信他的行迹确有查一查的必要之前，不想。

可兰斯·奥利里那双灰眼睛，仿佛径直看穿到了我脑后的发髻。

“然后呢？”他追问。

===Original===
“Yes.” I spoke with an accent of finality, and gazed nonchalantly out the window as if the subject were closed.

“Where was he?”

“Running around the hospital,” I replied curtly, wishing I had held my tongue.

“Around and around?” inquired O’Leary blandly.

“No,” I snapped. “Running along the east side of the wing. I﻿—he﻿—we collided.”

O’Leary sat up straighter.

===Chinese===
“是啊。”我说得斩钉截铁，一副话已说完的架势，还漫不经心地望向窗外，仿佛这个话题就此打住。

“他在哪儿？”

“在医院四下里跑，”我三言两语地答，悔不该开这个口。

“绕着圈儿跑？”奥利里温吞吞地问。

“不是，”我没好气地说，“是顺着病区东侧跑。我——他——我们撞了个满怀。”

奥利里坐直了些。

===Original===
“What!”

“I had gone out on the porch for a breath of fresh air,” I explained rather sullenly. “Just as I stepped off the porch I ran into him.”

I stopped as if the incident were concluded.

“Go on,” suggested the O’Leary man after waiting a moment; he was being very polite and very pleasant and altogether disagreeable.

“That’s all,” I said waspishly. I fastened my gaze on his extremely well-made shoes﻿—an attention that I have found invariably disconcerts men﻿—vain creatures! But this one was impervious.

===Chinese===
“什么！”

“我到门廊上透口气，”我有点赌气地解释道，“刚走下门廊，就跟他撞上了。”

我就此打住，仿佛事情已经说完。

“请讲下去，”等了片刻，奥利里这家伙提议道；他彬彬有礼，和颜悦色，也着实讨人嫌。

“都说完了，”我尖刻地说。我的目光钉在他那双做工极考究的皮鞋上——我发现此举向来叫男人发窘——爱虚荣的生灵！可这一位毫无所动。

===Original===
“And what did you say?” he persisted with the most insulting good-humour.

“I said ‘Well﻿—’ ” I stared steadfastly at the shoes.

“And what did he say?”

I resisted an evil impulse to tell him literally and with feeling.

“I hope you don’t think I’d repeat such language,” I replied, and I’m sure he smiled.

“Then what happened?”

===Chinese===
“那您说了什么？”他追问不休，好脾气得叫人冒火。

“我说了声‘唔——’”我死死盯着那双鞋。

“那他说了什么？”

我按捺住一股邪火，很想一字不差、绘声绘色地学给他听。

“但愿您不至于以为，我会把那种话复述出来，”我答道。我敢肯定他笑了。

“后来呢？”

===Original===
“He﻿—er﻿—set me on my feet again and kept on running.”

“Very chivalrous,” remarked O’Leary. “So he kept on running﻿—around the hospital?”

“No,” I answered peevishly. “He ran along the path toward the bridge.”

“What did you do?”

“I walked in the direction he had come from as far as the wing extends but saw nothing unusual.”

“Did you not call anyone? Were you not alarmed?”

===Chinese===
“他——呃——把我扶起来站好，又接着跑了。”

“真是骑士风度，”奥利里评论道，“这么说他接着跑——绕着医院跑？”

“不是，”我不耐烦地答道，“他是顺着小径朝小桥那边跑的。”

“您做了什么？”

“我朝他来的方向走去，一直走到病区的尽头，没看见什么异样。”

“您没有喊人吗？没有受惊？”

===Original===
“I thought of calling Higgins, the janitor, but when I found that things seemed to be all right I decided it was not necessary.”

“Then you came back to the hospital?”

“Yes.”

“And all that time you saw or heard nothing uncommon?”

“Well﻿—I smelled something.”

He made a perceptible motion of surprise.

===Chinese===
“我想过叫门房希金斯，可后来见一切如常，便觉得用不着了。”

“然后您就回医院了？”

“是。”

“这一路您再没看见或听见什么反常的？”

“唔——我闻到过一股味儿。”

他动了一下，看得出是吃了一惊。

===Original===
“You smelled something? Did you say *smelled*?”

I nodded, taking a small degree of satisfaction in his discomposure.

“As I passed that clump of elderberry bushes I smelled ether. It was quite distinct. You know ether has a penetrating odour.”

“But surely that was unusual?”

“Yes. But the night was so hot and ether out there in the apple orchard so impossible that I decided I must be mistaken, that it was just the mingled scents of alfalfa and clover and other growing things.”

“Well﻿—which was it? Ether or imagination?”

===Chinese===
“您闻到了味儿？您是说‘闻到’？”

我点点头，见他这副错愕，心里暗暗有几分快意。

“我走过那丛接骨木的时候，闻到了乙醚味。相当清楚。您知道，乙醚那股味儿是很窜的。”

“可这不是寻常事吧？”

“是不寻常。可那晚那么热，而乙醚出现在外面苹果园里又那么不可能，我便认定自己准是闻岔了，那不过是苜蓿、三叶草和别的花草混在一处的气息罢了。”

“那么——究竟是哪种？乙醚，还是想象？”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” I said firmly. “I’m just telling you what happened. I know it sounds queer﻿—but last night was a queer night. That dinner at Corole’s and everything,” I finished thoughtlessly.

“Dinner at Corole’s? That is Miss Letheny?”

“Yes.”

“Dr. Letheny was there?”

“Yes.”

“Anyone else?”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，”我斩钉截铁地说，“我只是把经过讲给您听。我知道这听起来邪门——可昨晚本来就是邪门的一夜。科罗尔家那顿晚饭，还有种种一切，”我心直口快地补了这么一句。

“科罗尔家的晚饭？就是莱瑟尼小姐？”

“是。”

“莱瑟尼医生也在？”

“在。”

“还有别人吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. Miss Day, Dr. Balman, Dr. Hajek, and a friend of Dr. Letheny’s﻿—a Mr. Gainsay. He is an engineer who stopped for a day’s visit with them.”

“And you and Miss Letheny?”

“Yes.”

“You implied that it was﻿—‘queer,’ I think was the word you used. How was it queer?”

“Oh﻿—I scarcely know. It was very hot and oppressive you know﻿—that sort of electric atmosphere that precedes a thunder storm.”

“Aside from the﻿—er﻿—electricity in the air, was everything quite as usual?”

===Chinese===
“有。戴小姐、巴尔曼医生、海耶克医生，还有莱瑟尼医生的一位朋友——一位盖恩赛先生。他是个工程师，顺路在他们家逗留一天。”

“再加上您和莱瑟尼小姐？”

“是的。”

“您刚才暗示那顿晚饭——‘邪门’，我记得您用的是这个词。怎么个邪门法？”

“哦——我说不上来。天特别热，闷得慌，您知道——就是雷雨来之前那种带电的空气。”

“撇开空气里这股——呃——‘电’不谈，一切都同往常一样？”

===Original===
I paused for a long moment before replying.

“No,” I said candidly. “I think we were all a little nervous and uneasy on account of the heat and suffocating air. That is, I was. And Dr. Letheny﻿—and Miss Day﻿—”

“But not the others?”

“Well﻿—” It was difficult to define that curious tensity I had felt in the air all the night. “No one seemed quite natural to me. It may have been only I who was a little nervous. I really can’t tell you anything definite.”

“Why did you say definitely that Dr. Letheny and Miss Day were unlike themselves?”

“Dr. Letheny is a rather quick-spoken man﻿—when you know him you will understand what I mean. He goes on his nervous energy, is very high-strung and temperamental. He seemed especially explosive last night. And Miss Day was a little abstracted, tired, I think.”

===Chinese===
我隔了好一会儿才作答。

“不，”我坦言道，“我想，因为燥热和窒闷，我们都有点紧张不安。至少我是这样。还有莱瑟尼医生——还有戴小姐——”

“其他人不这样？”

“唔——”那一晚我自始至终觉出的那股说不清道不明的紧绷劲儿，实在难以言传。“依我看，谁都有点不像平常的自己。也可能神经过敏的只有我一个。我实在说不出什么确切的来。”

“那您为什么明确指出，莱瑟尼医生和戴小姐不大对劲？”

“莱瑟尼医生是个说话急的人——等您跟他熟了，就明白我的意思了。他行事全凭一股神经劲儿撑着，极易紧张，喜怒无常。昨晚他尤其像一点就着。戴小姐则有点心不在焉，我想是累了。”

===Original===
“I suppose you talked﻿—played bridge﻿—had a little music?”

“Yes. All of that.”

“Any special topics of conversation?”

“No﻿—”

He noted the uncertainty in my voice.

“Radium wasn’t mentioned?”

===Chinese===
“我想你们聊了天——打了桥牌——还来了点音乐？”

“是。都有。”

“谈了什么特别的话题吗？”

“没有——”

他听出了我话音里的迟疑。

“没提到镭？”

===Original===
“Well﻿—yes. But only in a general way.”

“Didn’t speak of using it? Having it out of the safe?”

“Yes,” I admitted reluctantly.

“Didn’t say for what patient it was being used? In what room?”

“Yes. But only casually.” I explained Dr. Letheny’s request to leave the south door unlocked.

“Anything else?”

===Chinese===
“唔——提了。不过只是泛泛一提。”

“没说到正在使用？没说把它从保险柜里取出来了？”

“说了，”我老大不情愿地承认。

“没说是用在哪个病人身上？在哪间病房？”

“说了。不过只是顺口带过。”我把莱瑟尼医生嘱咐不上锁南门的事解释了一遍。

“还有别的吗？”

===Original===
“Nothing in particular. We just talked of general matters.”

“Such as﻿—”

I glanced at him impatiently.

“Such as?” he repeated.

“Oh﻿—how warm it was, and how everybody longs for something that money will buy, and how St. Ann’s is equipped with radios and expensive ambulances and a whole gram of radium and how much such things cost and all that﻿—and then we played a few hands of bridge and then Dr. Letheny played the piano and then Maida﻿—Miss Day﻿—and I came back through the orchard to St. Ann’s and changed into our uniforms and went on duty.”

“You talked of money and how everybody longs for something that money will buy,” mused O’Leary, adding with uncanny intuition, “I suppose several of you admitted a special desire for money?”

===Chinese===
“没什么特别的。我们净聊些日常琐事。”

“比如——”

我不耐烦地瞥了他一眼。

“比如？”他又问了一遍。

“哦——天有多热啦，人人怎样渴望着什么用钱买得到的东西啦，圣安医院如何配齐了收音机、昂贵的救护车和整整一克镭啦，这些东西又值多少钱啦，诸如此类——然后我们打了几圈桥牌，莱瑟尼医生弹了弹钢琴，再后来梅达——戴小姐——和我穿过果园回圣安医院，换上制服，上夜班。”

“你们谈到了钱，谈到人人都渴望什么用钱买得到的东西，”奥利里若有所思，又带着一种出奇的直觉补了一句，“想必你们中间有几位，承认了自己对钱有份特别的渴望？”

===Original===
“Every single one of us,” I confessed. “That is, except Dr. Hajek. He just listened and seemed amused.”

He smiled. “Don’t be alarmed over such an admission. That doesn’t mean anything, that you all wanted money. Everyone wants money. But suppose you tell me, word for word, as much as you can remember of the conversation. Don’t be afraid of implicating anyone, Miss Keate. I make the request only because I like to get as clear an idea of the general surroundings as possible.” He smiled again. He had an extraordinarily winning smile; it brightened his whole face, for all that it was so brief, and I found myself warming under its influence.

Not seeing how I could possibly harm anyone I repeated as much of our conversation as I could remember, and since I have usually a good memory I think I omitted very little of it.

When I had finished he sat for some time turning and twisting his pencil. I might say that I never but once saw him use that pencil sensibly as a pencil is meant to be used. I even grew to cherish a notion that the pencil aided his mental processes and that if it were taken away from him his ability to think might go along with it. Like Samson’s hair, you know. Then I aroused myself from such childish speculation.

===Chinese===
“我们每一个都承认了，”我招认道，“也就是说，除了海耶克医生。他只管听着，还像听得津津有味。”

他微微一笑。“这样的招认，不必惶恐。你们个个想要钱，这算不上什么。谁不想要钱呢。不过，请您把那晚的谈话，尽您记得的，一字一句讲给我听。别怕牵连到谁，基特小姐。我这样要求，只因我希望对周遭的一切情形，了解得越清楚越好。”他又笑了笑。他有一种极能打动人的微笑；虽然一闪即逝，却点亮了整张脸，连我也不由得被那笑意暖到了。

想不出这能害到谁，我便把记得起来的那晚谈话复述了一遍。我一向记性好，自认为漏掉的极少。

我讲完了，他坐着捻那支铅笔，捻了好一阵。可以说，我只见他有一回把那支铅笔正经当作铅笔用过。后来我甚至渐渐笃信一个念头：这支铅笔是助他思考的法器，一旦被人夺去，他思考的本事怕也要跟一块儿没了。就像参孙的头发，您知道。随即我把自己从这种孩子气的胡思乱想里拽了回来。

===Original===
“If that is all﻿—” I hinted. “This is a busy day for us, you know.”

“Not quite all, Miss Keate.” The smile had completely gone from his face; his expression lost its youthfulness and was very grave. “When did you last see Dr. Letheny?”

“Last night, shortly after twelve thirty.”

“He had come to see Mr. Jackson?”

“Yes. He was here only a few moments.”

===Chinese===
“要是都问完了——”我暗示道，“今天我们可忙得很，您知道的。”

“还没问完，基特小姐。”笑意从他脸上褪得干干净净；那副神情也失去了年轻气，变得十分凝重。“您最后一次见到莱瑟尼医生，是什么时候？”

“昨晚，十二点半刚过。”

“他是来看杰克逊先生的？”

“是。他只待了几分钟。”

===Original===
“You saw him leave?”

“Yes.”

“And he did not return, to your knowledge?”

“No.”

“He said nothing of leaving town?”

“Nothing.”

===Chinese===
“您看着他走的？”

“是。”

“据您所知，他没有再回来？”

“没有。”

“他没说要出城？”

“没有。”

===Original===
“He said nothing that would lead you to believe that he was﻿—er﻿—worried about anything? Had had any trouble?”

“Nothing. I really think, Mr. O’Leary, that he will return before the day is over. Some accident has detained him. There will be some explanation.”

“You﻿—admire Dr. Letheny?” Lance O’Leary was scrutinizing a dripping shrub outside the window as he spoke.

“Yes,” I replied dubiously. “That is, he is a splendid surgeon, very cool and very daring. I like to assist him.”

“You have known him for a long time?”

===Chinese===
“他没说过什么让您觉得他——呃——有心事的话？遇到了什么麻烦？”

“没有。奥利里先生，我真心认为，今天天黑之前他就会回来。多半是有什么意外绊住了他。总会有个解释的。”

“您——钦佩莱瑟尼医生？”说这话时，兰斯·奥利里正端详着窗外一株雨水淋漓的灌木。

“钦佩吧，”我迟疑地答道，“这么说吧，他是位了不起的外科医生，极冷静，又极大胆。我喜欢给他当助手。”

“您认识他很久了？”

===Original===
“Several years. That is, I have known him as everyone else knows him. I do not believe that any of us feel particularly well acquainted with him. He is rather distant, very much interested in some research that he is carrying on.”

“You don’t know the kind of research﻿—the special subject of study?”

“No.”

There followed a long silence; the rain beat steadily against the window; outside in the corridor I heard the sound of the four-o’clock nourishment trays being carried along, the glasses of orange-juice and eggnog clinking together. It was chilly there in the office and I shivered a little.

===Chinese===
“好几年了。也就是说，我对他的了解，同别人对他的了解并无二致。我觉得我们谁也不敢说跟他特别熟。他为人相当疏淡，一门心思扑在他正做的某项研究上。”

“您不知道是哪类研究——具体的课题？”

“不知道。”

接下来是一段长长的沉默；雨点不住地敲着窗子；走廊外，我听见四点钟营养餐盘推过的声响，一杯杯橙汁和蛋奶酒碰在一起，叮当作响。办公室里寒气袭人，我微微打了个哆嗦。

===Original===
“I do wish that Dr. Letheny would return,” I said. “It is bad for the head doctor of St. Ann’s to be away at such a time.”

Lance O’Leary turned slowly to me.

“Dr. Letheny will not return,” he said, eyeing me keenly.

===Chinese===
“我真盼着莱瑟尼医生回来，”我说，“圣安医院的当家医生在这种时候不在，影响太坏了。”

兰斯·奥利里缓缓转向我。

“莱瑟尼医生不会回来了，”他说，目光锐利地注视着我。

===Original===
“Not return? What do you mean?”

He shook his head.

“He will not return,” he said very slowly and distinctly. “Dr. Letheny is dead.”

===Chinese===
“不会回来？您这是什么意思？”

他摇了摇头。

“他不会回来了，”他说得极慢、极清晰，“莱瑟尼医生死了。”

## IV A Yellow Slicker and Other Problems / 黄色油布雨衣及其他难题

===Original===
The slow words beat their way into my brain like so many dull little hammers. I opened my mouth, tried to say something, but could not seem to make him hear, felt curiously sick and dizzy, had a flashing memory of the first time I served in the operating room and all at once the table before me began to waver, the room whirled, and a great black blanket overwhelmed me.

Then, without any interval at all, I found myself lying on the couch in the inner office. I still felt sick but my face was wet and cold and my uniform damp around my shoulders and someone was saying in a dull voice: “Dr. Letheny is dead—Dr. Letheny is dead.”

“All right now, Miss Keate?” inquired a voice anxiously.

Wearily I opened my eyes, saw a gray arm and met the gaze of a pair of clear gray eyes. Instantly my head cleared. I pushed away the supporting arm and sat up, feeling automatically for my cap, though my hands shook.

“That was beastly to shock you so,” Lance O’Leary was saying with honest contrition. “I hope you’ll forgive me, Miss Keate, I’m really awfully sorry.”

===Chinese===
那几个缓慢的字眼，像一把把钝笨的小锤，一下一下凿进我的脑子。我张开嘴，想说点什么，却似乎怎么也没法让他听见，只觉得一阵说不出的恶心和眩晕，脑中闪过头一回进手术室当差时的光景——刹那间，眼前那张桌子晃了起来，整个房间打着旋儿，一张巨大的黑毯兜头把我整个罩没了。

接着，竟没有一丝间歇，我发现自己正躺在里间办公室的卧榻上。我仍旧想吐，可脸上又湿又冷，制服的肩头也浸潮了，有个声音在木然地反复：“莱瑟尼医生死了——莱瑟尼医生死了。”

“现在好些了吗，基特小姐？”一个声音焦切地问道。

我疲乏地睁开眼，看见一条灰色衣袖的臂膀，迎上一双清亮的灰眼睛的目光。我的脑子霎时清醒了。我推开那条搀扶的臂膀，坐起身，下意识地去摸头上的护士帽——尽管双手还在发抖。

“那样吓坏了您，实在太狠心了，”兰斯·奥利里正满含真诚的悔意说着，“请您原谅，基特小姐，我实在抱歉得很。”

===Original===
“Did you say—Dr. Letheny is dead?” I asked, bringing out the words with the peculiar difficulty that one experiences in dreams.

“He is dead,” he answered gravely.

“Not—not—Tell me how he died.”

“Are you sure you can stand it? You’ve got to know sometime.”

“Go on,” I said, bracing myself.

“He has been dead for more than twelve hours. He was in the closet in Room 18.” He paused, regarding me doubtfully, but at my horrified gesture continued: “He had received a blow of some kind. It fractured his skull. He must have died immediately.”

===Chinese===
“您是说——莱瑟尼医生死了？”我问，吐出这几个字时，带着梦里说话才有的那种奇异的艰难。

“他死了。”他神色凝重地答道。

“不是——不是——告诉我，他是怎么死的。”

“您受得住吗？这事您迟早总得知道。”

“说吧。”我说，强自振作。

“他死了十二个多小时了。人就在18号病房（Room 18）那口壁橱里。”他顿了顿，疑虑地端详着我，见我做了个惊骇的手势，才继续说下去：“他头上挨了某种一击，颅骨骨折。他必定当场就死了。”

===Original===
“Wait.” Rising I walked to the window, stared with unseeing eyes at the rain-drenched landscape, found my palms were stinging under the pressure of my fingernails, unclinched my hands, clinched them again and turned to face O’Leary. It was true that I had felt no fondness for Dr. Letheny—but I had often worked at his side.

“I say, Miss Keate,” Lance O’Leary was protesting boyishly, “I’m awfully sorry to have been so brutal about it. But you see, I had to know whether this was news to you or not. Someone locked that closet door, you know. And in my business we suspect everything—everybody—the very walls themselves.”

I was too deeply shocked to be indignant at the lack of compliment in his implication; after a moment he continued.

“There is nothing more for you to help me with now,” he said. “We just found Dr. Letheny’s body this afternoon when we pried the closet door off its hinges. I examined everything at once, called the ambulance, and now the room can be cleaned and used again. The only reminder you will have of all this, I hope, is that I shall likely be about more or less for a few days—or longer. That depends upon the luck I have.” He smiled again. Evidently he was trying to be as considerate as possible and I found myself liking him. “Of course, there will have to be a coroner’s inquest, but that is merely a matter of form and need not annoy you. That is all now, thank you, Miss Keate. Can’t you take some rest? Do you have night duty again tonight?”

===Chinese===
“等等。”我起身走到窗前，目光空茫地瞪着大雨浇透的景致，这才发现手心被指甲掐得生疼；我把手松开，又攥紧，转身面对奥利里。我对莱瑟尼医生确实素无好感——可我常常在他身旁共事。

“我说，基特小姐，”兰斯·奥利里带着孩子气地辩白道，“我那样毫不留情，实在过意不去。可您明白，我必须弄清这对您是不是头一遭听说的新闻。您知道，那扇壁橱门是有人锁上的。干我们这一行，什么都得疑——疑每一个人——连这几面墙本身都得疑。”

我震惊得太深，竟顾不上为他话里那层不客气的意思动气；过了一会儿，他接着说。

“眼下没有别的要请您帮忙的了，”他说，“我们是今天下午把壁橱门从合页上撬下来，才找到莱瑟尼医生的尸体的。我当即查验了一切，叫了救护车，现在那间病房可以打扫出来重新使用了。但愿这一切留给您的唯一纪念，只是我多半要在此地出没几天——或者更久。那得看我的运气。”他又笑了。他显然在极力周全体贴，我发觉自己竟喜欢起他来。“当然，还免不了一场验尸审讯，不过那纯属走个形式，不会搅扰您。眼下就这些了，谢谢您，基特小姐。您就不能歇一歇吗？今晚还要值夜班？”

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered the last question. “Mr. O’Leary, do you have any idea as to who—who has done this?”

His face sobered instantly.

“No,” he said simply. “Will you help me find out?”

“Yes.” I spoke very thoughtfully. “It is only right and just to do so.”

===Chinese===
“要值的。”我只答了最后一个问题。“奥利里先生，您可有任何眉目，知道是谁——是谁下的手？”

他的脸色霎时严肃起来。

“没有。”他答得干脆。“您肯帮我查出来吗？”

“肯。”我说得极为郑重，“这么办，才是天公地道。”

===Original===
“Thank you.” He seemed sincere. “You may be interested to know that you have helped me already.”

“Helped you! How? There was nothing I told you—”

“I’ll see you again, Miss Keate. By the way, I am leaving one or two policemen about tonight. It may help to steady some of the nerves in St. Ann’s.” He opened the door and before I knew it I was in the main hall with my question still unanswered.

===Chinese===
“谢谢您。”他像是出自真心。“您也许有兴趣知道，您已经帮过我一个忙了。”

“帮过您的忙！怎么帮的？我什么也没告诉您——”

“回头见，基特小姐。对了，今晚我要留一两个警察在医院里转。这也许能叫圣安医院里某些人的神经定一定。”他打开门，不等我回过神，我已经站在正厅里，那个问题仍旧悬而未答。

===Original===
I still felt ill and weak from shock, and it was fortunate that the exigencies of the situation demanded action. That was the only saving feature of these fearful days; we were all so busy that we had little time for brooding.

The news could not be kept from the nursing staff, of course, though I hoped that we could keep it from the patients, many of whom had been directly under Dr. Letheny’s care. And there was Corole—in common decency I must go to her.

I snatched somebody’s slicker from the rack near the main door and turned into the corridor leading to the south wing, intending to slip out the south door and along the path to Corole’s cottage, it being much closer that way.

In the corridor I met Dr. Balman.

“I have heard,” I said briefly. “I am going to see Corole.”

He nodded.

===Chinese===
我仍旧因惊吓而虚弱难受，幸而局面的急迫容不得人闲坐。那是这些可怕日子里唯一可堪救赎的一点；人人都忙得团团转，几乎没有工夫去愁思。

这消息自然瞒不过护士们，我只盼能瞒住病人们——他们当中许多人一直直接由莱瑟尼医生诊治。还有科罗尔——凭起码的情面，我也必须去她那里走一趟。

我从正门边的衣架上随手抓了一件不知谁的油布雨衣，拐进通往南翼的走廊，打算从南门溜出去，抄小径直奔科罗尔的小屋——那样走近得多。

在走廊里，我遇见了巴尔曼医生。

“我听说了。”我简短地说，“我这就去看看科罗尔。”

他点了点头。

===Original===
“At the request of the Board of Directors I shall take Dr. Letheny’s place—temporarily at least. I have just called a meeting of the whole nursing staff, Miss Keate. You were with Mr. O’Leary so I did not disturb you. I told them of the situation, gave orders that this thing must not get to the ears of the patients, suspended training classes for a few days, and doubled the number of girls on duty in the various wings and wards until we get a working routine established. I find that the girls are nervous over being alone.” He spoke very calmly, but his extreme pallor caught my eye.

“You had better get some rest, Dr. Balman. And have that bruise on your cheek attended to—it looks bad.”

He passed his hand over the bruise.

“I bumped it while running through the apple orchard last night. I wasted no time after I talked to Miss Day over the telephone.”

“Oh—then you came by the side road?”

===Chinese===
“应董事会的请求，我将接替莱瑟尼医生的位子——至少暂时如此。我刚才召集了全体护士开会，基特小姐。您正陪着奥利里先生，我便没有打搅您。我把情况对她们交了底，下令此事绝不可传到病人耳中，培训课停上几天，在新的值勤安排理顺之前，各翼各病房的值班人手都加了一倍。我发现姑娘们独处一室便心里发慌。”他说得十分平静，可他那种极端的苍白却落进了我眼里。

“您最好歇一歇，巴尔曼医生。脸颊上那块淤伤也找人给瞧瞧——样子不轻。”

他抬手抚过那块淤伤。

“昨晚我抄苹果园跑过来时撞的。同戴小姐通完电话，我一分一秒也没敢耽搁。”

“哦——那您是打小路过来的？”

===Original===
“Yes. Thought I’d save time by not going around by the main entrance. I didn’t expect this.” He fingered the spot cautiously.

“Put iodine on it,” I advised.

“I’ll sleep here in the hospital for a while,” he said. “I’ll be there on the couch in the inner office. So if there is anything wanted I shall be right here.”

I nodded approvingly and went on, but while hurrying through the corridor I became conscious of something about the casual sentences that affected me disagreeably. What was it? Ah! “I bumped it while running through the apple orchard.” To be sure he had followed the words immediately with a very reasonable explanation, but wasn’t that in itself suspicious! On the other hand, however, I had been quite sure that the man with whom I collided had been Jim Gainsay.

Well, there was no way to make sure. And I resolved that I must not allow myself to become suspicious of anything and everything. The affair was strain enough on one’s nerves as it was, without adding the horror of suspecting one’s nearest associates.

===Chinese===
“是啊。想着不走正门绕大圈能省点工夫。没料到会撞出这么一块。”他小心翼翼地摸了摸伤处。

“上点碘酒。”我建议道。

“这一阵子我就在医院里过夜，”他说，“睡里间办公室那张卧榻。这样一来，一旦有什么事，我人就在跟前。”

我赞许地点点头，接着往前走；可匆匆穿过走廊的当口，我渐渐察觉他那几句随口的话里有什么东西令我不快。是什么呢？啊！“昨晚我抄苹果园跑过来时撞的。”不错，他紧跟着就补上了一个头头是道的解释，可这本身岂不正可疑！不过话说回来，同我撞了个满怀的那个人，我当时分明拿准了是吉姆·盖恩赛。

罢了，没法子核实。我暗自拿定主意，绝不能由着自己对什么都疑神疑鬼。这桩案子折磨人的神经已经够受了，犯不着再添一层可怕，去怀疑朝夕相处的同事。

===Original===
Immersed in my own not too pleasant thoughts I passed the door of Room 18 without seeing it, an occurrence that I was to find unusual. On the porch stood a policeman, his broad back to the door, but he made no effort to stop me when I descended the steps. Once in the path the trees dripped steadily on my head, the wind blew the light slicker so that it was difficult to hold it around me, and I bent my head and ran through the damp welter of leaves and small sticks, with the branches of the trees sweeping so low as to brush my hair and cap, and the shrubbery reaching out thorny twigs to clutch at my white skirt. It was shadowy there in the orchard and the hospital soon disappeared behind the intervening shrubbery and trees and gray mist. It was nearing five o’clock by that time and already growing dark so that the path was not an altogether agreeable place in which to linger.

I turned another little bend that sloped rapidly down to the bridge and almost ran into a tall figure that was leaning upon the railing. At my startled exclamation it turned to face me. It was Jim Gainsay, a sodden hat pulled low over his eyes and the collar of his capacious tweed coat turned up. He was smoking (it was a pipe I noted, thinking of the cigarette case) and casting pebbles across the water, which is not a rainy day pastime.

“Oh. It’s you.” I said.

“Miss Keate! Say, you are the very person I’ve been wanting to see. Can you tell me something of poor old Louis?”

===Chinese===
我沉浸在这些并不愉快的念头里，走过18号病房（Room 18）的门口竟视而不见——事后想来，这实在反常。小门廊上立着一个警察，宽阔的背影正对着门，我走下台阶时，他并没有拦我。一踏上小径，树上的雨水便不住点地滴在我头上，风把那件轻飘飘的油布雨衣吹得裹也裹不拢，我只得低着头，在湿漉漉的烂叶和细枝间一路疾走，头顶的树枝扫得那样低，拂着我的头发和帽子，两旁的灌木又伸出带刺的枝条，来揪我的白裙。果园里一片幽暗，医院很快便被层叠的灌木、树木和灰雾吞没在身后。那时已近五点，天色渐渐黑透，这条小径实在不是个宜于流连的地方。

我转过又一道弯，小径陡然下斜通向小桥，我险些撞上一个倚在栏杆上的高个子身影。听得我一声惊呼，那身影转过脸来。是吉姆·盖恩赛，一顶湿透的帽子压得低低的遮着眼，宽松花呢大衣的领子竖着。他在抽烟（我注意到那是烟斗，心里不由想起那只烟盒），还朝水面上扔石子——这可不是雨天的消遣。

“哦，是您啊。”我说。

“基特小姐！嘿，我正想见您呢。您能给我讲讲可怜的老路易斯的事吗？”

===Original===
“Louis? Oh, you mean Dr. Letheny.” I suppose I paled a little at the name. At any rate Gainsay glanced sharply at me.

“I didn’t mean to—disturb you,” he said apologetically. “You see, I only heard of it an hour or so ago, and only what that fellow O’Leary told me. Don’t talk if you would rather not.”

“Then I know no more than you, for the detective, Mr. O’Leary, told me of it, too. Of course, it was a shock.”

Jim Gainsay nodded, his gaze again on the little stream that, swollen by the night’s rain, swept in a bubbling current almost to our feet.

“Poor old Louis,” he muttered.

“You have known him a long time?” I said absently, my eyes too on the water.

===Chinese===
“路易斯？哦，您是说莱瑟尼医生。”听到这个名字，我大概是白了一白脸。至少，盖恩赛锐利地瞟了我一眼。

“我不是有意——惹您难过的。”他带着歉意说，“您瞧，我一个来钟头前才听说这事，而且只听了那个奥利里家伙说的几句。您要是不愿讲，就别讲。”

“那么我知道的不比您多，因为那位侦探奥利里先生，也是这么告诉我的。不用说，这对我是个打击。”

吉姆·盖恩赛点了点头，目光又落回那条小溪上——一夜骤雨涨满了它，翻着白沫的急流几乎扑到我们脚边。

“可怜的老路易斯。”他喃喃道。

“您认识他很久了？”我心不在焉地问，眼睛也望着水面。

===Original===
“Since university days,” said Jim Gainsay slowly. “I always liked Louis though I can’t say I understood him; no one ever did. In the last few years I have seen him only a few times. It was terrible to—go like that. Do they have any idea as to who—who killed him?”

“Not that I know of,” I said and shivered at the thought of the black night so recently past and of the unknown and ghastly presence that Room 18 had held. And I had taken that futile little candle and searched the room for the thing that some sixth sense warned me was there! I shivered again and caught my breath and Jim Gainsay turned to me again.

“Don’t let me keep you out here in the storm. You are cold in that slicker thing.”

“A little. I am going to see Corole. How does she take it?”

Jim Gainsay’s frown deepened.

“I hardly know. I can’t understand her any better than I could understand Louis. She looked—sort of bad—this morning. Tired, you know. And kept saying Louis would return. But she was terribly nervous. Prowled over the house like a cat.” He shrugged in distaste. “Fairly gave me the creeps to watch her. Then when they came up to the house to tell her that—that they had found him she just sort of froze all up. Hardly said a word.”

===Chinese===
“从大学时代起。”吉姆·盖恩赛缓缓地说，“我一直喜欢路易斯，虽说谈不上了解他；谁也从来没了解过他。这几年我统共只见过他几面。那样——那样去了，太可怕了。他们可有眉目，知道是谁——是谁杀了他？”

“据我所知没有。”我说着，想起方才逝去的那个黑沉沉的夜，想起18号病房（Room 18）里曾容纳过的那个不为人知的可怖存在，不由打了个寒噤。而我，竟还举着那支徒劳的小蜡烛，在屋里搜寻我那第六感分明警告过确实存在的东西！我又是一颤，倒抽一口气，吉姆·盖恩赛重新转向我。

“别让我把您堵在这风雨里了。您穿着那件油布雨衣，都冻着了。”

“有一点。我这就去看科罗尔。她还受得住吗？”

吉姆·盖恩赛的眉头皱得更深了。

“我简直说不清。我读不懂她，就像当初读不懂路易斯。今天早上她气色——有点不妙——您知道，一脸倦容。嘴里一直念叨路易斯会回来。可她紧张得厉害，像只猫似的在屋里踱来踱去。”他厌恶地耸耸肩。“看着她，我简直要起鸡皮疙瘩。后来他们上家里来告诉她——说他们找到他了，她整个人就像冻住了似的，几乎一句话也没说。”

===Original===
“Wasn’t she dreadfully shocked?”

“Well—I don’t know. You never can tell how Corole is feeling or what she is thinking about. Of course, she and Louis sort of got on each other’s nerves a little. That is—you know what I mean—” He glanced at me uncertainly.

“I know.”

“I suppose St. Ann’s is awfully upset?”

“We are trying to keep everything going as well as possible. It is a bad situation, naturally. The nurses are doing their best but there is a sort of undercurrent of hysteria.” My mind on Corole, I did not immediately note where his inquiries were leading.

“Miss Day was with you in the south wing last night, was she not?” He knocked his pipe carefully against the railing.

===Chinese===
“她没有大受震动吗？”

“这个——我说不好。科罗尔心里是什么滋味、在想些什么，你永远猜不透。当然啦，她同路易斯彼此有点儿——有点儿磨神经。也就是说——您明白我的意思——”他迟疑不定地看了我一眼。

“我明白。”

“圣安医院怕是闹翻了吧？”

“我们正尽力让一切照常运转。局面自然是糟的。护士们都在勉力支撑，只是暗地里浮动着一股歇斯底里的潜流。”我心里惦着科罗尔，一时没有留意他的问话正引向何处。

“昨晚戴小姐是同您一起在南翼值班的，对吧？”他把烟斗在栏杆上细细磕净。

===Original===
“Yes.”

“How—er—Is she feeling any bad effects from the fright?”

“I have seen her only for a few moments at lunch,” I replied; at another time I should have smiled at his elaborately impersonal air.

“I—don’t suppose I could see her? For a little while?”

“She will be free between six and seven o’clock. But we are allowing no visitors for a few days …” My voice trailed doubtfully into space.

“But see here, Miss Keate, I—it is important that I see her.” He spoke rather defiantly as if he dared me to ask why. “Will you carry a note to her, then?”

===Chinese===
“是的。”

“她——呃——受惊之后，身子没有什么不适吧？”

“午饭时我只见了她一小会儿。”我答道；换在旁的时候，他这份刻意装出的公事公办的架势，准会叫我失笑。

“我——能不能见见她？就一小会儿？”

“六点到七点之间她得空。不过这几天我们一律谢绝会客……”我的声音迟疑地散在风里。

“可是您听我说，基特小姐，我——我见她一面要紧得很。”他说得近乎挑衅，倒像是料定我不敢问为什么。“那么，您肯替我捎张条子给她吗？”
===Original===
Well, I was willing to carry a note to Maida, so I shivered under the folds of the flapping slicker while he stood with his back to the rain and wind, scribbling hastily on a bit of yellowish paper he pulled from his pocket. He held the paper close to him to protect it from the rain but I noted that it was an unused Western Union telegraph blank.

“There, and thank you, Miss Keate.” He handed me the folded scrap of paper and I slipped it into my pocket.

But at the end of the bridge I turned.

“Why, Mr. Gainsay,” I exclaimed. “I had forgotten. You were to leave this morning!”

His face had lost the youthful look with which he had begged me to take the note to Maida, and had become lined again, and his narrowed eyes were unfathomable under that shadowy hat brim.

“I shall not go for a few days,” he said after a barely perceptible pause. “I can scarcely leave at such a time. Louis was a friend. They have no relatives here. Corole needs someone.”

===Chinese===
好吧，替梅达捎张条子，我是情愿的。于是，裹在猎猎扑打的雨衣褶层里，我瑟瑟发抖；他背挡着风雨，从口袋里抽出一张发黄的纸片，匆匆地写。他把纸拢在身前挡雨，我却看清那是一张没用过的西联电报公司（Western Union）的空白电报纸。

“好了，多谢您，基特小姐。”他把折起来的纸片递给我，我把它塞进口袋。

可走到桥那头，我又回过身。

“哎呀，盖恩赛先生，”我失声道，“我都忘了。您本来是今天早上动身的！”

他脸上那副求我捎条子的年轻神气已经褪尽，又换回一脸纹路；帽檐的暗影下，那双眯起的眼睛深不可测。

“我要过几天再走了。”他略略一顿，短得几乎察觉不出。“这种时候我实在走不开。路易斯是朋友。他们在这里没有亲眷。科罗尔身边需要人。”

===Original===
His disjointed explanation did not please me. I restrained a rather obvious remark as to chaperonage; after all, Huldah was a militant and vigorous enough chaperon to suit the most meticulous Mrs. Grundy.

I daresay, however, that my disapproval was apparent in my expression, for Jim Gainsay added hastily:

“My boat doesn’t sail till next week.”

“Your boat?”

“I’m to sail on the *Tuscania*.”

“Oh,” I said flatly, and there being nothing further I took my way on around the hill.

===Chinese===
他这套前言不搭后语的解释并不能叫我满意。有一句关于“总得有女伴照应”的现成话，我咽了回去；毕竟真论起女伴，赫尔达这般泼辣麻利，只怕最讲究体统的格伦迪太太（Mrs. Grundy，事事苛求礼俗的典故人物）也挑不出错来。

不过我敢说，我的不以为然全写在了脸上，因为吉姆·盖恩赛急忙补了一句：

“我搭的船下星期才开。”

“您的船？”

“我订的是‘塔斯卡尼亚号’（*Tuscania*）的舱位。”

“哦。”我淡淡地应了一声。再没有别的话，我便绕着山坡上了路。

===Original===
The Letheny cottage looked cold and grim as I approached it. Puddles stood along the turf path; the flowers were beaten down by the wind and leaves had blown all over the porch. Huldah answered the bell, her eyes red and swollen and the cap that Corole had forced her to adopt hanging dejectedly over one ear.

“Miss Keate!” she cried. “And so wet!” She took my slicker, holding it so it could not drip on the rug. “Oh, Miss Keate! Such a t’ing! Such a t’ing!” It is only when Huldah is tremendously moved that she forgets her digraphs.

“Yes, it is dreadful, Huldah,” I said. “How is Miss Corole?”

Huldah shrugged her heavy shoulders oddly.

“There she is, in the study.” She motioned toward the door without answering my question and then followed me, her china-blue eyes curious and round like a rabbit’s between their pink rims.

“Oh, it’s you,” said Corole with not very flattering indifference. “For Heaven’s sake, Huldah, take that wet coat to the kitchen. And close the door behind you,” she added viciously. “I suppose you came to offer sympathy,” she went on, moving a pillow to a more comfortable position under her arm. She was half-lying, half-sitting on the big davenport. A fire had been built in the fireplace but had burned down to a few sullen ashes with a red gleam here and there. There was no light in the room beyond the gray, rainy dusk from the windows.

===Chinese===
走近时，莱瑟尼小屋显得又冷又森然。草皮小径旁积着一汪汪雨水；花儿被风打得伏倒在地，落叶吹得满门廊都是。应门的是赫尔达，她两眼红肿，那顶被科罗尔逼着戴上的女佣帽，蔫蔫地搭拉在一只耳朵上。

“基特小姐！”她喊道，“瞧您淋的，这么湿！”她接过我的油布雨衣，特意提着不让它滴水沾了地毯。“哦，基特小姐！出……出这样的事！出这样的事呀！”赫尔达只有激动到极点的时候，才会把她苦练多年的咬舌音丢个精光。

“是啊，太可怕了，赫尔达。”我说，“科罗尔小姐怎么样？”

赫尔达怪模怪样地耸了耸她那厚实的肩膀。

“她在书房里呢。”她朝门那边摆了摆手，没有回答我的问题，随后跟了进来；那双瓷蓝色的眼睛又好奇又圆，像粉红眼圈当中嵌着的一对兔眼。

“哦，是你。”科罗尔说，那股子冷淡劲儿实在算不上恭维。“看在老天的份上，赫尔达，把那件湿衣裳拿到厨房去。出去随手关门。”她又狠声狠气地添了一句。“我猜你是来吊慰的。”她接着说，把一只靠枕挪到臂弯下更舒坦的位置。她半躺半坐在那张大沙发榻上。壁炉里生过火，这会儿已烧成几块阴沉的余烬，间或透出一点红光。屋里没有灯，只有窗外透进来的灰蒙蒙的雨暮。

===Original===
Corole’s hair was disarranged a little from its usual flat gold waves, and her eyes had great dark circles under them, and her face in the ghostly gray light was sallow and drawn. But she was gowned in a coppery-green silk thing that clung smoothly to her rather luxuriant curves. A heavily embroidered Chinese scarf, whose usual place, I recalled, was on the long table near her, had been flung over her feet and somehow, I presume because she glanced obliquely at it and reached surreptitiously to rearrange it, I got the impression that she had hastily flung it over her feet as I came into the room.

“Yes,” I replied gravely. “I am very sorry that this thing has happened.”

“Oh, of course, it is terrible,” she agreed quickly. “The whole thing is simply unspeakable.”

There was little for us to say; I offered the usual remarks; Corole told me that Dr. Letheny’s body would be sent to New Orleans for burial with others of the family and thanked me perfunctorily for my offers of assistance.

===Chinese===
科罗尔平日熨帖的金色波浪发有些散乱了，眼睛底下罩着两大团黑晕，在幽灵似的灰光里，她的脸蜡黄而憔悴。可她身上那袭铜绿色的真丝长衣，仍旧光滑熨帖地裹着她那颇为丰腴的曲线。一条绣工繁重的中式绣花围巾搭在她脚上——我记得它平日是摊在她身旁那张长桌上的；也不知怎的，大概因为她斜着眼扫了它一下、又暗暗伸手去摆弄，我竟得到一种印象：就在我进屋的当口，她才匆匆把它扔上脚去的。

“是。”我郑重地答道，“出了这样的事，我很难过。”

“哦，当然，可怕极了。”她赶忙附和，“整桩事情简直没法说出口。”

我们实在没有多少可说的；我讲了几句照例的慰唁话；科罗尔告诉我，莱瑟尼医生的遗体将运往新奥尔良，同家中其他亲人葬在一处，又对我提出帮忙的话敷衍地道了谢。

===Original===
“Jim Gainsay is staying on for a few days,” she said. “Nice of him but I really don’t see what he can do.” There was a faint ring of resentment in her voice that surprised me. Corole was not a woman to resent masculine company.

“I suppose Huldah is making you comfortable?” I said for lack of something better.

“Oh, yes,” replied Corole discontentedly. “She does as well as usual. She was awfully upset about all this. Jumps every time I speak to her.”

“Would you like someone to come and stay with you for a few days?”

“No,” said Corole sharply. “No. Why should I?”

“Oh—in case of anything—er—happening. I should think you would be a little nervous.” My explanation sounded somewhat lame and I recalled that Corole actually had no idea of the things we had gone through last night. … A swift recollection of that shallow, locked closet in Room 18 came to me and I arose suddenly, moved to another chair, and tried to think of something else.

===Chinese===
“吉姆·盖恩赛要多住几天。”她说，“他倒好心，可我实在看不出他能顶什么用。”她的嗓音里有一丝怨气，让我诧异。科罗尔可不是那种嫌弃男人做伴的女人。

“赫尔达伺候得您还舒适吧？”我没话找话。

“哦，还行。”科罗尔不惬意地答道，“她跟平常一样。这一趟她给吓坏了。我一开口同她说话，她就一哆嗦。”

“要不要请个人来陪您住上几天？”

“不要。”科罗尔厉声说，“不要。我凭什么要人陪？”

“哦——万一有什么——呃——事情发生。依我想，您总该有点心神不定。”我这解释听上去有几分站不住，何况我这才想起，昨夜我们经历的种种，科罗尔其实一概不知。……18号病房（Room 18）里那口上了锁的浅壁橱蓦地闪回我心头，我霍地站起身，挪到另一把椅子上，竭力去想点别的。

===Original===
“… not that Huldah would be any good if something did happen,” Corole was saying. “She would simply pull the covers over her head and shriek. But there’s Jim.” She added the last name grudgingly as if to say, “such as he is,” and lapsed into silence.

“I must get back to the hospital,” I said presently, not seeing that my presence was vital to Corole.

“I don’t suppose they have any idea as to what happened to the radium,” she observed casually as I arose.

“No. I don’t know what to think.”

“It would seem natural to believe that whoever killed Mr. Jackson and—er—Louis—did so in order to get the radium.”

===Chinese===
“……真出了事，赫尔达也顶不了用。”科罗尔正说着，“她只会把被子往头上一蒙，扯着嗓子尖叫。不过还有吉姆。”末了那个名字，她添得老大不情愿，仿佛在说“聊胜于无”，说完便沉默下来。

“我得回医院去了。”过了一会儿我说；看不出我的在场对科罗尔有什么要紧。

“他们大概还摸不着那批镭的下落吧。”我起身时，她随口说道。

“摸不着。我也不知道该作何想法。”

“照理说，无论是谁杀了杰克逊先生和——呃——路易斯——图的都是那批镭。”

===Original===
“So it would seem,” I agreed. “For my part, I have not had time to speculate on possibilities. It is—too shocking.”

“Don’t you think that they will try to trace the radium?”

“I don’t think anything about it,” I replied caustically. Her interest in the radium annoyed me. I felt repelled at her callous lack of grief. Suppose she and Dr. Letheny had not been on the best of terms, nevertheless they were cousins and housemates.

“Well,” she kept on, “it all seems very strange. Didn’t you see or hear a thing while all that was going on?” Her catlike eyes, whose pupils shone large and flat and black in the semi-twilight, flickered over me with interest.

“No,” I said shortly. I did not relish being questioned by Corole Letheny. “If there is nothing I can do for you I am going.”

“No need to be in a hurry,” she said indolently, yawning a little as she moved with a luxuriant stretching of muscles to a more comfortable position among the cushions.

===Chinese===
“看似如此。”我附和道，“至于我，还没工夫去推想种种可能。这事实在——太骇人了。”

“您不觉得他们会设法追查那批镭的下落吗？”

“我什么都不觉得。”我语带讥诮地答道。她对镭这样上心，惹得我心头火起。她那副全无哀恸的凉薄样子叫我反感。就算她同莱瑟尼医生素来不睦，他们终归是表亲，还同住一个屋檐下。

“嗯，”她还不肯罢休，“这一切都蹊跷得很。事情闹出来的时候，您就没瞧见什么、听见什么吗？”她那双猫儿似的眼睛，瞳仁在半明半暗里又大又平又黑，饶有兴味地在我身上溜来溜去。

“没有。”我答得干脆。我可不愿受科罗尔·莱瑟尼的盘问。“要是没有别的吩咐，我就走了。”

“不必急嘛。”她懒洋洋地说，微微打了个呵欠，慵懒地舒展了一下身子，在靠垫间挪到一个更惬意的姿势。

===Original===
“Good night,” I said curtly. “And do have some light!” As I spoke I reached abruptly for the lamp cord, pulled it, and the green light fell on the davenport.

Corole sprang upright with a startled half word, clutched the Chinese scarf and pulled it more securely over her feet.

“Good night,” I said again and left.

Huldah was waiting in the hall. As I took my coat it seemed to me that there was something hesitant in her attitude, as if she wanted to speak to me, but I was in a hurry and furthermore in no mood to condole with her. So I threw the slicker over my shoulders and splashed along the sodden path.

I scarcely noticed the rain, however, nor the cold discomforts of the path. When I entered the south wing and slipped quietly along its hushed length, I was still rotating in my mind a certain question.

===Chinese===
“晚安。”我生硬地说，“可真该点个灯！”说话间，我猛地探手抓住灯绳一拉，绿色的灯光洒落在沙发榻上。

科罗尔惊得半个字卡在喉咙里，霍地坐直，一把攥住那条中式绣花围巾，把脚盖得更严实了些。

“晚安。”我又说了一遍，走了出去。

赫尔达在门厅里候着。我去取衣裳时，总觉得她的姿态里有几分欲言又止，像有话要对我说；可我赶时间，更没有心思去温言宽慰她。于是我把油布雨衣往肩上一披，蹚着泥水，沿湿透的小径走了。

不过我几乎没留意那雨，也没留意小径上种种又冷又难受的滋味。待我走进南翼，悄悄滑过那长长的一段静默走廊时，心里还在翻来覆去转着一个问题。

===Original===
When the light had flashed on there in Dr. Letheny’s study, I had caught a brief but distinct view of Corole’s slippers. They were beautiful pumps, high-heeled bronze kid with dainty, cut-steel buckles. But they were mud-stained and sodden with moisture and had wet leafmold clinging to them.

Where had Corole Letheny gone that afternoon? What errand had been so urgent that she had gone out of the house through the rain and storm in such haste that she had not had time to remove those dainty slippers?

Facing my own white, tired face in the mirror, I pushed my loosened hair together, removed little torn pieces of leaves from it and righted my cap. My shoes were soaked, so I changed them. Premonitory pangs of neuralgia began to shoot over my left temple, and I wished that I had not stood so long in the rain talking to Jim Gainsay.

With the thought came memory of the note with which I had been entrusted and I planned to give it to Maida at dinner; the bell was just ringing for the meal, then.

===Chinese===
方才在莱瑟尼医生的书房里，灯亮起的一瞬，我把科罗尔脚上的拖鞋看了个短暂却分明的正着。那是双漂亮的浅口鞋，铜色小山羊皮的细高跟，缀着精巧的切钢饰扣。可鞋上溅满泥污，湿得透透的，还沾着湿漉漉的腐叶土。

那天下午，科罗尔·莱瑟尼究竟去了哪里？什么样的差事那样十万火急，让她顶着风雨出门，急得连脚上那双精巧的鞋子都来不及换下？

对着镜中自己那张苍白倦怠的脸，我把散乱的头发拢拢好，摘去发间几片碎叶，扶正了帽子。鞋已经湿透，我换了一双。神经痛的前兆开始一下一下窜过我左侧太阳穴，我懊悔不该在雨里同吉姆·盖恩赛站那么久、说那么多。

想着这事，我便记起受托捎带的那张条子，打算晚饭时交给梅达；那时，开饭的铃声刚刚响起。

===Original===
In my abstraction I had worn the borrowed slicker to my room; as I started down to the dining room I threw it over my arm. Idly wondering whose coat I had appropriated I ran my hand into a pocket, drawing out a man’s handkerchief. It was large and white and had no distinguishing marks on it. But there was a faint scent—I pressed the square of linen to my nose, sniffed—and sniffed again with quickened interest. Faint but unmistakable, the scent of ether emanated from its folds.

I stopped midway on the stairs, stared at the thing and deliberately went through the other pockets. There was nothing more to be found; no identifying label or initials on the whole garment.

One yellow slicker is very much like another, and search though I did I found no means of discovering its owner. I felt, however, that I should like to have the ether smell clinging to that handkerchief explained. Possibly if I returned it to the rack and watched to see who came for it I should learn, at least, the identity of its owner. Thinking that no one would call for it during the dinner hour, the quietest time of the whole day, I replaced the handkerchief and hung the slicker on the hook from which I had taken it, and went down to dinner. But when I returned some fifteen minutes later, after hurrying over my dinner, the slicker was gone and I had not the faintest idea as to who had removed it.

===Chinese===
心不在焉之间，我竟把借来的油布雨衣穿回了自己房间；下楼去餐室时，我把它搭在手臂上。信手盘算着不知抓的是谁的衣裳，我把手伸进一只口袋，掏出一方男人的手帕。手帕又大又白，没有任何可资辨认的记号。可是有一缕淡淡的气味——我把那方亚麻帕子按到鼻子上，嗅了嗅——又兴味陡增地嗅了第二回。淡淡的，却错不了，那褶皱里散发出来的，是乙醚的气味。

我在楼梯中段站住，盯着那东西看，又存心把其余的口袋挨个翻了一遍。再无所获；整件衣裳上没有一条可认主的标签，也没有一个姓名缩写。

黄色的油布雨衣件件都长得相像，我虽翻了又翻，还是找不出认主的法子。不过，那股沾在手帕上的乙醚气味，我倒很想讨个解释。兴许，把它放回衣架，盯着看谁来认领，我至少能弄清它主人的身份。心想晚饭时分是全天最清静的时候，不会有人来取，我便把手帕放回原处，把雨衣挂回我取它的那只挂钩，下楼吃饭去了。可等我匆匆扒完饭、一刻来钟后转回去，那件油布雨衣已经不翼而飞，是谁拿走的，我连一丝一毫的眉目也没有。

===Original===
I gave the note from Jim Gainsay to Maida when I met her in the hall outside the dining room and had the dubious satisfaction of seeing her crimson vividly as she read it. The crimson, however, was succeeded by a pallor that went to her lips as she finished reading the few sentences, and during the meal she kept her eyes steadfastly on her plate and ate practically nothing. And shortly after dinner, happening to be standing near an east window, I saw a slim, shadowy figure, crowned in a white cap, winding its way into the apple orchard. Something after seven o’clock, when I was catching forty winks in my own room, Maida came in. The soft frame of black hair around her face had little beads of mist caught in it and I did not doubt that Jim Gainsay had succeeded in seeing her.

She did not mention him, however, but fussed around the room for a while, playing with the manicure things I had left on the dressing-table top, flipping through the leaves of the last *Surgical News*, and generally behaving as a woman does whose thoughts are elsewhere. She even picked up my tool kit, commenting on the curved bandage scissors and shining forceps and playing idly with the tiny plunger of my own hypodermic set.
