I shook myself free from such morbid reflections, said a brusque goodbye to Corole, and left. Maida went with me, and somewhere along the path Jim Gainsay turned up.

===Chinese===
恰巧他说这话时，我的目光正落在科罗尔身上，于是看见她柔软的褐色手指把捏着的那块杏仁饼攥得粉碎，直到它散落在茶具推车上，成了一小堆敷着糖粉的甜屑。我飞快地看了看奥利里，可他的目光显然正落在壁炉里那段木柴上。

那一刻真叫人难堪，就在那间屋子里——沿墙的书籍、壁龛里的三角钢琴，无不鲜活地诉说着莱瑟尼医生。我们「全体」都要被传唤作证了。而几天前的那个夜晚，我们还坐在这间屋里，听那双强健的白手弹奏《升c小调前奏曲》——那双手，再也不会触碰钢琴了。

我甩脱这些阴郁的念头，向科罗尔草草道了别，便离开了。梅达与我同行，走到小径半途，吉姆·盖恩赛又冒了出来。

===Original===
As the path narrowed under the trees and I preceded the other two, I am sure I heard Jim Gainsay say rather huskily to Maida:

“I had to see you alone. You must do as I say. It is import—”

“Sh! I know!”

===Chinese===
小径在树下渐渐收窄，我走在他们两个前头，我确信自己听见吉姆·盖恩赛用相当沙哑的嗓音对梅达说：

“我必须单独见你。你必须照我说的做。这事很要——”

“嘘！我知道！”

===Original===
“Try to see it my way.” (This in a still more urgent voice.) “It is dangerous to—”

“Hush!” she interrupted sharply again.

And just then I think that Maida stumbled over a branch that had blown across the path. At any rate I heard a quick motion and a sort of gasp and then Maida said rather breathlessly: “That branch—I nearly fell.” And I turned in time to see Jim Gainsay pick up the stick, bow to it gravely and say: “Thank you, old fellow,” before he tossed it off into the orchard. At which Maida turned quite pink and Jim Gainsay gave her a long look and laughed rather shakily.

===Chinese===
“你得试着按我的法子想。”（这一句声音愈发急迫。）“那样做很危险，会——”

“住口！”她又厉声打断。

想来就在那时，梅达被一根吹落在小径上的树枝绊了一下。总之，我听见一阵急促的窸窣和半声惊喘，接着梅达有些气喘吁吁地说：“那根树枝——我差点摔倒。”我回过头，正赶上看见吉姆·盖恩赛拾起那根枝条，朝它一本正经地鞠了一躬，道了声“多谢了，老伙计”，随即将它远远抛进果园。梅达登时红透了脸，吉姆·盖恩赛久久望了她一眼，笑得有些发颤。

===Original===
Then we were at the south entrance and Gainsay swung on his heel with a brief “Good night.”

And it was not fifteen minutes later that I glanced through the society section of the paper that someone had left on the chart desk, and my eyes fell on a small news item:

> Mrs. J. C. Allen left Tuesday of this week for New York City. She sailed on the *Tuscania*, Saturday night, June ninth.

On the *Tuscania*, Saturday, June ninth. That was yesterday. And I was positive that Gainsay had said the *Tuscania*.

===Chinese===
随后我们便到了南门，盖恩赛脚跟一转，丢下一句简短的“晚安”。

而不过一刻钟之后，我翻看有人落在病历台上的那份报纸的社交版，目光便落在一则小新闻上：

> J·C·艾伦太太于本周二离埠前往纽约市。她乘坐*塔斯卡尼亚号*，于六月九日，星期六夜间启航。

*塔斯卡尼亚号*，六月九日，星期六。那就是昨天。而我确信不疑：盖恩赛说的船，正是*塔斯卡尼亚号*。

## VII The Disappearing Key and Part of an Inquest / 消失的钥匙与验尸审讯（上）

===Original===
“Yes, I saw that this morning,” said a quiet voice beside me. It was Lance O’Leary; I did not know he was near until he spoke. “Our friend Mr. Gainsay seems to be a little confused as to his dates.”

I daresay my eyes reflected a question for he added, leisurely:

“He told me that he intended to sail on the *Tuscania* next week. I see that he told you the same thing. He is not a very discreet young man, else he’d have known that I should look up the *Tuscania*’s sailing date without delay.”

I sighed; all those unpleasant little doubts of Jim Gainsay were returning in full force.

===Chinese===
“是的，今天早上我也看到那条新闻了。”我身旁一个平静的声音说道。是兰斯·奥利里；他不开口，我竟不知他就站在近旁。“咱们那位盖恩赛先生，看来在日子上有点儿犯迷糊。”

想必我的眼里流露出了疑问，因为他慢条斯理地接着说：

“他告诉我，他打算下星期搭*塔斯卡尼亚号*出海。我瞧他对您说的也是同一套。这位年轻人可不太谨慎，否则他就该料到，我准会马不停蹄地去查*塔斯卡尼亚号*的启航日期。”

我叹了口气；对吉姆·盖恩赛那许多不愉快的小小疑心，又全须全尾地涌了回来。

===Original===
“If he has the radium, it is not in his room in the Letheny cottage,” said O’Leary meditatively.

“How do you know?” I inquired stupidly.

“We have searched the rooms and personal belongings of each of those present at that dinner party last Thursday night.”

“What!”

“In fact, I daresay that there is not a room in the whole of St. Ann’s, as well as in the Letheny cottage, that has not been thoroughly ransacked.”

===Chinese===
“就算镭在他手里，那也不在莱瑟尼小屋里他住的那间房。”奥利里若有所思地说。

“您怎么知道？”我傻乎乎地问。

“上星期四晚上出席那场晚宴的每一个人，我们都搜过了他们的房间和随身物品。”

“什么！”

“老实说，整个圣安医院，再加上莱瑟尼小屋，恐怕没有一个房间不曾被人彻底翻检过。”

===Original===
I ran my tongue over dry lips. This was getting down to work with a vengeance.

“Why?” I stammered.

There was a glimmer of impatience in his eyes.

“For the radium, of course. Surely you did not think we were going to let it get away from us without a struggle.”

There was a moment or two of silence during which I studied the polished glass surface of the desk before me without seeing it.

===Chinese===
我用舌尖舔了舔发干的嘴唇。这一回，人家可是来真格的了。

“为什么？”我结结巴巴地问。

他眼里掠过一丝不耐。

“自然是为了镭。您总不至于以为，我们会不加挣扎就任它从手里溜走吧。”

沉默了一两分钟，我盯着面前台面上光洁的玻璃出神，却什么也没看进去。

===Original===
“Did you ask Huldah about Miss Letheny’s errand through the rain last Friday afternoon?” inquired O’Leary after a contemplative pause.

“Yes.” I told him in a few words the little that Huldah had told me. “And there is something else I have discovered,” I went on miserably. “I’ve got to tell you, though I must say I do not want to do so. It is—that morphine. The morphine that killed Mr. Jackson, you know. I—I know where it came from!”

“You—what!” O’Leary was for once startled out of his usual composure.

“I know where it came from,” I repeated reluctantly. “At least, I think that I do. You see—there is morphine missing from our south wing drug supply.”

===Chinese===
“赫尔达那边，您问过莱瑟尼小姐上星期五下午冒雨出门办的事了吗？”奥利里沉吟片刻，问道。

“问了。”我把赫尔达对我讲的那一星半点，三言两语告诉了他。“另外，我还发现了一件事，”我苦着脸接着说，“我非得告诉您不可，尽管我实在不想说。就是——那吗啡的事。您知道，就是害死杰克逊先生的那剂吗啡。我——我知道它是从哪儿来的！”

“您——什么！”奥利里这可是头一回，被他一贯的镇定甩出了轨。

“我知道它是从哪儿来的，”我不情愿地重复了一遍。“至少，我自以为知道。您瞧——我们南翼的药品储备里，短少了吗啡。”

===Original===
I had to tell him the whole thing, of course, under his searching questions and no less searching gaze, and even explain our system of keeping account of the drugs. He had to see the drug room and the charts and the records for himself. It was while I was showing him the drawer in which the morphine was kept, that I made my regrettable slip about the hypodermic syringe.

I had started to show him how the needles were fitted into the small mechanism, and I reached for a hypodermic syringe. It turned out to be my own.

“This one is mine,” I said thoughtlessly, fitting the slim, hollow needle into the tiny instrument. “The other one that we were using disapp—” I stopped so suddenly that my breath came out in an explosive little pop and O’Leary’s face hardened slightly. It was an expression that I was growing to recognize.

“You may as well finish. So the other one ‘disapp’-eared, did it? When and how? Whose was it? There is still one in the drawer. What about the one that disappeared?”

===Chinese===
自然，在他刨根问底的诘问和同样刨根问底的目光之下，我不得不把整件事和盘托出，甚至连我们登记药品的账目制度也得讲解一番。药品间、病历单和各项记录，他都要亲眼过目。正是在我指给他看存放吗啡的那个抽屉时，我失言了，铸下那桩令人懊悔的疏失——事关那支皮下注射器。

我刚要演示针头是怎么装进那具小机件的，便伸手去取一支皮下注射器。拿到手的，偏偏是我自己那一支。

“这支是我的。”我心不在焉地说着，把那根细长的空针装进那具小小的器械里。“我们先前一直在用的那一支，不知去向——”我猛地收住话头，收得太急，一口气噗地迸了出来，奥利里的脸也微微一沉。这种神情，我已渐渐认得熟了。

“不如说完吧。这么说，另一支是‘不知去向’了，嗯？什么时候的事？怎么没的？那是谁的？抽屉里可还留着一支呢。不见了的那一支，又怎么讲？”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” I said flatly. “Then, you see, we take the sterile water and measure the liquid into—”

O’Leary looked at his watch.

“I haven’t much time,” he said pleasantly. “But I have enough time to wait right here until you tell me about the hypodermic syringe that disappeared. Or if necessary I can dog your footsteps the rest of the night, reiterating my question at frequent and embarrassing intervals. Of course, I can have the whole hospital searched extensively and every hypodermic needle accounted for, especially if missing. I can follow you to your meal—isn’t that the bell?—and keep on asking you.” He added meditatively: “I suppose it might cause considerable interest among the other nurses.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道。”我没好气地说。“接着，您瞧，我们取过无菌水，把药液量进——”

奥利里看了看表。

“我的时间不多，”他和颜悦色地说，“可要在这儿干等，等您讲出那支不见了的皮下注射器的下落，时间倒还绰绰有余。必要时呢，我也可以在今夜余下的时间里寸步不离地跟着您，隔三差五、专拣叫人难堪的当口，把我的问题重问一遍。当然，我还可以把整座医院兜底大搜一遍，把每一支皮下注射针都查个水落石出，尤其是丢了的那几支。我还能跟着您去用膳——听，这不是开饭铃吗？——一边跟一边问。”他若有所思地添了一句：“我想，这准会在其他护士中间引起相当大的兴趣。”

===Original===
I regarded him furiously. The thing was that he would be quite capable of doing just that. I began to understand the force of the words of the chief of police when he had said—“Once Lance O’Leary gets his teeth into anything, it is as good as done.”

“I suppose you’ll have to know sometime, anyway,” I said sulkily.

The flicker in his gray eyes was like a ripple across a very calm, deep lake.

“You are right, Miss Keate. So why not tell me now?”

===Chinese===
我怒冲冲地瞪着他。糟糕的是，这种事他干得出来，还干得毫不含糊。我开始领会到警察局长那句话的分量了——“兰斯·奥利里咬上一口的事，就等于办完了。”

“反正这事您早晚总得知道。”我绷着脸说。

他那双灰眼睛里掠过一丝微光，好似极静、极深的湖面上一圈涟漪。

“您说得对，基特小姐。那么，何不现在就告诉我呢？”

===Original===
Well, to make a long story short, I told him of the missing hypodermic, which after all, was little enough: barely the fact that Maida’s syringe had been removed and my own substituted, but this without my knowledge. And that Maida had had the opportunity to take my own, and if she had wished to use it, all that she needed to do was ask me for it.

“Which she conspicuously did not do,” commented O’Leary. “Oh, by the way, Miss Keate, have you ever attended an inquest?”

“No.”

“Well, don’t get bothered. Our coroner is a decent old fellow but he does love to be pompous. Just answer what he asks, tell your story as briefly as possible and don’t—er—volunteer anything. You see there are some things that you and I know that will not come up at the inquest.”

===Chinese===
好吧，长话短说，我把注射器丢失的事告诉了他；说到底，这事本身也实在有限：不过是梅达那支注射器被人取走、换上了我的一支，而这番调换，我毫不知情。至于梅达，她是有机会取走我那支的，倘若她要用，只消开口向我要就是了。

“而她偏偏没有这么做。”奥利里点评道，“哦，对了，基特小姐，您出席过验尸审讯吗？”

“没有。”

“嗯，别犯怵。我们的验尸官是个正派的老先生，就是爱摆谱。他问什么您答什么，把您的经过讲得越简短越好，千万别——呃——主动多说什么。您知道，有些事您和我心里有数，是不会在验尸审讯上提起的。”

===Original===
“You mean—they would warn the guilty one?”

He nodded briefly as he turned away.

Maida was already in the south wing when I rounded the right angle of the corridor leading from the main office, at exactly twelve o’clock that night.

“I locked the south door,” she said, hanging the key on its customary nail above the desk.

“That was right,” I approved, glancing over the charts. It appeared that Eleven’s digestive apparatus was still doing business at the old stand, so to speak, but otherwise all was well and I settled briskly into the business of second watch.

===Chinese===
“您是说——提了反倒会给有罪的人通风报信？”

他短短点了点头，转身走了。

那天夜里整十二点，我拐过从总办公室引出的那条走廊的直角弯，梅达已经先到了南翼。

“南门我锁上了。”她说着，把钥匙挂回病历台上方那枚惯常的钉子上。

“这就对了。”我赞许道，顺手把病历单翻看了一遍。看那情形，十一号的消化机器，可以说，照旧在老铺子里照常营业，不过其余一切安好，我便麻利地着手第二班的分内事务。

===Original===
Midnight temperatures had scarcely been taken, however, when Olma Flynn developed a sick headache, worse when I was within hearing distance, and had to be excused from duty. We didn’t actually need the extra help, as Maida and I had been accustomed to care for the whole wing ourselves, but nevertheless it was a little annoying, especially as, about two o’clock, the little student nurse burned her wrist over the gas flame in the diet kitchen, and the burn had to be salved extensively and the nurse sent to bed with an aspirin tablet.

Thus Maida and I found ourselves alone in the south wing for the first time since those terrifying events of Thursday night. This precarious situation was a matter that was, I think, predominant in our thoughts but neither of us mentioned it; we even manufactured an artificial sort of—not gaiety, that would be asking too much—but of brisk attention to work and a determined avoidance of conversation that might lead back to things we were anxious to forget.

All went well, in spite of our hidden fears, until about three o’clock. I was pouring out a small dose of bromide for Three, who had made up her mind not to sleep that night and naturally was not doing so, when Maida opened the door of the drug room.

Her face was so ghastly white that at first glimpse of it my hand began to tremble and the medicine poured all over the spoon. Blindly I set the bottle down.

===Chinese===
然而，午夜体温还没量完，奥尔玛·弗琳就犯了偏头痛，而且我一走到听得见的地方，她便头疼得更凶，只得准她下岗。其实我们并不真缺那双手——这一整片病房翼，梅达和我本来就惯于自己照管——可这终究有点叫人恼火，尤其到了两点来钟，这位小小的学生护士又在配膳室的煤气火焰上烫了手腕，烫伤处得大涂其药膏，人还得被打发回床上去，外带一片阿司匹林。

就这样，自星期四夜里那几桩骇人的变故以来，梅达和我头一回单独守在南翼。这步悬悬乎乎的处境，我想，在我们两人心里都占了上风，可谁也不提；我们甚至还装出一种做出来的——倒也谈不上快活，那未免要求过高——而是一种利利索索埋头做事的劲头，并且打定主意，绕开一切可能引回那些我们急于忘掉的事情的话题。

尽管各自心里藏着恐惧，一切倒也顺遂，直到三点来钟。三号打定了主意那晚偏不睡觉，自然也就没有睡。我正给她倒一小剂溴化物，梅达推开了药品间的门。

她的脸白得那么骇人，第一眼瞥见，我的手就开始发抖，药水全泼在了匙子外面。我懵懵地把药瓶放下。

===Original===
“What is it?”

“There’s something in Room 18!” she gasped through ashy lips.

“Room 18!”

“Just now—I saw something go into that room from the corridor!”

“Someone—is sleepwalking.” I grasped at the first rationality that presented itself.

===Chinese===
“出什么事了？”

“18号病房（Room 18）里有东西！”她灰白的嘴唇里挤出一口气来。

“18号病房！”

“刚刚——我看见有个东西从走廊进了那间病房！”

“是谁——在梦游吧。”我一把抓住了最先冒出来的那个说得通的解释。

===Original===
After the light in the diet kitchen the long corridor seemed peculiarly dark and shadowy and the green light over the chart desk was miles away. It never occurred to me to call for help, and we sped along toward that dark end of the wing that we had good cause to fear.

But we stopped stock still as we came close enough to see the door of Room 18, and a cold shiver crept up from my back.

The door of Room 18 was standing wide open!

It had not been opened, so far as I knew, since the police had left that room. It had been shunned by all the nurses. Who had opened it? Who would dare open it?

Who was inside that dark place?

===Chinese===
从亮着灯的配膳室出来，这条长走廊显得格外黑、格外多暗影，病历台上那盏绿灯隔着几里地似的遥不可及。我压根儿没想到喊人，我们便朝这侧翼幽黑的一头飞步赶去——那一头，我们有十足的理由害怕。

可是等奔到看得见18号病房（Room 18）房门的地方，我们猛地钉在了原地，一股寒气从我背上爬了上来。

18号病房的门，正敞得大开！

据我所知，自警察撤离那间病房之后，那扇门就不曾开过。所有的护士都对它退避三舍。是谁开的门？谁敢开那扇门？

那黑暗的地方，里面是谁？
===Original===
A long, shuddering sigh came from Maida beside me and I felt her cold hand grip my wrist. The contact nerved me and I did what, I afterward realized, was a very foolish thing.

I took a few steps forward, advanced to the very door of that grisly room, reached a shaking arm through the open doorway, groped for the electric-light button, found and pressed it.

The cold white dome on the ceiling flooded the room with light.

There was nothing out of the way to be seen. There were the plain dresser, the bedside table, two chairs, the folded burlap screen and the high, narrow bed—nothing else. Something caught in my throat as I glanced at the bed and toward the closet doors.

===Chinese===
我身旁的梅达发出一声悠长的、发颤的叹息，我觉出她一只冰凉的手攥住了我的手腕。这一触反倒给我壮了胆，我干了一件——后来我才明白——非常愚蠢的事。

我向前挪了几步，一直走到那间阴森病房的门口，一条打颤的手臂从敞开的门洞里探进去，摸索电灯的按钮，摸着了，按了下去。

天花板上那盏冷白的圆顶灯，把满屋照得通亮。

看不出有什么异样。素净的矮柜、床头小几、两把椅子、折拢的粗麻布屏风，还有那张又高又窄的床——再没有别的了。我朝床上、又朝壁橱的门望了一眼，喉咙口像被什么哽了一下。

===Original===
“The—closet—” breathed Maida at my side. “Oh! You are not going to open that!” as I took a more decisive step forward.

It was no easy thing to do, for I knew well that those shallow closets were yet large enough to hold—what one of them had held.

They were both unlocked this time. And there was nothing in them!

I turned to Maida, whose white face had been beside me during the ordeal. Without saying a word we retreated to the corridor.

===Chinese===
“那——壁橱——”梅达在我身旁屏息般地低语。“哦！你可别是要开它吧！”——我这时正迈出更坚决的一步。

这可不是桩容易的事，因为我很清楚，那几口浅浅的壁橱，毕竟也大得足以容纳——其中一口曾经容纳过的东西。

这一回，两扇壁橱都没上锁。而里面什么也没有！

我转向梅达，这场磨难里她那张白脸一直在我旁边。我们一言不发，退回了走廊。

===Original===
“Are you sure you saw something?” I asked, my voice hoarse.

“I am positive,” whispered Maida. “You see, I was just answering Fourteen’s light, which brought me fairly near to Eighteen. I came out the door and was starting down toward the chart desk when something—I don’t know what—some rustle or sound, perhaps, made me turn around, facing this way, so I could see the south door. And I was just in time to see a sort of movement at the door of Eighteen.” Her hands went to her throat as she spoke and I did not feel very comfortable myself.

“It couldn’t have been one of the patients?” I murmured.

“No! There isn’t a one of them who is able to walk.”

“Then who—”

“Or—*what*—” said Maida.

===Chinese===
“你确定看见了什么？”我哑着嗓子问。

“千真万确。”梅达悄声道，“是这样的，我正应十四号的铃，那正好把我带到离18号病房挺近的地方。我出了病房门，正要朝病历台那头走，忽然有什么东西——我说不清是什么——也许是一阵窸窣或响动，叫我回过头来，脸朝着这一边，正好望得见南门。而我恰恰来得及看见18号病房门口有什么东西动了一动。”她说着，双手抚上了自己的喉咙，而我自己的心里也不很自在。

“会不会是病人里的哪一个？”我喃喃道。

“不会！他们没一个能下地走路的。”

“那么是谁——”

“或者说——是*什么*——”梅达说。

===Original===
A remnant of common sense saved me, I think, from stark terror. I took a firmer hold on my imagination.

“Nonsense,” I spoke decidedly but still, for some reason, in a whisper. “There are no such things as—as—I mean to say, the shadow you saw was either an optical illusion or a living, breathing person.”

“Certainly,” agreed Maida, adding inconsistently: “I don’t see how a living person could have got past us, through this long corridor without one of us seeing him—*it*.”

My eyes fell on the south door near at hand; the tiny panes of glass winked blackly at me as I crossed to it, grasped the brass latch and pulled. The door swung slowly open, letting in a current of cold, mist-laden air.

===Chinese===
我想，是残存的一点常识把我从十足的恐怖里救了出来。我把自己的想象力攥得更紧了些。

“胡说。”我说得斩钉截铁，可不知怎么，仍旧压着嗓子，像耳语似的。“世上没有那种东西——那种——我是说，你看见的那个影子，要么是眼睛的错觉，要么就是个活生生、会喘气的人。”

“当然。”梅达应道，随即又自相矛盾地添了一句：“我就是不明白，一个大活人怎么能打我们身边溜过去，穿过这么长一条走廊，我们俩却谁也没瞧见他——瞧见*它*。”

我的目光落到近旁的南门上；我一侧身走过去，那一格格小玻璃黑黢黢地朝我眨着眼。我抓住黄铜门闩，往外一拉。门悠悠地荡开了，放进一股裹着雾气的冷风。

===Original===
“There, you see?” I said to Maida. “Only a real, material thing needs a door to go through.”

Maida was looking at me strangely.

“I don’t see that that helps matters any,” she said. “I locked that door myself, tonight. It just proves that someone was actually here. That the murderer is still about the hospital.”

“Not necessarily,” I said, though my heart was pounding in my throat. “You are sure you locked it?”

“Positive.”

===Chinese===
“这你瞧见了？”我对梅达说。“只有实实在在、有形有体的东西，才需要打门里走。”

梅达异样地望着我。

“我看这于事无补，”她说，“今晚是我亲手锁的这扇门。这恰恰证明，方才真有人到这儿来过。那凶手还在医院里外转悠着呢。”

“未必。”我说，虽然我的心咚咚地撞在喉咙口。“你肯定锁上了？”

“肯定。”

===Original===
“And you hung the key there on the nail above the chart desk?”

“Yes.”

We both swung hastily around. At the other end of the shadowy corridor gleamed the green-shaded light over the desk. With one accord we started toward it.

The key was not on the nail above the desk!

But even as our frightened gaze took in that amazing fact my eyes fell on the glass top of the desk. There on its shining surface lay the key. We stared at it for some time before our eyes met. Then I picked up the key, my fingers seeming to shrink from the cold, clammy metal, returned to the south door, locked it securely and put the key in my pocket.

===Chinese===
“那钥匙呢，你挂回病历台上方那枚钉子上了？”

“挂了。”

我们不约而同，霍地双双转过身去。走廊那幽暗的另一头，病历台上罩着绿罩的灯亮着一点微光。我们一条心似的朝它走去。

钉子上没有钥匙！

可就在我们惊惶的目光把这个惊人的事实收进眼里的当口，我的目光落到了台面的玻璃板上。就在那亮闪闪的台面上，躺着那把钥匙。我们盯着它看了好一阵，才彼此对望了一眼。随后我拾起钥匙——指尖仿佛在那又冷又黏的金属跟前畏缩了一下——回到南门，把它牢牢锁上，把钥匙揣进了口袋。

===Original===
And as I did so, a thought struck me. The person who had got into the wing by means of the south door must also have got out again. He couldn’t have gone through the corridor and south door for Maida was, by that time, in the hall.

The light was still glowing in Room 18, and I crossed the room, not without a queer feeling in the region of my knees, as if they would give way with me, without notice. Sure enough, the window was unlocked; it was even open a fraction of an inch at the bottom, though the screen, which has a spring snap, was closed.

I pushed the window the rest of the way down, locked it, not without an unpleasant impression that something was out in that gleaming darkness back of the window pane watching my every move, turned off the light, and closed the door again. Maida was standing in the corridor and we walked slowly toward the more cheerful region of the chart desk and diet kitchen.

===Chinese===
而就在我做这一切的时候，一个念头击中了我。那个借南门进来的人，必定也还得再出去。他不可能穿过走廊、再走南门出去，因为到那时候，梅达已经在走廊里了。

18号病房（Room 18）的灯还亮着，我便穿过那间屋子——膝盖那一带不是没有一种异样的感觉，仿佛它们随时会不打个招呼，就叫我瘫倒下去。果然，窗子没上锁；窗底甚至敞开了一线，不过装着弹簧卡扣的纱窗是扣上的。

我把窗子推到底，上了锁——这时心里掠过一丝不快的印象：窗玻璃后头那片闪着微光的黑暗里，仿佛有什么东西正望着我的一举一动——然后关了灯，又把房门重新掩上。梅达站在走廊里，我们慢慢朝病历台和配膳室那片较为明快的去处走去。

===Original===
“I suppose,” she mused at length, “that someone could have taken the key from the nail and returned it when we were in Room 18.”

“But who? It would have to be someone in St. Ann’s. And that is unthinkable.”

“There are likely ways in and out of St. Ann’s,” she said finally, and with that a signal clicked somewhere. I recalled Three who would be in a tantrum by this time, and we separated.

We were very busy for the rest of the night, and dawn was never so welcome a sight. But during those slow hours I came to the conclusion that there were only two things we knew without doubt. The key to the south door had been removed from the nail and left on top of the chart desk, and the sinister door of Room 18 had been opened and left open.

===Chinese===
“我想，”她末了出了神似的说，“兴许是有人从钉子上取下了钥匙，趁我们在18号病房（Room 18）里的时候，又把它放了回去。”

“可是谁呢？那就得是圣安医院里头的人了。这叫人没法想。”

“圣安医院进出的路子，只怕多着呢。”她末了这么说了一句；正说着，哪里的信号灯咔哒响了一声。我想起三号这时候准在闹脾气了，我们便分头去了。

那一夜余下的工夫我们忙得很，而黎明从不曾像这样叫人望眼欲穿。可就在那几个慢慢熬过去的钟头里，我得出一个结论：我们确凿无疑知道的事，只有两件。南门的钥匙被人从钉子上取下，搁在了病历台的台面上；还有，18号病房那扇不祥的房门，被人打开过，而且敞着没关。

===Original===
Who had done this and why was a matter of conjecture, and I resolved to say nothing of it save to Lance O’Leary. I should leave it to O’Leary.

Leave it to O’Leary! I had so far recovered from my fright that I smiled faintly at the phrase, but I could have embraced the day nurses when they came on duty, and the rattle of the dishes in the small, rubber-tired dumbwaiter, as it came up with the breakfast trays, sounded like music to my ears.

On the way to the basement for breakfast, I had time for a word or two with Maida.

“We’ll say nothing about it, save to O’Leary,” I murmured in a low voice and she nodded, just as Miss Dotty joined us with one of her insufferably bright good mornings.

===Chinese===
这是谁干的、又是为什么，全凭人猜了。我打定主意，此事除兰斯·奥利里之外，对谁都不提。就交给奥利里去办吧。

交给奥利里去办！我已从那场惊吓里缓过来不少，想到这个说法，不由得淡淡一笑。可等日班护士们来接班时，我简直想拥抱她们每一个人；那部带橡胶轮的小食梯载着早餐托盘上来时，碟盘的磕碰声在我耳朵里，竟如同音乐一般。

去地下室吃早饭的路上，我得了空，同梅达说上一两句。

“这事咱们谁也不说，只告诉奥利里。”我低声含糊地说，她点了点头——恰在此时，多蒂小姐加入了我们，道了一声她那灿烂得叫人消受不起的早安。

===Original===
Miss Dotty keeps a book at her bedside, entitled *Every Day a Sunny Day*, and memorizes verses from it. Her verse that morning was:

> If you’re lonely, sad and blue,
 

 Keep smiling.
 

 Luck will bring you someone true
 

 Who understands and loves just you.
 

 Keep smiling,

which seemed not only inane but downright offensive, as coming from one old maid to another.

---

===Chinese===
多蒂小姐床头放着一本书，题为《天天都是艳阳天》（*Every Day a Sunny Day*），她还常从里面背诵诗句。她那天早晨的诗句是：

> 你若孤寂、忧伤、闷闷不乐，
 

 那就保持微笑。
 

 运气终会给你带来一个人，
 

 他懂得你，也只爱你一个。
 

 那就保持微笑，

这话从一个老姑娘嘴里说给另一个老姑娘听，似乎不但空洞无聊，简直是无礼了。

---

===Original===
The inquest was set for nine thirty. We had an early operation and at eight o’clock prompt I was tying Dr. Balman into his white apron and hood and counting sponges in the operating room. The patient’s appendix proving to be elusive, turning up, in fact, on entirely the wrong side, the operation was more interesting than we had expected. Dr. Balman looked haggard from fatigue and worry, his thin hair and beard were dishevelled and his eyes were hollow, but his hands were steady, if rather slow, and every last detail was thoroughly attended to.

The inquest was held in the nurses’ library in the basement. It is not a cheerful room, particularly on wet, rainy mornings. It was chilly in the place; the whitewashed walls looked cold and bare, even the medical books along the walls had none too happy titles. The linoleum rug caught dismal highlights, the chairs borrowed from the dining room were slippery and uncomfortable, and moisture dripped steadily down the small windows. Someone had turned on the lights but they did not improve matters.

At a little table sat a stout, elderly gentleman, whom I had no trouble identifying as the pompous coroner. He wore a pair of nose-glasses attached to a button on his broad vest with an important black ribbon. The board of directors were ranged near at hand, some of them constituting the jury, which would have surprised me had I not known the weight in politics and otherwise that some of those names carried.

===Chinese===
验尸审讯定在九点半。我们那天一早有台手术，八点整，我正在手术室里替巴尔曼医生系白围裙、戴帽兜，清点纱布。病人的阑尾偏又难找——事实上长在了完全相反的一侧——这台手术比我们预想的还有意思。巴尔曼医生因劳累和忧心而形容憔悴，稀疏的头发和胡须乱蓬蓬的，两眼深陷，但他那双手还算稳当，只是慢了些，每一处细节都照应得一丝不苟。

验尸审讯在地下室的护士图书室里举行。那不是间叫人愉快的屋子，尤其是在落雨的湿冷早晨。屋里冷飕飕的；粉刷的白墙看上去又冷又光秃，连沿墙那些医学书的书名都没几个喜幸的。亚麻油地毡地毯泛着晦暗的光，从饭厅借来的椅子又滑又不舒服，小窗上水汽涔涔直淌。有人开了灯，可也无济于事。

一张小桌子后头坐着一位胖胖的老先生，我一望便认出，正是那位爱摆谱的验尸官。他戴一副夹鼻眼镜，用一条神气十足的黑缎带拴在肥大背心的扣子上。董事会成员们近旁一字排开，其中几位还充任陪审团——若非我深知其中几个名字在政界或其他方面的分量，这原是会叫我吃惊的。

===Original===
Corole Letheny was there in a soft brown frock daringly tailored and very short so that her silk-clad—er—ankles and so forth were much in evidence; she wore a small green hat pulled low over her eyes and carried a large and gorgeously beaded bag which made a spot of vivid colour in that neutral gray room. Huldah, very stiff in her Sunday black silk, sat beside her.

A little way off among a group of nurses sat Maida, her beauty and the distinctive air of breeding in the very lift of her chin making her stand out from the others as if they were only the frame for a picture. Jim Gainsay stood at the back of the room with a group of reporters. He wore an air of ease that was a shade too deliberate; his impenetrable eyes looked at nothing in particular but, I had no doubt, missed not the smallest movement in the room. He was attractive, clean, young, vigorous, but I could have wished him less restrained—less poised—less wary.

There were the staff doctors, of course, talking to Dr. Balman and Dr. Hajek. I was interested to note that a bit of Dr. Hajek’s ruddy colour had deserted him; he said little and his black eyes darted here and there about the room, occasionally lingering upon Corole. Save for those restless eyes he was as unmoved and stolid as was usual with him.

There were several policemen, too, Higgins, the cook and a few curious student nurses sitting with Miss Dotty, who being something of a simpleton took that occasion to shed a few tears, presumably for Dr. Letheny. And there was O’Leary, of course, gray and quiet, sitting near the coroner’s table.

===Chinese===
科罗尔·莱瑟尼也到了，穿一袭棕色软呢连衣裙，剪裁大胆，裙摆极短，害得她那裹着丝袜的——呃——脚踝等等无处遮掩；她戴一顶压得低低的小绿帽，挎一只缀满彩珠的大手袋，在那灰扑扑不辨颜色的屋子里点出一团刺目的艳色。赫尔达浆得硬邦邦的星期日黑绸衣裹在身上，僵僵地坐在她身旁。

稍远处，一群护士中间坐着梅达，她的美貌、还有她下巴一抬之间那股与众不同的教养气，使她在旁人中间脱颖而出，仿佛其余人不过是陪衬一幅画像的画框。吉姆·盖恩赛同一伙记者站在屋子后头。他一身自在从容的气派，只是刻意得略过了头；他那双深不可测的眼睛似看非看，什么都没落在实处，可我毫不怀疑，屋里最小的一动也逃不过它们。他讨人喜欢，干净，年轻，精力充沛，可我但愿他别这么克制——别这么沉着——别这么戒备。

自然还有本院的医师们，正同巴尔曼医生和海耶克医生说话。我留意到，海耶克医生脸上那点红润褪去了几分；他话不多，两只黑眼睛在屋里东扫西扫，间或停在科罗尔身上。除了那双不安分的眼睛，他一如平素，纹丝不动，木然迟钝。

还有几名警察，希金斯，厨师，外加几个好奇的学生护士，同多蒂小姐坐在一处——她多少是个缺心眼儿的，趁这机会掉了几点泪，想必是为莱瑟尼医生掉的。奥利里自然也在，灰衣裳，静悄悄的，坐在验尸官的桌子近旁。

===Original===
It being the one and only inquest I had ever attended (for which I am truly thankful), I was not able to compare it with others and did not know whether the undercurrent of excitement, the low whispers, the white faces, the nervous little movements and darting glances here and there, are typical of all inquests or peculiar to that one.

All at once the coroner put down the papers he had been studying, took off his nose glasses, and began to talk. I did not notice what he said, for at the same moment O’Leary rose quietly and moved toward the back of the room. As he passed me he dropped a small bit of folded paper in my lap. Under the cover of my wide cuff I read the brief message it contained. I read it again; it didn’t seem to make sense, but of course, I was willing to obey the terse request. Just as I slipped the paper into my pocket I heard my name being called and I rose and walked to a chair indicated by the coroner.

After convincing the coroner and the jury that I was actually Sarah Keate, superintendent of the south wing and on duty the night of Thursday, June seventh, I was allowed to proceed.

===Chinese===
那是我此生出席过的唯一一场验尸审讯（为此我真心感恩），无从同别的场合作比较，也就不知道那股暗涌的兴奋、压低的耳语、发白的面孔、神经质的小动作和四处乱飞的目光，是凡验尸审讯皆然，还是独独那一场如此。

验尸官忽然放下一直在研读的文件，摘掉夹鼻眼镜，开口讲话。他说了些什么，我没有留意，因为就在同一刻，奥利里悄然起身，朝屋子后头走去。经过我身旁时，他把一小方折起的纸片丢进我膝头。我借着宽大衣袖的遮掩，读了上面那行简短的字。我又读了一遍；看着不成个所以然，可我自然乐于照办那寥寥数字的嘱托。我刚把纸片塞进口袋，就听见叫到我的名字，我便起身，走到验尸官指给的那把椅子前坐下。

等我说服了验尸官和陪审团，使他们确信我确是萨拉·基特、南翼主管护士、六月七日星期四夜里当班，方才获准往下讲。
===Original===
It was not so difficult as I had feared it would be; I was allowed to tell my story in a brief and straightforward manner. The only time I became confused was when I got to the incident of the arrow-like projectile that had whizzed over my shoulder while I stood for a moment there on the little south porch. It was then, for the first time since the night it occurred, that I recalled the trifling incident, and I was already launched upon it and could not head off the coroner’s questions. I caught a reproachful look from O’Leary but had to continue; however, the coroner’s questions could prove nothing for there was little I could tell of the matter.

The coroner questioned me rather particularly, too, as to the man with whom I collided, but I had expected this and gave guarded replies. He also tried to make me identify the owner of the cigarette case which lay there on the table before him, but I refused to commit myself beyond telling how and when I found the thing.

As I say, it was not difficult—that is, until I reached the actual events leading to the crime. It was then that my voice faltered.

===Chinese===
这并不像我想的那样难；他们容我把经过简短而直截了当地讲了出来。我唯一乱了阵脚的一回，是讲到那支箭也似的飞物——那天夜里我在小门廊上站了片刻，它便嗖地一声擦过我肩头。直到那时，自出事的当夜以来，我才头一回想起这桩无关紧要的小事，话头已经带出，再拦不住验尸官的追问了。我接到了奥利里一道责备的眼色，却只能讲下去；好在验尸官的问题证明不了什么，因为那件事我本来就说不出多少来。

对于那个同我撞了个满怀的人，验尸官问得也相当细，但这我早有防备，答得滴水不漏。他还想叫我认下摆在他面前桌上的那只烟盒的主人，可除了交代我是何时、又是如何捡到这东西的，我拒绝把自己搭进去，多说一个字也不肯。

如我所说，这并不难——直到我讲到引向罪案的那些实际经过。就在那时，我的声音发颤了。

===Original===
“It was while I was sitting there at the chart desk, at exactly one thirty—I had just entered the time on a chart—that I heard a sort of—bang. It sounded like a door closing.” I went on speaking with more and more difficulty. “So I got up and walked along the corridor but the south door was still open. Then I went back to the chart desk and was there when the storm broke and I had to run to close the door and the windows. When I went into Room 18 to close the window I found—” I stuck and had to clear my throat.—“I found that the patient, Mr. Jackson, was dead. The lights had gone out but a flash of lightning lit the room and I felt for his pulse and knew that he was dead. I ran to the diet kitchen, found a candle, and ran back to Eighteen. Miss Day had been closing the windows in the wing and had just got to Room 18 when I returned with the candle. It was after we knew we could do nothing for him that we found the radium had been stolen.”

My testimony continued for some time after that, but I simply answered the coroner’s questions as briefly as possible and volunteered nothing, and presently resumed my seat, feeling that, with the one exception, I had conducted myself creditably.

Then Dr. Balman and Dr. Hajek were called in turn, to testify as to the causes of death, first of Mr. Jackson’s, and later of Dr. Letheny’s. They used technical terms, and told the methods of determining the length of time each had been dead before discovery. It was a difficult half hour for both of them, knowing Dr. Letheny as they had, and they both looked quite exhausted when the coroner had finished with them. Dr. Balman was frankly mopping his high forehead and even Dr. Hajek’s stolidity was shaken, for his eyes darted nervously about him and he retreated to the back of the room, where he lit a cigarette with unsteady hands.

===Chinese===
“当时我正坐在病历台旁——恰是一点半——我刚把时间登上一份病历单——就听见砰的一响，像是关门的声音。”我越往下说，越觉费劲。“于是我起身，沿走廊走了一趟，可南门还开着。随后我回到病历台旁，正坐着，暴风雨就来了，我只好跑去关门关窗。我进18号病房（Room 18）去关窗，发现——”我卡住了，只得清了清嗓子。“我发现病人杰克逊先生已经死了。灯灭了，但一道闪电照亮了病房，我去摸他的脉，知道他死了。我跑回配膳室，找到一支蜡烛，又跑回18号病房。戴小姐当时正把这一翼的窗子逐间关过去，我拿着蜡烛回来时，她刚走到18号病房。等我们明白对他已无能为力之后，才发现镭被偷了。”

此后我的证词又延续了一阵，但我只是尽量简短地回答验尸官的问题，绝不主动多说，不多时便回到自己的座位上，自觉除那一处之外，还算应对得体。

接着，巴尔曼医生和海耶克医生先后被传，就死因作证——先证杰克逊先生的，后证莱瑟尼医生的。他们用了些专门的术语，讲了判定两人各在被发现之前已死亡多长时间的方法。他们两位同莱瑟尼医生素来相识，这半个钟头实在难熬，验尸官问完时，两人都显得精疲力竭。巴尔曼医生索性掏出手帕，抹着他那高高的前额，连海耶克医生那份木然也被撼动了——他两眼不安地东瞟西瞟，退到屋子后头，用不稳的手点了支烟。

===Original===
Then Miss Maida Day was called and as she took the witness chair my hands gripped each other and I watched her with strained attention.

She testified very coolly, though. No, she had not seen Dr. Letheny when he called to visit his patient at twelve thirty. She had been busy in one of the sick rooms. Yes, she had stepped out on the porch for a breath of air. Yes, she had attended the dinner party given by Miss Letheny. The coroner seemed to be supplied with all the topics of conversation of that dinner and Maida agreed imperviously to every one of them, even to the fact that she had said she wanted money.

“I believe your words were ‘I’d give my very soul for money’?” inquired the coroner nastily.

“I think I did say something like that,” said Maida quietly, though a tiny flush mounted to her cheeks. “Of course, I didn’t mean exactly that. One often exaggerates one’s statements.”

The coroner did not comment on that but looked expressively at the jury.

===Chinese===
随后传唤梅达·戴小姐。她走向证人席时，我的双手不由得紧攥在一起，提心吊胆地盯着她。

不过她作证时十分冷静。没有，莱瑟尼医生十二点半来看他的病人时，她没有见到他；她当时正在一间病房里忙着。是的，她曾到门廊上透气。是的，她出席了莱瑟尼小姐的晚宴。那场晚宴上谈过的话头，验尸官仿佛一应俱全、全都得了底，梅达不动声色地一一认了，连她说过自己想要钱这一节，也不例外。

“我记得您的话是‘为了钱，我连自己的魂灵都肯给’？”验尸官刻薄地追问。

“我想我确实说过类似的话，”梅达平静地说，虽然一丝细细的红晕爬上了她的脸颊。“当然，我不完全是那个意思。人说话，总难免夸张。”

验尸官对此不置一词，只意味深长地看了看陪审团。

===Original===
Then she corroborated, under his questions, every detail I had told of our finding of the body of Mr. Jackson and of our subsequent actions. He made his questions very searching and important indeed, and I felt something between a fool and a liar during the process; I am not accustomed to having my word doubted.

“Miss Letheny answered the telephone, when I called for the doctor,” Maida explained, “and said that she couldn’t rouse him, and when I said we must have him immediately she went away from the telephone and when she came back told me that he was not in the house and she didn’t know where he had gone.”

“Then you telephoned to Dr. Balman?”

“Yes.”

“Did he answer immediately?”

“No. I think he must have been asleep. When he did answer I told him simply of Mr. Jackson’s unexpected death and that we could not locate Dr. Letheny.”

===Chinese===
随后，在他的盘问之下，她把我们发现杰克逊先生尸体及此后种种行动的每个细节，都逐一印证了我先前的陈述。他的问题问得着实穷究细诘、煞有介事，那一整个过程里，我觉得自己又像傻子、又像说谎的人；我不习惯让人怀疑我说的话。

“我打电话去找医生时，是莱瑟尼小姐接的，”梅达解释道，“她说她叫不醒他；等我说我们必须立刻找到他，她便离开了电话，回来后对我说，他不在屋里，她也不知道他去了哪儿。”

“随后您就打给了巴尔曼医生？”

“是的。”

“他马上就接了吗？”

“没有。我想他准是睡着了。等他接了，我只告诉他杰克逊先生猝然去世，以及我们找不到莱瑟尼医生。”

===Original===
“About how long was it until Dr. Balman arrived?”

“I’m not sure. I was—agitated naturally. But I should say about fifteen minutes.”

“How was he dressed when he arrived?”

“In—a dinner jacket, I think—and slicker. It was raining, you know.”

“Miss Day, have you lately lost a cuff link?” asked the coroner, without warning.

I was watching Maida closely and saw the little flush that had been in her face drain steadily away; her eyes darkened but did not falter in their steadfast gaze.

“Yes,” she replied quietly.

===Chinese===
“过了大约多久，巴尔曼医生才到？”

“说不准。我自然——心绪很乱。不过我想，大约一刻钟吧。”

“他到的时候，身上穿着什么？”

“穿——我想是一件无尾晚礼服——外头罩着油布雨衣。您知道，那晚下着雨。”

“戴小姐，您近来丢失过一枚袖扣吗？”验尸官猝不及防地发问。

我紧盯着梅达，眼看着她脸上那点淡淡的红晕一丝丝褪尽；她的眼睛暗了下去，可那坚定的凝视并未动摇。

“是的。”她平静地答道。

===Original===
“Is this it?” He placed a small object in her hand that I could not see but had no doubt was the square of lapis.

“It—seems to be,” she said, after a pause during which we others scarcely breathed. “It resembles the one I lost.”

“Do you think you can say that it is your cuff link?” asked the coroner smoothly.

“Why—yes. At least, it is identical with mine.”

“Can you explain its presence in Dr. Letheny’s coat pocket when he was found—dead?”

“No,” said Maida steadily, her steel-blue eyes meeting the coroner’s directly.

===Chinese===
“是这一枚吗？”他把一个小物件放进她手心；我看不见那是什么，但毫无疑问，正是那方青金石。

“这——看着像是，”她说。停顿的当口，我们其余的人都几乎屏住了呼吸。“它跟我丢的那一枚很像。”

“您觉得，您能断言这就是您的袖扣吗？”验尸官圆滑地问。

“嗯——是。至少，它同我那枚一模一样。”

“莱瑟尼医生被人发现——死去的时候，它就在他的上装口袋里。对此您能解释吗？”

“不能。”梅达镇静地说，她那钢蓝色的眼睛直直地迎着验尸官的目光。

===Original===
“When did you discover its loss?”

If possible, Maida went still whiter, and her nostrils took on a pinched look.

“Shortly after I had returned from the porch,” she said steadily enough, but her eyes went to the back of the room for a brief instant.

“How did this get into Dr. Letheny’s possession?” persisted the coroner.

“I do not know. I suppose I—dropped it. Lost it from my cuff, and Dr. Letheny must have—found it.”

===Chinese===
“您是什么时候发现它丢的？”

梅达的脸如果还能更白，此刻便是白到了家，鼻翼也缩出一种紧揪的样子。

“从门廊上回来后不久，”她的语气还够镇定，可她的眼睛有一刹那瞟向了屋子后头。

“那它怎么会落到莱瑟尼医生手里呢？”验尸官穷追不舍。

“我不知道。我想是我——失手掉了。从袖口里掉的，莱瑟尼医生想必是——捡到了。”

===Original===
“In the dark?” inquired the coroner suavely.

Maida flushed again but her chin went higher.

“I do not know.”

He continued to question her at some length but with no success, and finally he dismissed her, with a grudging “Thank you.”

===Chinese===
“摸黑捡的？”验尸官温文尔雅地问。

梅达又红了脸，可她的下巴扬得更高了。

“我不知道。”

他又盘问了她好一阵，却毫无收获，末了总算放过了她，老大不情愿地道了声“谢谢您”。

===Original===
Corole Letheny was the next witness and I settled myself more comfortably in my chair to listen. She was extremely self-possessed, and sat down as gracefully as if she had been paying a call. She looked rather nice, or would have, but for the clear beauty of the face that had just preceded her. Maida’s immaculate uniform, her clear white skin, her amazing blue eyes under their straight black eyebrows, that little, aristocratic air which somehow always surrounded her, made Corole seem a little tarnished, a little tawdry, a little theatrical, in spite of her perfect grooming and her expensive clothing.

By that time the repetition of the details of that oft-referred-to dinner party were growing stale and I did not pay the strictest attention to the first questions of the coroner. I was aroused, though, by hearing him say suavely:

“You will pardon me, Miss Letheny, but were you and Dr. Letheny on the best of terms?”

She stared at him, her yellowish eyes widening and reflecting green lights from her hat brim.

===Chinese===
下一位证人是科罗尔·莱瑟尼，我往椅子里坐得更舒服了些，听她作证。她镇定自若到了极点，落座的姿势优雅从容，仿佛是在出门拜客。她看上去相当不错，或者说本该不错——若不是前头刚经过那张明艳的脸的话。梅达那一尘不染的护士服、光洁白皙的皮肤、直黑眉下那双惊人的蓝眼睛，还有不知怎的总萦绕在她周身的那一点贵族气，衬得科罗尔尽管妆发一丝不乱、衣裳价值不菲，却显得有点走了色，有点俗艳，有点做戏。

到这时，那场被翻来覆去讲了多少遍的晚宴的种种细节，早已讲得发了馊，验尸官开头的问题我也就没怎么细听。可忽然听见他温文地这样问，我不由得竖起了耳朵：

“还望您见谅，莱瑟尼小姐——您和莱瑟尼医生，一向情谊极好吗？”

她瞪着他，微黄的眼睛睁大了，映出帽檐上投下的一泓泓绿光。

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“Following the departure of your guests that night, did you not have a heated disagreement?”

Her eyes slowly left the coroner and went to Huldah in an exceedingly unpleasant gaze.

“I suppose my maid told you that. Yes, we did quarrel. Louis—was not an easy man to get along with.”

“What was the subject of your quarrel that night?”

===Chinese===
“您这话是什么意思？”

“当晚客人告辞之后，你们不是有过一场火气很旺的口角吗？”

她的眼睛慢慢离开验尸官，落到赫尔达身上，那目光说不出的阴狠。

“想必是我的女仆告诉你们的。是的，我们吵了。路易斯——不是个好相处的人。”

“那晚你们吵的是什么？”

===Original===
“About as usual. Nothing in particular.”

“Can you recall any of the exact—er—subjects?”

“Why—no,” said Corole slowly. “That is, he told me I was running the house too extravagantly. He always said that.”

The coroner surveyed her for a moment or two.

===Chinese===
“跟平常差不多。没什么特别的。”

“您能记起任何一条确切的——呃——话题吗？”

“呃——记不起，”科罗尔慢吞吞地说，“也就是说，他嫌我持家太挥霍。这话他老挂在嘴上。”

验尸官打量了她一两分钟。

===Original===
“Is this your revolver?” he said suddenly, reaching for the shiny revolver and holding it before her.

She started, quite visibly. One brown hand, with a great topaz shining on it, reached out as if to clutch the thing, and then drew slowly back.

===Chinese===
“这是您的左轮手枪吗？”他忽然说，伸手拿起那支亮闪闪的左轮手枪，举到她面前。

她一惊，惊得明明白白。一只棕色的手伸了出去——手上那枚托帕石大戒指熠熠生光——仿佛要去攫住那东西，随即又慢慢缩了回来。

## VIII A Gold Sequin / 一枚金色亮片

===Original===
"Does this revolver belong to you?" the coroner repeated.

"Why, yes," Corole said huskily. "That—is mine."

"Can you explain its presence in the closet in which your cousin's body was found?"

She ran her tongue nervously over her lips.

"No," she said. "No."

===Chinese===
"这支左轮手枪是你的吗？"验尸官又问了一遍。

"唔，是的，"科罗尔嗓音沙哑地说。"那——是我的。"

"你能解释一下，它怎么会出现在发现你表兄尸体的那间壁橱里吗？"

她紧张地用舌头舔了舔嘴唇。

"不能，"她说，"不能。"

===Original===
"When did you see it last."

"I—don't know. It was usually kept in the drawer of the table in Louis' study. I—don't remember just when I saw it last."

"You didn't bring it to the hospital, then?"

"Certainly not," flashed Corole. Her eyes narrowed so suddenly that I almost expected her to flatten her ears and spit like a cat.

"When did you last see your cousin, Dr. Letheny?"

===Chinese===
"你最后一次见到它是什么时候。"

"我——不知道。它一向放在路易斯书房那张桌子的抽屉里。我——记不清最后一回见到它是什么时候了。"

"那么，不是你把它带进医院的？"

"当然不是。"科罗尔霍然反唇。她的双眼骤然眯起，我几乎以为她就要像猫那样抿平耳朵、啐出口来。

"你最后一次见到你表兄莱瑟尼医生，是什么时候？"

===Original===
"When I went upstairs at a little after twelve." Mentally I figured that their quarrel must have been short and to the point.

"Where did you leave him?"

"He was sitting in his study."

"When you answered the telephone when Miss Day called, did you search the house?"

"Yes."

===Chinese===
"十二点刚过一点，我上楼回房的时候。"我在心里盘算，他们那场争吵必定短促而直中要害。

"你离开他时，他在哪里？"

"他坐在书房里。"

"戴小姐打电话来、你去接的时候，你搜过这屋子吗？"

"搜过。"

===Original===
"Had his bed been disturbed?"

"Apparently not."

"You can swear, then, that he was not in the house at—two o'clock?"

"If that was when Miss Day telephoned, yes. I did not look at my watch."

There were a few more, rather unimportant, questions, then Corole was dismissed.

===Chinese===
"他的床有人动过吗？"

"看样子没有。"

"那么你能起誓，两点钟的时候——他已经不在屋里了？"

"如果戴小姐打电话正是在那时候，那我能。我没有看表。"

接着又问了几个无关紧要的问题，科罗尔便退下了。

===Original===
After that the inquest rather dragged for awhile, although Huldah telling very succinctly of Jim Gainsay taking out the Doctor's sedan a short time before the storm broke was one of the points of interest. Several policemen had to tell just what they found; during the description of finding Dr. Letheny's body, I saw Corole wince for the first time and raise her laced handkerchief to her face.

Then Dr. Balman was summoned to tell of his movements following the dinner party. He had gone directly to his room, it appeared, and was asleep when the telephone rang.

"Asleep?" said the coroner astutely. "Not in your dinner jacket, Doctor."

===Chinese===
此后验尸审讯便有些拖沓地绵延下去，不过赫尔达简明扼要地说出吉姆·盖恩赛在暴风雨来临前不久开走了医生的轿车，倒算是引人注目的一处。几名警察不得不逐一交代各自发现了什么；在讲到发现莱瑟尼医生尸体的经过时，我头一回看见科罗尔瑟缩了一下，举起那方镶花边的手帕掩住脸。

接着传巴尔曼医生上来，陈述晚宴之后的行踪。看样子，他直接回了自己房间，电话铃响时已经睡着了。

"睡着了？"验尸官机敏地说。"可不是穿着无尾晚礼服睡的吧，医生。"

===Original===
"I was very tired that night, having worked hard all day. I sat down in an armchair to rest and went to sleep. The first thing I knew the telephone was ringing."

"And what did you do then?"

"Miss Day sounded frightened—and it had been my impression that Mr. Jackson was doing very well indeed. I took my coat, for it was raining, got into my car and drove as fast as I could to St. Ann's."

===Chinese===
"那天夜里我累极了，一整天都干得辛苦。我坐进扶手椅里歇息，就睡着了。等我醒觉过来，头一件事就是电话铃在响。"

"那么你随后做了什么？"

"戴小姐的声音听上去吓坏了——而我素来的印象是，杰克逊先生的情形实在很好。我拿了外套，因为天正下雨，上了车，尽可能快地把车开到圣安医院。"

===Original===
Dr. Hajek, too, corroborated as far as possible every feature of the testimonies Maida and I had given. No, he had not heard any knocks on the door of his room, until I knocked. The lights were out and he did not understand at once what was wanted. However, when he did understand that there was some trouble in Room 18, he hurried to that room. He had only time to make the briefest of examinations, when Dr. Balman arrived. Dr. Balman came by the south door into the wing, instead of going around to the main entrance. The south door had been closed and the key in the lock and Miss Day had let Dr. Balman into the corridor. Yes, they had immediately agreed as to the cause of death.

Mr. James Gainsay was the next witness. As he advanced a queer little stir crept over the room.

He admitted freely that he had been walking in the orchard previous to the storm. The night was hot and sultry, he said, and he had thought it might be cooler outdoors. As freely he admitted that the cigarette case belonged to him.

===Chinese===
海耶克医生也尽其可能地印证了梅达和我证词里的每一处细节。不，直到我去敲门之前，他没有听见有谁敲过他房间的门。灯已经熄了，他没有立刻明白出了什么事。不过，等他弄明白18号病房（Room 18）出了乱子，他便赶忙奔向那间屋子。他刚来得及做最简短的检查，巴尔曼医生就到了。巴尔曼医生是从南门进病房翼的，并没有绕到正门去。南门当时关着，钥匙插在锁上，是戴小姐把巴尔曼医生让进走廊的。是的，对于死因，他们当即达成了一致。

下一位证人是詹姆斯·盖恩赛先生。他上前时，一缕古怪的轻微骚动悄悄掠过满屋。

他爽爽快快地承认，暴风雨来之前自己正在果园里散步。他说，那晚又热又闷，他想着室外也许凉快些。他又同样爽快地承认，那只烟盒是他的。

===Original===
"I'm certainly glad it was found," he said, grinning a little. "I value that cigarette case and did not know where I had lost it."

The coroner frowned; this levity was out of place. He moved the slim, gold case to the side of the table farther away from Gainsay.

"Was it you who collided with Miss Keate, there at the porch steps?" he asked.

Jim Gainsay's suntanned eyebrows drew closer together, but his mouth retained a half-amused smile.

"I think it likely," he said easily. "At least I—collided with someone."

===Chinese===
"它被找到了，我真是高兴，"他说，咧嘴笑了笑。"我很珍视那只烟盒，一直不知道丢在哪儿了。"

验尸官皱起眉头；这份轻佻实在不合时宜。他把那只纤薄的白金烟盒挪到桌面上离盖恩赛更远的一侧。

"在门廊台阶那儿撞上基特小姐的，是你吗？"他问。

吉姆·盖恩赛晒得黝黑的眉毛拧近了些，嘴角却仍挂着半觉有趣的笑意。

"我想多半是，"他从容地说。"至少我——是撞上了什么人。"

===Original===
His candid air did not remove, to my mind, any of the significance of his presence near the hospital.

"Why were you running?"

"I was in a hurry," said Gainsay simply.

"Where were you going?"

"To Dr. Letheny's garage."

===Chinese===
在我看来，他这副坦然的神气，丝毫也没有消解他出现在医院近旁这件事的分量。

"你当时为什么在跑？"

"我赶时间。"盖恩赛答得简单。

"你要去哪里？"

"去莱瑟尼医生的车库。"

===Original===
"Did you go directly to the garage?"

There was the barest possible hesitation. Then:

"Yes."

"What did you do, then?"

"Took Dr. Letheny's car and drove into town."

===Chinese===
"你是直接去的车库吗？"

迟疑短得几乎无从察觉。然后：

"是。"

"那么你做了什么？"

"开了莱瑟尼医生的车进城去了。"

===Original===
"How long were you gone?"

"About an hour, I should say. The roads were new to me and the rain made it bad driving."

"You wanted to send a telegram?"

If Jim Gainsay was surprised, he gave no sign of it.

"Yes," he said quietly. I don't know what it was in the tightening of his mouth and the quality of his voice that made me quite sure that the question had, in some manner, put him on his guard.

===Chinese===
"你离开了多久？"

"大概一个钟头吧。那些路我不熟，雨又下得厉害，车不好开。"

"你是想去发一封电报？"

倘若吉姆·盖恩赛吃了一惊，他可没露出半点端倪。

"是。"他平静地说。我说不清，究竟是他绷紧的嘴角，还是他嗓音里的某种质地，让我确信这个问题已经以某种方式叫他起了戒心。

===Original===
"What was the telegram?"

"A matter of business," replied Gainsay smoothly.

At this point Lance O'Leary reached over the coroner's table and pushed something across it to the coroner. The coroner took it in his hands, a slip of yellow paper, and adjusted his spectacles. After reading what was written there, he glanced disapprovingly over his glasses at Gainsay, deliberately read the message again and finally spoke.

"Was this the message you sent?"

"I'm sure I don't know," said Jim Gainsay, good-naturedly, though there was a wary look in his half-closed eyes.

===Chinese===
"电报里说了什么？"

"一桩生意上的事。"盖恩赛圆滑地答道。

就在这时，兰斯·奥利里探身越过验尸官的桌子，把一样东西推到验尸官面前。验尸官拿在手里——一纸黄条——扶了扶眼镜。读完上面的字，他从镜片上方不满地瞥了盖恩赛一眼，又从容地把电文重读一遍，这才开口。

"这就是你发的那封电报？"

"我实在说不好。"吉姆·盖恩赛答得和和气气，半阖的眼里却透着提防。

===Original===
A little gust of laughter was frowned upon by the coroner, who poised his spectacles again to read in a measured way: " 'Delayed owing to unexpected development stop cannot make the *Tuscania* stop may not get away soon signed J. Gainsay.' That yours, huh?"

I was astounded to see that Gainsay had gone rather white and his jaw was set.

"Yes," he said very quietly.

"What do you mean, 'unexpected development'?"

"I—am not at liberty to state."

===Chinese===
屋里掠过一小阵哄笑，被验尸官皱眉压了下去。他重新扶正眼镜，一字一顿地念道："'因意外变故延误 stop 赶不上*塔斯卡尼亚号* stop 一时恐难动身 署名 J·盖恩赛。'这是你发的吧，嗯？"

我愕然看见，盖恩赛的脸色已经相当发白，牙关紧咬。

"是我。"他说得极轻。

"你所说的'意外变故'，是什么意思？"

"我——不便奉告。"

===Original===
Something in Gainsay's manner seemed to irritate the coroner.

"Not at liberty to state! Well, see here, young man, you'd better be at liberty to state and that mighty fast! You've admitted to skulking around St. Ann's, at a time of night when respectable people are in bed and leaving cigarette cases—"

"Now, now," remonstrated Jim Gainsay gently. "I object to the word 'skulking.' "

"You object! You object!" The coroner removed his eyeglasses for freer gesticulation and somehow they detached themselves from the ribbon and flew out of his hand. He paused in slight discomposure and Lance O'Leary stooped, returned the eyeglasses, and as he did so, leaned over and said something in a low voice.

"H'm‑m. R'r'h'm!" remarked the coroner weightily, fixing a profound gaze upon Jim Gainsay, as if his blackest doubts of this young man had been justified.

===Chinese===
盖恩赛态度里的某种东西似乎惹恼了验尸官。

"不便奉告！好啊，听着，年轻人，你最好还是奉告，而且要快！你已经承认，深夜里在圣安医院一带鬼鬼祟祟地转悠——那样的时辰，体面人都已上床睡了——还落下烟盒——"

"哎，哎，"吉姆·盖恩赛温言劝阻。"我反对'鬼鬼祟祟'这个字眼。"

"你反对！你反对！"验尸官摘下眼镜，好让手势挥得自在些，不知怎么，眼镜竟从系带上脱开，从他手里飞了出去。他微觉狼狈地顿了顿；兰斯·奥利里俯身拾起眼镜递还给他，顺势凑近低声说了句什么。

"唔——嗯！咳——嗯！"验尸官郑重其事地哼了两声，把深沉的目光牢牢钉在吉姆·盖恩赛脸上，仿佛他对这个年轻人最阴黑的疑心全数得到了证实。

===Original===
"That is all, Mr. Gainsay. For the present." He surveyed Gainsay unpleasantly and added, as if he liked the sound of the words: "For the present."

The rest of the inquest was not interesting and was mostly a matter of repeating things that I already knew. The coroner seemed rather addled but very determined to catch somebody in an untruth. I knew where his trouble lay; it was not that he lacked clues, it was rather that he had too many of them and they all seemed to point in different directions. I was glad that Lance O'Leary appeared to have kept his own counsel about certain matters of which I had told him, though I should have liked to see the faces of the board members if it had been brought to their attention that the morphine had very likely been stolen from our own south wing.

The bell was ringing for lunch when the coroner concluded his somewhat pointless inquiries, and after a few moments in which the room was in utter silence the decisions were given. I was not surprised to hear that Dr. Louis Letheny had come to his death at the hands of a person or persons unknown. And a little later, in a hush so tense that we could hear the dripping of rain from a gutter pipe outside the windows, the same decision was given as to the death of our patient, old Mr. Jackson.

===Chinese===
"就到这里，盖恩赛先生。暂时。"他不怀善意地打量着盖恩赛，又添上一句，仿佛很爱这几个字出口时的声气："暂时。"

审讯的余下部分乏善可陈，多半是把我知道的事情重复一遍。验尸官显得颇为晕头转向，却又铁了心要抓住谁的一句谎话。我知道他的难处在哪里；不是缺少线索，倒是线索太多，而且条条似乎指向不同的方向。让我宽心的是，兰斯·奥利里对我讲过的那几件事似乎守口如瓶；不过我倒真想看看董事会诸公的脸色——倘若让他们知道，那吗啡极有可能就是从我们自己的南翼偷出来的话。

验尸官结束他那场不着边际的讯问时，午饭的钟正响着。满屋一片死寂，片刻之后，裁定宣布了。听到路易斯·莱瑟尼医生死于一个或数个身份不明者之手，我并不意外。稍后，又是在一片紧绷得连窗外檐管滴雨声都听得见的屏息里，对我们那位病人老杰克逊先生的死，宣布了同样的裁定。

===Original===
We stirred our cramped muscles, rose slowly and straggled out of the room by twos and threes. To tell the truth I felt as if nothing but a formality had been accomplished. But as I left the room I turned for a look backward and saw Lance O'Leary's smooth brown head bending close over the coroner's bald spot in earnest consultation. That one glimpse convinced me that O'Leary actually, if not openly, controlled the inquest and did so to suit his own inexplicable motives. I longed to tell him of the mysterious visitor the south wing had had the previous night but had no opportunity until later in the day.

What with one thing and another troubling me I did not rest well that afternoon. By the time I had napped spasmodically, had a bath, and got into a fresh uniform and cap it was four o'clock and I wandered through the curiously hushed corridors, down the stairs and into the general office. Miss Jones was writing in the record book of incoming cases and I paused to find out who had been entered. It was something to know that even the disagreeable publicity we had been given had not affected St. Ann's prestige.

"I'm putting him in Eighteen, in your wing," she said as I bent over her shoulder.

===Chinese===
我们舒展着坐僵了的筋骨，慢慢起身，三三两两、稀稀拉拉地散出屋去。说老实话，我觉得这场审讯不过是走完了过场。但出门时我回头望了一眼，看见兰斯·奥利里那颗光洁的棕色脑袋正紧紧凑在验尸官的秃顶旁，郑重地商议着什么。只这一瞥便让我确信，这场验尸审讯实际上——尽管不明着来——正由奥利里一手操控，而且是顺着他自己那难以索解的动机在操控。我巴不得把昨夜南翼那位神秘来客的事告诉他，可直到当天晚些时候才有机会。

这也烦那也烦，那天下午我没歇息好。断断续续打了会儿盹，洗了澡，换上干净的护士服和帽子，已经四点了。我在出奇安静的走廊里信步走去，下了楼，进了总办公室。琼斯小姐正在新入院病例登记簿上写着什么，我停下来看看登记的是谁。即便我们招来了那般难堪的风闻，竟也不曾损及圣安医院的名望——知道这一点，总归是桩安慰。

"我打算把他安排进18号病房，就在你们南翼。"我俯身到她肩后看时，她说。

===Original===
"In Eighteen!"

"Why, yes. The room is available for use, isn't it? He wants a downstairs room and that is the only one left."

"Whose patient?"

"Dr. Balman's, I think—yes." She referred to the typed card.

At the moment Dr. Balman entered the room from the inner office.

===Chinese===
"进18号病房！"

"是呀。那间屋子空着可用，不是吗？他要楼下的房间，而楼下就剩那一间了。"

"谁的病人？"

"巴尔曼医生的，我想——没错。"她翻了翻那张打字卡片。

正在这时，巴尔曼医生从里间办公室走了进来。

===Original===
"Just copy this history, please, Miss Jones, and let me know if—" he glanced at the record she was preparing. "Are you putting him in Eighteen?" he asked sharply.

"Yes. Isn't that right, Doctor?"

His long fingers sought his beard perplexedly.

"This affair is so recent—" he said doubtfully. "But, if there is no other room?"

"He especially asked for a downstairs room."

===Chinese===
"请把这份病史抄一下，琼斯小姐，若是有——"他瞥了一眼她正在填写的记录。"你们要把他安排进18号病房？"他尖声问。

"是。不对吗，医生？"

他修长的手指为难地捻着胡须。

"那桩事才过去没几天——"他迟疑道。"不过，若是没有别的屋子了呢？"

"他特意要求要楼下的房间。"

===Original===
"Very well, then," he agreed after a moment during which his thoughtful, rather kind eyes studied the record. He spoke wearily. "Put him in Eighteen. We will have to use the room sooner or later, in any case. Oh—Miss Keate. Better warn the nurses to say nothing of Eighteen's—er—history. The patient will be here at least two or three weeks, perhaps longer."

"Yes, Doctor," I said as meekly as if I shouldn't have known that I must do that, anyway. And I must say I did not relish the idea of a patient in Eighteen, knowing, as I did, that if it proved to be a surgical case with no private nurse, much of the care would fall on my shoulders, which meant many errands into Room 18.

"Very good," he said and turned toward the door.

"Oh, Dr. Balman," Miss Jones called him back hastily. "Did you not want me to copy that history?"

Dr. Balman wheeled, glanced at the typed paper still in his hand.

===Chinese===
"那好吧。"他应允了；此前他那双若有所思、颇为和善的眼睛把那份记录端详了片刻。他说得倦倦的。"就住18号病房吧。反正那间屋子迟早总得再用起来。哦——基特小姐，最好嘱咐护士们，别提18号病房的——呃——往事。这位病人至少要住两三个星期，兴许更久。"

"是，医生。"我答得乖乖的，仿佛原本不晓得这事横竖都归我做似的。而且说实话，我可不喜欢18号病房住进病人的前景——据我所知，倘若来的是位外科病人，又没请私人护士，大半护理就要落到我肩上，那就意味着一趟趟往18号病房（Room 18）跑。

"很好。"他说着转身朝门口走去。

"哦，巴尔曼医生，"琼斯小姐急忙唤住他。"您不是要我把那份病史抄一份吗？"

巴尔曼医生转过身，看了看仍拿在手里的那张打字纸。

===Original===
"I forgot," he said abstractedly. "Thank you, Miss Jones." He handed her the paper. "The patient will be in about six o'clock, I think," he added, as he disappeared.

He had not any more than got out of the room, when Dr. Hajek entered.

"Was there a telephone call for me—thank you," as Miss Jones handed him the pad with a number scribbled on it.

He took down the telephone.

"Main 2332, please," he said into the mouthpiece, adding aside to Miss Jones, "Any new cases this afternoon?"

===Chinese===
"忘了这事，"他心不在焉地说。"谢谢你，琼斯小姐。"他把纸递给她。"病人我想六点钟前后到。"他补了一句，人已经不见了。

他前脚刚出屋，海耶克医生后脚就进来了。

"有我的电话吗——谢谢。"琼斯小姐把写着一个号码的便条簿递给他时，他说。

他拿起电话听筒。

"请接Main 2332。"他对着话筒说，又侧过头问琼斯小姐："下午有新病人吗？"

===Original===
"Yes, Doctor. A Mr. Gastin is coming in. I have put him in Eighteen."

"In Eighteen! What? Oh, yes—Main 2332—" he turned again from the telephone. "Did you say you put him in Eighteen? Eighteen in the south wing?" he asked sharply.

"Why, yes," said Miss Jones. "That was the only downstairs room left."

"But—" began Dr. Hajek, only to be interrupted by the operator's voice again. "Yes. Main 2332—Oh, there you are. Yes, this is Dr. Hajek. What's that? … Did you take his temperature? Oh—yes I see. … Try a hot water bag and a little warm milk. … Yes. … Yes." He hung up with a click. "About that new patient, Miss Jones, I really don't think it wise to put him in Eighteen. If he is inclined to be nervous—"

===Chinese===
"有，医生。一位加斯廷（Gastin）先生要住院。我把他安排进18号病房了。"

"18号病房！什么？哦，是的——Main 2332——"他又从电话上转过脸来。"你是说把他安排进18号病房了？南翼的18号病房？"他尖声问。

"是呀。"琼斯小姐说。"楼下的房间就剩那一间了。"

"可是——"海耶克医生刚开口，又被接线员的声音打断了。"是，Main 2332——哦，接通了。是，我是海耶克医生。什么？……量过体温了吗？哦——是，我明白。……试试热水袋，再喝点温牛奶。……好。……好。"他咔哒一声挂上听筒。"关于那位新病人，琼斯小姐，我真不觉得把他放进18号病房是明智的。要是他这人容易神经紧张——"

===Original===
I was tired of discussing the matter.

"He will not know a thing about what has happened there," I interrupted very rudely and not at all in accordance with professional etiquette. "We've got to use the room sometime. Why not now?"

"Yes," agreed Dr. Hajek, surveying me absently. "Yes, I suppose so. Yes. Did you say he comes this afternoon?"

"About six o'clock," said Miss Jones, and with a nod he swung toward the inner office.

===Chinese===
这个话题我已经谈烦了。

"那里出过什么事，他一点儿也不会知道。"我打断得非常无礼，全然不合职业礼数。"那间屋子早晚总得用。为什么现在不能用？"

"是。"海耶克医生应道，心不在焉地打量着我。"是，我也这么想。是。您刚才是说他今天下午到？"

"六点钟前后。"琼斯小姐说。他点了点头，一转身进了里间办公室。

===Original===
Thinking that I must see that Room 18 was in order, I hurried toward the south wing. The room had not been cleaned beyond a brief straightening up, so I sent two nurses to clean it and went along myself to superintend the affair.

It was not pleasant to open that door, but I had opened it under still less agreeable circumstances. The room was very gloomy and cold with dismal shadows on the white walls, and the window panes so beaded with moisture that the gray light from outside filtered but faintly into the place. I relented so far as to turn on the electric light, which threw the whole room into sharp relief, and the two girls set to work.

The air was stale, so I crossed to the low window near the porch, unlocked the catch and flung it wide, letting in the damp mist. I stood there, thinking of the intruder of the previous night. Who had been in this room? What had been his purpose? What would O'Leary say when I told him of it? Had the visitor escaped by this window? I looked at the wide sill. There was a screen there, to be sure, but it worked on a spring catch and could easily be opened from either side, this to facilitate the shaking of rugs and dusters and the adjusting of awnings. Idly I pushed back the screen, running my finger along the sill. I was about to close it again when a faint reflection of light from something in the corner of the sill caught my eye. I leaned toward that corner to look more closely, reached out and slowly turned the tiny flat thing over with my fingernail.

===Chinese===
想着我必须去看看18号病房（Room 18）是否收拾停当，我便匆匆往南翼去。那间屋子除了草草理了理还没打扫，我于是派了两名护士去打扫，自己也跟去督着。

开那扇门可不是桩愉快的事，但我曾在还要不堪的境况下开过它。屋里阴冷昏沉，白墙上笼着惨淡的暗影，窗玻璃上凝满水珠，外面的灰色天光只能幽幽地渗进屋来。我心一软，索性打开电灯——灯光把整间屋子照得轮廓分明——两个姑娘便动手干了起来。

屋里空气滞闷，我便走到门廊近旁那扇低窗前，拨开窗闩，把窗子大大推开，放进潮湿的雾气。我站在那里，想着昨夜那位不速之客。进过这间屋子的是谁？他来做什么？我把这事告诉奥利里，他会怎么说？那人是打这扇窗逃走的吗？我望着宽阔的窗台。那里确有一扇纱窗，不过是弹簧扣的，从里从外都一推即开——为的是抖地毯、抖掸子、调遮阳篷方便。我漫不经心地推开纱窗，手指顺着窗台抹过去。正要重新关上，窗台一角有什么东西反射出一线微光，攫住了我的目光。我朝那个角落俯下身去细看，伸出手，用指甲把那件扁扁的小东西慢慢翻了过来。

===Original===
It was a gold sequin!

I should never have seen it save for that minute reflection of light, for the upper surface was all tarnished and stained, though the under side was still bright. A wisp of frayed green thread still clung to the small hole for a needle at the top of the flimsy bit of metal.

I needed no one to tell me from where the thing had come; the night of June seventh Corole Letheny had worn a dress of gold sequins cunningly arranged over net with flashes of green here and there.

And it did not seem probable to me that she had worn the gown since that night.

By the time I had digested this amazing fact the girls, to whom fear seemed to lend astonishing speed, had got the room cleaned.

===Chinese===
那是一枚金色亮片！

若不是那一星微弱的反光，我根本看不见它——它的正面早已乌暗污损，背面却还亮着。那片薄薄的金属顶端有个穿针用的小孔，一缕磨毛了的绿线还缠在孔上。

这东西打哪儿来，无须任何人告诉我；六月七日那天夜里，科罗尔·莱瑟尼穿的正是那样一条裙子——金色亮片巧妙地缀满网纱，其间处处闪着绿光。

而自那夜之后，她似乎再没有穿过那条裙子。

等我消化完这个惊人的事实，两个姑娘——恐惧仿佛给了她们惊人的手脚——已经把屋子打扫完了。

===Original===
"A patient is coming," I explained to them. "That is all now." They were glad to be dismissed and hurried away.

I did not remain alone in that room. Strolling down the corridor, the tiny sequin still in my hand, it occurred to me that it would be a fine thing to be able to tell Lance O'Leary, when I gave him the sequin, whether or not Corole had worn her gold gown since the night of her dinner party. Huldah would know, and somehow in the face of this last development, I had no scruples as to inquiring of Huldah concerning the affairs of her mistress.

It was a matter of only a few moments until I was on my way.

The path through the orchard squdged wetly under my feet; the trees dripped steadily on my starched, white cap, and the mist lay so heavy and close that I could not see more than ten or fifteen feet ahead of me. The shrubs massed around the trees were hazy, shadowy outlines, and the raw air fairly hurt my throat.

I walked on slowly through the wet alfalfa field, passed the clump of pines that made a black blot amidst the fog, and through the gate. The porch of the Letheny cottage still looked dreary and Huldah had not swept it that day.

===Chinese===
"要来一位病人。"我向她们交代。"现在没别的事了。"她们乐得脱身，急急走了。

我没有独自留在那间屋里。沿着走廊缓缓走回去时，小小的亮片仍攥在我手心，我忽然想到：若是把亮片交给兰斯·奥利里的时候，还能顺带告诉他，科罗尔自晚宴那夜之后到底还穿没穿过那条金裙，那该多好。赫尔达一定知道，而不知怎的，面对眼前这桩新变故，向赫尔达打听她女主人的事，我心里竟没有半点顾忌了。

不过片刻工夫，我就已经上了路。

果园里的小径在我脚下湿漉漉地咕唧作响；树上的水珠不住滴在我浆硬的白帽上，雾又浓又沉地逼压下来，前方十到十五英尺外的东西便看不真切。四下丛聚在树边的灌木只剩朦胧昏暗的轮廓，湿冷的空气简直割得我喉咙生疼。

我缓缓穿过湿透的苜蓿地，绕过雾中一团墨影似的松树丛，进了院门。莱瑟尼小屋的门廊看上去依旧荒凉，那天赫尔达没有扫过。

===Original===
Corole came to the door.

"Oh," she said unenthusiastically. "Oh, hello, Sarah. Come in. I was boring myself over a book." She threw my cape on a chair and I followed her into the study.

"You decided not to go to New Orleans, then, with the—that is, for the funeral?"

"There was no need to." Her face darkened. "He has relatives there who—never cared for me."

"Mr. Jackson's body was sent East," I said. We lapsed into silence. Presently Corole stirred.

===Chinese===
来开门的是科罗尔。

"哦，"她意兴阑珊地说。"哦，你好，萨拉。进来吧。我正对着一本书闷得发慌呢。"她把我的披肩丢在椅子上，我随她进了书房。

"那么，你决定不去新奥尔良了——不同那……呃，我是说，不去参加葬礼了？"

"没必要去。"她的脸色阴沉下来。"他在那边有亲戚，他们——从来就不喜欢我。"

"杰克逊先生的遗体已经运往东部了。"我说。我们陷入沉默。过了片刻，科罗尔动了动。

===Original===
"I'd give you tea but Huldah is in bed with a headache. She went to sleep right after lunch but I suppose is awake by now. I told her not to worry about dinner; I'll drive in to the Brevair. I've been wanting to get out, anyhow, and they say there's a wonderful new chef there."

"Well, for goodness' sake, Corole, don't make yourself conspicuous. There's that matter of the revolver at the inquest, you know."

Corole glanced curiously at me and laughed.

"But my dear, I don't know a thing about that revolver getting over to St. Ann's. And I do happen to know that there are other, far more intriguing things, that might bear a little investigation." She smoothed the flat wave in her hair gently. "A little investigation, at least," she murmured as if amused, but her expression was not pleasant. Indeed a look in her flat, yellow eyes reminded me that this woman had lived in strange places, had seen strange things, was likely familiar with dark secrets and black rites and primitive passions—product of the twentieth century though she seemed, with her laces and jewels and sophisticated eyebrows. Jungle nights, tom-toms and the word "voodoo" came into my mind—and though I have never been at all sure just what voodoo means, I found my skin crawling.

===Chinese===
"我本该请你喝茶，可赫尔达头疼躺下了。她午饭后就睡着了，不过这会儿该醒了。我让她别管晚饭；我要开车进城去布雷韦尔（Brevair）。反正我也一直想出去走走，听说那儿新来了一位了不起的主厨。"

"哎，看在老天爷份上，科罗尔，别太招摇。你知道的，验尸审讯上还有左轮手枪那么一档子事呢。"

科罗尔好奇地瞥了我一眼，笑了。

"可是亲爱的，那把左轮手枪怎么跑去圣安医院的，我一概不知。倒是我偏偏知道些别的、勾人得多的事，兴许值得小小查上一查。"她轻轻抚平发间那绺平伏的波纹。"至少，值得小小一查。"她低声自语，像是觉得有趣，神情却并不好看。的确，她那双扁平的黄眼睛里的某种神色提醒我：这个女人在古怪的地方生活过，见过古怪的事情，多半熟稔种种黑暗的秘密、阴黑的仪式和原始的激情——尽管她看上去十足是个二十世纪的产物，满身花边珠宝，描着入时的眉黛。丛林之夜、汤姆鼓，还有"巫毒"（voodoo）这个词一齐涌上我的心头——虽说巫毒究竟作何解，我从来就没弄确切过，我却发觉自己的皮肤正阵阵发麻。

===Original===
"I think I'll go up to see Huldah," I said, rising abruptly, and feigning a professional interest.

"Do," said Corole, smiling not at all tenderly into the fire. "You know the way to her room? I'm too lazy to go along." At my nod she continued indolently: "Huldah highly approves of you, Sarah. She has never quite trusted me, you know." She laughed again.

I found Huldah very drowsy and her headache worse.

"It is on account of the cloudy weather," I said, and we talked for some moments very pleasurably of our experiences with neuralgia; my own were much more interesting than Huldah's but I listened forbearingly to her tale.

===Chinese===
"我想我上楼去看看赫尔达。"我说着蓦地起身，装出一副职业性的关切。

"去吧。"科罗尔应道，对着炉火微微一笑，那笑意没有半分温柔。"去她房间的路你认得吧？我懒得动了。"见我点头，她懒洋洋地接着说："赫尔达非常看得上你，萨拉。她可从来没怎么信过我，你知道。"她又笑了。

我见到赫尔达时，她昏昏欲睡，头疼得更厉害了。

"是阴天闹的。"我说。随后我们颇为惬意地聊起各自与神经痛打交道的经历；我那些比赫尔达的有意思得多，但我还是宽容地听她讲完。

===Original===
"I felt better for awhile after Miss Letheny gave me some medicine she has," went on Huldah. "I went right off to sleep. She gives it into your arm so it doesn't take long to—"

"Into your arm!" I cried, struck by the phrase. "What do you mean?"

"Why with one of them—what do you call them? Sort of like a needle."

"You don't mean a hypodermic needle?"

"Yes!" Huldah smiled happily. "That is it. I just couldn't think of what she called it. It is fine. You see, that way the medicine goes directly into your—"

===Chinese===
"莱瑟尼小姐给我用了点她手头的药之后，我好受了一阵。"赫尔达接着说。"我一下就睡着了。她是往你胳膊里打的，所以不消多大工夫就——"

"往胳膊里打！"我被这个说法惊住，喊了出来。"你是什么意思？"

"呃，就是用那玩意儿——你们管它叫什么来着？有点像一根针似的。"

"你说的不会是皮下注射针吧？"

"对！"赫尔达喜滋滋地笑了。"就是它。我就是想不起她管它叫什么了。那东西可好啦。你瞧，那样药就直接进到你的——"

===Original===
"Huldah! Do you mean to say that Miss Letheny gave you a hypodermic?"

She nodded, pulling up the sleeve of her outing-flannel wrapper and showing me the tiny scar. I scrutinized it closely. It had been most deftly done. There were no skin abrasions, the vein had been carefully avoided, the needle quite evidently had been thrust into the flesh by a practised and unfaltering hand. And that hand belonged to Corole Letheny!

"Wasn't that all right, Miss Keate? It didn't hurt at all—"

I recalled myself to the present.

"Huldah," I said severely, "never let anyone but a doctor or a trained nurse give you a hypodermic. Never!" And as her face turned rather green I added, "That was likely just some headache medicine that Dr. Letheny, or someone, had given Miss Letheny. So it is all right this time." And, indeed, I could tell that Corole had actually given her only a mild opiate to relieve, most unwisely, her headache.

===Chinese===
"赫尔达！你是说，莱瑟尼小姐给你做了皮下注射？"

她点点头，捋起法兰绒家居袍的袖子，把那个小小的针痕给我看。我凑近细察。手法干净利落之极。皮肤没有丝毫擦损，血管被小心避开，那根针分明是由一只熟练而毫不颤动的手扎进肉里去的。而那只手，属于科罗尔·莱瑟尼！

"那样做不对吗，基特小姐？一点儿也不疼——"

我把自己唤回眼前。

"赫尔达，"我正色道，"除了医生或受过训练的护士，千万别让任何人给你做皮下注射。千万别！"见她的脸吓得有些发青，我又补上一句："那八成不过是莱瑟尼医生、或者别的什么人给过莱瑟尼小姐的头疼药罢了。所以这回没事。"而且说实话，我也看得出来，科罗尔给她的其实不过是一剂温和的阿片制剂——为的是替她止住头疼，实在极不明智。

===Original===
"Now," I went on, as I caught sight of my wrist watch pointing to five o'clock. "Can you tell me something that I want to know, and forget that I asked you?"

Huldah is shrewd; she raised herself on one gray flannel elbow and looked at me keenly.

"I can keep a secret, Miss Keate. There's many a thing I could tell if I wanted to."

"What I want to know is this: Has Miss Letheny worn her gold dress lately?"

===Chinese===
"这样吧，"我接着说，一眼瞥见腕表已指向五点。"你能不能告诉我一件我想知道的事，然后忘掉我问过你？"

赫尔达精明得很；她用一条灰法兰绒衣袖的臂肘撑起身子，目光锐利地看着我。

"我守得住秘密，基特小姐。只要我愿意，好些事我都能说出来。"

"我想知道的是这个：莱瑟尼小姐近来穿过她那条金裙子吗？"

===Original===
"You mean that green and gold, snaky thing with the scales on it?"

"Yes."

"Let me see. She wore it last the night Dr. Letheny was killed."

"Are you sure, Huldah?"

===Chinese===
"你是说那条绿金相间、满身鳞片、蛇一样的玩意儿？"

"是。"

"让我想想。她最后一回穿它，是莱瑟尼医生被杀的那天晚上。"

"你确定吗，赫尔达？"

===Original===
"Yes'm. I remember well. Poor Dr. Letheny!"

"Feeling better, Huldah?" said a voice from the doorway.

It was Corole, of course.

===Chinese===
"确定，小姐。我记得清清楚楚。可怜的莱瑟尼医生！"

"感觉好些了吗，赫尔达？"门口有个声音说。

自然，是科罗尔。

## IX Under the Barberry Bush / 伏牛花丛下

===Original===
I left rather abruptly. But on the darkening path toward St. Ann’s I decided that Corole could not have heard our conversation. Feeling that I must get these last two pieces of news, as well as the occurrence of the previous night to O’Leary as soon as possible, I walked rapidly along through the fog. I crossed the little bridge and was hurrying through the apple orchard when I came face to face with O’Leary.

“You are the very person I want to see, Miss Keate,” he said at once.

Taking my arm he drew me a few steps from the path; he motioned and following his gesture I found I had a view of the south door and small colonial porch.

“Tell me, Miss Keate, exactly where you were standing the night of June seventh when the﻿—er﻿—arrow-like affair was thrown over your shoulder?”

“I had truly forgotten that﻿—I should have told you.”

===Chinese===
我告辞得颇为仓促。但在通往圣安医院那条渐暗的小径上，我已拿定主意：科罗尔不可能听见我们那番谈话。自觉必须尽快把这两桩新消息、连同昨夜发生的那桩事一并向奥利里报告，我便在雾中疾步赶路。过了小桥，正匆匆穿过苹果园，迎面便撞上了奥利里。

“我要见的正是您，基特小姐。”他当即说道。

他挽住我的手臂，引我离开小径几步；他做了个手势，顺着他指的方向望去，我发现自己正好望得见南门和那座殖民地式小门廊。

“告诉我，基特小姐，六月七日那天夜里，那件——呃——箭矢似的玩意儿越过您肩头掷出去的时候，您究竟站在哪里？”

“我竟真把那事给忘了——本该早些告诉您的。”

===Original===
“That’s all right,” he brushed my apology aside. “Can you recall about where you were standing and the line it took over your shoulder?”

“I think so,” I replied slowly and thoughtfully. “It seems to me it should have fallen somewhere about that clump of barberry. Over there.” I pointed with my finger toward the shrubbery that edges the apple orchard. “I suppose you are trying to find what it was.”

“If no one else has found﻿—or retrieved it yet,” agreed O’Leary.

With O’Leary going ahead and holding back the more importunate branches and shrubs, we made our slow way to the spot I had indicated. I remember that we took some pains not to be seen from the hospital and, bending over as I did, to keep my white cap invisible from those windows, I had an absurd feeling that I was playing a grim game of hide-and-seek. In the excitement of the search I did not notice my soaked shoes and my wet hair, and remember only how I groped along the sodden leaf mold, and around the slippery brown roots of the shrubs and trees. If we had known what to look for, it would have been an easier task, O’Leary informed me, after some twenty minutes’ vain delving in the wet underbrush. He was inclined to be a little pettish about it, implying that I might have noticed the thing more carefully. That remark was made the time he slipped on some wet leaves and flung his hands into a barberry bush to keep his balance. He looked amazingly human and ordinary, picking out the thorns. It was just a few moments after that that I heard him utter a sudden ejaculation of pain. He was on the opposite side of a large clump of barberry bush and I crawled cautiously around to discover what had happened.

===Chinese===
“不打紧。”他对我的道歉置之不理。“您还记得自己当时大致站在什么位置、那东西越过您肩头时走的是哪条线吗？”

“我想还记得。”我缓缓地、若有所思地答道。“依我看，它多半落在那丛伏牛花附近一带。就在那边。”我伸手指向苹果园边缘的那片灌木。“我猜您是想查明那东西究竟是什么。”

“要是还没被旁人发现——或者取走的话。”奥利里表示同意。

奥利里走在前头，替我撩开那些纠缠不休的枝条灌木，我们慢慢腾腾地走到我指过的地方。我记得我们颇费了些心思，不让医院那边望见；而我弓着身子、好让白帽不落进那些窗子的视野时，心里生出一种荒唐的感觉，仿佛在玩一场阴森森的捉迷藏。搜寻得入了神，我竟没有留意鞋子早已浸透、头发早已濡湿，只记得自己如何在湿漉漉的腐叶土上摸索前行，绕过灌木和树木那些滑腻的褐色根。奥利里告诉我，倘若早知道要找的是什么，这差事本会轻松些——说这话时，我们已在湿透的灌木底下白白翻寻了约摸二十分钟。他为此有些恼意，话里带着埋怨，仿佛我当初本该把那东西瞧得更仔细些。那句怨言，正是他踩上湿叶打滑、双手甩进一丛伏牛花里才算稳住身形的那一回说的。他剔着刺，看上去竟出奇地像个平平常常的凡人。而就在那之后不多一会儿，我听见他突然发出一声痛呼。他在一大丛伏牛花的另一侧，我小心翼翼地爬绕过去，看看出了什么事。

===Original===
I found him squatting on his heels, with his thumb in his mouth and the other hand clasping a small object that, from his glance, seemed to have pleased him inordinately.

“I’ve found it, Miss Keate,” he said, achieving triumphant utterance in spite of the thumb. “Look! Could it have been this?”

It was a mercy I was so near the ground for my knees simply caved in under me. In his hand was a small hypodermic syringe. The nickel on it was rusted a little from the weather.

This syringe whizzing over my shoulder was exactly what I had seen. It was heavy enough to acquire considerable velocity and, as I peered through the shrubbery and trees to the porch, I knew that it would have fallen about here. The trouble was that it looked very much like the south wing’s missing syringe. Of course, all hypodermics are much alike, but I knew a certain way that it could be identified, for Maida had taken a cue from me and marked all her tools with a small scratched “D.”

===Chinese===
只见他蹲踞在地上，大拇指含在嘴里，另一只手攥着一件小东西——看他的眼色，那东西似乎让他称心如意到了极点。

“我找到了，基特小姐。”他说，嘴里含着拇指也拦不住那股得意扬扬。“瞧！会不会就是这个？”

幸亏我离地本就不远，双膝一软，径直瘫坐了下去。他手里是一支小小的皮下注射器。器身的镍层经了几日风雨，已略略生锈。

嗖地掠过我肩头的那支注射器，正是我看见过的东西。它分量够沉，足以蓄出可观的速度；我透过灌木与树木朝那座门廊望去，便知道它该落在这一带。麻烦在于，它像极了南翼失踪的那支注射器。当然，皮下注射器个个都长得差不多，可我知道一个准能辨认的法子——梅达学了我的样，把她所有的器械都刻上了一个小小的“D”。

===Original===
“Let me see it,” I said.

Without a word he handed the thing to me. On the top of the little flat button was a rudely scratched *D*, rusty but still distinct.

“I see you recognize it,” said O’Leary, taking his thumb out of his mouth, and regarding it as thoughtfully as if he had not another object in the world. “It is Miss Day’s, is it not?”

I nodded.

===Chinese===
“给我瞧瞧。”我说。

他一言不发，把那东西递到我手里。那枚小小的扁平按钮顶上，刻着一个潦草的*D*，锈迹斑斑，却仍分明。

“看来您认出它了。”奥利里说着，把大拇指从嘴里抽出来，端详得那样专注，仿佛普天之下再没有另一件东西入得了眼。“这是戴小姐的，对不对？”

我点了点头。

===Original===
“Everyone in the wing had access to it. The fact that it may have originally belonged to Maida doesn’t mean that she threw it out here.”

“No﻿—no, of course not,” he said contemplatively. “Well﻿—we found it, Miss Keate. Though I could wish that I had not run into it so forcibly.”

He regarded the scratch on his thumb. “You don’t suppose that rust harbored any tetanus germs?”

“It has bled enough by this time to clean itself,” I said without much sympathy, feeling indeed that if he *would* find things it served him right to get stuck!

===Chinese===
“南翼里人人都拿得着它。它原先或许归梅达所有，可这并不能说明，就是她把它扔到这儿来的。”

“不——不，当然不能。”他若有所思地说。“好吧——我们总算找到它了，基特小姐。只是我倒宁愿，自己没跟它撞得这么结实。”

他端详着拇指上的划痕。“您该不会觉得，那锈里藏着什么破伤风病菌吧？”

“血流到这会儿，早把伤口自己冲干净了。”我说得没什么同情——心里着实觉得，他*偏要*去翻寻东西，扎了一下也是活该！

===Original===
“You nurses!” he said, looked at me and laughed. “I wish you could see yourself, Miss Keate.”

Conscious not only of my undignified posture but also of an increasing dampness penetrating my skirts, I rose. He followed me through the shrubbery toward the path.

“This is as secluded a place in which to talk as we can find, Miss Keate,” he said. “Have you come upon any new developments that I’d like to hear about?”

“How did you know I had?” I asked, not any too pleasantly.

===Chinese===
“你们这些护士！”他说着，望了望我，笑了。“真希望您能瞧瞧自己这副模样，基特小姐。”

我意识到自己那副有失体面的姿势，也意识到湿意正一分一分浸透裙裾，便站起身来。他跟着我穿过灌木丛，朝小径走去。

“这可是我们能找到的最僻静的谈话去处了，基特小姐。”他说。“您可曾遇上什么我爱听的新进展？”

“您怎么知道我有？”我问道，口气不很客气。

===Original===
He smiled. “By the look in your eyes and your general aspect of﻿—er﻿—having swallowed the canary, so to speak.”

“Well, as a matter of fact there is a thing or two.” As briefly as I could, I told him of the gold sequin and of the fact that Corole had last worn that gown the night of June seventh. I also told him that she was an adept at the use of a hypodermic needle. And then, somewhat reluctantly, and glancing rather nervously into the foggy shadows that were increasing under the dripping trees about us, I told him of the visitor to Room 18 of the previous night. He asked several questions, seeming to be extremely interested.

“It goes without saying that the person, whoever it was, who entered Room 18 last night had some purpose. That there was﻿—or is﻿—something yet in Eighteen that he wanted.” He frowned. “I don’t see what I could have missed.”

===Chinese===
他笑了。“凭您眼里的神色，还有您周身那股——呃——不妨说是吞下了金丝雀的得意劲儿。”

“好吧，说实话，确有一两件事。”我尽量长话短说，把金色亮片的事告诉了他，还说科罗尔上一次穿那身衣裙，正是六月七日夜里。我又告诉他，她使注射针头是个中老手。然后，我有些不情愿地——一边还相当不安地朝我们四周滴水的树下那渐渐浓重的雾影里张望——把昨夜18号病房（Room 18）来了访客的事讲给他听。他问了好几个问题，显得极感兴趣。

“不言而喻，昨夜进18号病房的那个人，不管是谁，都带着某种目的。18号病房里当时有——或者说至今仍有——他想要的东西。”他皱起眉头。“我看不出自己漏掉了什么。”

===Original===
“There was the sequin,” I suggested.

“Yes, there was the sequin. You said it was under the screen? Yes, I missed that. But somehow I don’t see Corole Letheny coming back for it.”

“She has likely not missed it﻿—there are hundreds of the things on that dress.”

“Huldah was positive she hadn’t worn the dress since Thursday night? She might have left it there last night, you know.”

I shook my head.

“No. Huldah was sure. And it must have been then, for the side of the sequin that lay uppermost is all tarnished from the rain.”

===Chinese===
“还有那枚亮片呢。”我提醒道。

“对，还有那枚亮片。您说它是在纱窗底下？没错，那个我漏掉了。可我怎么也想不出，科罗尔·莱瑟尼会为它跑回来一趟。”

“她多半根本没察觉少了——那身裙子上这种亮片有好几百颗呢。”

“赫尔达一口咬定，她自星期四夜里之后就没再穿过那身裙子？您要知道，也可能是她昨夜才把它落在那儿的。”

我摇了摇头。

“不。赫尔达很确凿。而且必定就是那一晚落下的，因为亮片朝上的那一面，已经全叫雨水沤得失了光泽。”

===Original===
“That is true. Then the question is, Who was in Room 18 last night? And what did he want?” His gray eyes were like two clear lakes.

“Miss Keate,” he said suddenly. “This radium: I’ve been hunting through encyclopedias about the stuff, but there is something I want to know. Could it be carried about in a pocket? Without burning, you know.”

“Yes, if it were in the box made for it. Radium is used in a variety of tools, for many purposes. But in this case it was in a sort of boxlike container, quite small.”

“And could be carried in a pocket or in one’s hand?” he insisted. “Or even hidden in one place or another?”

“Goodness, yes!” I replied. “I’ve seen tools containing it carried about in doctors’ bags often enough.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假。那么问题就是：昨夜在18号病房（Room 18）里的是谁？他想要什么？”他灰色的眼睛，像两泓清澈的湖水。

“基特小姐。”他忽然开口。“说这镭：我把百科全书里讲这东西的条目翻了个遍，可有件事我想弄明白。它能揣在口袋里随身带着走吗？您知道，不会灼伤人。”

“能，只要装在为它特制的盒子里。镭被用在多种器械里，用途很广。不过在这一案里，它是装在一种盒子似的容器里，很小。”

“揣得进衣袋，也握得在手心里？”他追问。“甚至藏在这个或那个地方？”

“天哪，当然能！”我答道。“装着镭的器械搁在医生的出诊包里带来带去，我见得多了。”

===Original===
He did not speak for a moment or two, studying me in the meantime with thoughtful eyes that did not in the least see me.

“I should think that it would be hard to dispose of﻿—though as to that﻿—” He broke off abruptly. “Look here, Miss Keate, how is it that such a valuable thing is used with so little precaution against theft?”

“In some hospitals radium is guarded,” I explained. “There is one very large hospital, to which patients come from all over the world, where guards are placed in the sickroom whenever radium is used. But it was not deemed necessary here at St. Ann’s. Nothing of the kind has ever happened before and our class of patients is, as a rule, of the most respectable. St. Ann’s, you know, has really the best standing﻿—” I stopped in the middle of my rather snooty remark as I saw the half smile on his face.

===Chinese===
有一两分钟他没有作声，只管若有所思地打量我——那双出神的眼睛，压根儿没有看见我本人。

“照我想，这东西该很难脱手——不过，话又说回来——”他突兀地收住话头。“我说，基特小姐，这么贵重的一样东西，用起来怎么竟这么不设防、不怕人偷？”

“有些医院里，镭是有人看守的。”我解释道。“有一家很大的医院，病人从世界各地慕名而去，每逢使用镭，病房里都要派人看守。可在我们圣安医院，没人觉得有此必要。从前从未出过这一类事，而且我们收的这一阶层病人，照例都极为体面。圣安医院的声望，您知道，实在是首屈一指——”瞥见他唇边那抹似笑非笑，我那番颇带势利气的夸耀说到一半便停住了。

===Original===
“Still it did happen,” he said softly.

“Yes,” I retorted. “And it is your business to recover it.”

His face sobered instantly.

“Not an easy task, Miss Keate,” he said at once and most amiably. “And I’m grateful for the help you give me. In return I shall tell you, since you ask it, that there are a few possible premises that interest me. You might give me your opinion of them. For one thing﻿—we have found that there were three possible means of death, in or about Room 18. There was a revolver, ether, and a hypodermic syringe and enough morphine missing from the drug room to more than accomplish﻿—what we believe it did accomplish. Three weapons where only one was necessary! Dr. Letheny met his death by a fourth means. Three weapons! Does that not indicate that there was more than one person interested in your patient’s death?”

===Chinese===
“可它终究还是发生了。”他轻声说。

“是啊，”我回敬道，“把它追回，正是您的本分。”

他的面容霎时肃然。

“这可不是桩容易的差事，基特小姐。”他当即答道，语气极为和蔼。“您给我的种种帮助，我很感激。作为回报——既然您问了——我就告诉您，有几种可能的推断很让我感兴趣，您不妨替我评一评。其一——我们发现，在18号病房（Room 18）之内或近旁，曾有三种可能的致死手段。一支左轮手枪、乙醚，还有一支皮下注射器，外加药品间短缺的吗啡——其份量足以酿成的，远不止我们相信它确已酿成的那桩祸事。明明一件凶器足矣，却冒出三件！而莱瑟尼医生死于第四种手段。三件凶器！这岂不表明，对您那位病人之死怀有兴趣的，不止一人？”

===Original===
“Three!” I gasped. “Three!” In consternation I went over the list of names of that dinner party of ill memory. “But there were only six of us at that dinner to whose personnel you limit your suspects.”

“Seven,” corrected O’Leary. “There is Dr. Letheny, you know.”

“But Mr. O’Leary, it sounds like a club.” I was very much in earnest but the man had the impudence to laugh.

===Chinese===
“三件！”我倒吸一口凉气。“三件！”我惊惶不已，在心里把那场不堪回首的晚宴的宾客名单一一数过。“可那顿晚饭席上，一共只有我们六个人，而您的嫌犯也仅圈定在这几位之中。”

“七位。”奥利里纠正道。“别忘了，还有莱瑟尼医生呢。”

“可是奥利里先生，这听着倒像个俱乐部了。”我说得郑重其事，那人却放肆地笑出了声。

===Original===
