随后那骇人的回声渐渐消歇。黑暗朝我围拢过来，比先前更加窒息，死寂再度君临一切。

===Original===
For a moment I must have been numb with shock. Then there were footsteps running, a cry, the clicking of signal lights that did not light, and I was running, stumbling, gasping, bumping into doors, trying to reach the end of the corridor. And Room 18.

Along the way I collided with something, something moving that twisted away from me and cried out. It was Maida and at my voice she answered.

“What is it! What has happened! Was it Room 18?”

“Room 18! What can we—”

===Chinese===
有一小会儿我准是惊得麻木了。随后有脚步声奔跑起来，一声喊叫，信号灯咔哒作响却点不亮，我也跑了起来，跌跌撞撞，喘着粗气，一路撞在门上，只想赶到走廊尽头去。赶到18号病房（Room 18）去。

半路上我撞上了个东西，一个会动的东西，从我身边一扭闪开，叫出了声。是梅达，听见我的声音，她应了。

“怎么了！出什么事了！是18号病房吗？”

“18号病房！我们能——”

===Original===
“We must have a light. In the kitchen—there’s a candle—” I heard the swift, soft thud of her feet as they moved away and I kept on, feeling along the cold, dank wall, groping my way past open doors. It seemed an eternity before I reached the end of the corridor and felt the small panes of glass in the south door under my fingers. I turned sharply to the left. Beyond that black void stretched Room 18. I paused at its threshold but something drove me on, into the room.

Here was the wall. Here was the electric light button. Here the bedside table. I bent, feeling along the rough weave of the counterpane on the bed, took a few steps further, trod on something hideously soft and yielding, and sprang backward in stark terror.

Afraid to move, afraid to breathe, my heart clamouring in my throat choking me, my hands pressed against my teeth, I could not even scream.

What lay there? What was in that room?

===Chinese===
“我们得有光。配膳室——那儿有支蜡烛——”我听见她那双脚嗒嗒地轻快远去，便继续往前，贴着冰冷潮湿的墙摸索，一路蹭过一扇扇敞开的门。仿佛过了一世那样久，我才摸到走廊尽头，指头触到南门上那些小小的玻璃格。我猛地向左一转。那片黑暗的虚空之外，便是18号病房（Room 18）。我在门槛上顿了顿，可有什么东西驱着我往前，进了那间屋子。

墙在这儿。电灯按钮在这儿。床头柜也在这儿。我俯下身，顺着床罩粗糙的织纹摸索，又朝前走了几步，一脚踩上一样软得骇人、往下一陷的东西，顿时吓得倒弹回来。

不敢动，不敢喘，心在喉咙口狂喊，噎得我透不过气，双手抵着牙齿，我连尖叫都叫不出来。

躺在那里的是什么？那间屋子里有什么？

===Original===
Then I realized dimly that Maida was coming, that a small circle of light was at the door, that a hand was holding a lamp unsteadily and the wavering flame was casting grotesque shadows on Maida’s chin and mouth. Above them her eyes were wide and black and mirrored my terror.

I saw her hand advance, pointing at my feet. It shook. Her mouth opened in a voiceless cry and I forced myself to look downward.

It was Higgins, sprawled there at the foot of the bed. He had been shot!

Neither of us spoke. Neither of us moved.

===Chinese===
随后我迷迷糊糊地意识到梅达来了，门口有一小圈光，一只手端端不稳一盏灯，摇曳的火苗把奇形怪状的影子投在梅达的下巴和嘴上。那上面，她的眼睛睁得又大又黑，映着我满眼的恐怖。

我看见她的手伸了出来，直指我的脚边。那只手在抖。她的嘴张开，喊出一声无声的惊呼，我这才逼着自己朝下看去。

是希金斯，摊手摊脚地扑在床脚那里。他中枪了！

我们俩谁也没出声。谁也没动。

===Original===
At last Maida withdrew her hand.

“Set the lamp down,” I heard someone saying—it must have been I. “Set the lamp down before you drop it.”

We did not hear O’Leary enter the south door. All at once he was there with us, staring at the thing there on the floor, holding his electric torch to illumine it.

“When did it happen? How? Come into the hall. Tell me. Was I too late?”

===Chinese===
末了，梅达缩回了手。

“把灯放下，”我听见有谁在说——那必定是我自己。“趁还没摔了，把灯放下。”

奥利里进南门时我们谁也没听见。转眼间他已到了我们身旁，盯着地上那东西，举着手电筒照着它。

“什么时候的事？怎么出的事？到走廊里来。说给我听。我是不是来迟了一步？”

===Original===
Somehow we were out in the corridor; the lamp was left on the table in Room 18. The light from its small flame trembled and cast eery, creeping shadows.

“Quick,” said O’Leary. “Take that lamp to the basement, Miss Day. The light switch has been pulled out. You know where the switch-box is—”

I saw Maida flinch but she took the lamp, averting her eyes from the floor.

“Hurry! No, Miss Keate, stay here, please, at the door. If anyone tries to get in, stop him! Scream! I’ll not be far away.”

===Chinese===
也不知怎么的，我们就到了走廊里；灯留在了18号病房（Room 18）的桌上。那一小簇火苗的光颤颤巍巍，投下鬼气森森、蠢蠢欲动的影子。

“快，”奥利里说，“把灯拿到地下室去，戴小姐。照明开关被人拔掉了。您知道配电箱在哪儿——”

我看见梅达瑟缩了一下，可她还是接过了灯，眼睛避开了地面。

“快点！不，基特小姐，请您留在这儿，守在门口。要是有人想进来，拦住他！喊叫！我不会走远。”

===Original===
In a flash he was gone, out the south door. I was still standing as if petrified, there in front of the south door, when the green light over the chart desk at the opposite end of the corridor flashed up and the little red signal lights gleamed suddenly all up and down the hall. I breathed a sigh of relief; Maida was all right, then. And in another moment or two her white uniform came into view at the chart desk.

“All right, thank you, Miss Keate,” came a voice at my elbow. It was O’Leary, his hat gone, his hair ruffled, his eyes shining like phosphorescent flashes on a deep-lying sea. “Come with me, please,” he said.

“Was the fuse burned out?” asked O’Leary as we met Maida, who was hurrying to answer the signals.

She shook her head. Her eyes were hollow and dark and her face as white as her cap.

“The main switch had been pulled out.”

===Chinese===
一晃眼他就没了影，出了南门。我还直挺挺钉在南门前，仿佛石化了一般，这时走廊对面病历台上方的绿光倏地亮起，小小的红色信号灯霎时间在整条走廊上上下下闪出光来。我舒了一口气；这么说，梅达没事。过了一两分钟，她那身白制服便在病历台边现出了身影。

“好了，谢谢您，基特小姐，”我肘边响起一个声音。是奥利里，帽子没了，头发蓬乱，一双眼亮得像深海里倏忽明灭的磷光。“请跟我来。”他说。

“保险丝是烧断的吗？”我们迎上匆匆赶去应信号的梅达时，奥利里问。

她摇了摇头。她两眼深陷发黑，脸白得同帽子一样。

“总闸被人拉掉了。”

===Original===
“What I expected,” muttered O’Leary, as we sped along the corridor.

Lights were gleaming from the north wing, and the night-duty nurses from that wing were clustered in a frightened group in the main hall. As they saw us they ran forward.

“What was it, Miss Keate—we heard a shot—what has happened?” And down the stairs tumbled several nurses in uniforms and kimonos and Miss Dotty with her hair in paper curlers and her eyes distracted.

O’Leary paid no attention to them. I followed him into the general office. He rapped sharply, first at Dr. Hajek’s door, then at the door of the inner office. Then he put his hand on the latch of the door to the inner office and pushed. It was not locked and opened readily; the light from the office streamed through the door.

===Chinese===
“正如我所料，”奥利里低声道，我们沿走廊疾行。

北翼灯火通明，那一翼的夜班护士们挤在大厅里，吓作一团。一见我们，她们便迎上前来。

“怎么了，基特小姐——我们听见枪响——出什么事了？”好几名护士连滚带爬跌下楼来，有的穿着制服，有的裹着和服式晨衣，多蒂小姐也在其中，头发上还卷着纸发卷，眼神发直。

奥利里没理会她们。我跟着他进了总办公室。他叩门叩得干脆，先敲海耶克医生的门，再敲里间办公室的门。然后他把手搭在里间那扇门的门闩上一推。门没锁，应手而开；办公室里的灯光从门口涌流出来。

===Original===
“O’Leary! What has happened? What is it?” Dr. Balman, his eyes blinking anxiously in the light, was tossing back the covers and springing from his bed.

“There has been another murder in Room 18,” said O’Leary.

“Another—what! Who?”

“The janitor—Higgins.” And at that second the door to Dr. Hajek’s room opened and Dr. Hajek, his bathrobe hugged about him, ran toward us.

===Chinese===
“奥利里！出什么事了？怎么了？”巴尔曼医生在灯光里焦灼地眨着眼，一把掀开被子，从床上跳了起来。

“18号病房（Room 18）里又出了一桩命案。”奥利里说。

“又出——什么！谁？”

“门房——希金斯。”话音未落，海耶克医生的房门开了，海耶克医生紧裹着浴袍，朝我们跑来。

===Original===
“What was that? What did you say? Higgins? Dead?”

In a few, terse words O’Leary explained and by that time we were all hurrying back to the south wing, Dr. Balman’s white pajamas leading the way. I did not enter Room 18 again with them.

There was plenty of work waiting for me in the wing. As if to make bad matters worse the nurses from all over the hospital were crowding into the south-wing corridor, their pallid faces and wild questions adding to the confusion. The excitement was becoming tumultuous when Dr. Balman came into the corridor, a strange figure in his pajamas and bare feet, his thin hair rumpled and his eyes worried.

“There has been an accident,” he said. His voice carried though it was very low. “Please return immediately to duty. Do not be alarmed.” And it was curious to see the nurses scattering hastily like frightened children caught in mischief.

===Chinese===
“刚说什么？您说什么？希金斯？死了？”

奥利里三言两语讲明了，这时我们已都在匆匆赶回南翼，巴尔曼医生那身白睡衣打头。我没有随他们再进18号病房（Room 18）。

这一翼里有的是活儿等着我。更糟的是，全院的护士纷纷挤进南翼走廊，苍白的脸和七嘴八舌的盘问愈发添乱。乱哄哄快要压不住的时候，巴尔曼医生来到走廊上——睡衣赤足，一副怪模样，稀疏的头发乱着，眼里满是忧色。

“出了点意外，”他说。声音虽低，却传得远。“请立即回到岗位上去。不要惊慌。”说来也怪，护士们慌忙四散，倒像一帮淘气被拿住、吓坏了的孩子。

===Original===
For a while I had not time or eyes for anything but work. It was difficult enough to calm and soothe the patients of our wing and I paid no attention to the closed door of Eighteen, the flying trips through the corridor made by the two doctors, or O’Leary’s gray suit and thoughtful countenance and shining eyes here and there about the wing.

When the police began to arrive, entering the wing by the south door so as not to be seen by those from other wings, it was a great deal like the repetition of a bad dream. It continued so until along about four o’clock when an ambulance, gleaming oddly white and distinct in the cold gray dawn, was drawn up at the south door. I did not see them leave.

I was trying to control my still shaking hands in order to get the neglected charts written up before turning things over to the day nurses, when O’Leary paused beside me and sat down in the vacant chair.

===Chinese===
有一阵子，我除了手上的活计，什么也无暇看、无暇想。光是安抚宽慰我们这一翼的病人就已够难的了，我没去留意那扇关着的18号病房的门，没留意两位医生在走廊里来去如飞的穿行，也没留意那身灰西装、那张沉思的脸和那双发亮的眼在这一翼里时隐时现。

警察们陆续到了，为免被别翼的人瞧见，都从南门进这一翼——那情形，活脱脱是一场噩梦的重演。如此一直闹到四点来钟，一辆救护车在清冷灰白的曙色里驶到南门前停住，白得出奇、轮廓分明。他们离去时我没有看见。

我正竭力稳住还在发抖的双手，好赶在移交日班护士之前把耽搁下的病历单补齐，奥利里在我身旁停住，在那把空椅子上坐了下来。

===Original===
“What is that on your hands?” he asked suddenly as I wrote.

I glanced at my hands and jumped.

“Oh!” I remembered. “It is only red ink. I was cleaning up some that I had spilled when—when the lights went out.”

“When the lights were turned out,” he corrected. “How soon will you finish that thing?”

“I am through now.” I verified the chart hastily and thrust it in its place in the rack. “Have you—found anything?”

===Chinese===
“您手上是什么？”我写着，他忽然问。

我看了一眼自己的手，吓了一跳。

“哦！”我想起来了，“不过是红墨水。我正擦着打翻的墨水，就——就赶上了灭灯。”

“是灯被人关掉的时候。”他纠正道，“这活儿您还有多久完？”

“这就完了。”我草草核签了病历单，把它插回架上原处。“您——发现了什么吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.” He spoke coolly. “I have—found a good deal. First, though, why did you telephone for me?”

“Why, it was Higgins! It was Higgins and now it is too late!”

His gray eyes studied me.

“What do you mean?”

My heart began to thump as speculation aroused within me.

===Chinese===
“发现了。”他语气平静，“我——发现了不少东西。不过先说说，您打电话找我做什么？”

“唉，是为了希金斯！我要说的是希金斯，如今已经迟了！”

他那双灰眼睛端详着我。

“您这话怎么讲？”

心里的猜疑被勾了起来，我的心开始咚咚直跳。

===Original===
“Higgins,” I said, dropping my voice to a whisper. “Higgins saw the face of the man that killed Mr. Jackson.”

There was a moment of silence so profound that the very walls seemed to whisper and echo my words; someone in the kitchen nearby dropped a spoon and at the metallic little rattle O’Leary stirred.

“Higgins—saw the face of the man who killed Jackson,” he repeated slowly. “How do you know, Miss Keate?”

As rapidly as possible I repeated to him the whole of my amazing conversation with Higgins. Then, more reluctantly, I told him of Jim Gainsay’s presence back of the willows where he could overhear every word we had spoken. I told him also of what the cook had said.

His inscrutable eyes studying me shrewdly, O’Leary said nothing until I had finished.

===Chinese===
“希金斯，”我压低声音，成了耳语，“希金斯见过杀死杰克逊先生的那人的脸。”

一时间静得深不见底，仿佛四壁都在低声复述、回响我的话；近旁配膳室里有人失手掉了一柄勺子，那叮的一声细碎金属响里，奥利里动了动。

“希金斯——见过杀死杰克逊那人的脸，”他缓缓复述道，“您是怎么知道的，基特小姐？”

我尽量快地把我同希金斯那场惊人的对话原原本本复述了一遍。然后，较为不情愿地，我告诉了他吉姆·盖恩赛就守在柳树后头，我们说的每一个字他都听得见。我还把厨娘的话也说了。

奥利里那双莫测的眼睛精明地审视着我，直到我说完，他一言未发。

===Original===
“Then Jim Gainsay heard Higgins not only admit his dangerous knowledge but promise to tell you tonight the name of that man. *Tonight.*”

“Yes.” Then as I caught the emphasis, I went on hurriedly: “But Jim Gainsay had nothing to do with his death. I saw nothing of Jim Gainsay tonight. I—I am sure …” My voice trailed breathlessly away under O’Leary’s sharp regard.

“And as far as we know now Jim Gainsay was the last person to see Higgins alive?” He continued quite as if I had not rushed to Jim Gainsay’s defence.

“As far as we know *now*,” I pointed out. “We may find that someone talked to him after he was seen with Jim Gainsay.”

“Gainsay overheard your conversation. The man whose face Higgins saw had everything to lose at such evidence. No one but Gainsay and you, Miss Keate, knew of its existence. I’m sorry; Gainsay seems to be a decent enough young fellow.” He paused, fumbled in his pocket, drew out the shabby stub of a pencil and began turning it over and over in his slender, well-kept fingers.

===Chinese===
“这么说，吉姆·盖恩赛听见希金斯不但认下了他那危险的内情，还答应今晚就把那人的名字告诉您。*今晚。*”

“是的。”待我咂摸出这话里的着重，又急急往下说：“可吉姆·盖恩赛同他的死毫无干系。今晚我根本没瞧见吉姆·盖恩赛。我——我确信……”在奥利里锐利的注视下，我的声音气喘吁吁地低了下去。

“而就我们目前所知，吉姆·盖恩赛是最后一个见到活着的希金斯的人？”他接着说，全然像没听见我抢着为吉姆·盖恩赛辩白似的。

“就我们*目前*所知，”我指出，“也许会查明，有人在他同吉姆·盖恩赛照面之后还同他说过话。”

“盖恩赛偷听了你们的谈话。希金斯见过脸的那个人，一旦坐实这样的证据便什么都完了。除盖恩赛和您之外，基特小姐，再无人知道有这档子事。我很遗憾；盖恩赛瞧着倒是个挺正派的年轻人。”他顿了顿，在口袋里摸索，掏出一截秃了头的铅笔，在那双修长光洁的手指间翻来覆去地捻着。

===Original===
The light above my head was paling in the slow, gray light of early morning which was struggling in through the windows and making the whole place more desolate and more grim and forbidding than it had been in the dark of night.

“It is a difficult situation,” he said presently.

I pushed my cap farther back on my head and rubbed my hand across my eyes—eyes that were tired and weary with what they had seen that night.

“I dread the effect of this night’s doing; it will almost demoralize our staff, to say nothing of its effect upon outsiders. We are looking to you to straighten out this hideous tangle. And it must be soon.”

===Chinese===
头顶的灯光渐渐淡了，融进清晨迟缓的灰光里；那灰光费力地从窗户挤进来，把整个地方照得比深夜时分更荒凉、更阴森、更叫人却步。

“局面很棘手，”过了一会儿他说。

我把帽子朝脑后推了推，抬手揉了揉眼睛——这双眼，已被那一夜之所见熬得又累又乏。

“我怕这一夜的祸事惹出的后果；这几乎要把我们的员工搅得军心涣散，更不必说对外人的影响了。我们都指望您来解开这团乱麻。而且必须快。”

===Original===
His face was very sober.

“I hope to do so,” he said gravely. “I think I am not saying too much when I tell you that I have good reason to hope for success.”

There was a restrained little throb of exhilaration in his voice.

“Do you mean—” I began sharply. He interrupted me.

“I mean only that I am beginning to arrive at some conclusions.” And without giving me a chance to ask what those conclusions were he continued at once: “Are you sure Higgins said it was a *man’s* face that he saw?”

===Chinese===
他面色凝重。

“我希望能够做到，”他郑重地说，“我告诉您我有充分的理由指望成功，这话我想说得并不算满。”

他的嗓音里有一丝按捺着的振奋。

“您是说——”我急急开口。他打断了我。

“我只是说，我开始得出些结论了。”不等我有机会问是些什么结论，他紧接着便说：“您能肯定希金斯说的是他见到了一张*男人的*脸吗？”

===Original===
I went back in my memory, over that brief and baffling conversation, now never to be finished. Poor Higgins!

“No,” I said thoughtfully. “He did not definitely say it was a man. I—I’m afraid I just assumed it to be a man.”

“Assuming is dangerous,” said O’Leary quietly. “But he did say that he saw three people?”

“He said he knew that there were three people in Room 18 that night.”

“And that there were four people—Corole Letheny and Dr. Hajek and Jim Gainsay and—Dr. Letheny in and about St. Ann’s that dark midnight?”

===Chinese===
我在记忆里回头走去，把那场简短而叫人捉摸不透的谈话重温了一遍——如今永远续不上了。可怜的希金斯！

“不能，”我若有所思地说，“他并没有明说是个男人。我——我怕是我自己想当然，当成了男人。”

“想当然是危险的，”奥利里平静地说，“可他确实说过，他看见了三个人？”

“他说他知道那天夜里18号病房（Room 18）里有三个人。”

“还说那个漆黑的午夜，科罗尔·莱瑟尼、海耶克医生、吉姆·盖恩赛，还有——莱瑟尼医生，四个人都在圣安医院里里外外？”

===Original===
I nodded confirmatively.

“He said, too, that he saw Jim Gainsay’s face by the light of a match. And that he saw the face of the man—or person—who killed Jackson by the light of the match.”

“But that doesn’t prove—” I began hotly.

“No—no, of course not,” he said absently. “You say he was of the opinion that the man—”

“He kept saying the ‘party,’” I interpolated.

“Who killed Jackson and the—the party—” with a rather grim tightening of his lips, O’Leary adopted Higgins’s terms—“Who killed Dr. Letheny was not the same person.”

“He said ‘no, that couldn’t hardly be.’” Strange how vividly I recalled his hesitating confession.

===Chinese===
我点头称是。

“他还说，他借着火柴的光看见了吉姆·盖恩赛的脸。又说借着那火柴的光，看见了杀死杰克逊的那个男人——或者说那个人——的脸。”

“可那证明不了——”我急赤白脸地开口。

“不——不，当然证明不了，”他心不在焉地说，“您说他认为那男人——”

“他翻来覆去说的是‘那位主儿’。”我插了一句。

“杀死杰克逊的，和那位——那位主儿——”奥利里嘴唇绷紧，神情冷峻，沿用了希金斯的用词——“杀死莱瑟尼医生的，并不是同一个人。”

“他说‘不，那恐怕不大可能’。”说来奇怪，他那吞吞吐吐的自白，我回想起来竟如此真切。

===Original===
“It is apparent, of course, that the man in Room 18 must have had some sort of light, if only for a second, in order to conceal the radium. Higgins knew where it was all the time. He swore to me that he had slept through the whole night. Well—” O’Leary’s shoulders lifted a little.

“We will never know now what Higgins saw,” I commented, my thoughts sombre.

O’Leary raised his eyes from the pencil for a moment.

“Don’t be too sure of that, Miss Keate. Did you get the speaker for me?”

“Yes.”

“Put it in a *safe* place?”

I nodded. “I longed to look inside it but did not.”

He smiled.

“Suppose we look now.”

===Chinese===
“当然，显而易见，18号病房（Room 18）里的那个人藏匿镭的时候，手头必定有过某种光亮，哪怕只亮一秒钟。希金斯自始至终都知道镭的下落。他对我赌咒发誓，说他一整夜睡得死死的。呃——”奥利里的肩膀微微一耸。

“希金斯看见了什么，如今永远无从知道了。”我说，心思黯淡。

奥利里的目光从铅笔上抬起来，望了片刻。

“这话也别说得太满，基特小姐。您替我把扬声器取来了吗？”

“取来了。”

“搁在*稳妥*地方了？”

我点点头。“我很想看看里头，可到底没看。”

他笑了。

“那我们现在就看看。”

===Original===
The rustle of my starched skirts echoed against the empty gray-white walls. The general office was deserted, likewise the stairs and corridors. Once in my room I unlocked the door of the chifferette, withdrew the speaker, and holding it carefully, hastened back to the south wing. O’Leary was still sitting beside the chart desk, his gray gaze on Maida, who was bent over an entry she was making on Three’s chart. If she wondered what I was doing with the loud speaker she did not say so but returned immediately to Three.

I set the speaker down on the shining glass top of the chart desk. My hands were shaking a little and I held my breath while O’Leary removed one of the sides of the speaker. We both peered into it. Then O’Leary put his hand inside and groped around.

We stared at the compact arrangement of wires and tiny coils and screws, then met each other’s gaze.

===Chinese===
我那浆硬的裙子窸窣作响，回声撞在空落落的灰白墙上。总办公室阒无一人，楼梯和走廊也是一样。一到自己房里，我便开了小衣柜的锁，取出扬声器，小心翼翼捧着，赶回南翼。奥利里仍坐在病历台旁，灰色目光落在梅达身上，她正伏案往三号的病历单上登记。她若纳闷我抱着扬声器做什么，也没有说出来，只立刻又回三号那边去了。

我把扬声器搁在病历台亮晶晶的玻璃台面上。我的手有些发抖，屏住呼吸，看奥利里卸下扬声器的一块侧板。我们俩都朝里张望。然后奥利里伸手进去，四下摸索。

我们盯着那排得密密实实的电线、小线圈和螺丝，随即对视了一眼。

===Original===
“Nothing!” I said.

“Nothing!” confirmed O’Leary. He studied the thing thoughtfully for a moment.

“Did anyone see you take this to your room?”

“No one. That is, no one but—but Maida. I met her at the door just as I was carrying it to my room.”

“Miss Day—h’mm.” And after another pause: “Are you sure that this is the same speaker that was in Room 18?”

===Chinese===
“什么都没有！”我说。

“什么都没有！”奥利里证实道。他对着那东西若有所思地端详了片刻。

“您把它拿回房去，有人看见吗？”

“没人。就是说，只有——只有梅达。我抱着它回房时，恰在门口撞见她。”

“戴小姐——唔。”又停了停：“您能肯定，这就是原先放在18号病房（Room 18）里的那只扬声器？”

===Original===
“Why, yes. No. That is—” I hastened to explain as he cast a decidedly irritated glance at me—“that is, I mean that this is the speaker that was in Sonny’s room and I just assumed it to be the one that I had left there.”

“Assuming again,” remarked O’Leary with dry disapproval. “It might have been one from another room, then?”

“Yes. It might have been. But I think—”

“Did you know that the speaker at present in Room 18 has been torn open, probably during the night?”

“What!”

“Evidently the—er—visitor in Room 18 tonight thought what we thought and did not know that the original speaker in Room 18 had been removed. Or else—” He left his sentence uncompleted, turned abruptly and strode down the hall to Sonny’s room.

===Chinese===
“呃，是。不是。也就是说——”见他朝我投来一记分明透着恼意的一瞥，我赶紧解释——“也就是说，我的意思是，这是原先在桑尼病房里的那只，我便想当然，当我留下的就是它。”

“又是想当然，”奥利里干巴巴地说，颇不以为然。“那么，它也可能是别的病房里的一只？”

“是。有可能。不过我想——”

“您可知道，如今在18号病房（Room 18）里的那只扬声器，已经被人拆开过了，多半就是在夜里？”

“什么！”

“显然，今晚18号病房里的那位——呃——来客，想到了我们所想的，却不知道18号病房原先那只扬声器已被换走。再不然就是——”话没说完，他猛地转身，大步沿走廊往桑尼的病房去了。

===Original===
I followed him to the door. Sonny was awake.

“Good morning,” said O’Leary kindly. “It is rather early in the morning for young fellows like you to be awake. Look here, Sonny, the other night Miss Keate brought in a loudspeaker for the radio attachment, just like this one in my hand. She left it here and took away the one that you already had on your table. Then last night, she came in and took away the speaker she had left with you. I want to know whether the loud speaker she took away last night was the very same speaker she brought in here.”

Sonny looked bewildered and O’Leary repeated his question patiently and clearly.

===Chinese===
我跟到门口。桑尼醒着。

“早上好，”奥利里和颜悦色地说，“像你这样的小伙子，这个钟点就醒了，未免太早了些。听着，桑尼，前几天夜里，基特小姐拿来一只配无线电用的扬声器，就同我手里这只一样。她把它留在这儿，把你桌上原有的那只拿走了。然后昨天夜里，她又进来，把她留在你这儿的那只拿走了。我想知道，她昨晚拿走的那只扬声器，和她当初拿来的，是不是恰好同一只。”

桑尼一脸茫然，奥利里便耐心而清楚地把问题又问了一遍。

===Original===
“Why, no,” said Sonny finally. “That speaker she brought in wouldn’t work.”

“What happened to it, then?”

“Why”—Sonny frowned—“Miss Day was in to see me and I told her the speaker wasn’t working so she took it away and brought me another. The one she brought in worked fine. But Miss Keate came and got it last night.” He looked reproachfully at me.

“Thank you, Sonny,” said O’Leary briefly.

===Chinese===
“哦，不是，”桑尼末了答道，“她拿来的那只不响。”

“那它后来怎么了？”

“呃——”桑尼皱起眉——“戴小姐来看我，我说扬声器不响了，她就把它带走，另给我送来一只。她送来的那只好用得很。可基特小姐昨晚来把它拿走了。”他埋怨地望了我一眼。

“谢谢你，桑尼。”奥利里简短地说。

===Original===
I have never seen O’Leary showing any feeling or excitement, but there were eighteen rooms in that wing and I don’t think it took him eighteen minutes to examine all the loud speakers in the whole wing. He did not omit one save, of course, that already rifled speaker in Room 18.

When he had finished, still without any results that I could see, he went to Maida.

“Miss Day,” he began, “you took a loud speaker exactly like this one”—he still carried under his arm the instrument that I had so futilely treasured—“from Sonny’s room last night. What did you do with it?”

Maida put back a wisp of black hair that had strayed from under her immaculate cap; her blue eyes regarded us steadily from the weary, dark circles about them.

===Chinese===
我从没见奥利里流露过什么情绪或兴奋，可那一翼共有十八间病房，我敢说他查遍全翼所有的扬声器，断断用不了十八分钟。一间也没有漏，当然，18号病房（Room 18）里那只已被撬开的不算。

查完之后，照我看仍是一无所获，他便向梅达走去。

“戴小姐，”他开口道，“昨晚您从桑尼的病房拿走了一只同这只一模一样的扬声器”——他腋下还夹着那件我曾煞有介事地珍藏、终究白费工夫的物什——“您把它怎么处置了？”

梅达把一绺从一丝不苟的帽子底下溜出来的黑发抿了回去；她那双蓝眼睛从疲惫的黑眼圈里定定地望着我们。

===Original===
“I put it on the table in Room 18,” she replied at once. “It was out of order somehow, and I thought likely Room 18 would be unoccupied. So I simply exchanged the speakers.”

“Thank you, Miss Day. You did not—er—examine it closely to see what was wrong with it?”

“No,” she said. “I know nothing of such things; I couldn’t possibly have repaired it.”

She went on about her errand.

===Chinese===
“我把它放在18号病房（Room 18）的桌上了。”她随即答道，“它不知怎么坏了，我想18号病房多半空着不会有人住。所以我索性把两只扬声器对调了一下。”

“谢谢您，戴小姐。您没有——呃——仔细查看过它坏在哪儿吧？”

“没有，”她说，“这类东西我一窍不通；我根本不可能修得好它。”

她继续忙她的活儿去了。

===Original===
“A strange case,” mused O’Leary, his clear, gray eyes following the slim, white-clad figure moving away from us. “The speaker in Room 18 was the right one, after all. The question is, was the radium in it and if so who took it? Who has it now? When we know that answer we will know who shot poor old Higgins.” He went to the window over the chart desk, flung it up to the sash, and took a deep breath of the fog-laden air. His intent young face, his curiously lucid gray eyes, showed no hint of a night without sleep.

“A strange case,” he repeated absently. He turned from the dripping gray orchard beyond the window, fingered idly the bronzed surface of the loud speaker there on the desk.

“Another thing, Miss Keate—did you notice that when Dr. Hajek came from his room tonight, presumably from his bed, he wore trousers under that bathrobe that he held so tightly to him? And that those trousers had fresh, wet mud stains about the cuffs?”

I murmured something, I don’t know what, and O’Leary met my shocked gaze quietly.

“And furthermore,” he said softly, “I found fresh mud stains on the window sill of his room. Really, Miss Keate, this hospital of yours should have been built with its first floor higher from the ground. Entrances and exits are too easy.”

===Chinese===
“一桩离奇的案子，”奥利里沉吟道，那双清亮的灰眼睛追着那窈窕的白衣身影远去。“18号病房（Room 18）里的扬声器，到底还是原来那只。问题是，镭可在它里面？若在，是谁取走的？如今又在谁手里？等我们知道了这个答案，也就知道是谁枪杀了可怜的老希金斯。”他走到病历台上方的窗前，一把推到顶，深深吸了一口饱含雾气的空气。他那张专注的年轻的脸、那双出奇清明的灰眼睛，看不出一点彻夜未眠的痕迹。

“一桩离奇的案子。”他又心不在焉地重复了一遍。他把目光从窗外滴水的灰蒙蒙的果园收回来，信手摩挲着桌上那只扬声器古铜色的表面。

“还有一桩，基特小姐——您注意到没有，今晚海耶克医生从房里出来、想必是从床上起来的时候，他那件紧紧裹住的浴袍底下，穿着长裤？而且裤脚上还沾着新鲜湿泥的印子？”

我含含糊糊应了句什么，自己也不知说了什么，奥利里静静地迎住我震惊的目光。

“再者，”他轻声说，“我在他房间的窗台上也发现了新鲜的泥印。说真的，基特小姐，你们这座医院，当初真该把一楼砌得离地高些。进出未免太容易了。”

## XIII The Radium Appears / 镭的现形

===Original===
How true it is that time, in retrospect, is measured by the events occurring therein. By which I mean, of course, that while the whole sequence of mysterious and shocking events that so deeply troubled us there at St. Ann’s really occurred during a period of only a few days, when I look back at the affair it seems to have extended over weeks. It was true, too, that every day seemed to bring its problems and those before yesterday’s problems were solved. In fact these problems so crowded each other that the only way in which I can recall their exact sequence is by referring to the days on which they took place. For instance, I see that in my account book where I have an orderly habit of noting certain things such as birthdays of relatives, dates when my insurance falls due, and such matters, I have noted several items under Wednesday, June 13th:

> Higgins killed in Eighteen during second watch, last night. No clue so far as I know. Wish J. G. would go on about his bridge building. Whole hospital much upset; several nurses threatening to leave. Police underfoot everywhere and suppose it means the whole thing over again. Sent laundry this a.m. Am getting nervous about second watch. Twelve pearl buttons. Wish this affair were safely over.

The “twelve pearl buttons” entry, of course, referred to the fact that I had forgotten to take them out of one of my uniforms before it went to the laundry and must remember to telephone the laundry about them. It was owing to these buttons, however, that one of the most singular and troublesome facts of the whole week came to my attention.
===Chinese===
时间这东西，回头望去，原来是拿其间发生的事件来计量的——此话何其真切。我自然是指：在圣安医院里深深困扰我们的那一连串离奇骇人的事件，实际发生不过寥寥数日；可如今回望这桩案子，却仿佛绵延了好几个星期。同样不假的是，每一天似乎都送来它各自的难题，而且是在昨日的难题解决之前便已压到。其实这些难题你推我搡、拥挤不堪，我要记起它们确切的先后次序，唯一的办法就是对照它们各自发生的日子。比方说，我那本记事簿——我有条不紊的习惯，亲戚的生日、保险到期的日子之类事项都记在上面——在6月13日星期三那一栏底下，就记着这么几条：

> 希金斯昨夜第二班期间在18号病房（Room 18）遇害。据我所知尚无线索。但愿J. G. 还是回去操心他的造桥为好。全院人心大乱；几名护士闹着要走。警察满地绊脚，想必这一切又得从头再演一遍。今晨送洗了衣物。开始对第二班心里发怵。珍珠纽扣十二颗。但愿这桩事早早了结，平安无事。

“珍珠纽扣十二颗”那一条，自然是指我忘了把它们从一件制服上拆下来，那件制服便连着纽扣一起进了洗衣房，我得记着为这事给洗衣房打个电话。然而，恰恰是多亏这些纽扣，整个星期里最离奇、最棘手的一桩事实才落进了我的眼里。

===Original===
If the second watch of the previous night had seemed like a repetition of a bad dream, then that day, Wednesday, was its continuation. The directors, irate and fussy and hysterically horrified, descended upon St. Ann’s. There were the police, O’Leary, and newspaper men just as it had been before. The only difference was that this last development seemed more terrible than that other﻿—if that were possible. There was a rather grisly fear stalking through the hushed hospital corridors: Who would be the next victim?

The inquest was held at once, that very morning. It was a brief and formal affair, held in the main office with only a few present. Nothing was proved beyond the immediate fact of Higgins’s death and nothing was mentioned that I did not already know. It was evident that O’Leary regarded Higgins’s death as another piece in the puzzle that confronted him and not as an isolated crime.

Shortly after lunch Lance O’Leary called me into the office.

“Why did you not tell me that the key to the south door disappeared last night?” he began abruptly.

“I forgot it. I ordered a new key to be made for that lock and will have it before night. But of course I had to leave the south door unlocked last night.”
===Chinese===
倘若说前一夜的第二班已像一场噩梦的重演，那么这一天，星期三，便是那噩梦的延续。董事们又怒又躁、歇斯底里地惊骇莫名，成群压到了圣安医院。警察、奥利里、报馆的人，一如从前。唯一的分别是：这最新的一幕似乎比先前那桩更加可怕——倘若还有更可怕的余地的话。一种颇为阴森的恐惧，正悄悄巡行在噤了声的医院走廊里：下一个受害者会是谁？

验尸审讯当即开庭，就在那天上午。一桩简短而照章办事的过场，地点在总办公室，在场者寥寥。除了希金斯死亡这一直接事实，什么也没查证出来；所提到的一切又无一不是我早已知道的。看得出，奥利里把希金斯之死看作摆在他面前那盘谜题里的又一块拼板，而不是一桩孤立的罪行。

午饭过后不久，兰斯·奥利里把我叫进了办公室。

“您为什么不告诉我，昨夜南门的钥匙不见了？”他劈头就说。

“我把这事忘了。我已叫人照那把锁配了新钥匙，天黑前就能到手。可昨夜南门自然只能敞着不落锁。”

===Original===
“It seems to me you forget rather important things.” He spoke sharply.

“I have certain duties to think of,” I responded as sharply. “And anyway you didn’t ask me.”

The tightness around his eyes relaxed somewhat but he did not smile. He rose, went to the door, and after a dissatisfied glance into the main hall he beckoned me into the inner office, shut the door and sat down at the desk. For a moment he sat there silently, his face in his hands.

“Sit down, Miss Keate,” he said presently, motioning toward Dr. Balman’s cot, and as I did so he swung around in the swivel chair to face me. “Hope nobody wants to use this room for a few moments,” he said wearily. “I’ve got to think. Look here, was that key gone when you came on duty last night at twelve o’clock?”

“Yes. Olma Flynn, who has first watch, could not find it. She told me of its disappearance as soon as I came on duty.”
===Chinese===
“照我看，您忘掉的事情可相当要紧。”他的话说得尖锐。

“我还有一摊职责要操心呢。”我回敬得同样尖锐，“再说您也没问过我。”

他眼角紧绷的神色松了松，却没有笑。他起身走到门口，朝大厅里不满地扫了一眼，然后招手让我进了里间办公室，关上门，在写字台前坐下。有一阵他默默坐在那里，双手掩着脸。

“坐吧，基特小姐。”过了片刻他说，朝巴尔曼医生那张帆布床摆了摆手；我坐下的当口，他已随着转椅转过来，正对着我。“但愿这几分钟没人要用这间屋子。”他倦倦地说。“我得想一想。听着，昨夜您十二点钟来接班的时候，那把钥匙已经不见了么？”

“是的。值第一班的奥尔玛·弗琳怎么也找不到它。我一接班，她立刻就告诉了我钥匙失踪的事。”

===Original===
He nodded slowly.

“Thus providing an easy way into St. Ann’s.﻿ ﻿… Into the south wing﻿—” he murmured, and broke off, staring into space, his eyes clouded and far away.

Then all at once he began to talk, leaned back in the chair, and linked his hands together.

“In the first place,” he began, “I am convinced that the three crimes are all linked together and that the possession of the radium is the guiding motive. Other motives, such as protection or fear, may enter into the affair but the radium is the main thing. If the radium was actually placed in that loud speaker, it is now in the hands of the person who killed Higgins. To secure the radium was the reason for his entrance into the south wing and into Room 18 last night. We can’t know why Higgins was there﻿—unless﻿—unless﻿—You say that he knew where the radium was hidden; he may have tried to take it himself into his own hands.”
===Chinese===
他缓缓点头。

“这便给出了一条进圣安医院的便道。……进南翼的便道——”他喃喃道，随即收住话头，怔怔望着虚空，眼神晦暗而渺远。

接着他陡然打开了话头，往椅背上一靠，十指交叉扣在一起。

“首先，”他开腔道，“我确信这三桩罪行彼此关联，而对镭的占有正是贯穿始终的主导动机。别的动机，比如自保或者恐惧，也可能掺进这案子里，但镭才是主要的。倘若镭当真被放进了那只扬声器，那么它如今就在杀死希金斯的那个人手里。夺取镭，正是他昨夜进入南翼、进入18号病房（Room 18）的缘由。我们无从知道希金斯为何在那儿——除非——除非——您说过，他知道镭藏在何处；他也许动过念头，想把那东西亲自抓到自己手里。”

===Original===
He paused as if to consider that possibility; it did not appear to convince him, for he made an impatient gesture.

“Dr. Hajek,” he resumed, “has flatly denied that he was out last night; the mud has been brushed off his trousers and off the window sill and it is my word against his. Why is he lying? Then, too, there was someone from the Letheny cottage about the grounds last night. Huldah says that someone left the house about midnight; she heard footsteps on the stairs, and the front door squeaks. She did not know whether it was Gainsay or Miss Letheny, but she is certain that someone went out of that house about midnight and returned probably an hour later.”

“Huldah tells the truth always﻿—” I began, but checked myself. If he was willing to talk I was more than willing to listen.

However, I had interrupted him; he looked at me directly and began to speak more briskly and less as if he were thinking aloud.

“You see, Miss Keate, it is all simmering down to the same group, the same circle of those in and about St. Ann’s. No one else could have stolen the key to the south door. And as I say, I am inclined to believe that all three crimes had the same motive, if not the same motivating force.”
===Chinese===
他顿了顿，像是在掂量这个可能；这念头似乎说服不了他自己，因为他做了个不耐烦的手势。

“海耶克医生，”他接着说，“一口咬定昨夜没出过门；他裤子上和窗台上的泥都刷掉了，如今是我的话对他之说。他为什么要撒谎？再者，昨夜还有个从莱瑟尼小屋出来的人在院子里转悠。赫尔达说，午夜前后有人离开了那座房子；她听见楼梯上有脚步声，前门也吱呀响了一声。她说不上那是盖恩赛还是莱瑟尼小姐，但她确信午夜前后有人走出那座房子，大约一小时后又回来了。”

“赫尔达向来句句实话——”我起了个头，又把话咽了回去。他既肯讲，我巴不得只管听。

不过我总算打断了他；他直直地看着我，说得起劲了些，不那么像自言自语了。

“您瞧，基特小姐，一切都在收拢到同一伙人身上——圣安医院里里外外这同一个圈子。换了旁人，谁也偷不走南门的钥匙。而且如我方才所说，我倾向于认为三桩罪行同出一个动机，纵然未必出自同一股驱动的力量。”

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“I mean that in the first two crimes we find several means of death. This leads me to believe that there was a definite plan to steal the radium, possibly on the part of more than one person. In fact, I am quite sure that more than one person had determined to get hold of that radium. But the radium was left in the room. Hidden, but still in Room 18. Why? There is only one possible reason. The thief was interrupted, was forced to hide it there in order to return for it later. But why did the radium remain for so long in the speaker? Why did not the thief return for it earlier in the game? It all points to there being several people interested in that radium, which means, of course, that we may be trying to discover three murderers instead of only one.”

“*Three!*”

“There were three murders,” he said, laconically cool in the face of my horror. “And Higgins’s statement seems to make it sure that the first two murders were not committed by the same man.”

“I am positive that the radium was concealed in the loud speaker,” he continued after a short pause. “There was no place else for it to be and it must have been in Room 18, for otherwise we would not have had such a series of disturbances in and about that room. Yes, it is evident that several people were convinced that the radium was still in the room and were searching for it. The thing that bothers me is the failure of the﻿—er﻿—original thief to return and remove the radium before anyone else found it.”
===Chinese===
“这话怎么讲？”

“我是说，在前两桩罪行里，我们见到了不止一种致死手段。这使我相信：窃镭是有一份明确预谋的，而且谋划者可能不止一人。事实上我相当确信，打那镭主意的决不止一个人。可镭被留在了病房里。藏起来了，却仍在18号病房（Room 18）。为什么？可能的缘由只有一个。窃贼被人搅了局，只得先把它藏进那儿，日后再来取。可镭为什么在扬声器里一待这么久？窃贼为何不在这局棋的早段就回来取走？这一切都指向：觊觎那镭的不止一人。这自然也就意味着，我们要揪出的可能是三个凶手，而不是一个。”

“*三个！*”

“一共出了三桩命案。”他说，面对我的惊怖，口气冷峻简省。“而希金斯留下的那番话，似乎坐实了前两桩命案并非同一人所为。”

“我可以断言，镭当时就藏在扬声器里。”略停了停他又道。“旁处没有可藏的地方，而它必定就在18号病房，否则那间病房内外不至于闹出这一连串的骚动。是的，显然有好几个人认定镭仍在那间病房里，一直在搜寻它。让我犯嘀咕的是，那位——呃——头一个窃贼，竟迟迟不回来赶在旁人发现之前把镭取走。”

===Original===
“Perhaps it was he last night,” I suggested.

O’Leary did not appear to hear me.

“There is only one reason and that﻿—if true﻿—is amazing.” He reached absently for the shabby little stub of pencil and began twisting it in his fingers, which convinced me that he was on his feet again, so to speak.

Whatever the “amazing” speculation was that had occurred to him, he said nothing more of it.

“I have eliminated certain factors. The first thing to do, you know is to narrow the field of investigation. I find that Mr. Jackson’s relatives, who might be supposed to have an interest in his death, have ironclad alibis.”

“Oh.” I spoke none too brightly as I had never given a thought to Mr. Jackson’s relatives.
===Chinese===
“也许昨夜回来的正是他呢。”我提了一句。

奥利里像是没有听见。

“缘由只有一个，而这个缘由——倘若属实——可就惊人了。”他心不在焉地伸手够到那截寒碜的铅笔头，用指头捻来捻去；这个动作让我确信：不妨打个比方，他又重新下地走动了。

他心里闪过的那个“惊人的”猜想究竟是什么，他没有再多说一个字。

“我已经排除了若干因素。头一件事，您知道，就是把侦查的圈子收窄。我查明，杰克逊先生的亲属——照理说，他们对他的死亡最可能有利可图——个个都有铁打的不在场证明。”

“哦。”我应得并不起劲，因为我从未想起过杰克逊先生还有什么亲属。

===Original===
“Likewise I am gradually eliminating the unknown factor﻿—I mean by that the possibility of an outsider, a hobo, perhaps, or professional thief acting on the spur of the moment, or following out a planned course of action. It seems more and more certain that those guilty of these crimes are people who are in and about St. Ann’s. But since that phase of the matter is so distasteful to you.﻿ ﻿…” His voice trailed away into nothing, he dropped the pencil, adjusted his tie, looked at his watch, ran a hand through his hair and reached for the pencil again.

“There are a few matters of which I’ve been wanting to talk with you, Miss Keate. This﻿—” he lowered his voice﻿—“this Hajek. Somehow I have got the impression that he and Miss Letheny see a good deal of each other. Different people have mentioned seeing them together. Huldah says he is a frequent caller. What do you think?”

“Why, yes﻿—now that I think of it, it does seem to me that they have a sort of﻿—” At loss for a word I stopped. O’Leary completed the sentence.

“Understanding?”

“Well, yes. And yet I have seen nothing definite. It is just a feeling that I have. And of course, the fact that he has been up at the Letheny cottage a great deal. I’ve seen him there often.”

He twisted the pencil up and down; I wondered that there was any shred of paint remaining on the shabby thing.
===Chinese===
“同样，我也在逐步排除未知因素——我是指外来者的可能：兴许是个流浪汉，或是见机起意的职业窃贼，或是照既定步骤行事的惯盗。越来越可以断定：犯下这些罪行的，都是圣安医院内外的人。不过，既然这一层如此令您不快。……”他的声音低下去，散得没了踪影；他丢开铅笔，整了整领带，看了看表，一只手抹过头发，又把铅笔捞了回来。

“有几桩事，我一直想同您谈谈，基特小姐。这——”他压低了嗓音——“这个海耶克。不知怎么，我落下了这么个印象：他和莱瑟尼小姐过从甚密。好些不同的人都提起过撞见他们俩在一起。赫尔达说，他是小屋的常客。您怎么看？”

“呃，是啊——这会儿想起来，他们之间倒确实像是有那么一种——”我一时找不着字眼，住了口。奥利里替我把那句话续完。

“默契？”

“唔，是啊。可我没见识过什么确凿的。只不过是我心里的一种感觉罢了。当然喽，还有他频频上莱瑟尼小屋去这一层。我在那儿常见到他。”

他把铅笔捻上捻下；我诧异那寒碜玩意儿上竟还剩得下一星半点的漆皮。

===Original===
“Another thing,” he began rather hesitantly. “They say﻿—don’t ask me who says, for it is a sort of drifting gossip that we detectives have to encourage﻿—they say that Dr. Letheny admired the pretty nurse.”

“The pretty nurse. Who?”

“I thought you’d guess,” he said quietly. “I mean Miss Day.”

“If he did admire her, I never knew it,” I said with vigour.

“You never even surmised it?” he persisted gently.

“No,” I said bluntly. “Certainly not.” And then recalled certain things. That last dinner﻿—Dr. Letheny’s smouldering eyes on Maida﻿—the gesture with which he took her wrap﻿—those burning, restless eyes seeking her in the corridor of the south wing before he turned away through the door and I caught my last glimpse of Dr. Letheny alive. “That is﻿—perhaps﻿—yes,” I amended in a smaller voice.
===Chinese===
“还有一件事。”他开腔得颇为踌躇。“他们说——别问我是谁说的，这类随风飘的闲话，正是我们做侦探的不得不下工夫引逗的——他们说，莱瑟尼医生倾心于那位漂亮的护士。”

“那位漂亮的护士。谁？”

“我当您猜得着。”他安安静静地说。“我指的是戴小姐。”

“他若真倾心于她，我可从不知情。”我说得斩钉截铁。

“连一丝一毫也没揣度过？”他柔声追问。

“没有。”我答得干脆。“绝对没有。”话音才落，却记起了几件事。最后那场晚宴——莱瑟尼医生落在梅达身上那双阴燃的眼睛——他接过她外衣时的那一姿态——还有他在南翼走廊里四处搜寻她的、那双灼灼不安的眼睛，随后他才转身出门；我最后一眼望见活着的莱瑟尼医生，便是在那一眼。“这就是说——兴许——是啊。”我放小了声音改口。

===Original===
“Did Miss Day return his﻿—interest?”

“No. I’m sure that she did not. Quite the contrary.”

“Quite the contrary?”

“I mean that I believe she disliked him particularly. I do not know why.”

He lifted his eyes from the pencil. They were clear now and very gray.

“You would likely know,” he said casually. “You possess the strangest aura of﻿—integrity. One feels you are a respecter of confidences. I presume you are the repository of many secrets.”
===Chinese===
“戴小姐可回报过他的——青睐？”

“没有。我敢肯定没有。恰恰相反。”

“恰恰相反？”

“我是说，我相信她对他格外厌恶。个中缘故，我不得而知。”

他把目光从铅笔上抬起来。那双眼睛此刻清朗，灰得纯粹。

“您多半会知情的。”他随口说道。“您周身有一股再奇特不过的——正气。让人觉出，您是个守得住旁人心事的人。我料想，许多秘密都以您为存放之处。”

===Original===
“I’m sure I don’t know any secrets,” I replied hastily. The man needn’t think he could worm things out of me. “I wish,” I added, “I wish that you had talked to Higgins.”

His expression became serious at once.

“I wish so too,” he said soberly. “Though as far as that goes I did talk to Higgins, but couldn’t get a thing out of him. He must have been desperately afraid of getting into trouble.” He eyed the stub of pencil solicitously. “… getting into trouble,” he repeated musingly.

“If I had only known the danger he was in,” I said regretfully. “But somehow we never know until it is too late.”

“About this matter of the lights going out last night. It seems to coincide too strangely with the affair of Thursday night. The lights being out at that time was, of course, an accident, but one is inclined to think that someone profited by that accident to such an extent that he decided to repeat the fortuitous circumstances. But it was actually no accident this time; the switch plug had been purposely pulled out. Now then, the switch box is in the basement, on the wall next to the grade door that leads out just below the main entrance.”

I nodded as his keen, serious eyes rose to mine.
===Chinese===
“我敢说什么秘密也不知道。”我慌忙回答。这个人休想从我嘴里套出话去。“我但愿，”我又添上一句，“我但愿当初您找希金斯谈过话。”

他的神色霎时郑重起来。

“我也但愿如此。”他沉声说。“不过话虽如此，我确实找希金斯谈过，却一个字也没能掏出来。他准是怕极了惹祸上身。”他殷殷地端详着那截铅笔头。“……惹祸上身。”他又咀嚼似的念了一遍。

“要是我早知道他所处的险境就好了。”我懊悔地说。“可不知怎么，我们总是懂得太迟。”

“再说说昨夜灯灭的事。它跟星期四夜里那桩事重合得未免过于蹊跷。那一回灯灭当然是个意外，可人不免要想：有人从那场意外里渔了偌大的利，索性决意把这凑巧的情势再演一回。而这一回其实绝非意外；电闸的插销是叫人蓄意拔掉的。要知道，配电箱在地下室，就装在地坪门（grade door）旁边的墙上；那扇门通向户外，出口恰在正大门下方。”

他那双锐利而郑重的眼睛抬起来望住我，我点了点头。

===Original===
“That grade door was locked and the key inside the lock as it should be. Was there time, Miss Keate, between the lights going out and the sound of the shot for someone to come from that grade door around the corner of the hospital, enter the south door in the darkness, go into Eighteen, which is right next to the south door, take the radium from the loud speaker and﻿—and that is as far as we know. We can only surmise, now, how Higgins came into it.”

“The intruder might have been Higgins, himself.” I was suddenly struck by the thought. “He would have access to the basement, could have stolen the key from the chart desk that would open the south door if it were locked. Perhaps he was taking the radium out of the speaker; he told me, you know, that he knew where it was hidden.”

“All the circumstances point to what we call an inside job,” admitted O’Leary slowly. “But someone besides Higgins was in Room 18.”

“The window?” I suggested.

“No. He could not have come through the window for it was still bolted. How about it, Miss Keate?” He returned to his inquiry. “How long a time elapsed between the lights going out and the sound of the shot?”

“It seemed a long time,” I said hesitantly. “You see, it was so still and dark and I was a little frightened. I waited for a few moments, thinking that the lights would come on again. Yes, I think there was time enough for﻿—for all that you think took place. While I waited I felt a current of air on my shoulders.”
===Chinese===
“那扇地坪门锁着，钥匙也照规矩留在锁眼里。基特小姐，从灯灭到枪响之间，时间够不够一个人从地坪门绕过医院的墙角、摸黑进了南门、走进紧挨着南门的18号病房、从扬声器里取走镭，然后——往后的事，我们就不得而知了。希金斯是怎么卷进去的，如今只能凭推测。”

“闯进去的，兴许正是希金斯本人。”我突然被这个念头击中了。“他进得了地下室，也可能从病历台上偷走那把南门钥匙——门若锁着，那钥匙一开就开。说不定他当时正把镭往外取呢；您知道，他对我说过，他知道镭藏在哪儿。”

“种种情形，都指向我们所谓的内部人作案。”奥利里缓缓地承认。“可是除了希金斯，另有别人也进过18号病房（Room 18）。”

“窗户呢？”我提示道。

“不。那人不可能打窗户进来，窗闩还闩得好好的。怎么样，基特小姐？”他回到他的盘问上。“从灯灭到枪响，中间过了多久？”

“似乎过了很久。”我迟疑地说。“您知道，四下那么静、那么黑，我又有些害怕。我等了一会儿，指望灯还会再亮起来。是的，我想时间尽够——够您方才设想的一切发生。等候的当口，我觉得肩头掠过一股气流。”

===Original===
He looked up quickly.

“That was the door opening, then. You are sure about the length of time? You see it is rather important that we settle that point definitely for if there was not time for all that to go on, it would indicate that there were two people, *besides Higgins*, who were interested in getting into Room 18 last night. And that one of them managed the business of turning off the lights and the other came into Room 18 with the results of which we know. Confound it!” He broke off suddenly. “I wish I needn’t have to figure on more than one or two ways of getting in and out of this old hospital. Don’t you *ever* have thieves in a hospital! Don’t you ever have to safeguard yourselves!”

“Only the third and fourth floor windows,” I said absently.

He snorted.

“The third and fourth floor windows! That does me a lot of good!”
===Chinese===
他霍然抬头。

“那准是门开了。时长的事您有把握？您瞧，这一点非敲定不可：倘若时间不够这一切依次进行，那就表明，*除希金斯之外*，昨夜还有两个人惦记着进18号病房（Room 18）；其中一个操持了灭灯的勾当，另一个进了18号病房，闯出了我们所知道的这个结局。见鬼！”他蓦地打住。“但愿这座老医院进出的门道不至于多过一两条，省得我费这份神。你们医院往后可*千万*别进贼！可千万别叫你们操自保的心！”

“只有三楼和四楼的窗户。”我心不在焉地说。

他嗤了一声。

“三楼和四楼的窗户！这可真帮了我大忙了！”

===Original===
“On account of delirious patients,” I said rebukingly. “And as for there being two people trying to get the radium, I think there must be at least that many. I don’t believe that one person, alone and unaided, could make so much trouble.”

He grinned faintly at that, and then frowned.

“The chief of police wants to arrest the whole outfit at once. He is convinced that you are all in a conspiracy and that Gainsay is the leader. Of course, I don’t want to make such a wholesale cleaning. Especially since I﻿—I believe that I’m getting warm. But I don’t want any arrests yet. I don’t want to put anybody on guard.”

“Mr. O’Leary,” I said eagerly, emboldened by his half-confidence. “I have heard things of you, of course﻿—what wonderful success you have and all that. What methods do you use?”

He thrust his hands into his pockets, leaned back in the chair and sighed.
===Chinese===
“那是防着谵妄的病人。”我责备道。“至于说惦记那镭的有两个人，我看至少也得有这么多。我不信一个人孤身无援，能搅腾出这么多乱子。”

闻言他淡淡咧了咧嘴，随即又皱起眉头。

“警察局长想把这窝人一气全端了。他认定你们统统在合谋，头领就是盖恩赛。我自然不想来这么一场一锅端的大扫除。尤其是因为我——我自觉已经摸到热乎气儿了。可我还不想抓人。还不想叫任何人起了戒心。”

“奥利里先生。”我受他这半吐半露的心事壮了胆，热切地说。“您的种种事迹我自然有所耳闻——怎样的战功赫赫，等等等等。您用的是什么法子？”

他把双手插进口袋，往椅背上一靠，叹了口气。

===Original===
“Methods? I don’t have any methods. And as to success﻿—wait a few days.”

“You don’t have any methods?”

“The moment when I’m feeling most useless and most like a failure is not the moment to ask me to tell of my successes. Or my methods. I don’t have methods. I take what the Lord sends and am thankful. Sometimes it is a matter of luck. Mostly it is a matter of drudgery and hard work. Always it is a matter of thinking, thinking, thinking. Of eating, living, sleeping with problems for days and nights. Usually, just about the time you have decided that none of the pieces of the puzzle can possibly fit, all at once something happens and﻿—Click! Things clarify. There is a reason for everything. Nothing just happens. Nothing is an isolated fact. If you have a fact, you know that certain circumstances had to combine to bring it about. It is just logic, reason, the physical, material quality of cause and effect. There isn’t anything mysterious about it. It is just the﻿—the arithmetic of analysis. I don’t mean that I am infallible. I have to reconsider and revise and correct mistakes, just like anybody else. I’m human﻿—and young. But when you *know* that there is a solution, to the most puzzling problem, all there is to do is worry it out. I suppose the subconscious mind helps.”

“That is rather abstract,” I said slowly.
===Chinese===
“法子？我没有什么法子。至于战功——且等上几天再说不迟。”

“您没有什么法子？”

“我自觉最无用、最像个败军之将的时刻，可不是请我细数战功的时机。也不是数说法子的时机。我没有法子。上帝打发来什么，我便领受什么，并且心怀感激。有时靠的是运气。多半靠的是苦役和死功夫。始终靠的是琢磨，琢磨，再琢磨。是同难题一道吃、一道住、一道睡，一连几天几夜。通常，就在你断定这谜题的碎片一块也拼不拢的时候，冷不丁出了件事，于是——咔嗒！万事豁然。凡事皆有缘由。没有什么事凭空发生。没有什么事是孤立的事实。您手里既有一件事实，便知道必是若干情势凑在一起才生得出它。这不过是逻辑，是理性，是因果那种物理的、材质的品性。这里面没有任何神秘。这只是——分析的算术罢了。我并不是说自己一贯无误。我也得重新掂量、修订、纠正错处，跟旁人一样。我是人——而且还年轻。可当你*确知*那道最伤脑筋的难题必有答案时，剩下要做的只有一件事：把它死死啃下来。我想，潜意识也在暗中帮衬。”

“这也未免太玄虚。”我慢慢地说。

===Original===
“I suppose it sounds that way. Well﻿—here is one definite and concrete trick. As a rule, given enough rope a man can hang himself. Often I find that there will be one little circumstance that only the guilty man knows. Sooner or later he lets it out. Sometimes I have to trap the man I suspect into such an admission.”

I’m sure my eyes were popping out.

“Then that is why you made that extraordinary request of me at the first inquest!” I exclaimed. “I could not understand it. The thing you mentioned seemed so insignificant.”

It was remarkable that his eyes could be so clear and so unfathomable at the same time.

“I trust you are discreet,” he said evenly.

“Oh, I shan’t tell, if that is what you mean,” I promised hastily. “I am as interested in solving this mystery as you are. Indeed, I think I may say that I am far more deeply interested.”
===Chinese===
“听来想必如此。好吧——这儿倒有一条确凿而具体的窍门。照例，绳子放得够长，人自会把自个儿吊死。我常发现，总会有一处小小的情由，只有那有罪的人自己知情。或早或迟，他会露出来。有时，我还得设个套，教我疑心的那个人自己走进招认里去。”

想必我的眼珠子都要瞪出来了。

“那么，头一回验尸审讯的时候，您对我提的那桩离奇要求，为的正是这个了！”我喊出声来。“我当时实在不解。您提的那件事，看上去那么无足轻重。”

同样一双眼，竟能又清澈又深不可测，真是奇了。

“我信得过您口风紧。”他平平稳稳地说。

“哦，我不说出去——若您指的是这个。”我急忙应承。“解开这桩谜，我同您一样上心。说实在的，我想我可以讲：我上心得远比您更深。”

===Original===
“Well, keep your eyes and ears open,” he said, smiling and rising to open the door for me, and I found myself out in the main hall before I knew it.

It was only a few moments later that I saw him leave; I remember standing at the window beside the main entrance, and watching his long gray roadster swoop silently and swiftly around the curve of the main driveway and into the road. He was seated at the wheel, a slight gray figure, intent only on the muddy highway ahead of him. There was a suggestion of power, of invincibility, in the very repose and economy of motion with which he controlled the long-nosed roadster.

As I turned away I met Maida.

“Such a day!” she murmured with a sigh. “Have you been able to sleep?”

“I haven’t tried,” I said. “I knew it would be no use.”

“Miss Dotty is still upset,” went on Maida. “And the training nurses are following their own devices, and everybody is afraid of her own shadow. I wish this business was all settled and forgotten about.”
===Chinese===
“好，那就睁大眼睛、竖起耳朵。”他说着笑了，起身替我开门；不知不觉间，我人已经站到了大厅里。

不过几分钟之后，我便目送他离去；我记得自己站在正门旁的窗前，望着他那辆灰色长鼻跑车悄无声息、迅疾如掠地转过主车道的弯，汇上大路。他坐在方向盘后，一个瘦小的灰色身影，一心只盯着眼前泥泞的公路。那份操控长鼻跑车时的沉静与动作的省俭里，自透着一股力量，一股不可战胜的气概。

我转过身，迎面撞见梅达。

“这样的一天！”她叹口气，喃喃说。“你还睡得着么？”

“我没去睡。”我说。“我知道睡也无用。”

“多蒂小姐还惊魂未定。”梅达接着说。“学生护士们各行其是，人人都怕起自己的影子来了。这桩事要是全办结了、忘个干净才好。”

===Original===
“You don’t wish it any more than I do,” I agreed fervently. “But I do think that O’Leary is doing everything within his power.”

“I suppose so,” said Maida, without much conviction. She was looking pale and rather ill. “Wasn’t that Mr. O’Leary driving away a moment ago?”

“Yes.”

“I didn’t know that he was here at St. Ann’s. He hasn’t seen fit to question me yet”﻿—she smiled rather ruefully﻿—“as to poor Higgins. Except, of course, as he did at the inquest and that was so little. I felt he was reserving his inquiry, didn’t you? But I thought Mr. O’Leary had gone back to town long ago.”

“No. He just left.” I paused to yawn. “I’m going to try to get some sleep. Better do likewise.” But she shook her head, murmuring something about work, and I went to my room.

Luckily I managed to fall into an uneasy sleep. It was when I had awakened that I found I possessed but one remaining clean uniform and it was of a style that demanded the buttons I had sent to the laundry. Recalling the fact that Maida had an extra set, I went to her room to borrow. She was not there but I went boldly into the room.
===Chinese===
“你不会比我盼得更切。”我热切地附和。“不过我确实认为，奥利里正在竭尽他之力。”

“但愿如此。”梅达说，底气不很足。她面色苍白，颇有病容。“方才开车走的，不是奥利里先生么？”

“是。”

“我不知道他到了圣安医院。关于可怜的希金斯，他还不曾觉得有必要来盘问我”——她笑得颇为自嘲——“当然，验尸审讯上问过一回，就那么一星半点。我觉出他把问询攒在后头呢，你没有这个感觉么？可我还当奥利里先生早回城里去了。”

“没有。他刚走。”我顿了顿，打个哈欠。“我打算去睡一觉。你最好也一样。”她却摇头，咕哝了句活儿脱不开身之类的话，我便回自己房里去了。

所幸我总算迷迷糊糊睡着了，睡得并不安稳。醒来之后才发现，我手上仅剩一套干净制服，而那套的式样，偏偏非用上送去洗衣房的那几颗纽扣不可。想起梅达另有一套备用的，我便去她房里借。她不在，可我竟大着胆子进了房间。

===Original===
And I found the radium!

It was in the pincushion, a pretty trifle of mauve taffeta ruffles that I picked up idly to examine more closely. When I felt the shape under the taffeta, when my fingers outlined it, I could not have resisted tearing it apart. The cotton stuffing had been removed and the small box that held the radium was there instead.

I don’t know how long I stood as if frozen to the spot. I remember noting that the neat sewing had been torn out as if hastily, and that wide hurried stitches held the seam together. And I remember hearing the voices of several girls passing in the hall outside and thinking that Maida would be coming to her room.

O’Leary had said: “The person who has the radium is the one that killed Higgins.”

I could not face Maida with this thing in my hand.
===Chinese===
而我找到了镭！

就在那只针插里——一件淡紫色塔夫绸褶边的小巧玩意儿，我随手拿起来细看。指尖隔着塔夫绸摸到那个形状、勾出它的轮廓时，我便再也忍不住，把它扯开了。棉花填料已被掏出，装镭的小盒子正躺在那儿。

我在原地僵立了多久，自己也不知道。只记得我注意到，原先细密的缝线像是被匆匆拆去，一道潦草的宽针脚把缝口勉强缀在一起。也记得我听见外面走廊里几个姑娘的说话声经过，心想梅达就要回房来了。

奥利里说过：“手里有镭的人，就是杀了希金斯的人。”

攥着这东西在手，我没法面对梅达。

===Original===
And I could not leave the radium where it was.

In another moment I found myself back in my room, the radium, pincushion and all, locked away, the key securely hidden and my mind made up. Painful though it was I should have to tell O’Leary immediately of this thing. I do not hold friendship lightly and the shock of finding the stolen radium in Maida’s possession almost unnerved me.

I had forgotten about the buttons and it was something of an anticlimax to catch myself starting down to dinner in a black silk kimono. I had to go to the bottom of my trunk for an old uniform that I had cast aside as being too tight. It was still too tight and very uncomfortable, being made with a Bishop collar which is high and stiff and scratched the lobes of my ears.

There was no need to telephone to O’Leary, for as I neared the general office I caught a glimpse of his smooth brown head bent over some papers on the long table. I entered.

“I have found the radium,” I said quietly.
===Chinese===
可我也不能把镭留在原处。

转眼间我已回到自己房里：镭，连针插带一切，锁藏停当，钥匙藏得稳妥，主意也已拿定。尽管痛心，我也必须立刻把这件事告诉奥利里。友谊于我绝非轻飘飘的东西，而在梅达的什物里搜出被盗的镭，这一惊几乎叫我乱了方寸。

纽扣的事早忘到了脑后，待我发觉自己竟穿着一件黑色丝质和服式晨袍就要下楼吃晚饭，这收场未免有些泄气。我只得翻到箱底，找出一件早先嫌紧、弃置一旁的旧制服。它如今仍旧紧得厉害，穿着极不舒服——做的是毕晓普领（Bishop collar），领子又高又硬，磨得我耳垂生疼。

也犯不着给奥利里打电话了，因为走近总办公室时，我一眼瞥见他那个光滑的棕色脑袋正伏在长桌上的几份文件间。我走了进去。

“我找到镭了。”我平静地说。

===Original===
He looked up, jumped to his feet. I did not need to repeat my words.

“Where is it?”

“In my room. Shall I bring it to you?”

He hesitated, his eyes travelling around the office with its several doors and windows.

“This is too public. Someone would be sure to see it. Where did you find it?”

I swallowed.
===Chinese===
他抬起头，一跃而起。我的话无须说第二遍。

“在哪儿？”

“在我房里。要我给您拿来么？”

他迟疑了一下，目光在这间开着好几扇门窗的办公室里逡巡。

“这里太招眼。保不齐叫人瞧见。您是在哪儿找到的？”

我咽了口唾沫。

===Original===
“In﻿—Miss Day’s room.”

His gaze narrowed thoughtfully.

“You must tell me about it later. First I must have the radium.”

Our voices had dropped to whispers and my heart was pounding.

“Shall we put it in the safe?” I motioned toward the inner office which holds a great steel safe, in a prepared compartment of which the radium is usually kept.

“No.” O’Leary shook his head decisively. “No. I must put it in the hands of the chief of police at once. Look here, Miss Keate; in three minutes I shall walk slowly across the main hall with this bundle of newspapers under my arm. At the foot of the stairway I shall pass you just descending. It is rather dark there by the stairs. Hand me the box and keep right on going. Don’t stop. Later I shall see you and hear how you found it.”
===Chinese===
“在——戴小姐房里。”

他若有所思地眯起眼睛。

“经过您回头必须讲给我听。眼下我得先拿到镭。”

我们的嗓音都低成了耳语，我的心咚咚直撞。

“要不要把它放进保险柜？”我朝里间办公室那边示意。那里面立着一座大钢保险柜，柜里特备的一格，平日就存放镭。

“不。”奥利里断然摇头。“不。我必须立刻把它交到警察局长手里。听好，基特小姐；三分钟后，我会挟着这一卷报纸，慢悠悠地穿过大厅。在楼梯脚下，我会与您擦肩而过——您正拾级而下。楼梯那一带相当昏暗。把盒子递给我，脚步别停。千万别停。回头我再找您，听您讲是怎么找到的。”

===Original===
I followed his bidding. As I came slowly down the last flight of stairs he walked carelessly across the hall. There was no one about and I was sure that the transfer was effected without anyone’s knowledge.

With a casual nod I went on around the turn and followed the basement stairs down to the dining room. I ate what was set before me and kept my eyes from Maida.

It must have been about twenty minutes later that I ascended the stairs again and paused in the main hall. There was a light in the general office, excited voices, and Dr. Hajek and Dr. Balman were bending over something that lay on the long table.

I entered.

Lance O’Leary was stretched on the table, his face lead-gray, his eyes closed. Dr. Balman had out his stethoscope and was listening intently and Dr. Hajek was forcing aromatic ammonia through O’Leary’s pale lips.
===Chinese===
我依言而行。当我缓步走下最后一段楼梯时，他正漫不经心地穿过大厅。四下不见人影，我确信这场交接做得人不知鬼不觉。

我随意颔首，绕过拐角，顺着通往地下室的楼梯下到餐厅。端上什么我便吃什么，眼睛始终避着梅达。

大约二十分钟后，我重新上楼，在大厅里停住脚。总办公室里亮着灯，人声激动，海耶克医生和巴尔曼医生正俯身在长桌上躺着的什么东西上。

我走了进去。

兰斯·奥利里直挺挺躺在长桌上，脸是铅灰色，双眼紧闭。巴尔曼医生取出了听诊器，凝神听着；海耶克医生正把芳香氨醒剂往奥利里毫无血色的嘴唇里灌。

===Original===
There was a rapidly swelling lump back of O’Leary’s right ear and the small box that was so precious was not to be seen.

At a glance I understood.

“Is he﻿—alive, Dr. Balman?”

Dr. Balman nodded, detaching the stethoscope with long hands that shook.

“Dr. Hajek and I were starting down to dinner,” he explained. His voice sounded hoarse and his anxious eyes were fixed upon O’Leary. “We found him like this. All huddled on the floor there near the stairway.”
===Chinese===
奥利里右耳后面鼓起一个正迅速肿大的包，而那只无比贵重的小盒子，已不知去向。

只消一眼，我便明白了。

“他——还活着么，巴尔曼医生？”

巴尔曼医生点点头，用一双发抖的长手取下听诊器。

“海耶克医生和我正要下楼吃晚饭。”他解释道。嗓音听着沙哑，焦急的目光定在奥利里身上。“我们发现他的时候，他就是这副样子。整个人蜷在楼梯那边的地板上。”

## XIV A Matter of Evidence / 证据之事

===Original===
I must say that I was considerably relieved to see O’Leary’s eyelids flutter, the colour return to his face, and to note that his breath began to come more naturally. In a few moments he was sitting upright on the edge of the table, supported by Dr. Balman’s arm.

“What on earth happened to you?” inquired Dr. Balman, looking relieved also.

“I don’t know,” replied O’Leary rather dazedly. “All I remember is something coming down on my head. When did you find me?”

“About fifteen minutes ago. Dr. Hajek and I were just going downstairs. It was not very light in the hallway and you were in the shadow there by the stairs. It﻿—gave us a nasty shock. Do you know why you were attacked?”

===Chinese===
必须说，看见奥利里的眼皮颤动起来、脸上恢复了血色，又注意到他的呼吸渐渐来得自然，我着实松了一口气。不多几分钟，他已经在桌沿上坐直了身子，由巴尔曼医生的一条手臂扶着。

“您这到底是怎么了？”巴尔曼医生问道，神色也同样宽慰。

“我不知道，”奥利里答道，还带着几分恍惚，“我只记得有什么东西照着我脑袋砸了下来。你们是什么时候发现我的？”

“约莫一刻钟之前。海耶克医生和我正要下楼。那——可把我们吓了一跳，不轻。您知道自己为什么挨打吗？”

===Original===
O’Leary flicked a warning glance at me and shook his head.

“Haven’t the least idea,” he said flatly.

Dr. Hajek, who had been standing silently by, stirred at this.

“Then you were not on the point of making a﻿—er﻿—disclosure?” he asked with an air of disappointment. His ruddy face was as unmoved and stolid as ever, but it seemed to me that those dark, knowing eyes were restrained and secretive and did not meet O’Leary’s gaze squarely.

===Chinese===
奥利里朝我飞快地递了个警示的眼色，摇了摇头。

“半点头绪也没有。”他干巴巴地说。

一直默默站在一旁的海耶克医生，听到这话动了动。

“那么说，您并不是正要作出什么——呃——披露了？”他问道，带着一脸失望。他那张红润的脸，还同往常一样不动声色、呆板迟钝，可依我看，那双黑黢黢、透着世故的眼睛里满是克制和隐瞒，没有正面迎住奥利里的目光。

===Original===
“No such luck! By the way, were you men coming up from downstairs when you found me?”

“No,” replied Dr. Balman. “No. I had been OK-ing some orders in the inner office; Dr. Hajek came out of his room into the general office just as I, too, entered it and we walked together out into the hall and toward the basement stairs.”

“You saw nothing unusual?”

“Nothing. We were talking of advertising for a new janitor. It was﻿—” Dr. Balman’s kind, distressed eyes roved over O’Leary anxiously as if to be quite sure he was not hurt﻿—“it was, as I said, a shock. For a moment we feared the worst.” He drew out a handkerchief and wiped his pale lips nervously, his fingers lingering to pull at his thin beard. “Mr. O’Leary, I know that you are working hard and I don’t mean to criticize but really﻿—I﻿—” he hesitated as if put to it to find words. “You see for yourself to what terrible straits this thing has brought us. We don’t know what to expect next. Can nothing be done to stop it?”

===Chinese===
“没那样的好事！对了，你们两位发现我的时候，是从楼下上来么？”

“不是，”巴尔曼医生答道，“不是。我方才一直在里间办公室核签医嘱；海耶克医生从他的房间出来，走进总办公室，恰巧我也进了那里，我们便一道出来，穿过大厅，朝地下室的楼梯走去。”

“没看见什么异样？”

“什么也没有。我们正谈着登广告另招一名门房。那——”巴尔曼医生那双善良而痛楚的眼睛不安地在奥利里身上来回打量，仿佛要确认他没有受伤——“就像我方才说的，吓了一跳。有那么一刻，我们都担心出了最坏的事。”他掏出一方手帕，紧张地擦着发白的嘴唇，手指还流连着去捋他那撮稀疏的胡须。“奥利里先生，我知道您一直在尽心竭力，我也无意指摘，可是说真的——我——”他踌躇着，像是难得找出话来。“您亲眼看得见，这桩事情把我们逼到了何等可怕的绝境。我们不知道接下来还会出什么变故。难道就没有法子叫它停止吗？”

===Original===
It was just at this interesting point, of course, that Miss Dotty had to interrupt and summon me away, and it was something after midnight before I saw O’Leary again.

I was on duty at the time, Maida assisting me as usual, and our force augmented again, according to another whim of Miss Dotty’s, by two training nurses, both obviously unnerved at their contact with the south wing of such ill repute. Their blue-and-white striped skirts rattled nervously as they trotted here and there about the wing. While I did not feel unduly alarmed myself, still it seemed all too clear that the guilty one was still about, an unknown menace and hence more terrible, and I don’t mind admitting that my ears were alert to any alien sounds.

I was sitting at the chart desk when I heard O’Leary’s quick, light steps coming along the corridor from the general office. I turned to watch him approach, his gray suit and grave, keen face gradually emerging into the green circle of light that surrounded me.

===Chinese===
当然，偏偏就在这个要紧的节骨眼上，多蒂小姐又来打断，把我唤走了；待我再见到奥利里，已是午夜过后了。

当时我正在值班，梅达照旧从旁协助，而且遵照多蒂小姐又一时兴起的主意，我们的人手还添了两名学生护士——她俩一沾着这声名狼藉的南翼，显然就吓得心神不定，蓝白条纹的裙子随着她们在这一翼里小跑来去，紧张得窸窣作响。我自己虽说不觉得过分的惊惶，可有一点再明白不过：那有罪的一个仍在近旁逡巡，是无名的祸患，因而愈发可怖；我也不怕承认，我的两耳时刻警惕着任何生分的声响。

我正坐在病历台前，听得奥利里那轻快急促的脚步声顺着走廊从总办公室那边过来。我回过头，看他渐渐走近——他的灰色西服和那张端肃而敏锐的脸，缓缓浮现进围着我四周的那圈绿光里。

===Original===
“Have you found the radium?” I asked at once.

He shook his head.

“Nor who took it from you?”

“Nor who took it﻿—naturally.” He dropped into a chair beside me. “Now, Miss Keate, tell me exactly how you came upon it.”

===Chinese===
“镭找到了吗？”我劈头就问。

他摇了摇头。

“也没查出是谁从您手里拿走的？”

“也没查出谁拿走的——那是自然。”他一屁股坐进我身旁的椅子里，“好了，基特小姐，把您怎么撞见那镭的经过，原原本本讲给我听。”

===Original===
Feeling that it was no time to mince matters I complied, much as I disliked the implications the story involved. He listened thoughtfully, drawing a red pencil from his pocket and actually using it to scribble some notes in a small, shabby notebook he brought forth. He did not comment when I had finished, save to ask if Miss Day was on duty. And at the moment Maida herself entered the corridor from some sick room, O’Leary rose and intercepted her at once and the two disappeared into the drug room.

I was left to wait, always a difficult task for one of my temperament and particularly unpleasant that night. It seemed hours but was actually not more than twenty minutes by my watch, before they emerged. Maida’s chin was in the air, her cheeks quite scarlet, and her eyes flashing blue fire, but O’Leary was imperturbable. He stopped for a word with me and I suppose noted the anxiety which I was at no pains to hide. He smiled into my gaze a bit ruefully.

“She has a reason for everything,” he said quietly. “If I could only be sure that she is telling the truth!”

“She always tells the truth!” I cried indignantly.

===Chinese===
我觉得眼下不是遮遮掩掩的时候，便照实说了，尽管很不喜欢这段叙述牵出来的种种干系。他听得若有所思，从口袋里抽出一支红铅笔，竟还真用它在一个掏出来的小小破旧笔记本上涂写了几笔。我讲完，他不置一评，只问戴小姐是否当班。恰在那时，梅达本人从一间病房走进走廊，奥利里起身，立刻拦住了她，两个人一同消失在药品间里。

只留我一个人等着——对我这性子的人来说，等向来是件难事，那一晚尤其难熬。看表不过二十分钟，却像熬了几个钟头，两人才出来。梅达下巴扬得高高的，两颊烧得通红，眼里迸着蓝色的火苗，奥利里却不动声色。他停下来同我说了一句话，想必也看出了我毫不刻意掩饰的焦灼。他迎着我的目光，微微懊然地笑了一笑。

“她事事都有一个说法，”他轻声说，“要是我能确信她说的是实话就好了！”

“她说的向来都是实话！”我愤然叫道。

===Original===
“I hope so﻿—” he hesitated. “It is difficult to explain, but all through her story I had the strangest impression that she had﻿—rehearsed the whole thing.”

“What did she say of the radium?”

“Says she found it in a pot of lobelia that was in the hall outside Room 18. She noted that the flowers were withered and needed water, took it to the kitchen to water, noted that it had been disturbed and﻿—found the radium hidden below the plant! She took the radium to her own room until she could get in touch with me. She says she did not know that I was in the hospital after the inquest until she saw me leaving.”

“That is true,” I said quickly. “And I remember the pot of lobelia, too. Only﻿—” I wrinkled my forehead thoughtfully. “Why, the last I saw it, the thing was still in Room 18! Not in the corridor, at all!”

===Chinese===
“但愿如此——”他迟疑了一下，“这事很难讲清楚，可是听她从头讲到尾，我总有一种说不出的奇怪感觉：她是把整件事——预先排演过的。”

“镭的事她怎么说？”

“说是她在18号病房（Room 18）外走廊上的一盆半边莲里发现的。她看出花蔫了、该浇水了，便把它端到配膳室去浇，又发觉盆里被动过手脚——于是发现了藏在植株底下的镭！她把镭拿回自己房里，直到能同我接上头。她说，验尸审讯之后并不知道我还在医院里，直到看见我离开才晓得。”

“这是真的，”我赶紧说，“那盆半边莲我也记得。只是——”我思索着皱起额头，“怪了，我末一次看见它的时候，那东西明明还在18号病房里！根本就不在走廊上！”

===Original===
“When did you see it last?” he asked quickly.

“Last night﻿—about dusk.”

He looked at me soberly.

“Then the man in Room 18 last night﻿—if it was a man﻿—must have hidden the radium for fear he would be caught with the incriminating box. He must have thrust it hurriedly into the flower pot and left plant and all in the corridor in the hope of being able to get hold of the radium more easily than if it were left again in Room 18. That is, if we are to believe Miss Day’s statement. Positively that lobelia *was not in Room 18* when I examined the room almost immediately after Higgins’s death. I did not miss a square inch!”

===Chinese===
“您末一次见到它是什么时候？”他急急地问。

“昨天夜里——黄昏光景。”

他神色凝重地望着我。

“那么昨晚18号病房（Room 18）里的那个人——如果那真是个男人——必定是怕带着那只能定罪的盒子被人当场拿住，才把镭藏了起来。他想必是慌里慌张把它塞进了花盆，又连盆带花丢在走廊上，指望日后把它弄到手，总比再撂回18号病房里容易。当然，这得以我们相信戴小姐的陈述为前提。千真万确，希金斯死后我几乎是马上搜查了那个房间，那盆半边莲*当时绝不在18号病房里*！一寸见方的地方我都没有漏过！”

===Original===
I was still thinking of Maida.

“Did you ask her about the hypodermic syringe?”

He nodded.

“She says that she found her own needle had disappeared, naturally disliked calling anyone’s attention to the fact, in view of the existing circumstances, and simply substituted your tool for the lost one. She says she acted hastily and only from a dislike of being even remotely connected with the tragedies. My own opinion is that someone advised her to do so. Especially since there was a cut-and-dried air about everything she said.”

===Chinese===
我心里还惦着梅达。

“皮下注射器的事，您问她了吗？”

他点了点头。

“她说她发现自己那枚针头不见了，碍着眼下的情势，自然不愿叫人注意到这个事实，便干脆拿您的家伙顶了那枚丢失的。她说自己行事仓促，只因不愿同这些惨案沾上哪怕一丝一毫的干系。依我个人的看法，是有人劝她这么办的。尤其是，她说的每一件事，都带着一种照本宣科的现成腔调。”

===Original===
“How did the hypodermic syringe get out there in the shrubbery?”

“Miss Day insists that she knows nothing of that. And I’m more than half inclined to believe her, there.”

“The cuff link?” I persisted anxiously.

His clear eyes narrowed.

===Chinese===
“那皮下注射器又是怎么跑到外面灌木丛里去的？”

“戴小姐一口咬定，这事她毫不知情。而在这一点上，我倒有六七分愿意信她。”

“那枚青金石袖扣呢？”我焦灼地追问。

他清亮的眼睛眯缝了起来。

===Original===
“The cuff link is the reason that I doubt her whole story. She still declared that she lost the cuff link and that Dr. Letheny must have picked it up. If she would only tell me the truth about that!” He pulled a yellow slip of paper from his notebook. I recognized it immediately. It was the note Jim Gainsay had written and asked me to take to Maida.

“Read it,” said O’Leary.

Thinking it more discreet to say nothing of my own connection with the note, I did as he requested. It was headed: “Friday afternoon” and read thus:

> Must see you at once. Important. C. knows about last night. Say nothing and let me advise you. Will wait at the bridge. Very anxious since news of this afternoon. Be warned. Cannot urge too emphatically. Please meet me at bridge.

===Chinese===
“那枚袖扣，正是我怀疑她整套说辞的缘由。她仍旧一口咬定，袖扣是她丢的，必是莱瑟尼医生捡去了。哪怕她肯把这件事的真相告诉我呢！”他从笔记本里抽出一张黄色的纸片。我立刻就认了出来。那正是吉姆·盖恩赛写下、托我捎给梅达的那张便条。

“念念看。”奥利里说。

想到还是不提我自己同这张便条的干系为妙，我便依言念了。便条抬头是“星期五下午”，全文如下：

> 必须立刻见你。要紧事。C. 已知道昨晚的事。什么都别说，让我来给你拿主意。我在小桥那里等。自得了今天下午的消息，心急如焚。务必当心。再怎么切切叮嘱都不为过。请到小桥来会我。

===Original===
It was signed with a vigorously scrawled “J. G.”

I read the thing, read it again and raised my eyes to O’Leary’s.

“It was found in Miss Day’s room,” he explained. “In a pocket of a uniform, in fact; when I asked her to explain it she said at first that it was a message of a personal nature and that she would not explain it. I was forced to urge and she finally admitted exactly three things. First, that the note was written by Jim Gainsay. Second, that *C* referred to Corole Letheny. And third﻿—” He paused as if to give the coming words more emphasis.

===Chinese===
落款是一个笔力遒劲、潦潦草草的‘J. G.’。

我读了一遍，又读一遍，抬眼望向奥利里。

“这是在戴小姐房里找到的，”他解释道，“其实是藏在一件制服的口袋里；我要她说明，她起先说这是封私事性质的便条，不肯解释。我不得不加意催逼，她总算确切承认了三件事。第一，便条是吉姆·盖恩赛写的。第二，*C*指的是科罗尔·莱瑟尼。第三——”他顿了顿，仿佛要给下面的话再添几分分量。

===Original===
“And third﻿—simply this: That Jim Gainsay was strolling in the orchard about one o’clock on the night Dr. Letheny was killed. He passed the open window of the diet kitchen, saw her within, and stopped for a word or two through the window. Corole Letheny was also in the orchard, heard their conversation, and threatened to start a scandal, knowing that it would not sound well for a nurse to be seen visiting thus when she was on duty and at such an hour. For some reason Corole Letheny has developed a violent dislike for Jim Gainsay. According to Miss Day, then, he wrote to warn her against Corole.” O’Leary’s clear gray eyes searched my face. “Somehow the reasons Miss Day gave do not seem to warrant the extreme urgency expressed in this note. Do you think so, Miss Keate?”

“I hardly know,” I said thoughtfully. “Of course, it takes less than that to start a scandal, particularly if the starter is determined and malicious. And Corole is both. She is naturally rather﻿—feline, you know.”

He took the note from my hand.

“ ‘Since news of this afternoon’ can only refer to Dr. Letheny’s death. No, Miss Keate. What would a little breath of evil comment such as Miss Letheny could start have to do with Dr. Letheny’s death? No﻿—there is a deeper reason. I wish I could persuade Miss Day to be frank with me. Well, now to see if Gainsay tells the same story. Probably he will, but we will see.” He paused to regard me soberly. “Is there any room here in the wing where Gainsay and I could be undisturbed for a time? This is a case where the very leaves of the shrubbery seem to have ears and I don’t want Miss Corole to overhear us﻿—or anyone else.”

===Chinese===
“第三——就是这么回事：莱瑟尼医生遇害那晚一点来钟，吉姆·盖恩赛正在果园里溜达。他路过配膳室敞开的窗，望见她在里面，便停下隔着窗说了两句话。科罗尔·莱瑟尼当时也在果园里，听见了他们的交谈，便威胁要闹出一桩丑闻——她知道，一名护士在值班的时候、又是在那样的钟点被人撞见这样同男人搭话，传出去可不好听。不知出于什么缘故，科罗尔·莱瑟尼对吉姆·盖恩赛生出了一股强烈的憎恶。照戴小姐的说法，他写这封信，就是为了提醒她提防科罗尔。”奥利里那双清亮的灰眼睛审视着我的脸。“不知怎么，戴小姐给出的这些缘由，似乎撑不起这张便条里十万火急的口气。您觉得呢，基特小姐？”

“我很难说，”我若有所思地答道，“当然，要掀起一桩丑闻，比这更小的由头也尽够了，尤其是倘若发难的人既有决心、又存歹意。而科罗尔两样俱全。她天生就颇有一股——猫儿似的脾性，您是知道的。”

他从我手里接过便条。

“‘自得了今天下午的消息’，只能是暗指莱瑟尼医生之死。不，基特小姐，莱瑟尼小姐所能掀起的那一点点恶毒闲话，同莱瑟尼医生的死有什么相干？不——这里面另有一层更深的缘故。但愿我能劝得戴小姐对我开诚布公。好吧，这就去看看盖恩赛讲的是不是同一套。多半会是，可我们走着瞧。”他顿了顿，正色端详我。“这一翼里可有什么屋子，能让盖恩赛和我清静待上一阵、不受搅扰？眼下这案子，简直连灌木丛里的片片叶子都长着耳朵，我可不愿叫科罗尔小姐偷听了去——也不愿叫任何别人听了去。”

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I said slowly. “There is the drug room.”

The red light above Six gleamed. No other nurse was about, so I interrupted myself to answer and bring Sonny a fresh drink.

“I’ve been wishing you would come in to see me,” said Sonny cheerfully. “I’ve been alone ever since a man named Gainsay stopped to see me just at supper time. He wanted to know where everyone was and I told him it was just the time when you were all eating. Say, do you know him? I like him. He is a friend of Dr. Letheny’s. Say, why doesn’t Dr. Letheny come in to see me?”

“Sonny, did you say that Mr. Gainsay was here in St. Ann’s? Here in your room at dinner time?”

===Chinese===
“哦，有的，”我缓缓地说，“药品间就行。”

6号病房门上方的红色信号灯亮了。近旁没有别的护士，我便中断了手头的事去应铃，给桑尼端去一杯新倒的水。

“我正巴望着您进来看我呢，”桑尼欢欢喜喜地说，“打晚饭时一位姓盖恩赛的先生来看过我，我就一直一个人待着。他想知道大家都在哪儿，我告诉他，这正是你们统统吃饭的钟点。哎，您认识他吗？我喜欢他。他是莱瑟尼医生的朋友。哎，莱瑟尼医生怎么不进来看我呀？”

“桑尼，你是说盖恩赛先生到圣安医院来了？晚饭时就在你这间屋里？”

===Original===
“Why, sure, he was here! Just about six o’clock.”

“Where did he go when he left your room?”

“I think he went on up the corridor toward the general office. I can’t be sure, though, for I was working a new crossword puzzle and didn’t listen for his steps. Say, Miss Keate, want to see my new puzzle?”

I forestalled the thin hand groping on the bedside table.

“Another time, Sonny. You must go to sleep now.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，他来过！就六点钟模样。”

“他离开你屋子以后，上哪儿去了？”

“我想他是顺着走廊往总办公室那头去了。不过我说不准，那会儿我正玩一个新的填字游戏，没留神听他的脚步声。哎，基特小姐，想不想看看我的新填字游戏？”

我抢先按住了那只在床头几上摸索的瘦手。

“下回再看吧，桑尼。你这会儿该睡了。”

===Original===
O’Leary’s fingers sought the red pencil stub as I told him.

“So,” he pondered, “Jim Gainsay was here in St. Ann’s.”

“Where he had no business to be,” I interpolated grimly.

“And he was here at about the time I was knocked out and the radium stolen. This increases my interest in Mr. Gainsay.” He thrust his stub of a pencil into his pocket, ran a hand over his already smooth hair, and glanced at his watch. “I think we’ll have to disturb Jim Gainsay’s rest tonight﻿—if he is asleep. You are sure the drug room will not be in use, Miss Keate?”

“If we need anything I’ll get it myself,” I promised hastily. There was a sort of repressed smile on his face as he turned away, though I’m sure I don’t know why.

===Chinese===
我把这情况讲给奥利里时，他的手指又摸向了那截红铅笔头。

“这么说，”他沉吟道，“吉姆·盖恩赛到过圣安医院。”

“一个他本没有权利待的地方。”我冷冷地插了一句。

“而且他来的时辰，恰恰是我被打晕、镭被偷走的时候。这可就添了我对盖恩赛先生的兴味。”他把那截铅笔头插回口袋，一只手抹了抹本就服帖的头发，看了看表。“我看今晚我们只好搅扰吉姆·盖恩赛的清梦了——如果他睡着的话。您能担保药品间不会被占用么，基特小姐？”

“要拿什么东西，我自己去取。”我急忙应承。他转身走开时，脸上有一种隐忍的笑意，虽说是什么缘故，我确实说不上来。

===Original===
Jim Gainsay must not have been asleep, for within five minutes the two men were coming along the corridor from the main entrance. One of the student nurses saw me lead them into the drug room and her eyes would likely have popped out had I not spoken sharply to her. On the theory that every cloud has a silver lining I considered it fortunate that Eleven chose that very time for a rather cataclysmic upheaval which kept Maida thoroughly engrossed for an hour or so, and I don’t think she ever knew of the interview that took place there in our wing.

She came very near it once, when she hurried for some soothing drops, but I forestalled her by offering to get the medicine myself. If she thought my hasty offer curious, she said nothing and went back to her patient.

Opening the drug-room door I walked into an electric atmosphere. Jim Gainsay, lounging tall and bronzed against the window sill, was clearly furious; his eyes were narrow and wary, his lean jaw was set, his lips tight and guarded.

I caught the words﻿ ﻿… “entirely a personal matter” in no very pleasant voice from Jim Gainsay.

“I must insist upon an answer, however,” said O’Leary. His voice had the keenness of a slender, shining steel blade.

Then both men became aware of my presence, and though I was rather deliberate in measuring the drops, they said nothing further until I left, when the murmur of their voices began again.

===Chinese===
吉姆·盖恩赛想必没有睡着，因为不出五分钟，那两个人已经顺着走廊从正门那边过来了。一个学生护士瞧见我把他们领进药品间，若不是我厉声说了她一句，她的眼珠子只怕都要瞪出来。本着乌云总镶着银边的道理，我倒觉得万幸：十一号偏偏挑了那个时辰，来了一场翻天覆地的大发作，足足让梅达全副精神忙了一个来钟头——我想她始终不曾知晓，我们这一翼里进行过的那场问话。

她有一回险些撞上——她急匆匆来取安神滴剂——却叫我抢了先，主动提出由我亲自去取药。她若觉得我这仓促的自告奋勇有些古怪，也没说什么，便回她的病人那儿去了。

推开药品间的门，我一头撞进了一触即发的空气里。吉姆·盖恩赛那高大、晒成古铜色的身影斜倚在窗台上，分明正怒火中烧；他双眼细眯、满含戒备，瘦削的下颌绷得死紧，嘴唇抿得严严实实。

我捕捉到几个字眼……“纯属私事”，出自吉姆·盖恩赛之口，口气很是不善。

“不过，我必须坚持讨一个回答。”奥利里说。他的声音带着一柄细窄雪亮的钢刃才有的锋利。

随后两人都察觉了我在跟前。我量滴剂虽量得从容不迫，他们也再没接着往下说，直到我出去，那低低的语声才重新响起来。

===Original===
The interview prolonged itself and it was a good half hour before I had a chance﻿—that is, needed to go into the drug room again, and it was only for an ice bag.

“And yet you remain a welcome guest in Corole Letheny’s house?” said O’Leary.

“Not so darned welcome,” replied Jim Gainsay, and I caught the flicker of a smile on O’Leary’s face as I closed the door.

In a few moments, however, O’Leary opened the door, peered down the corridor, saw me and beckoned.

His eyes were shining with that peculiarly lucent look as he motioned for me to precede him into the drug room.

“I want you to hear this, Miss Keate,” said O’Leary, his voice very quiet but with a tense, alert overtone that caught my ears. “Now, Gainsay, will you repeat that about Higgins?”

===Chinese===
那场问话拖延下去，足足过了半个来钟头，我才得到机会——不如说有了必要——再进一趟药品间，还只是为了取一只冰袋。

“可您至今仍是科罗尔·莱瑟尼家里受欢迎的客人？”奥利里说。

“受欢迎个鬼。”吉姆·盖恩赛答道。关门时，我瞥见奥利里脸上掠过一丝笑影。

然而过了不多时，奥利里开了门，朝走廊两头探望，看见我，便招了招手。

他眼里闪着那种特有的清亮光泽，示意我在他前头先走进药品间去。

“我要您听听这一段，基特小姐，”奥利里说，嗓音极轻，却带着一种绷紧而警觉的泛音，钻进我的耳朵。“好了，盖恩赛，把关于希金斯的那段，再讲一遍，可肯？”

===Original===
Jim Gainsay glanced at me rather sheepishly.

“I was telling O’Leary how it happened that I overheard most of your talk with Higgins the afternoon before his death. It struck me as foolish to let such a mine of information get away, and later in the evening I got hold of Higgins and wormed some more of the story out of him. For the most part he just repeated what he had already told you, Miss Keate. But he did tell me the scrap of conversation that he promised to tell you﻿—remember?”

I nodded.

“It seems that he heard it when he stopped there near the south entrance on his way to see Dr. Letheny. I suppose it was this conversation as much as anything that made him suspicious of what was going on in Room 18. It seems that he knew at once who was on the other side of the bush; it was Corole and Dr. Hajek. Corole said﻿—according to Higgins﻿—‘To follow would be easy, now,’ and Hajek said ‘Wait till he comes out.’ Then Corole said something about it not being difficult and Hajek said ‘Leave it to me.’ Then Higgins thought he must have made some noise among the leaves, for Corole whispered ‘Hush’ and he heard them slipping away. Higgins followed but soon lost them in the dark and himself returned to the interesting vicinity of Room 18.” He paused.

===Chinese===
吉姆·盖恩赛颇为忸怩地瞟了我一眼。

“我正跟奥利里讲，希金斯死前那个下午，您同他谈的话，我怎么碰巧听去了大半。我心里想，让这样一座消息的富矿白白放跑，未免太傻，于是当晚晚些时候，我寻到希金斯，又从他嘴里多套出了一些。大体上，他不过是把已经告诉过您的话又重复了一遍，基特小姐。可他果真把答应要讲给您听的那段只言片语，讲给我听了——记得么？”

我点了点头。

“据说，那是他在去见莱瑟尼医生的路上，在南门近旁停下歇脚时听来的。我想，正是这段对话，比什么都更叫他起了疑心，觉出18号病房（Room 18）里正在闹什么名堂。据说他当下就听出了那丛接骨木那一侧是谁：是科罗尔和海耶克医生。据希金斯说，科罗尔道：‘现在，跟上去是容易的。’海耶克道：‘等他出来。’随后科罗尔又说了句这事并不难，海耶克道：‘交给我。’接着希金斯想，自己准是弄响了树叶，因为科罗尔低低‘嘘’了一声，他便听见他们悄悄溜走了。希金斯跟了上去，可不多时就在黑暗里丢了人，自己也回到了18号病房那耐人寻味的左近。”他停了下来。

===Original===
“Go on,” said O’Leary grimly.

“Higgins told you how he came back to the porch and stumbled over a coat. I got him to tell me something about the coat. He said it must have been ‘one of them slickers,’ for it felt cold and oily. And at the same time he told me a peculiar thing.” Again he paused as if what he was about to say was distasteful to him. I glanced at O’Leary; his eyes still wore that strangely luminous expression. Even the glass doors of the cupboards all around us and the shining white tiles seemed to wait expectantly.

“Go on,” said O’Leary sharply, his words breaking into the crystal silence.

Jim Gainsay cleared his throat, felt in his pocket for a cigarette, remembered that he was in a hospital and replaced it.

“He said the coat smelled of﻿—ether!”

===Chinese===
“接着讲。”奥利里冷冷地说。

“希金斯跟你们讲过，他怎样回到门廊上，又让一件外套绊了一下。我让他说了些那件外套的情形。他说那准是‘那种油布雨衣’里的一件，因为摸上去又冷又油腻。而与此同时，他还告诉了我一件蹊跷事。”他又顿了顿，仿佛要说出口的东西叫他生厌似的。我瞟了奥利里一眼；他的眼睛仍带着那种奇异的发亮的神情。连我们四周一只只药柜的玻璃门和雪亮的白瓷砖，都仿佛在屏息等候。

“说下去。”奥利里厉声道，他的话音劈进了水晶般凝澈的寂静里。

吉姆·盖恩赛清了清嗓子，往口袋里摸烟，想起这是在医院，又把它放了回去。

“他说，那件外套上有一股——乙醚味儿！”

===Original===
There was a moment of silence. Then I turned to O’Leary.

“Ether! It was the same slicker! The one that I wore Friday afternoon!”

O’Leary nodded thoughtfully.

“It might be. At any rate we know that no slicker was found on the porch or about the grounds, so it is likely that the murderer of Dr. Letheny carried it away with him. We had a strict guard on St. Ann’s the day following the murders. It is barely possible that we can yet trace the coat that you wore, Miss Keate.”

===Chinese===
片刻的静默。随后我转向奥利里。

“乙醚！是同一件油布雨衣！正是我星期五下午穿过的那件！”

奥利里若有所思地点了点头。

“很可能是。无论如何，我们知道门廊上和宅子四周都没有搜到油布雨衣，所以多半是杀害莱瑟尼医生的凶手把它随身带走了。命案发生的第二天，圣安医院有我们严密的把守。要把您穿过的那件雨衣追出个下落，也并非全无可能，基特小姐。”

===Original===
“Poor Higgins,” said Jim Gainsay gravely. “I had a hard time getting him to tell me that much. He refused to the last to tell me whose face he saw there in Room 18.”

“But he failed to raise an alarm even though he had reason to think that the radium was being removed,” murmured O’Leary. “Well, that’s all now, thank you, Gainsay.”

Jim Gainsay paused for a moment outside the door and I saw him look carefully up and down the dim corridor. No white uniform was in sight, however. Thinking to facilitate his departure I took the key to the south door from its hiding place and let him out that way. When I returned O’Leary was standing under the green light, studying his small notebook. He slipped it into his pocket as I approached.

“Nothing, Miss Keate, but what you heard,” he said rather wearily. “His explanation of the note to Miss Day and his activities during the night of the murders are identical with what Miss Day tells us. He sticks to the story of his telegram to his business associates that he told at the inquest. He says he took Dr. Letheny’s car and left the grounds of St. Ann’s very soon after you met him in the dark. And that he was at a ‘corner about half a mile from St. Ann’s’ when the storm broke﻿—which does not coincide with what we know.
