That is, if the lights you saw were the lights of the car he was driving, and it seems reasonable to believe that they were.”

===Chinese===
“可怜的希金斯，”吉姆·盖恩赛正色道，“我费了好大的劲，才让他说出这么多。至于他在18号病房（Room 18）里看见的是谁的脸，他到末了也不肯告诉我。”

“可他分明有理由认定镭正在被人搬走，却没能喊响警报。”奥利里低声道，“好了，现在就这些，谢谢你，盖恩赛。”

吉姆·盖恩赛在门外略停了停，我看见他仔细地朝昏暗走廊的两头张望。好在望不见一件白制服。为了让他走得顺当，我从藏钥匙的地方取出南门的钥匙，打那扇门送他出去。我回来时，奥利里正站在那圈绿光底下，研读他的小本子。我一走近，他便把它塞进了口袋。

“没有了，基特小姐，您听到的就是全部。”他说，语气颇为疲惫，“他对给戴小姐那张便条的解释、对命案当夜的所作所为，同戴小姐对我们讲的一字不差。验尸审讯上讲过的、拍给他生意伙伴的电报那套说法，他咬定不放。他说他开走了莱瑟尼医生的车，在黑暗里同您相遇后不久，便离开了圣安医院的地面。还说暴风雨袭来时，他正在‘离圣安医院约半英里的一处街角’——这同我们已经掌握的对不上。也就是说，倘若您望见的那几盏灯火，正是他开的那辆车发出的，而这么想，似乎也在情理之中。”

===Original===
“How about his presence here in St. Ann’s tonight at the time the radium was taken from you?”

“That got a rise out of him,” said O’Leary with an unexpected flash of whimsical satisfaction. “He was angry in a second. Every time Miss Day’s name came up he turned savage. If the radium had not disappeared I should be inclined to think that he came to St. Ann’s in the hope of seeing Miss Day, but with the radium gone again﻿—” he stopped abruptly, his face becoming grave again and dubious.

The green light cast crawling shadows; the black window pane stared impenetrably at us; far down the corridor a light went on with a subdued click, a glass clinked thinly against something metal, and I heard the soft pad-pad of a nurse’s rubber heels.

Presently O’Leary stirred. His eyes, still shining with that very lucent look, met mine intently.

“Corole Letheny is next. Do you want to go to see her with me? Very well, then, suppose we say at eight in the morning. You might just happen into the Letheny cottage and I’ll come. I may be a little delayed.”

===Chinese===
“那么今晚镭从您手里被拿走的时候，他人在圣安医院，这又怎么说？”

“这一下可捅着了他的火气，”奥利里说，闪过一丝出人意料的促狭得意，“他转眼间就动了怒。每逢戴小姐的名字冒出来，他便变得凶狠。倘若镭没有再度失踪，我倒倾向于认为，他来圣安医院是盼着见戴小姐一面，可是镭又没了——”他蓦地打住，脸色重新凝重起来，而且狐疑不定。

绿光投下蠕蠕爬动的暗影；黑黢黢的窗玻璃深不可测地瞪视着我们；走廊深处，一盏灯咔哒一声轻响亮起，一只玻璃杯当的一声碰上什么金属物件，我又听见护士胶底鞋跟柔软的吧嗒、吧嗒声。

不多时，奥利里动了动。他那双仍闪着清亮光芒的眼睛，专注地迎住了我的目光。

“下一个就是科罗尔·莱瑟尼了。您愿不愿意同我一起去见她？很好，那就说定明早八点。您只管碰巧走进莱瑟尼医生的小屋，我随后便到。我兴许要稍耽搁一会儿。”

===Original===
The remaining hours of second watch dragged a little but passed quietly, and promptly at eight o’clock I wrapped myself in my blue, scarlet-lined cape, adjusted the wrinkled folds of the detestable Bishop collar, and let myself out the south door. The path was still wet, the trees and shrubbery were veiled in heavy mist, and the whole world very sombre and desolate.

At the bridge I came upon Jim Gainsay. He was sitting disconsolately on a log a little aside from the path, staring at a toad hopping across his feet and apparently lost in his own morose thoughts. I don’t think he had slept for he looked haggard and cold and must have been smoking steadily for hours, for there was a little white circle of cigarette ends around him.

“Young man,” I said with some acerbity, “don’t you know that cigarettes are coffin nails and you are making yourself subject to indigestion, nervous disorders, tuberculosis and asthma?”

He rose grudgingly and surveyed me without enthusiasm.

===Chinese===
第二班余下的几个钟头略觉难熬，总算平静无事；八点整，我裹上那件红衬里的蓝红两色护士斗篷，抚平那令人厌憎的毕晓普领上皱起的褶子，从南门独自出去。小径还湿着，树木和灌木笼在浓雾里，整个世界一派阴沉荒凉。

在小桥那里，我撞见了吉姆·盖恩赛。他蔫头耷脑地坐在离小径不远的一截木头上，盯着一只从脚背上跳过去的蟾蜍，显然正陷在自己的阴郁心思里。我想他准是一夜没睡：形容憔悴，带着寒色，想必还一支接一支抽了好几个钟头的烟——他四周已经围出一小圈白色的烟头。

“年轻人，”我话里带了几分尖酸，“你难道不知道香烟就是棺材钉，再抽下去，消化不良、神经失调、肺结核和气喘，都要找上你么？”

他老大不情愿地站起身，兴味索然地打量我。

===Original===
“To say nothing of measles and hay fever,” he said dourly. “Say, Miss Keate, is there any chance of seeing﻿—her?”

I did not ask whom.

“I don’t know. Have you not seen her lately?”

He glanced at me suspiciously, then motioned to a seat on the log and I found myself seated none too comfortably, with the moisture of a tree dripping down and completing the demoralization of my collar, and beside me a man whom I suspected of theft and﻿—theft, to say the least.

===Chinese===
“还有麻疹和花粉热，那更不在话下。”他闷闷地说，“哎，基特小姐，还有没有机会见一见——她？”

我没有问这个“她”是谁。

“我不知道。你近来没见过她么？”

他狐疑地瞥了我一眼，随即指了指木头上的一处座位；我便老大不舒服地坐了下去——树上滴下的水珠，把我领子上残存的那点体面也作践尽了，身旁坐着的，又是一个我疑心犯了窃案——窃案，往轻里说——的男人。

===Original===
“That was why I was in the hospital last night about dinner time,” he said companionably. “I haven’t seen her for days and days. She is too damn devoted to duty.”

“Miss Day is a nice girl,” I said uncertainly.

“Nice!” He glared at me. “Nice! Is that all you can say for her? Lord! She can have me! The minute I saw her, I thought, ‘There she is! There’s my girl!’ Say!” he drew a long breath﻿—“and after I talked to her alone, there at that kitchen window last Thursday night, I knew Jim Gainsay’s time had come! I drove straight into town and wired the company that I should be delayed. And here I stay until she goes with me.” He paused and added ruefully: “It may take quite a while to convince her that she wants to go.”

===Chinese===
“昨晚晚饭时分我泡在医院里，就是为了这个，”他一副亲亲热热的口气，“我已经好多好多天没见着她了。她把职分看得比天大，真见鬼。”

“戴小姐是个好姑娘。”我拿不准地说。

“好！”他瞪着我。“好！您对她就讲得出这么一个字？老天！她只管把我整个人拿去！见她头一眼我就想：‘就是她了！这就是我的姑娘！’哎！”他深深吸了口气——“上星期四夜里，我在配膳室那扇窗前同她单独说过话之后，我就知道吉姆·盖恩赛的时辰到了！我径直开车进城，拍电报给公司，说我要耽搁些日子。我不走，直到她肯跟我走。”他顿了顿，又懊然添了一句：“要说服她相信她自己想走，怕还得好一阵子。”

===Original===
“So that was what your message meant?”

He looked at me quickly. No matter what O’Leary said later I do not believe that Gainsay’s explanation was anything but spontaneous.

“Why didn’t you explain before?” I asked tartly, thinking of the trouble he had caused us.

“Explain!” cried Jim Gainsay in high derision. “The woman says ‘Explain’! Explain that I’ve gone straight off my head about a girl! Please, kind gentlemen, excuse me for hanging around just when murders are being perpetrated and no strangers wanted on the premises. But really, you know, I’ve just fallen in love. Explain! Hell!”

===Chinese===
“原来你那张便条是这个意思？”

他敏捷地看了我一眼。不论奥利里后来怎么说，我始终不信盖恩赛这番解释不是脱口而出、发自本心的。

“那你先前为什么不解释清楚？”我尖刻地问，想起他给我们惹下的麻烦。

“解释！”吉姆·盖恩赛高声嘲弄道，“这位太太居然说‘解释’！解释我为了一个姑娘神魂颠倒、昏了头！劳驾各位好心的先生多多包涵：偏偏在凶案一起接一起、最忌生人露面的当口，我还在这一带流连不去。可是说真的，您知道，我不过是坠入了情网。解释！活见鬼！”

===Original===
In some dignity I rose; even justifiable ill-nature can go too far.

“Good morning, Mr. Gainsay,” I said coldly. But as I turned the bend in the path, I looked backward. “I’ll ask her to take a walk this afternoon,” I promised, being a fool of an old maid. He brightened up at that and the last I saw of him he was casting pebbles at the frog with the liveliest interest on the part of each.

The porch of the Letheny cottage was still unswept and desolate, and though I rang and rang apparently no one heard me. Finally I opened the heavy door and walked into the hall. No one seemed to be about. The door to the study was closed, and thinking to find Corole there if anywhere I approached it. But with my hand actually on the brass knob, I paused, for the door itself swung gently a few inches toward me, I heard a low murmur of voices and realized that someone was on the other side of the door and in the very act of emerging from it.

===Chinese===
我端着几分体面站起身来；即便是情有可原的坏脾气，也有个分寸。

“早安，盖恩赛先生。”我冷冷地说。可走到小径拐弯的地方，我又回头望了一眼。“今天下午，我请她出来散散步。”我许下这个诺言，十足是个痴心的老处女。他闻言顿时开朗起来；我最后望见他时，他正朝那只青蛙丢石子——一人一蛙，都兴头十足。

莱瑟尼医生小屋的门廊仍旧无人打扫、一派荒凉；我按了又按门铃，显然没有人听见。末了我推开那扇厚重的门，走进门厅。四下里似乎没有人。书房的门关着，我想科罗尔若在何处，必是在那里，便朝它走去。可是我的手才真碰到黄铜门把，便停住了——那扇门本身朝我这边轻轻荡开了几寸，我听见低低的语声，明白有人正在门的那一侧，恰恰正要从里面出来。

===Original===
“You are sure it is safe there?” said someone clearly.

It was Dr. Hajek’s voice.

“Quite,” said Corole, whose accents were unmistakable.

“Then today is as good as any.”

“I﻿—suppose so.” Corole seemed reluctant.

“Are you backing down?” I had not believed that Fred Hajek’s voice could be so ugly.

===Chinese===
“你担保放在那儿安全？”有人清清楚楚地说。

那是海耶克医生的声音。

“相当安全。”科罗尔说，她的口音绝不会叫人认错。

“那就今天，同哪天都一样。”

“我——想是吧。”科罗尔似乎很不情愿。

“你想打退堂鼓？”我从前竟不信，弗雷德·海耶克的嗓音能这样难听。

===Original===
“No,” said Corole. “No.”

“Then why not today?” The door closed sharply on the last syllable as if propelled by a vigorous motion on the part of the speaker.

In some perplexity I waited. I could still hear the sounds of voices, but the words were unintelligible. All at once, however, the man’s voice rose as if in anger, and without pausing to consider my action I simply grasped that brass knob and flung the door open.

I interrupted a strange tableau.

Corole was leaning backward against the table, her lips drawn back in a snarl and her eyes gleaming green fire. Dr. Hajek was no less moved; his face was dark red, his fists clinched, his dark eyes glittering unpleasantly between slitted lids. He was speaking when I opened the door and I caught his last words. They were thick with fury.

===Chinese===
“不，”科罗尔说，“不退。”

“那为什么不定在今天？”最后一个音节上，门砰然关拢，仿佛被说话人一个干脆利落的动作推了上去。

我满腹狐疑地等在外面。语声仍隐约可闻，字句却听不真切。可是蓦地，那男人的嗓音高了起来，像是动了怒；我来不及掂量自己的举动，一把攥住那黄铜门把，猛地推开了门。

我打断了一幅奇异的景象。

科罗尔向后仰靠在桌上，嘴唇向后咧开，一副龇牙欲吼的模样，眼里迸着绿火。海耶克医生也激动得不遑多让：他满脸紫红，双拳紧攥，黑亮的眼睛在眯起的眼缝里闪着不祥的光。我推开门时他正说着话，我赶上了他末尾的几句，字字浸透了狂怒。

===Original===
“… and now you refuse. After all I have done﻿—*for you!*”

“Oh, I don’t refuse,” cried Corole.

Then they both saw me.

Dr. Hajek’s dark face flushed a still deeper, painful red. By an effort, apparently of will, he relaxed his hands, reached for a cap that lay on the table, muttered something under his breath, and wheeled toward the door. Corole recovered her self-possession more easily; she raised her eyebrows and shrugged as if in amusement. She wore an amazing Chinese coat, stiffly embroidered in gold and green, dancing pumps with rhinestone heels and shabby toes, and *no stockings*!

“Good morning,” she said with shameless calm.

===Chinese===
“……如今你倒拒绝了。我做下的一切——*全是为了你！*”

“哦，我没有拒绝，”科罗尔叫道。

随后他们两个都看见了我。

海耶克医生黧黑的脸涨得更深，成了痛楚的紫红。他显然全凭一股意志，才松开了双手，伸手去拿桌上的一顶帽子，嘴里低低咕哝了句什么，霍地转向门口。科罗尔定神定得容易得多；她扬起眉毛，耸了耸肩，仿佛只觉有趣。她身上是一件惊世骇俗的中式外套，用金绿两色绣得硬邦邦的；脚上是镶水钻鞋跟、鞋头却已寒酸的舞鞋，而且*没有穿长袜*！

“早安。”她说，恬不知耻地平静。

===Original===
I think O’Leary must have met Dr. Hajek in the hall, for I heard his voice before he entered the study. At the door he stopped.

“You, too,” said Corole, losing her amused smile.

“May I come in, Miss Letheny?” O’Leary asked. He looked as fresh and well groomed as if he had had a long night’s sleep. “I rang the bell but no one answered.”

Corole pulled her bizarre coat tighter about her.

“Huldah decided she no longer liked it here,” she said. “She left last night﻿—rather abruptly. Yes, do come in, Mr. O’Leary.”

===Chinese===
我想奥利里准是在门厅里撞见了海耶克医生，因为他人还未进书房，我先听见了他的声音。他在门口站住了。

“你也来了。”科罗尔说，脸上那副觉得有趣的笑意敛去了。

“我可以进来么，莱瑟尼小姐？”奥利里问。他看上去精神焕发、衣冠齐整，仿佛酣睡了一整夜。“我按过门铃，没有人应。”

科罗尔把那件古怪的衣裳往身上拢紧了些。

“赫尔达觉出她不再喜欢这地方了，”她说，“她昨晚走了——走得相当突然。请进吧，奥利里先生。”

===Original===
There followed an hour I shall not soon forget. I had never seen Lance O’Leary so mercilessly intent. I was both fascinated and awed to note the way he cut through Corole’s pretences and poses, her feline evasions and her suave smiles, and by sheer strength of will forced her to give to his inquiries answers that were direct if they were not entirely truthful.

He began with the revolver, but she repeated the denial of all knowledge regarding its presence in Room 18 she had given at the inquest. Also, to further questions as to her visit to Room 18 on the night it had sheltered that irascible patient, Mr. Gastin, she repeated the lame explanation she had given at the time. She admitted coolly enough that she understood the use of a hypodermic outfit, and as coolly, though with an evil glance at me, that she had made a trip through the orchard immediately after hearing of Dr. Letheny’s death; she had wished to see Dr. Hajek, she said brazenly, to discuss with him the news of the tragedy.

It was then that O’Leary held before her eyes the small gold sequin.

===Chinese===
接下来那一个钟头，我不会轻易忘记。我从未见过兰斯·奥利里这样毫不容情地专注。我看得又是着迷，又是心慑：他一路剖开科罗尔的矫饰与做态、她猫儿似的躲闪和她那圆滑的微笑，全凭意志之力，逼得她对他的讯问给出虽不全然真实、却直截了当的回答。

他从那把左轮手枪问起，可验尸审讯时她怎么说的，如今照旧怎么说：对它怎会出现在18号病房（Room 18），一概不知。至于18号病房收住那位脾气暴烈的病人加斯廷先生的那晚、她的那次造访，任凭追问下去，她也把当时那套站不住脚的解释照样重复了一遍。她相当冷静地承认，皮下注射的一整套器械她会摆弄；也同样冷静地——只是恶狠狠地瞥了我一眼——承认一听到莱瑟尼医生的死讯，她便穿过果园走了一遭；她恬不知耻地说，是想见海耶克医生，同他谈谈这一惨案的消息。

就在这时，奥利里把那一小枚金色亮片举到了她眼前。

===Original===
“Enough of this, Miss Letheny,” he said coldly. “It would be better for you to give me your fullest confidence. Why were you at the window of Room 18 last Thursday night? This ornament was found on the window sill. How did it get there?”

Corole stared blankly from the gold sequin to O’Leary, but back of those queer topaz eyes I felt that she was thinking desperately.

“Well,” she said finally, “I *was* near Room 18. In fact, I went as far as the window sill. You see, I was walking in the orchard. I was near the porch of the south wing when I heard something﻿—a sort of noise, there at the window of the corner room.” She stopped and ran a quick, catlike tongue over her lips. “Room 18, that is. I was rather curious so I crept up nearer the window. A man was opening the screen and crawling into Room 18. He left the screen up and I slipped quietly up to the window. I am rather tall, you know, and as I leaned for a moment on the sill I suppose the sequin got detached from my gown.”

===Chinese===
“够了，莱瑟尼小姐，”他冷冷地说，“您还是对我推心置腹为好。上星期四夜里，您在18号病房（Room 18）的窗前做什么？这片饰物是在窗台上发现的。它是怎么到那里的？”

科罗尔的目光茫然地从那枚金色亮片移到奥利里脸上，可我感觉得到，那双古怪的托帕石眼睛背后，她正在拼命盘算。

“好吧，”她终于开口，“我*当时确实*在18号病房附近。事实上，我一直走到了窗台跟前。您瞧，我本来在果园里散步。我走到南翼门廊近旁时，听见了什么——角上那间屋子的窗口那儿，有一种响动。”她停住，猫似的舌尖飞快地舔过一圈嘴唇。“也就是说，18号病房。我有些好奇，便蹑手蹑脚挨近那扇窗。一个男人正推开纱窗，往18号病房里爬。他没把纱窗放下，我便悄悄溜到了窗前。您知道，我个子颇高，我在窗台上倚了那么一小会儿，想必亮片就是那时从我裙子上脱落的。”

===Original===
“It was very dark that night. Did you see all this?”

She moistened her lips again; they were taking on a bluish tinge.

“I﻿—I see in the dark better than most people.” (Which I, for one, did not doubt.) “And anyway I could hear, you know.”

“What could you hear? Why should you think that the noise you heard was made by a man crawling in the window of Room 18? That is just a little farfetched, Miss Letheny.”

“It is true, anyhow,” she said sulkily. “I heard the screen catch as he pulled it up and the sort of﻿—scrambling sound he made, and I could see the patch of light that was his shirt front.”

===Chinese===
“那天夜里很黑。这些您都看得见？”

她又润了润嘴唇；唇上正泛出一层青色。

“我——我在黑暗里，看得比多数人清楚。”（这一点，至少我个人毫不怀疑。）“况且不管怎样，我还听得见，您知道的。”

“您能听见什么？您凭什么认为，听见的响动是一个男人爬进18号病房（Room 18）窗户弄出来的？这就未免有点牵强了，莱瑟尼小姐。”

“反正这是真的，”她怏怏地说，“他拉起纱窗时卡扣的响动，我听见了；他弄出的那种——手脚并用的爬动声，我也听见了；我还望得见他衬衫前襟那一方光斑。”

===Original===
“If all that is true, why did you not rouse St. Ann’s at once?”

“Because I knew who the man was.”

There was a brief, electric silence.

“Who was the man?” said O’Leary very quietly.

“My cousin, Louis Letheny.” She brought the name out with a suggestion of triumph. I do not know whether it was a surprise to O’Leary or not; however, he said nothing for a full moment. His clear gray eyes were studying Corole’s face.

===Chinese===
“倘若这些都是真的，您为什么不立刻惊动圣安医院？”

“因为我知道那个男人是谁。”

一阵短促的、通了电似的寂静。

“那男人是谁？”奥利里问，声音极轻。

“我的表兄，路易斯·莱瑟尼。”她吐出这个名字，隐隐带着一丝得胜的意味。这是否出于奥利里的意料，我不得而知；总之他足足有一晌没有作声，那双清亮的灰眼睛端详着科罗尔的脸。

===Original===
“Naturally,” went on Corole with a degree of malicious satisfaction, “naturally I could not arouse the hospital to advertise the fact that the head of the institution had just crawled through a window. Who was I to know Dr. Letheny’s purpose?”

“You are lying,” said O’Leary. “I warned you not to lie. The man you saw crawling through the window of Room 18 was not Dr. Letheny. You and Dr. Hajek were together in the orchard that night and you actually did lean at the window sill, intending to enter Room 18, but Dr. Letheny was already in Room 18. You and Dr. Hajek discussed whether it would be better to wait until Dr. Letheny came out of Room 18, or for Dr. Hajek to follow him into that room.”

In the twinkling of an eye Corole had become saffron yellow, the dabs of orange rouge on her cheeks stood out, emphasizing her high cheekbones with grisly clearness; her eyes were flat and gleaming and her lips had drawn back a little from her teeth and the garish Chinese coat accentuated her ugly pallor.

===Chinese===
“自然，”科罗尔接着说，带着几分恶毒的快意，“我自然不能惊动全院，去张扬一院之长刚刚从窗户里爬进来这个事实。莱瑟尼医生的用意是什么，我凭什么知道？”

“您在撒谎，”奥利里说，“我警告过您，别撒谎。您看见爬进18号病房（Room 18）窗户的那个男人，不是莱瑟尼医生。那天夜里，您和海耶克医生一道在果园里，您也确实倚在那处窗台边，存心要进18号病房，可莱瑟尼医生已经先在18号病房里了。您和海耶克医生商量的，是等莱瑟尼医生从18号病房出来为好，还是由海耶克医生跟进那间屋里去为好。”

一眨眼间，科罗尔已变成一番藏红花似的黄色；颊上那一抹抹橘红胭脂凸显出来，把她高高的颧骨衬得可怖地分明；她的眼睛扁平面放光，嘴唇微微咧开、露出了牙齿，那件刺目的中式外套愈发凸显了她那难看的苍白。

===Original===
“Who told you that?” she whispered through those hideous lips.

“Higgins told me,” replied O’Leary very distinctly.

“Higgins!” cried Corole hoarsely, flinging up one brown, jewelled hand toward her throat. “Higgins! But he is dead!”

“Higgins told me,” repeated O’Leary. “Now then, tell me. What did you and Dr. Hajek do?”

“We﻿—we met at the bridge. We walked together through the orchard.” Corole’s desperate effort to regain her self-control was not nice to witness.

===Chinese===
“这是谁告诉您的？”她从那两片狰狞的嘴唇间挤出耳语。

“希金斯告诉我的。”奥利里答道，一字一顿。

“希金斯！”科罗尔嘶声叫道，一只戴着珠宝的棕色手猛地扬向喉咙。“希金斯！可他已经死了！”

“希金斯告诉我的。”奥利里重复道，“好了，说吧。您和海耶克医生都做了些什么？”

“我们——我们在小桥碰的头。我们一道穿过果园散步。”科罗尔拼命想收回自持，那副挣扎的样子，旁观的人实在不好受。

===Original===
“Go on.”

“Then﻿—as I said﻿—we heard a man in Room 18. And wanted to know what he was doing there. That was natural, I think.” She paused.

“Possibly,” said O’Leary. “Why did you not wait until this﻿—this man﻿—came out from Eighteen?”

“We did not wait for him. Someone came along. We never knew just who it was, though I thought that it was Jim Gainsay. He was in the orchard that night, too.”

“Seems to have been a popular rendezvous,” commented O’Leary grimly. “So this approaching person frightened you away?”

===Chinese===
“往下说。”

“然后——像我说的——我们听见18号病房（Room 18）里有个男人，就想知道他在那儿做什么。我想，这总在情理之中。”她停了停。

“也许吧，”奥利里说，“那你们为什么不等等，等这个——这个男人——从那间病房里出来？”

“我们没有等他。有人走过来了。我们始终没弄清那究竟是谁，不过我当时想，是吉姆·盖恩赛。那天夜里他也在果园里。”

“看来那是个热闹的约会之地。”奥利里冷冷地评了一句。“这么说，是那个走近的人把你们吓走的？”

===Original===
“Not at all,” denied Corole with a flash of her normal ease. “We just﻿—left.”

“Where did you go?”

“Through the apple orchard.”

“And having eluded this﻿—er﻿—unknown person, you returned to the intriguing vicinity of Room 18?”

“No,” said Corole flatly. “I came immediately home.”

===Chinese===
“才不是，”科罗尔否认道，平日那副从容闪现了一瞬，“我们只是——走了。”

“去了哪里？”

“穿过苹果园。”

“那么甩开了这位——呃——不明人士之后，您又回到了18号病房（Room 18）那耐人寻味的左近？”

“没有，”科罗尔断然说，“我径直回家了。”

===Original===
“And Dr. Hajek?”

“Returned to his room at St. Ann’s.”

“Are you sure?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“海耶克医生呢？”

“回他在圣安医院的房间去了。”

“您能肯定？”

“能。”

===Original===
“How do you know?”

Corole hesitated.

“He told me so, later,” she said lamely.

“Why were you intending to intercept Dr. Letheny﻿—or rather, the man whom you thought to be Dr. Letheny?”

===Chinese===
“您怎么知道的？”

科罗尔迟疑了。

“他后来是这样告诉我的。”她吞吞吐吐地说。

“您当时为什么打算拦下莱瑟尼医生——或者说得确切些，拦下您以为的那位莱瑟尼医生？”

===Original===
Corole leaned forward.

“Look here, I *know* that Louis Letheny was in Room 18 that night!”

“I know that, too,” agreed O’Leary quietly.

She leaned back on the cushions, her eyes puzzled and her swinging rhinestone heels catching red and green lights.

===Chinese===
科罗尔朝前探过身来。

“你听着，我*知道*那天夜里路易斯·莱瑟尼就在18号病房（Room 18）里！”

“这一点我也知道。”奥利里平静地表示同意。

她向后靠回软垫上，眼里一片困惑，晃晃荡荡的水钻鞋跟捕捉着红红绿绿的灯光。

===Original===
“Why did you intend to intercept him?” repeated O’Leary.

“Because﻿—Dr. Hajek felt he should know the reason for Louis’s strange actions.”

“Dr. Hajek being an interne and Dr. Letheny the head doctor,” commented O’Leary skeptically.

Corole’s eyes shot a vicious, sidelong look at the detective but she said nothing.

===Chinese===
“您当时为什么想拦他？”奥利里又问了一遍。

“因为——海耶克医生觉得，他应该知道路易斯这些古怪行径的缘由。”

“海耶克医生不过是个实习医生，莱瑟尼医生才是主任医生。”奥利里满腹狐疑地评了一句。

科罗尔的眼睛朝这位侦探恶狠狠地横扫了一眼，却什么也没有说。

## XV Corole Is Moved to Candour / 科罗尔吐露实情

===Original===
A silence fell in the room. O’Leary walked to the window, pulled the heavy, mulberry-coloured drapes aside, and stood there for a moment. The world outside was sodden and cold; the dense green shrubbery strange and unfamiliar with its fallen leaves rotting and its heavy branches dripping. The piano in the alcove opposite me was shrouded with a great black velvet cover, but it seemed to me that ghostly fingers took up the first haunting strains of the “C Sharp Minor Prelude.” I stirred impatiently and O’Leary turned to face Corole, who sat sullenly still in the davenport, her fingernails digging into an orange pillow.

“Come, Miss Letheny. Give up the radium and tell me the whole truth.”

“I have not got the radium. If you don’t believe me you can search the house.”

“The house has already been searched. Your maid was ordered not to tell you. It was done yesterday while you were﻿—out.”

“While I was out yesterday﻿—Tell me, Mr. O’Leary, is anyone else honoured with a﻿—guard? The man from the police department followed me all day yesterday and I suppose is out there now, sitting on the porch railing or somewhere.”

===Chinese===
房间里落下一阵沉默。奥利里走到窗前，拉开厚重的桑葚色帷幔，在那里站了片刻。窗外的世界浸得湿透，一片寒冷；茂密的绿灌木落叶腐烂、沉重的枝条滴着水，显得陌生而异样。我对面壁龛里的钢琴罩着一大块黑丝绒琴罩，可我恍惚觉得，有幽冥的手指已弹起了《升c小调前奏曲》开头那几声萦绕不散的曲调。我不耐烦地挪了挪身子，奥利里便转过身来面对科罗尔，她正阴沉着脸一动不动地坐在卧榻（davenport）上，指甲抠进一只橙色的靠枕里。

“得了，莱瑟尼小姐。把镭交出来，把全部实情告诉我。”

“镭不在我手里。您若不信，大可搜一搜这幢房子。”

“房子已经搜过了。您的女仆奉命瞒着您。是昨天您——外出的时候办的。”

“我昨天外出的时候——告诉我，奥利里先生，还有哪位有幸享受——护卫？警察局派来的那个人昨天跟了我一整天，我料想这会儿就在外头，坐在门廊栏杆上还是别的什么地方的。”

===Original===
“O’Brien, his name is,” said O’Leary amiably. “No, you are not the only person thus watched.”

“I thought not.” Her eyes glinted with malicious satisfaction. “I thought not. What about Jim Gainsay? And Maida Day?”

“Well, what about them?”

“What about them!” To do Corole justice she did hesitate for the barest fraction of a second before she went on: “Is it possible that you do not know that Maida Day was the last person to talk to Louis?”

There was a pause, during which Corole looked in vain for any change of expression in O’Leary’s face.

===Chinese===
“他叫奥布赖恩，”奥利里和颜悦色地说。“不，受这种监视的可不止您一位。”

“我就料到了。”她的眼里闪过一丝恶毒的快意。“我就料到了。吉姆·盖恩赛怎么说？还有梅达·戴怎么说？”

“哦，他们怎么了？”

“他们怎么了！”说句公道话，科罗尔确也曾迟疑了那么一刹那，才接着往下说：“难道您竟不知道，梅达·戴是最后一个同路易斯说过话的人？”

一时无人作声，这当儿科罗尔徒劳地在奥利里脸上搜寻着任何表情的变化。

===Original===
“Are you not surprised!” she cried impatiently. “Goody-goody Maida with her fastidious, touch-me-not ways was in the orchard with Louis after midnight last Thursday night.”

“No,” said O’Leary. “No. I am not surprised.”

“I heard the whole conversation,” continued Corole, as if bent on getting some sort of more spirited reaction out of the detective. “I was there in the shadows and heard the whole thing. Louis was wild about Maida﻿—I’m sure I don’t know why. Anyway, she did not hesitate to tell him that she didn’t return his love.” Corole smiled a very cruel little smile. “Poor Louis! They talked for some time. Louis was one of these cold-natured men, as a rule. I was surprised to hear him. It was better than a play.”

“Could you see them?” inquired O’Leary drily.

“It was black as tar. But I knew their voices. And anyway I can see in the dark like a cat, so I could tell about where they were﻿—could see the outline of Maida’s uniform and Louis’s shirt front.”

===Chinese===
“您不觉得意外吗！”她不耐烦地叫道。“那个假正经的梅达，平日一副洁癖挑剔、谁也碰不得的做派，上周四午夜过后竟同路易斯待在果园里。”

“不，”奥利里说。“不，我不觉得意外。”

“整场谈话我都听见了，”科罗尔接着说，仿佛存心要从这位侦探身上挤出些更有生气的反应。“我就在暗处，从头到尾听了个全。路易斯为梅达神魂颠倒——我实在不明白为什么。反正，她毫不迟疑地告诉了他，说她不领他的情。”科罗尔绽出一个十分残忍的小小的微笑。“可怜的路易斯！他们谈了好一阵子。路易斯平日是个冷性子的人。听他说话我都觉得稀奇。那可比看戏还精彩。”

“您看得见他们吗？”奥利里干巴巴地问。

“黑得像沥青。可我认得他们的嗓音。再说了，我像猫一样能在黑暗里看见东西，所以约莫估得出他们身在何处——看得见梅达那身护士服和路易斯衬衫前胸的轮廓。”

===Original===
“They could see each other, of course?” asked O’Leary nonchalantly.

“No, I shouldn’t think so. I thought I told you that I can see in the dark better than most people. I’m sure they couldn’t see each other for I remember that when they met and began to talk, Maida sort of gasped and said ‘Who is it?’ and Louis answered her.”

“How long did they talk?”

“Not long. Perhaps ten or fifteen minutes.”

“That was about what time?”

Corole paused before replying, I suppose to be sure that she was admitting nothing as to her own activities on that dark night.

===Chinese===
“他们自然也看得见彼此了？”奥利里漫不经心地问。

“不，我看未必。我不是告诉过您，我在黑暗里比大多数人看得清吗。他们准是谁也看不见谁，因为我记得他们碰上、开始说话的时候，梅达倒抽了一口气，问了声『是谁？』，是路易斯回答的她。”

“他们谈了多久？”

“不长。也许十到十五分钟。”

“那是几点钟前后？”

科罗尔答话前顿了一顿，我猜想，她是拿准了绝不招认自己在那个黑夜里干了些什么。

===Original===
“It must have been just before one o’clock. I think it was just after Louis had gone to Room 18 with Sarah to visit his patient there.”

“Did Miss Day return at once to the hospital?”

“Yes, I think so. She was in a rage. I think poor Louis managed to kiss her and Maida is deplorably high-spirited. She struck him at the last; I was sure of that. They parted on very unfriendly terms.” Her eyes slanted maliciously at O’Leary, but he was engrossed in studying the soft figures on the rug at his feet.

“Why do you tell me all this, Miss Letheny?” he asked quietly.

She raised her thin, plucked eyebrows at this, delicately.

“Didn’t you ask me to tell you anything that could help you? I should think that it would be of value to know that the last person known to have been with Louis was quarrelling violently with him.”

===Chinese===
“准是快到一点钟的时候。我想那是在路易斯刚同萨拉去过18号病房（Room 18）、探望过他那位病人之后。”

“戴小姐随即回医院了吗？”

“回了，我想是回了。她一腔怒火。我想可怜的路易斯到底是想法子亲了她一下，而梅达那股烈性子实在惊人。最后她打了他一记；这一点我看准了。他们闹得极不痛快地分了手。”她斜着眼睛恶毒地瞟向奥利里，他却只顾端详脚前地毯上那些柔和的花纹。

“您为什么把这些一股脑儿告诉我，莱瑟尼小姐？”他平静地问。

闻言，她纤巧地扬起那两道修得细细的眉毛。

“不是您请我凡是对您有帮助的事都讲出来吗？我想，得知已知最后一个同路易斯在一起的人正同他激烈争吵，这总该有点价值吧。”

===Original===
“As a matter of fact, you have done Miss Day a favour,” remarked Lance O’Leary. “You have very kindly explained the presence of Miss Day’s lapis cuff link in the pocket of Dr. Letheny’s dinner jacket.”

Corole’s eyes flickered.

“I thought you said you were not surprised to hear this﻿—as if you already knew it.”

“I suspected some such affair. Miss Day made a point of saying that the last time she *saw* Dr. Letheny was when she left your house Thursday night, and it was perfectly true. She did not *see* him when she talked to him later. But, of course, I knew that she must have had some sort of meeting with him. Indeed,” he went on quietly, “I can quite understand Miss Day’s reluctance to tell of the matter. Any young woman would shrink from the headlines﻿—can’t you see them: ‘Beautiful young nurse﻿—Love quarrel with Doctor’﻿—all that sort of thing? Doubtless the cuff link got detached from the cuff and into Dr. Letheny’s hand and he thrust it into his pocket thinking to return it﻿—not knowing what was to happen. Thank you for telling me this, Miss Letheny.” He walked to the door and paused with his hand on the knob. His face was very stern as he glanced back at Corole. “You are only making things worse for yourself when you refuse to tell the whole truth. Good morning, Miss Letheny.”

===Chinese===
“说实话，您倒是帮了戴小姐一个忙，”兰斯·奥利里评道。“您好心解释了戴小姐的青金石袖扣怎么会落进莱瑟尼医生无尾晚礼服的口袋。”

科罗尔的目光闪了一下。

“我记得您方才说，听了这些并不意外——倒好像您早知道了似的。”

“我疑心的正是这么一档子事。戴小姐特意声明，她最后一回*见*到莱瑟尼医生，是周四夜里离开您家的时候，这话千真万确。她后来同他说话的时候，确实没有*见*到他。可我自然明白，她必定同他有过某种会面。的确，”他不动声色地往下说，“戴小姐不愿谈起此事，我完全能够理解。随便哪位年轻姑娘都要躲着报纸标题——您还想象不出来吗：『年轻貌美的护士——与医生闹情场纠纷』——诸如此类？十有八九是袖扣从袖口上脱落，落进了莱瑟尼医生手里，他把它塞进口袋，想着日后物归原主——却不知道后来要出什么事。多谢您告诉我这些，莱瑟尼小姐。”他走到门边，手按在门把上停了停。他回头望向科罗尔，脸色非常严峻。“您不肯把实情和盘托出，只会把自己的处境弄得更糟。早安，莱瑟尼小姐。”

===Original===
Once again on that damp path we said little.

“It was Miss Day’s meeting with Dr. Letheny that Corole overheard, then, and threatened to tell of; that is what Gainsay’s note to Miss Day meant,” said O’Leary musingly as we approached the south door. “Well, that meeting does throw a new light on things﻿—doesn’t it? By the way, Miss Keate, I expect to stay in St. Ann’s for a night or two. I want no one but you to know of it.”

“But where﻿—in what room will you be?”

“Room 18.”

I could feel the colour draining from my face.

“That﻿—room is not safe!”

===Chinese===
在那条湿漉漉的小径上，我们又是一路无话。

“这么说，科罗尔偷听到的正是戴小姐与莱瑟尼医生的会面，还扬言要捅出去；盖恩赛给戴小姐的那张字条，说的就是这个，”走近南门时，奥利里若有所思地说。“唔，那场会面确实让事情透出了新的光亮——是不是？对了，基特小姐，我打算在圣安医院留宿一两夜。这件事除了您，我不要任何人知道。”

“可是您住哪儿——住哪间屋子？”

“18号病房（Room 18）。”

我只觉得脸上的血色一寸寸褪了下去。

“那间——病房不安全！”

===Original===
“Nonsense.”

“But, Mr. O’Leary﻿—I have not told you what I heard this morning!”

“What’s that!”

“Corole﻿—Corole and Dr. Hajek﻿—” He waited in silence while I told him of the singular dialogue that I had interrupted.

“Thank you, Miss Keate,” he said quietly when I had finished.

“But﻿—aren’t you going to arrest them at once? Before they do﻿—whatever it is they are planning? We don’t want another murder in St. Ann’s!”

===Chinese===
“胡说。”

“可是，奥利里先生——我今天早晨听见的事还没告诉您呢！”

“什么事！”

“科罗尔——科罗尔和海耶克医生——”他默然等着，我便把那场被我撞断的离奇对话讲给了他听。

“谢谢您，基特小姐，”我讲完了，他静静地说。

“可是——您不打算立刻逮捕他们吗？趁他们还没来得及——还没来得及干出他们筹划的无论什么事？我们可不想圣安医院再出一桩人命案子！”

===Original===
He shook his head.

“I don’t think it will come to that. And anyway, you know﻿—give a man rope enough﻿—” He did not complete his sentence.

I tightened my lips disapprovingly; it seemed to me that handcuffs would be far more efficacious.

“Can you keep a secret, Miss Keate?” said Lance O’Leary suddenly.

I nodded.

“Then, if all goes well, another twenty-four hours will see the end of this affair.” And with that he was gone, leaving me to stand as if frozen on the step and watch that slight gray figure till it vanished around the corner of the hospital.

===Chinese===
他摇了摇头。

“我看不至于闹到那一步。再说，您是知道的——给一个人的绳子足够长——”他没有把这句话说完。

我大不以为然地抿紧了嘴唇；依我看，手铐要管用得多。

“您能守住一个秘密吗，基特小姐？”兰斯·奥利里突然说。

我点了点头。

“那么，倘若一切顺利，再过二十四个小时，这桩案子就见分晓了。”说罢他便走了，撇下我仿佛冻僵了似的站在台阶上，望着那瘦削的灰色身影，直到它拐过医院的墙角，消失不见。

===Original===
Another twenty-four hours!

I was still on the step, staring absently into the surrounding greens, when a movement through a lane of trees caught my eyes. There, strolling through the wet orchard, was Jim Gainsay. At his side was Maida, her white cap distinct against that green curtain, her soft black hair waving gently about her lovely face. The navy-blue cape she wore was thrown back so that its scarlet lining gleamed against a fold of her white dress and the scarlet seemed to match her cheeks and lips. As I watched, the two suddenly faced each other. Jim caught at Maida’s hands and held them against his face and slowly drew her toward him. She yielded for a moment, then glanced toward St. Ann’s windows and pulled away. He relinquished her hands and laughed and after a second she laughed, too. Then they resumed their slow pace, and the white cap and scarlet fold of cape and brown Stetson hat disappeared among the dense green thickets.

He had succeeded in seeing her, then, and I did not need to fulfil my promise.

The rest of the day passed quietly but none too pleasantly, for the hospital was gloomy and dark and very hushed, the nurses uneasy and nervous, and there was a sort of subdued terror that lurked in the very walls of the great, old place.

===Chinese===
再过二十四个小时！

我还站在台阶上，失神地望着四下的绿意，忽然，一行树木间有什么动静攫住了我的目光。那是吉姆·盖恩赛，正漫步走过湿漉漉的果园。梅达在他身侧，她的白色护士帽在那道绿幕的映衬下格外分明，柔软的黑发在她那可爱的脸庞旁轻轻拂动。她那件藏青色斗篷向后敞开着，猩红的衬里贴着一褶白裙熠熠发亮，那猩红正配她的脸颊与双唇。我望着望着，两人忽然彼此转过脸来。吉姆一把攥住梅达的双手，贴在自己脸上，缓缓把她引向自己。她顺从了一瞬，随即朝圣安医院的窗户望了一眼，抽身挣开。他松开她的手，笑了，片刻之后她也笑了。然后他们重新放缓脚步走去，那顶白护士帽、斗篷上猩红的一褶，还有那顶棕色斯泰森帽（Stetson），一起消失在浓密的绿树丛中。

这么说，他到底还是见到她了，我也就可以不必兑现我的承诺了。

这一天余下的时光过得平静，却绝不痛快，因为医院里阴郁、昏暗、静得出奇，护士们一个个心神不宁、坐立不安，那座老宅的墙壁本身，都潜藏着一种被压住的恐怖。

===Original===
I could not sleep, as was my custom, during first watch, and it was fortunate, as it happened, that I could not for I went down to the south wing a little early and thus, I believe, prevented another panic. I am sure that any other nurse seeing Corole as I saw her would have gone completely to pieces.

This is the way it happened.

I found myself in the south wing a good half hour before midnight and strolled casually along the corridor. The south door was locked as it should be, the new key having duly arrived and hanging, very bright and new, on the nail above the chart desk. I remember that I had just decided to find a new and less well-known place for it, and having selected a spot at the right of the door in question was endeavouring to push in a nail with a glass paper weight, and not having much success, when a sort of scratching outside the door caught my ears. I paused to peer through the small squares of glass.

The wind had risen again and the low branches of the trees outside were tossing and moaning. The corridor was not sufficiently light to enable me to see beyond the black panes of glass and they glittered emptily, so that I felt as if eyes were looking in at me. Then, all at once, a face pressed up against the glass. It was a face so haggard, so wild, so fraught with terror that I did not recognize it at once to be Corole’s.

===Chinese===
我照例在第一班（first watch）里睡不着，而这回也幸亏睡不着，因为我提早了一点下到南翼，我相信，这才防止了又一场恐慌。我敢肯定，换任何一个护士，见到我见到的科罗尔那副模样，都得彻底垮掉。

事情是这样发生的。

我发觉自己早在大约午夜前整整半个钟头就到了南翼，便信步沿走廊踱去。南门照规矩锁着，那把新钥匙已如期送到，挂在病历台上方的钉子上，亮晃晃的簇新。我记得我刚打定主意要给它另找一个不那么人人皆知的地方，而且已在那扇门的右边相中了一处，正用一个玻璃镇纸（glass paper weight）往木头里砸钉子，砸得不大顺手，这时，门外一阵窸窸窣窣的刮擦声传进了我耳朵。我停下脚，凑近那一格一格的小玻璃往外张望。

风又刮起来了，外面低垂的树枝摇曳着、呻吟着。走廊里的光线不够亮，我望不透那几块黑玻璃，它们空空地闪着光，倒叫我觉得自己仿佛正被一双眼睛向里盯着。忽然，一张脸贴上了玻璃。那张脸那么憔悴、那么狂乱、那么被恐怖占满，我一时竟没认出它就是科罗尔。

===Original===
As I stared she made an imperative gesture and moved her pale lips in words that I could not hear. The key was in my hand and I unlocked the door. Corole slipped stealthily inside and I closed the door hastily on the wind and rain, locking it before I turned to her.

She was panting, her hair was flying in wet strings about her face and her eyes had great, fiery, black pupils that caught and reflected the light. She was wrapped in a dark silk cloak trimmed with monkey fur that was wet and hung about her neck in long, dank wisps that added to her wild aspect. One hand clutched the cloak across her breast and the other carried a square, leather-covered jewel-case.

I found my voice.

“What are you doing here?” I whispered.

She cast a furtive glance toward the south door.

“Did you lock the door? Come, is there some place where we can talk? Here﻿—” With a swift motion she pushed open the door of Room 18, and pulled me inside.

===Chinese===
我怔怔望着，她打了个不容分说的手势，惨白的嘴唇翕动着，吐出我听不见的话。钥匙正在我手里，我便开了锁。科罗尔悄然闪身进来，我急急把风雨关在门外，先上了锁，才转身向她。

她喘着气，头发化作一绺绺湿发披散在脸旁，眼睛里那对黑色的瞳孔又大又亮，火似的摄住灯光又反射出来。她裹着一件镶猴毛皮（monkey fur）的深色丝斗篷，毛皮湿透了，一绺绺潮乎乎地垂在脖颈四周，愈发添了她的狂态。一只手把斗篷攥紧在胸前，另一只手提着一只方形皮面首饰盒。

我这才找回了自己的嗓音。

“你在这儿做什么？”我低声问。

她朝南门偷偷投去一眼。

“你锁了门吗？来，有没有个地方能让我们说话？这儿——”她手疾眼快，一把推开18号病房（Room 18）的门，把我拽了进去。

===Original===
“Don’t turn on the light,” she warned me in a tense whisper. And indeed, I had no intention of so doing, for as she spoke I recalled O’Leary’s presence in the room. I looked sharply toward the bed and chair but could not tell if either were occupied.

Corole took several deep, shaking breaths before she spoke.

“I’ve been running,” she whispered presently. “I had to get rid of O’Leary’s watchdogs.” Actually there was an undercurrent of mirth in her whispered accents, though I was sure that she had recently had a bad fright of some kind.

“Did someone follow you?” I asked.

She held her breath for a second; then she released it.

“Yes,” she said. “I don’t know who it was. Sarah, I had to come here. I﻿—I am afraid to stay in the cottage alone all night. Huldah is gone, you know. I﻿—am afraid. Can’t I stay here?”

===Chinese===
“别开灯，”她屏着气低声警告我。其实我也压根儿不打算开灯，因为她开口时，我想起了奥利里就宿在这间屋里。我锐利地朝床和椅子望去，却辨不出那上面究竟有没有人。

科罗尔深深吸了几口发颤的气，才开了口。

“我一路跑来的，”她随即悄声道。“总得甩掉奥利里那些盯梢的看门狗。”其实她那低语里暗暗藏着一丝笑意，尽管我敢肯定，她方才着实大受了一番惊吓。

“有人跟着你？”我问。

她屏住呼吸，停了一秒；随即又吐了出来。

“有，”她说。“我不知道是谁。萨拉，我不得不上这儿来。我——我一个人在小屋里待一整夜，我怕。赫尔达走了，你是知道的。我——害怕。我不能留在这儿吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly not. Don’t be foolish, Corole. St. Ann’s is not a hotel.”

She gripped my arm and her hand was trembling.

“I tell you I am afraid. Sarah, you must let me stay here. I’ll sleep anywhere. I’ll sleep right here in this room.”

“No. No. You can’t do that!”

“I must stay in St. Ann’s. You can’t put me out bodily. I’ve got to stay.” I felt her shiver violently. “I cannot go through that terrible orchard again. I cannot sleep in Louis Letheny’s house tonight. There are ghosts, Sarah, ghosts﻿—oh, you don’t know!”

===Chinese===
“当然不能。别犯傻了，科罗尔。圣安医院可不是旅馆。”

她一把攥住我的胳膊，手直打颤。

“我告诉你，我害怕。萨拉，你一定得让我留下。睡哪儿都行。我就睡在这间屋里。”

“不行。不行。你可不能那样！”

“我必须留在圣安医院。你总不能架着我的胳膊把我硬扔出去。我非留下不可。”我觉出她猛地打了个寒战。“我再也走不过那可怕的果园了。今晚我说什么也睡不到路易斯·莱瑟尼的屋子里去。有鬼，萨拉，鬼——唉，你不知道！”

===Original===
“Ghosts! There are nothing of the kind.” I felt my scalp prickle as I spoke.

“Maybe not. Anyway, I must stay here.”

“No,” I repeated but she must have felt me weakening for she renewed her pleas, even promising to make herself eligible to a room in the hospital by having tonsillitis, if I insisted. She said she felt it coming on owing to her getting so wet and being bareheaded. Which was not only silly, as I assured her, but was not even to be believed, Corole being as sleek and healthy as a young jaguar, and about as even-tempered.

“But you can stay,” I relented, “if you will do as I say and keep quiet about it.”

“Heavens, yes!” agreed Corole fervently. “All I want to do is keep quiet about it. Shall I just stay right here in Eighteen? I am not afraid.” She moved toward the bed.

I grasped her cloak and jerked her back.

===Chinese===
“鬼！根本没有这种东西。”说这话时，我觉得自己头皮发麻。

“也许没有。反正，我必须留在这儿。”

“不行，”我又说了一遍，可她准是觉出我的口气在软下来，便重新哀求起来，甚至许诺说，倘若我坚持，她可以害一场扁桃体炎，好让自己够上在医院里住一间病房的资格。她说她淋得那么透，又光着头，觉出扁桃体炎就要找上身来了。这话不但傻气——我如此向她担保——而且根本叫人无法相信，科罗尔的身子光滑健壮得像一只小美洲豹，脾气也差不多同样喜怒无常。

“不过你可以留下，”我松了口，“只要你肯照我说的做，并且守口如瓶。”

“天哪，正是！”科罗尔热烈地应道。“我求之不得的就是守口如瓶。那我就干脆待在这间18号病房里好了？我可不害怕。”她朝床边走去。

我一把抓住她的斗篷，把她拽了回来。

===Original===
“No,” I said hastily. “No. You cannot stay in this room.” There may have been a note of consternation in my voice and I am quite sure I heard a sort of subdued snicker from the direction of the bed.

Corole heard it, too.

“What was that?” she whispered sharply, starting back against me. I shuddered aside from contact with that dripping monkey fur.

“Probably a cat,” I said at random.

“A cat!” I could feel her pull her short skirt tighter around her. “I hate cats. They remind me of﻿—I hate cats.”

“Corole, stay right here for a moment or two. *Don’t move from the door!* I shall come back and open the door, and you go as fast as you can through the corridor and as far as the general office door. Don’t let anyone see you if you can help it and wait there for me.”

===Chinese===
“不，”我慌忙说。“不。你不能待在这间屋里。”我的话音里也许带了一丝惊惶，而且我敢肯定，我听见床那个方向传来了一声压得低低的窃笑。

科罗尔也听见了。

“那是什么？”她尖着嗓子悄声问，猛地缩身躲到我身上。那湿淋淋的猴毛皮蹭着我，我打了个寒噤，闪身躲开。

“多半是只猫，”我随口应道。

“猫！”我觉出她把短裙子往身上裹得更紧了。“我恨猫。它们叫我想起——我恨猫。”

“科罗尔，你就在这儿站上一两分钟。*千万别离开这扇门！*我一会儿回来开门，你就尽快穿过走廊，一直走到总办公室门口。能不让任何人瞧见就不让任何人瞧见，在那儿等我。”

===Original===
She murmured something in assent and in less time than it takes to tell, I had manufactured errands to get the nurses into the diet kitchen and drug room, had watched Corole move with the lithe swiftness of an animal through the long shadowy corridor and myself had followed her. My own room was, of course, the only place where I could let her sleep. I even loaned her a night garment; she looked at its long sleeves and high neck dubiously but accepted it.

I gave myself the satisfaction of locking the door and carrying the key away; I did not know whether Corole heard the click of the key or not but I did not intend that Corole Letheny should be allowed to prowl at large through the dark corridors of St. Ann’s.

It was a little after twelve when I found myself in the south wing again. Maida was already there and Olma Flynn and the same little, blue-striped student nurse.

I don’t mind admitting that I slipped into the diet kitchen at my first opportunity and brewed myself a cup of very strong, black coffee. Corole’s advent had shaken my nerves a bit and I did not like the way the wind was murmuring around the corners of the great old building, stirring up forgotten drafts and rattling windows and slapping rain against them.

===Chinese===
她咕哝了一声算是应允，说时迟，那时快，我已编派出几桩差事，把护士们统统打发进配膳室和药品间，眼看着科罗尔以野兽般轻捷的身手穿过那条长长的幽暗走廊，我自己也跟了上去。我自己的房间，自然是唯一能容她睡觉的地方。我甚至借了她一件睡衣；她瞧着那长袖子和高领口，满脸狐疑，到底还是收下了。

我心满意足地锁上房门，把钥匙带在了身上；不知科罗尔听没听见钥匙的咔哒声，但我可不打算放任科罗尔·莱瑟尼在圣安医院黑黢黢的走廊里四处游荡。

刚过十二点，我又到了南翼。梅达已经在了，还有奥尔玛·弗琳，还是那个小小的、穿蓝条纹布的学生护士。

我不介意承认，一得着空子我就溜进配膳室，给自己冲了一杯极浓的黑咖啡。科罗尔的到来让我神经受了些震动，我也不喜欢风在那座老宅四角呜呜低语的样子——它搅起一股股被遗忘的穿堂风，震得窗子格格作响，把雨点一把一把摔在玻璃上。

===Original===
Second watch, however, passed quite as usual, save for the little air of uncertainty and uneasiness that made itself manifest in our fondness for each other’s company, our frequent glances into the shadows, and one or two broken thermometers owing to the sudden crashes of the wind. The light flickered once as if about to go out but mercifully did not do so. I might add that the prevalence of broken thermometers was one of the minor troubles of that week; a thermometer is an easy thing to slip from one’s fingers, especially when shaking it, and it is not surprising that Dr. Balman had had to order new thermometers for every wing in St. Ann’s.

The hours seemed very long, particularly when it occurred to me that if Corole and Dr. Hajek expected to carry out their scheme that “day” there were only a few hours left in which to do so. Of course, I had Corole safely locked up and if her coming to St. Ann’s in well-simulated terror to beg a refuge was actually, as I half suspected, only a part of their plan, why then I had stopped any further activity on her part. But I could not wholly believe that Corole’s coming had been prearranged; her panic had been too genuine.

We were not very busy, so I had plenty of time to think. More than once I caught myself eyeing Maida as she went quietly about her business.

Once, when we were both at the desk, engaged in a desultory and halfhearted conversation, footsteps padding softly along the corridor back of us caught our attention and I turned simultaneously with Maida. I noted that her eyes flared black as she whirled and her lips were a quick, set line, and wondered if my own face showed such immediate alarm. However, it was only Olma Flynn, advancing to tell me through chattering teeth that she was sure there was *Something* in Room 18. I was startled for a flash, though at once I realized that it was O’Leary, and Maida went white though she held her shoulders straighter than ever.

===Chinese===
不过第二班照常过去了，只是多了一点悬心不定、惴惴不安的气氛，它显现在我们谁都爱凑作一堆上，显现在我们频频往暗处张望上，还显现在那一两支被骤起的风声吓得失手摔碎的体温计上。灯闪了一下，眼看就要熄灭，幸而没有。我还可以补一句，体温计频遭不测是那一周的小麻烦之一；体温计这东西很容易从指间滑脱，甩表的时候尤其如此，所以巴尔曼医生不得不给圣安医院每个病区都添置新体温计，也就不足为怪了。

那几个钟头显得格外漫长，尤其当我想到，倘若科罗尔和海耶克医生指望就在那个“白天”里施行他们的计划，剩下的时辰已经寥寥无几。当然，科罗尔已被我稳稳锁住，她若是真像我心下疑心的那样，装出一副吓得魂不附体的模样来圣安医院乞求庇护，只不过是他们计划的一环，那么，我已然断了她这一头的后续动作。可我不能完全相信科罗尔的到来是事先安排好的；她那场惊慌，实在太真了。

我们不大忙，所以我有的是工夫想心事。不止一次，我发觉自己正盯着梅达出神，看她安安静静地忙她分内的活计。

有一回，我们俩都在病历台前，正有一搭没一搭、没精打采地闲谈，身后走廊里一阵轻软的脚步声攫住了我们的注意，我和梅达同时转过身去。我注意到她旋身时那双眼睛黑得发亮，双唇霎时抿成一道细线，心里不禁纳闷，我自己的脸上是否也露出这般立时的惊惶。然而来的不过是奥尔玛·弗琳，她磕着牙走上前来告诉我，她确信18号病房（Room 18）里有*什么东西*。我一刹那也吃了一惊，虽然立刻明白那是奥利里；梅达白了脸，肩膀却挺得比先前更直。

===Original===
I managed to calm Olma, though she clung to her point with a firmness that in my heart I labelled plain mule stubbornness.

“If we are all murdered before morning, Miss Keate, it will be your fault,” she said at last.

“Nonsense! If it is a ghost, as you seem to believe, you need not be alarmed. Ghosts can’t do anything but moan around the corners.” It was unfortunate that just then the wind swept through the draughty old corridor with a most realistic moan, upon which Olma turned green and vanished into the diet kitchen. It was this, I think, that gave rise to a swiftly travelling tale that Room 18 was haunted, a tale that the south wing has never yet been able to live down.

Thinking to warn O’Leary that he must be more circumspect in his behaviour if he wished his presence in that ill-omened room to remain a secret, I watched my chance to slip unobserved into Eighteen. Dawn was creeping into the room by that time and the furniture loomed up dark and black in the cold half-light. The room was quite empty of human presence, though to my tired nerves it seemed that there might be other presences. I shrugged aside the unwelcome thought. A glance at the window showed me that the bolts had been slipped and the screen opened. I had no doubt that O’Leary was making use of that low window as others had done. I resisted a childish impulse to fasten the bolts against his return and returned to the corridor.

---

===Chinese===
我好歹安抚住了奥尔玛，虽说她咬定那一点死不松口，那股执拗劲儿，我在心里干脆封它作十足的骡子脾气。

“要是天亮前我们全叫人谋害了，基特小姐，那就是您的不是了。”她终于说。

“胡说！倘若那真是鬼，照你信的那样，你也用不着惊慌。鬼除了贴着墙角呜呜咽咽，什么坏事也干不成。”不巧就在那时，风带着一声活灵活现的呻吟，扫过那条四处透风的老走廊，奥尔玛顿时脸色发青，一转眼就没进了配膳室。我想，正是这一下，生出了一段不胫而走的传闻，说18号病房（Room 18）闹鬼——这传闻，南翼直到今天也没能洗刷掉。

我有意提醒奥利里，他若想让自己宿在那间不吉利的屋里的事不被张扬出去，行事就得更收敛些，便瞅准机会，神不知鬼不觉地溜进了18号病房。那时晨光正悄悄爬进屋里，家具在清冷的微明中黑沉沉地显出轮廓。屋里已经空无一人，只是在我疲惫的神经看来，似乎还可能有别的什么存在。我耸了耸肩，把这不受欢迎的念头甩在一边。朝窗上瞥了一眼，我看见插销已被拨开，纱窗也敞着。毫无疑问，奥利里正像先前那些人一样，把那扇低窗用作进出的门路。我按捺住一个孩子气的冲动——想插上插销，不让他回来——自己回到了走廊上。

---

===Original===
With the tinny sound of the breakfast bell away down in the basement, the straggling through the corridors of the day nurses, freshly uniformed if a trifle gray about the eyes, the fragrant smell of coffee floating through the halls, my vigilance relaxed a bit. The night was past and so far as I knew nothing out of the way had occurred. Knowing Corole to be a late sleeper I did not go immediately to my room to release her. Instead I followed Maida and Olma and the student nurse downstairs to the dining room. It was a sorry meal with buckwheat cakes which I despise and which, besides, give me hives, and Miss Dotty relating a very lurid dream and dissolving into tears under Melvina’s interpretation. The tears dripped dismally down Miss Dotty’s inefficient nose, Melvina enlarged upon the meaning of dreams, and I found that I had sugared my coffee twice. I was glad when the meal was over.

In the intervals of Melvina’s sinister monologue I had come to the conclusion that Corole Letheny under lock and key was not a situation to be lightly relinquished. I sought O’Leary at once, surreptitiously avoiding the day nurses. He was not in Room 18, so I straightened the wrinkled counterpane on the bed and left. As I passed through the corridor of the second charity ward I took a breakfast tray off the dumbwaiter standing there unguarded; the disappearance of the tray caused considerable excitement in the ward, I found later, which was augmented by its reappearance later in the morning in the second-floor linen closet where I had thoughtfully left it, with only the coffee splashed a little, for Corole did not even see that breakfast tray.

When I unlocked the door to my room it required only a glance to see that my bird had flown, so to speak. I set the tray on the dresser and advanced into the room. The bed was tossed and had been slept in, though the night garment I had loaned her was still decorously folded on a chair. The window was open, letting in gusts of rain on my flowered voile curtains which were running in pink and green streaks and later had to be replaced. I crossed to close it and in doing so found the mode of her exit. St. Ann’s, as I have said, was an old building with numerous turrets and towers and roof irregularities which included various ledges and wide window casings. From the window beyond mine dropped an old-fashioned, iron fire escape fastened to the old red bricks with rusted bolts. And from my window to the next ran a sort of ledge, narrow, to be sure, and slippery, but there was the ivy to cling to and shrubbery below to break a fall. For a woman of Corole’s build and propensities it was not a difficult climb and once on the fire escape the rest was easy. I leaned out the window. Had I still been unconvinced, there was proof of her passage, for caught on an ivy strand there hung a dejected, wet, black wisp of monkey fur.

===Chinese===
随着地底深处早餐铃叮叮当当的声响，随着白班护士们三三两两穿过走廊——她们制服崭新，眼圈却微微发灰——随着咖啡的香气浮荡在各个厅堂里，我的警觉松弛了几分。一夜过去了，据我所知没有出任何岔子。我知道科罗尔贪睡，便没有立刻回房去放她，反而跟着梅达、奥尔玛和那个学生护士下了楼，进了餐厅。那是一顿败兴的饭：荞麦饼（buckwheat cakes）我本就看不上眼，吃了还要起荨麻疹；多蒂小姐又讲了一个极其阴森的梦，经梅尔维娜一番解说，当场泪如雨下。泪珠凄凄惨惨地淌下多蒂小姐那只不中用的鼻子，梅尔维娜则愈发滔滔不绝地铺陈梦的种种含义，我发觉自己往咖啡里放了两回糖。这顿饭总算吃完，我真是求之不得。

在梅尔维娜阴森森的独白的间隙里，我得出一个结论：科罗尔·莱瑟尼被锁在我的房门里，这个局面可不能轻轻撒手。我立刻去找奥利里，偷偷避着白班护士。他不在18号病房（Room 18），我便把床上揉皱的床罩抻平，退了出来。路过第二济贫病房的走廊时，我从那架无人看管的送餐升降机上取下一份早餐托盘；后来我才知道，托盘的失踪在病房里引起了好一阵骚动，而当天上午稍晚，它又出现在二楼布草间——那是我“体贴入微”地存放在那儿的——骚动于是更上一层楼；托盘不过是咖啡洒出了一点，因为科罗尔压根儿连那份早餐托盘的面也没见着。

我开锁进自己房间，只消一眼便看了出来，可以这么说，我这只鸟儿已经飞了。我把托盘搁在梳妆台上，往房间里走去。床上凌乱，分明有人睡过，只是我借她的那件睡衣还端端正正叠在椅子上。窗户开着，阵阵急雨扑在我那印花玻璃纱（flowered voile）窗帘上，粉红和翠绿的颜色一道道往下淌，后来只得换掉。我走过去关窗，就在这一走之间，发现了她出逃的门路。圣安医院，如我先前所说，是座老房子，塔楼角亭众多，屋顶高低错落，其间自不乏各式檐沿和宽宽的窗框。我这扇窗过去一扇的窗下，垂着一道老式的铁消防梯（fire escape），用生了锈的螺栓固定在老红砖上。而从我这扇窗到旁边那扇，横着一道檐沿，固然又窄又滑，可有常春藤可以攀附，底下还有灌木丛可以缓冲坠势。对科罗尔这副身手和脾性的女子来说，这算不上一段难爬的路，一旦上了消防梯，其余更是易如反掌。我探身窗外。倘若我此前还有半点疑心，这里便是她经过的铁证——一缕常春藤上，挂着一条蔫头蔫脑、湿漉漉的黑色猴毛皮。

===Original===
So Corole was gone! I felt guilty for letting her slip through my fingers but reflected that O’Leary had known of her presence in St. Ann’s, and moreover, a woman cannot go far in a drenched coat and no hat.

This comfortable reflection lasted until I went to the wardrobe and found that my best hat was gone. The hat was a very beautiful thing with quantities of artificial violets on it and three yards of looped purple ribbon, and had cost me twenty-five dollars owing to my having it made to order to fit my bobless head. And Corole had brazenly worn it out in the rain, which did not increase my affection for Corole.

I set forth again to find O’Leary, feeling that he should know at once of Corole’s flight, pausing only to leave the tray in the second-floor linen closet.

===Chinese===
这么说，科罗尔跑了！竟让她从我指缝里溜走，我心里颇觉歉然，但转念一想，奥利里原本知道她在圣安医院，再说，一个女人裹着湿透的大衣、头上没顶帽子，也走不出多远去。

这份自宽自慰，一直维持到我走近衣柜，发现我那顶最好的帽子不见了。那顶帽子是件很美的物事，缀着数不清的人工紫罗兰，还有三码打成环扣的紫色缎带，花了我二十五块大洋——只因我是特意定做的，好合我这颗不烫波波短发的脑袋。科罗尔竟恬不知耻地戴着它冒雨出了门，这可增进不了我对科罗尔的好感。

我又动身去找奥利里，觉得科罗尔逃走的事该立刻让他知道，只中途停了停，把托盘撂在二楼布草间里。

===Original===
O’Leary turned up at last in the vast old stable, now converted into a garage, that is out back of St. Ann’s. He was apparently engaged in sniffing at something that I did not see and that he thrust hurriedly into his pocket at the sound of my approach.

In as few words as possible I told him of Corole’s departure.

His face became very sober.

“That’s bad,” he said. “That’s bad. I figured she was safe in your hands. So she got away across that ledge.” The place was visible from where we stood, and he surveyed it thoughtfully through the gray streaks of rain.

“Well, it can’t be helped now. You say she wore your hat?”

===Chinese===
奥利里最后是在那座如今改作车库的宽大老马厩里露面的，就在圣安医院后头。他显然正凑着一样什么东西嗅闻，我没能看清那是什么，只听见我走近的动静，他就慌忙把它塞进了口袋。

我三言两语，把科罗尔出走的事告诉了他。

他的脸色变得十分凝重。

“这可不妙，”他说。“这可不妙。我还当她稳稳当当地在您手里呢。这么说，她是打那道檐沿过去的。”那处地方从我们站的地方望得见，他隔着灰蒙蒙的雨丝，若有所思地端详着它。

“唉，事已至此，没法子了。您是说，她戴了您的帽子？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

“She would not be apt to return to the Letheny cottage,” he mused. “Let me see; it is barely seven o’clock﻿—the stores will not be open for another hour. There is plenty of time.”

“The stores?”

“She will go straight to buy a hat,” he explained with remarkable lack of tact. “Corole Letheny is not going far in a hat that﻿—” He noted my unsympathetic countenance. “A hat that﻿—er﻿—does not suit her. I mean that she did not choose herself,” he amended hastily.

Without saying a word I turned toward the gravelled path that leads back to St. Ann’s.

===Chinese===
“是的。”

“她多半不会回莱瑟尼家的小屋去，”他沉吟道。“让我想想；眼下才刚七点——铺子还有一个钟头开门。时间绰绰有余。”

“铺子？”

“她这就要直奔商店买帽子去了，”他解释道，那份不识轻重的直率实在少见。“科罗尔·莱瑟尼戴着那样一顶帽子，可走不远——”他瞧见我一脸毫不体谅的神色。“一顶——呃——不合她意的帽子。我是说，不是她自己挑的那顶。”他赶忙改口。

我一言不发，转身走上那条通回圣安医院的碎石小径。

===Original===
“Wait a minute, Miss Keate,” begged O’Leary contritely, seeing perhaps that he had offended me in a matter that no woman can freely forgive. “Please, wait. If you’ll forgive me I’ll tell you something of interest.”

Being exceedingly curious I went back. He drew me into the shadow of a big gray ambulance.

“I want you to keep an eye on Miss Day,” he said in a low voice and with an odd glance into the shadows of the place.

“Miss Day!”

“Especially if you see this fellow, Gainsay, hanging around.”

“Why, what do you mean? Is Jim Gainsay﻿—”

===Chinese===
“请留步，基特小姐，”奥利里满脸悔意地央告道，也许是见自己冒犯了我，而且冒犯在一件天下女人谁也不肯痛快宽赦的事情上。“求您等等。您若肯饶我这一回，我就告诉您一件有意思的事。”

我好奇心极重，便走了回去。他把我拉进一辆灰色大救护车的阴影里。

“我要您替我留意着戴小姐，”他低声说，一边怪模怪样地朝这地方的暗影里瞥了一眼。

“戴小姐！”

“尤其要是您看见盖恩赛这家伙在左近转悠。”

“哎，这是什么意思？难道吉姆·盖恩赛——”

===Original===
“Jim Gainsay is the man who was following Corole last night. O’Brien was stationed up at the cottage last night and saw him. It seems that Corole slipped out a side door. She came out so unexpectedly that she was into the orchard before O’Brien was after her. He was going full tilt when he found that someone else was ahead of him, both of them after Corole, who was having the devil’s own luck, according to O’Brien, in avoiding tree trunks and shrubbery. O’Brien says she can see in the dark. At the bridge O’Brien caught up with the man and can swear it was Gainsay, but just then a low-hanging branch knocked O’Brien down and senseless for a moment and when he got to his feet Corole and Gainsay were both gone. O’Brien wandered about the orchard hunting them for half the night and I ran into him about five o’clock, soaked to the skin and his face a welt of scratches and his disposition permanently warped.”

“So it was Jim Gainsay who gave Corole such a fright,” I murmured. “I wonder what he wanted.”

“It looks bad for Gainsay,” said O’Leary thoughtfully. “Whether he killed Dr. Letheny in a mistaken effort to defend Miss Day, or whether he killed Jackson for the sake of the radium, or whether, thinking that the radium is still at large he is determined to secure it for his own use, in any case it looks bad. I hope your little friend, Miss Day, is not going to be too much hurt.”

“You mean if she cares for Gainsay? Maida is not one to wear her heart on her sleeve. If we could only find that radium,” I concluded hopelessly.

“Oh, I have the radium,” said O’Leary simply.

===Chinese===
“昨晚跟踪科罗尔的，正是吉姆·盖恩赛。奥布赖恩昨夜守在小屋那头，看见了他。似乎是科罗尔从一扇边门溜了出去。她出来得太突然，等奥布赖恩追上去，她人已经进了果园。他正撒开腿猛追，却发现前头另有一个人，两个人都在追科罗尔——照奥布赖恩的说法，科罗尔躲树干、绕灌木，运气好得邪门。奥布赖恩说她黑地里看得见东西。到了小桥，奥布赖恩追上了那个人，可以起誓那就是盖恩赛，可偏偏在那时，一根低垂的树枝把奥布赖恩打翻在地，一时人事不省，等他爬起身来，科罗尔和盖恩赛都已经没了影。奥布赖恩在果园里转了半夜搜寻他们俩，五点钟光景我撞见了他，浑身湿透，脸上横一道竖一道全是抓伤，脾气也给磨得再没法瞧了。”

“原来把科罗尔吓成那样的，是吉姆·盖恩赛，”我喃喃道。“不知道他想要什么。”

“盖恩赛这嫌疑可不轻，”奥利里若有所思地说。“不管他是为了护戴小姐而失手杀了莱瑟尼医生，还是为了那点镭杀了杰克逊先生，抑或是以为镭还在外头流通、打定主意要弄到手自己受用，反正嫌疑都不轻。但愿您那位小朋友戴小姐，别伤得太重。”

“您是说，倘若她心里有盖恩赛？梅达可不是把心事挂在脸上的人。要是我们能找到那镭就好了。”我绝望地说完了这句话。

“噢，镭在我手里。”奥利里平平淡淡地说。

## XVI The Red Light Above the Door / 门上方的红灯

===Original===
“*You have the radium!*”
He nodded. My mouth open I waited for him to tell me more. In the little silence I heard a sort of rustle and I looked about me in some alarm. O’Leary heard the rustle, too, but his face wore the most peculiar expression of mingled satisfaction and anxiety. He made the barest perceptible gesture against comment, and just at the moment Morgue dropped casually down from an opening above what was formerly a hay loft. I jumped a little at his﻿—I mean, *her* unexpected advent and O’Leary spoke unconcernedly.
“Yes, I have the radium. Or rather it is in Room 18 which is, I believe, the safest place in the world for it, inasmuch as there is not a soul in St. Ann’s who would willingly enter that room﻿—save perhaps your intrepid self.”
“How did you find it?”

===Chinese===
“*镭在您手里！*”
他点点头。我张着嘴等他往下说。在这小小的静默里，我听见一阵窸窸窣窣的响动，便有些惊慌地四下张望。奥利里也听见了那响动，脸上却是一副得意与焦灼交织的古怪神情。他做了个几乎察觉不到的手势，示意我别作声；恰在此时，太平间从原先当干草棚使的那间棚子上方的豁口里，慢条斯理地跳了下来。它——我是说，*她*——这突如其来的驾到吓了我小小一跳，奥利里却若无其事地开了口。
“是的，镭在我手里。或者不如说，它在18号病房（Room 18）里——我相信，那是普天之下藏它最稳妥的地方，因为圣安医院里没有一个活人肯自愿踏进那间屋子——兴许除了您这位浑身是胆的角色。”
“您是怎么找到它的？”

===Original===
“Corole brought it to Room 18 last night.” O’Leary’s voice had lifted to a normal pitch and I recall thinking that he should speak lower. “Corole brought it in her jewel case. The jewel case is there, too; she must have doubted your﻿—er﻿—hospitality.”
“Do you mean to say that she had that box of radium in her jewel case!” I cried. “And that she left the whole thing there, in Eighteen?”
“Possibly she agreed with me that it was the safest place in which to leave it. No one would suspect its being back in Room 18. No one would voluntarily enter that room. Oh, she took the precaution to cross to that closet and place the jewel case away back on the shelf. She did that while you were clearing the way for her passage through the halls to your room. She came very near sitting down on the bed to wait,” went on O’Leary drily. “And I was endeavouring to give an imitation of a mattress when you opportunely returned.”
“Oh,” I said brilliantly. “Oh.”

===Chinese===
“昨夜，是科罗尔把它带回了18号病房（Room 18）。”奥利里的嗓音已经回升到寻常的高度，我记得自己当时还想，他说话该压低些才是。“科罗尔把它装在她的首饰盒里带来的。首饰盒也还在那儿；想必她对您的——呃——盛情款待起过疑心。”
“您是说她竟把那盒镭装在首饰盒里随身带着！”我叫出声来。“还把这一整套东西都撂在那儿，撂在18号病房？”
“兴许她跟我的看法一致，觉得那是藏它最稳妥的地方。谁也不会疑心镭竟又回到了18号病房。谁也不会自愿走进那间屋子。哦，她还小心谨慎地绕到那口壁橱跟前，把首饰盒一直塞进架子最里头。她干这桩事的时候，您正替她清道，好让她一路穿过走廊去您的房间。她差一点儿就坐到床上等着了，”奥利里干巴巴地接着说，“幸亏您回来得凑巧，那会儿我正竭力把自己扮成一张床垫呢。”
“哦。”我妙语如珠地应了一声。“哦。”

===Original===
“It was the same closet that hid Dr. Letheny’s body,” added O’Leary meditatively. “I will leave the radium in Room 18 until tonight; it will be under close guard all day, Miss Keate, but I think it safer to wait till tonight, during second watch, when the guards are gone and the wing is quiet, to remove it. I’m not going to run the risk of Gainsay’s knocking me senseless again. Of course, we shall have to locate Corole and keep her out when she returns, as she will, for the radium. Then I’ll get the stuff away while the hospital is asleep.”
“Do you think that is wise?” I asked hesitantly. “Do you think that will be﻿—”
“Ready to go back to the hospital?” interrupted O’Leary, and as we walked along the clean white gravel path he conversed so fluently and determinedly about the effect of the continued moisture upon the crops that I could not get a word in edgewise. At the grade door we paused and O’Leary said a peculiar thing.
“See you later in the day, Miss Keate. Twelve of the twenty-four hours I gave myself are gone, you know. And by the way, you couldn’t have done better if you had rehearsed.” And with that he was gone, leaving me entirely in the dark as to his meaning and feeling rather irritated. Morgue, who had followed us along the path, brushed against my skirts. She had already lost her air of pride and was taking on a certain harassed appearance besides being very thin. But her yellow eyes raised to mine were still complacent and knowing and so like Corole’s that I thrust her impatiently aside with my foot and closed the door sharply.

===Chinese===
“藏过莱瑟尼医生尸体的，正是同一口壁橱。”奥利里若有所思地补上一句。“我要把镭留在18号病房（Room 18），留到今天夜里；它一整天都会受到严密看守，基特小姐。不过我想，还是等到今夜、第二班的时候——看守撤了、病区静了——再取走它更稳妥。盖恩赛把我砸得不省人事那一遭，我可不想再冒第二回。当然，我们得找到科罗尔，等她回来取镭——她准会回来的——把她挡在外头。然后，趁全院熟睡，我再把那玩意儿弄走。”
“您觉得这样妥当吗？”我迟疑地问。“您觉得那样子会——”
“准备回医院了吗？”奥利里打断了我。沿着那条洁净的白色砾石小径走着，他滔滔不绝、不容分说地大谈连绵的雨水对庄稼的影响，叫我一句嘴也插不上。在地坪门前我们停了步，奥利里说了句古怪的话。
“今天晚些时候再见，基特小姐。我给自己限定的二十四小时，您知道，已经过去了十二个。对了，就是事先排练过，您也未必能演得比方才更出色。”说完他就走了，撇下我对他的用意全然摸不着头脑，心里还相当着恼。一路跟着我们走小径的太平间，蹭了蹭我的裙子。她早先那股傲气已经丢尽，添了几分憔悴仓皇的模样，还瘦得厉害。可她抬眼望我的那双黄眼睛，依旧又自足又通透，与科罗尔的眼睛像得出奇，惹得我不耐烦地一脚把她拨开，砰地关上了门。

===Original===
The rain continued, steadily increasing in fervour as the dreary day passed. All morning I remained in my room, the door locked securely, a chair in front of the window lest Corole should take a notion to return the way she had gone, and myself trying to sleep and succeeding for the most part in staring at the ceiling or at the rain-smeared window.
At noon I rose, dashed ice water on my tired eyes, dressed and started downstairs. The dark day made the vast old place gloomier than ever and lights had had to be turned on all over the building which, however, failed to dispel the lurking shadows. Apparently the nurses were doing their duty as well as might be expected, though I noted that they gathered in groups and that there was a noticeable lack of smothered talk and laughter.
In the north wing of the second floor I caught a glimpse, as I rounded the stairs, of Dr. Hajek, clad in fresh, white duck trousers and coat and certainly not much resembling a thief and a murderer, making his morning rounds, and at the door of the maternity ward I met Dr. Balman, an attendant nurse at his elbow.
It was strange to see the everyday routine going on almost as usual, almost as if we were not held in the cold grip of horror. No, not quite as usual, for there was somehow about the place, emanating from the very, white and expectant walls, an air of suspense, of breathless waiting.

===Chinese===
雨下个不停，随着这阴郁的一天挨过去，雨势愈下愈猛。整个上午我都待在自己房里，门锁得严严实实，窗前抵着一把椅子，防的是科罗尔心血来潮、从她出走的那条道再摸回来；我呢，想睡上一觉，结果多半只是睁着眼望天花板，或是望那扇被雨水抹得一片模糊的窗子。
正午我起了床，往疲倦的眼睛上撩了些冰水，穿好衣裳下楼。天色晦暗，把这座庞大老旧的宅子衬得比哪天都阴沉；满楼的灯都只得亮起来，却仍旧驱不散那些潜伏的暗影。护士们看样子还算各尽其职，只是我留意到她们成群凑在一处，而且平日那种压着嗓子的谈笑，明显稀少了。
在二楼北翼，我刚绕下楼梯，便瞥见了海耶克医生：一身浆洗一新的白帆布裤和白外套，正在做早间查房，怎么瞧也不大像个小偷和凶手；在产科病房门口，我遇见了巴尔曼医生，一名随班护士跟在他肘边。
眼看日常事务几乎照旧运转，几乎仿佛我们并没有被攥在恐怖那冰冷的掌心里，这真是桩怪事。不，又不尽然如常，因为这地方不知怎的，从那一面面惨白而屏息以待的墙壁里，透出一种悬念的气氛、一种叫人屏息等候的气氛。

===Original===
Dr. Balman had noted it, too.
“Even the patients are upset and restless today,” he said wearily, as I stopped to ask him about Sonny, whose cast did not satisfy me. He rubbed his hand over his high, benevolent forehead, drew it gently over the bruise that still looked red and angry, and sighed.
“It is the weather,” I suggested, though it was nothing of the kind.
“Yes. Yes, it must be the weather. A constant succession of cloudy, rainy days such as we have been having is bad for the nerves. I hope this rain sees the end of it.” His anxious eyes went past me toward the window at the end of the corridor.

===Chinese===
巴尔曼医生也察觉了这一点。
“连病人今天都心烦意乱、坐卧不宁。”他倦倦地说。我停下来问起桑尼——他那副石膏我瞧着不大放心。他抬手抹过自己那高高的、一团忠厚的额头，又轻轻抚过那块仍旧红肿刺眼的瘀伤，叹了口气。
“是天气的缘故吧。”我提了一句，尽管根本不是那么回事。
“是啊。是啊，准是天气。像近来这样阴一阵雨一阵地连着下，最伤神经。但愿这场雨下过就算到头了。”他那双忧心忡忡的眼睛越过我，望向走廊尽头那扇窗。

===Original===
“One wonders where it is all coming from,” I commented. “I think, too, that the patients feel the﻿—er﻿—atmosphere of the hospital. The nurses are uneasy and nervous, jump at every sound, and there is a distinct feeling of suspense and﻿—breathlessness in the air.”
Dr. Balman nodded; his eyes looked tired and sad under his thin eyebrows.
“I understand what you mean. There is a psychic undercurrent of unrest and alarm that is bound to communicate itself to the sick.”
“You aren’t looking well, Dr. Balman,” I said. “You should have that bruise attended to.” And I thought, though I did not say it, that he would profit by some liver pills.
“I haven’t had time﻿—” he began; a nurse rattling up to us in her crisp skirts interrupted him with a question and I went on downstairs.

===Chinese===
“真不知道这一切都打哪儿来。”我议论道。“我还觉得，病人也感觉得到医院里的——呃——气氛。护士们又不安又紧张，一点动静就吓得一跳，空气里分明浮动着一种悬念的、还有一种——叫人透不过气的东西。”
巴尔曼医生点点头；他细眉底下那双眼睛，瞧着又疲倦又悲伤。
“我明白您的意思。有一股不安与惊恐的心理潜流，冥冥中流转，迟早要传到病人身上去。”
“您气色不大好，巴尔曼医生。”我说。“那块瘀伤也该治一治了。”我心里想——嘴上没说——他吃几帖肝药丸必有好处。
“我一直没腾出工夫——”他刚起了个头；一名护士挎着窸窣作响的挺括裙子快步过来，用一个问题打断了他，我便接着下楼去了。

===Original===
A letter was waiting for me on the rack in the hall. I did not recognize the handwriting, which was square and distinct and very painstaking; the signature, however, caught my attention and I ran through the note hastily, read it again more carefully, and with an involuntary glance about me I withdrew into a secluded corner of the hall and read it once more. It was short and to the point.

> Dear Miss:
> I think it is my dooty to tell you somthing I heerd. It is about Mr. Gansie I liked him but he is croked. He thinks Miss C whuz name I will not menshun has the radeyum, she said you know more than you will tell about those murders too and he said well what if I do what I want is the radeyum. Then she said youd better get out of here before you land in jail and he said speak for yourself. Then the kitchen door blew shut. You can tell that little man with the gray eyes if you want to.
> That Gansie is a bad man he has a revolver in his pocket.
> I have left Miss C for good.
> Yours respectfully,
 

 **Huldah Hansinge.**

Aside from reading “croked” to be “croaked” and thinking for a wild second that she was announcing Gainsay’s death, I had no difficulty in understanding Huldah’s amazing epistle. It sounded exactly like her, and Huldah is honest, so I did not even have the dubious satisfaction of doubting her word. It was my duty, too, to turn the thing over to O’Leary, and I should have done so at once had I been able to find him. But he was not to be found and I finally went down to lunch with a heavier heart.

===Chinese===
大厅的信架上，有一封信等着我取。那笔迹我不认得——方方正正、笔画分明，写得极费功夫；落款却一下子抓住了我的视线。我匆匆扫了一遍短信，又更仔细地读了一遍，然后不由自主地四下一瞥，退进大厅里一个僻静的角落，再读了一遍。信写得简短，一针见血。

> 亲爱的小姐：
> 我有责仟告诉您一件我听来的事。是关于甘西先生的。我从前是喜欢他的，可他学坏了。他以为C小姐——她的名字我不提——手里有镭，她说那几桩凶杀的事，您知道得比肯说的多，他说哼，我知道又怎样，我要的是镭。后来她说，你趁早离开这儿，省得去蹲大牢，他说，你还是先管管自己吧。后来厨房的门让风吹得哐当一声关上了。您愿意的话，可以把这些告诉那个灰眼睛的小个子。
> 甘西是个坏人，他兜里揣着一把左轮手枪。
> 我已经离开C小姐，再也不回去了。
> 敬上
 

 **赫尔达·汉辛格。**

除了有一霎把她写的『学坏了』（croked）恍惚看成『翘辫子了』（croaked）、还当她是来通报盖恩赛的死讯之外，赫尔达这封惊人的书信，我读来毫无滞碍。这口气一听就是她本人；而赫尔达为人诚实，所以我连怀疑她话真话假的那一点可疑的快意都捞不着。再说，把这东西转交给奥利里是我的本分，倘若找得到他，我本该立刻就交的。可他遍寻不见，我终究揣着一颗愈发沉重的心，下楼吃午饭去了。

===Original===
The afternoon passed as slowly as the morning. O’Leary stayed out of sight, I heard no news about Corole or the radium, and the note from Huldah was simply burning a hole in my pocket. I tried telephoning to O’Leary but could not even get an answer from his servant. It was while I was in the general office that someone telephoned for Dr. Hajek. Miss Jones was at the telephone and asked me to call him, saying he was in the south wing.
“It’s a woman,” she said, winking at me. “She wouldn’t give her name or number.”
I found Dr. Hajek in Room 17 changing a dressing. He dropped his forceps and pulled off his rubber gloves so hastily that they split across one palm.
“Pick up those forceps and sterilize them,” he directed the attendant nurse. “I’ll be back in a moment.”

===Chinese===
下午过得同上午一样慢。奥利里踪影全无，科罗尔和镭的消息我一条也没听着，赫尔达那封信简直在我口袋里烧出一个洞来。我想法子给奥利里打电话，可连他仆人的回话都讨不到一句。我在总办公室里的时候，有人打电话来找海耶克医生。琼斯小姐守着电话，请我去叫他，说他人在南翼。
“是个女的。”她朝我眨眨眼说。“她不肯报姓名，也不肯留号码。”
我在17号病房（Room 17）找到了海耶克医生，他正在换敷料。他失手掉了镊子，又急急匆匆扯下橡胶手套，扯得一只手掌上都裂开了。
“把镊子捡起来，消消毒。”他吩咐随班护士。“我一会儿就回来。”

===Original===
I suppose he noted the disapproval in my face, for as we left he murmured something about having expected an important call and Seventeen being all right until he returned. In the corridor, tipped back against the door of Eighteen, lounged a policeman. Dr. Hajek regarded him speculatively but said nothing concerning his presence, which was, to my mind, an extraordinarily stupid arrangement. It seemed far better, to me, to remove the radium under guard to a place of safekeeping, but O’Leary’s business was O’Leary’s.
It seemed a singular thing that this man Hajek was at liberty to go about the hospital, his opinions deferred to by the nurses, his duty to administer to the sick, and at the same time he was most certainly involved somehow in the ugly, sordid tragedy that had befallen us. I followed his white coat through the intervening corridors and, recalling a record I had meant to look into, also into the general office. But as I bent over the filing cabinet, though every word of his brief conversation was audible to me, I could make nothing of it. It consisted of three “Yes’s,” one “No,” and finished with “All right.” Upon which he hung up the receiver and departed briskly toward the south wing and Seventeen. Miss Jones was no wiser than I, for his eyes had been on her as he talked and she had not dared listen in.
“There’s one thing I know, Miss Keate,” she said as I was about to leave. “That voice at the other end sounded for all the world like Miss Letheny’s.”
And some twenty minutes later I was quite sure that I saw Dr. Hajek going unostentatiously out the grade door toward the garage, though when the bell rang for dinner he was sitting in the general office smoking a forbidden cigar and reading the evening papers with the utmost composure.

===Chinese===
想必他看出了我脸上的不以为然，因为出门的时候，他咕哝了句什么正等着一要紧电话、十七号在他回来之前不会有什么事。走廊里，一名警察斜倚在18号病房的门上，懒洋洋地站着。海耶克医生打量了他一眼，一脸琢磨，却对他守在那儿不置一词——依我看，这部署蠢得出奇。要依我说，把镭在严守之下挪到什么保险的地方去，不知要好上多少；可奥利里的事，毕竟是奥利里的。
说来也怪，这个姓海耶克的人竟可以满医院自由来去，护士们事事听他的，由他尽责照料病人，而与此同时，他几乎肯定以某种方式搅在我们遭上的这桩丑恶、肮脏的惨剧里。我跟在他那件白大褂后头穿过一道道走廊，想起有一份档案我原打算查一查，便也跟进了总办公室。可是，当我俯身在档案柜上时，他那通简短谈话虽然一字一句都送进我耳朵，我却什么名堂也听不出来。统共三个“是”，一个“不”，末了一句“好的”。说完他便挂上听筒，脚步轻快地朝南翼、朝17号病房去了。琼斯小姐也不比我多知道什么，因为他通话时眼睛一直盯着她，她没敢偷听。
“有一件事我知道，基特小姐，”我正要走，她说。“电话那头的声音，听上去千真万确就是莱瑟尼小姐的。”
大约二十分钟后，我相当有把握地看见海耶克医生不事声张地出了地坪门，朝车库去了；可晚饭铃响的时候，他却坐在总办公室里，抽着一支犯禁的雪茄，读着晚报，从容到了极点。

===Original===
I spent most of the intervening time wandering about the halls; I was very restless and could not settle down to anything, and altogether the afternoon was a total loss so far as anything interesting was concerned, so I was not in the best of humours at dinner.
Once I caught a fragment of conversation from a little group of nurses down at the end of the table.
“… and I said, ‘What on earth is that man doing out in the elderberry bushes in all this rain?’ and she said, ‘He is watching Room 18.’ ”
“Why are they watching Room 18?” asked Miss Ferguson, wide-eyed.

===Chinese===
中间那段时光，我大半都在各个大厅里游荡；我心神不定，什么事也安不下心去做，单论有没有什么有趣的事，整个下午算是彻底泡汤，于是吃晚饭时我心绪好不到哪儿去。
有一回，我捕捉到桌子尽头一小圈护士谈话的碎片。
“……我就问：‘这么大的雨，那人蹲在外头接骨木丛里到底干什么？’她说：‘他在看守18号病房。’”
“他们干吗看守18号病房（Room 18）？”弗格森小姐睁圆了眼睛问。

===Original===
“Don’t ask me!” The first girl shrugged her shoulders. “But there have been a couple of men, besides that policeman in the south wing, hanging around all day; I don’t think they are police because they don’t wear uniforms, but they didn’t have their eyes off the windows of Room 18 all day long.”
“What do you suppose is the reason?” whispered someone in a tense, shrill whisper that carried.
“I don’t know!”
“Mercy, I’m glad I’m not on duty in the south wing,” said someone else, and all the eyes at the table immediately focused on me.
“Well, whatever it is, I wish it would be settled,” announced Miss Ferguson vigorously. “I’m getting so nervous I drop everything I touch. And my neck is stiff from twisting it to look back over my shoulder.”

===Chinese===
“别问我！”头一个说话的姑娘耸了耸肩。“不过除了南翼那个警察，还有两个人转悠了一整天；我看他们不是警察，因为不着制服，可一天到晚，他们眼睛就没离开过18号病房（Room 18）那几扇窗。”
“你们说，这是图什么？”有人耳语道，那声音又紧又尖，全桌都听得真真的。
“我哪儿知道！”
“天哪，幸亏我不在南翼当班。”另外有人说，桌上所有的眼睛立刻齐刷刷落到了我身上。
“唉，不管那是怎么回事，我只盼着早点有个了结。”弗格森小姐斩钉截铁地宣布。“我都紧张得不行了，碰什么掉什么。脖子也扭僵了——老得回过头去瞧背后。”

===Original===
Melvina Smith cleared her throat and I left the table at once. I have nothing against Melvina, but if she had been in the south wing during the past week she would have got her fill of horrors.
With the gathering darkness the feeling of impending catastrophe that had hung over us all day intensified itself. By midnight I was as jumpy as a race horse, my heart leaping to my throat at every sound and my hands shaking so that I could scarcely turn off my alarm clock and adjust my cap.
The storm had grown steadily worse and by twelve o’clock was blowing a gale with thunder and lightning making the night hideous. The old building seemed to tremble at each onslaught, and every window casing rattled and every curtain flapped and the whole place seemed to quiver and shudder as if it were alive.
On the way down to the south wing, I don’t mind saying that I suffered from something very near to stage fright, at least there was a rock in the pit of my stomach and the backs of my knees felt shaky and not to be depended upon. I very nearly shrieked when I heard footsteps back of me on the stairs, but it was only Maida, going down to duty, and together we walked through those deserted, creaking halls.

===Chinese===
梅尔维娜·史密斯清了清嗓子，我便立刻离了席。我对梅尔维娜并无成见，可要是过去这一星期她待的是南翼，恐怖这东西她早该尝个够了。
随着夜色四合，笼罩了我们一整天的那种大祸临头之感愈演愈烈。到午夜时分，我已紧张得像匹赛马，每有一点声响，心就跳到嗓子眼，两只手抖得差点关不掉闹钟、戴不正护士帽。
暴风雨越闹越凶，到十二点钟已经刮起了狂风，雷鸣电闪把黑夜搅得狰狞可怖。老楼仿佛在风暴的每一次冲击下发颤，每一扇窗框咔咔作响，每一幅帘幔噼啪乱摆，整座宅子仿佛都在颤栗、抽搐，像活了一样。
去南翼的一路，我不妨承认，我害的简直就是怯场病：至少胃底坠着一块石头，膝弯发软、靠也靠不住。听见背后楼梯上有脚步声，我差点叫出声来，结果只是梅达，下楼去当班的。我们便一道穿过那些空无一人、吱呀作响的门厅。

===Original===
I had not been on duty more than twenty minutes when I found a note pinned to the order blank and addressed “Miss Keate”! It was sealed, and across the paper was a single sentence splashed hurriedly:

> When the red light shines above 18 answer it.

I wheeled to stare down the length of corridor toward that closed, inscrutable door at its far end. The corridor lost itself in the shadows and the door was itself indistinguishable, but it seemed to me that the faraway panes of glass in the south door caught green glints of light from the shade above my head.
“When the red light shines above 18 answer it.”
What was going on in the dark room? What did it mean?

===Chinese===
我当班还不到二十分钟，就在医嘱单上发现一封用别针别着的信，封皮上写着“基特小姐”！信是封了口的，横贯信纸只潦草涂着一句话：

> 18号病房上方的红灯亮起时，去应答。

我霍然转身，顺着长廊朝尽头那扇关着的、深不可测的门望去。走廊没入暗影里，那扇门本身也已分辨不清；可我恍惚觉得，远处南门的玻璃格上，正闪烁着我头顶灯罩透下的一线绿光。
“18号病房（Room 18）上方的红灯亮起时，去应答。”
黑屋子里正在发生什么？这话是什么意思？

===Original===
It was fortunate that I had plenty of assistance, for I could not possibly have gone about my duty with this amazing thing in my mind. In fact, I paid very little attention to the demands of the wing and alternated my gaze between my wrist watch and that shadowy end of the south wing corridor.
When the red light shines above 18!
When would it shine﻿—what would I see upon opening that heavy gumwood door?
When the red light shines﻿ ﻿… After what seemed eons of time I strolled casually and with attempted calm in that direction. My heart began to pound violently as I approached that mysterious door. I paused at the south end of the corridor, pretending to scrutinize a thermometer that hung on the wall and listening with all my ears toward that dully gleaming panel of gumwood. Not a sound came from it, and though I lingered for some time in the vicinity, still I heard nothing.

===Chinese===
幸亏我手下人手充裕，不然脑子里悬着这么一件骇人的事，我根本没法当班办事。事实上，病区里的种种传唤我几乎没放在心上，只把目光在腕表和南翼走廊那片黑影幢幢的尽头之间来回逡巡。
18号病房（Room 18）上方的红灯亮起时——！
它几时会亮——推开那扇厚重的桉木门，我将会看到什么？
红灯亮起时……挨过仿佛万世长的一段时光，我装出偶然路过、力求镇定的样子，朝那边踱去。走近那扇神秘的门时，我的心开始狂跳起来。我在走廊南端停下脚步，假装端详挂在墙上的一支体温计，耳朵却全数竖着，听那扇幽幽泛光的桉木门板。里面没有一丝声响；我在那左近逗留了好一阵，仍旧什么也没听见。

===Original===
On the way back Olma Flynn stopped me.
“Eleven says he will not take his medicine, Miss Keate. What shall I do?”
I must have answered her rather vaguely and, in fact, barely heard her question. At any rate, she gave me a strange look, whirled to follow my gaze down the corridor south and, seeing nothing, faced me again.
Her eyes were very wide and her mouth hung open.

===Chinese===
往回走时，奥尔玛·弗琳拦住了我。
“十一号说他不肯吃药，基特小姐。我该怎么办？”
我想必答得含含糊糊——实际上，她的话我几乎没听进耳朵。总之，她怪模怪样地看了我一眼，猛地转身顺着我的视线朝走廊南头望去，什么也没瞧见，又重新转回来对着我。
她眼睛瞪得溜圆，嘴也张着合不拢。

===Original===
“What﻿—what did you say, Miss Keate?”
“I’ll see about it in the morning,” I replied, quite at random. She retreated, eyeing me with trepidation, and later I saw her whispering with the student nurse in the drug room and both of them regarding me distrustfully.
Somehow the seconds dragged along. I took up my post at the chart desk, turning the chair so that it faced the long length of empty, dark corridor, and the dark space above Eighteen was visible to me.

===Chinese===
“您——您刚才说什么来着，基特小姐？”
“明早再说吧。”我随口答道。她退了下去，提心吊胆地觑着我；后来我看见她在药品间里跟那个学生护士咬耳朵，两个人都满腹狐疑地瞅着我。
秒针不知怎么走得这般磨蹭。我在病历台前就了位，把椅子转过去正对着那条长长的、空荡荡的黑走廊，好教18号病房上方那一方暗处始终在我视野之内。

===Original===
Maida stopped at the desk now and then, and once paused to survey me curiously.
“What on earth is the matter with you, Sarah?” she asked.
“Nothing,” I replied, looking for the thousandth time at my watch. It was then a quarter of two.
She studied me oddly for a moment.
“What a night! The wind and rain is getting awfully on my nerves.” She unpinned her thermometer, took off the cap and held it closer to her eyes. “I was taking a temperature a moment ago when that loud crack of thunder came and it startled me so that I dropped the thermometer. I don’t think”﻿—she paused to squint interestedly along the small glass tube﻿—“I don’t think I broke it. For heaven’s sake, Sarah!” she broke off in sudden irritation. “Stop staring down the corridor. You make me edgy. What are you looking for? What do you﻿—”

===Chinese===
梅达时不时到病历台前停一停，有一回还站住脚，好奇地打量我。
“你到底怎么了，萨拉？”她问。
“没什么。”我答道，第一千次看表。那会儿是差一刻两点。
她古怪地端详了我片刻。
“什么鬼天气！这风声雨声简直要把我的神经磨断了。”她取下别着的体温计，摘掉帽子，把它举到眼前细看。“方才我正量体温，那声响雷炸开来，吓得我把体温计都掉了。我看”——她停下来，饶有兴味地眯起眼，顺着那支细玻璃管望——“我看是没摔坏。看在老天份上，萨拉！”她突然烦躁地打住。“别再盯着走廊那头瞧了。你弄得我心里发毛。你到底在找什么？你在——”

===Original===
I did not hear the rest of the sentence. I sprang to my feet, peering through the semidarkness to be sure my eyes had not mistaken me.
They had not!
Gleaming above the door of Eighteen was a single, small red light!

===Chinese===
后半句我没听见。我一跃而起，眯眼望进半明半暗里，想确认不是自己看走了眼。
没有看错！
18号病房的门上方，亮着一盏孤零零的小红灯！

## XVII O’Leary Tells a Story / 奥利里讲了一个故事

===Original===
The next thing I remember is finding myself at the door of Room 18, my fingers on the door knob, my breath coming in gasps and my heart literally in my throat.

What would the opening of that door disclose?

I took a long, shuddering breath, pushed open the door and took a few steps forward.

Intense blackness met my eyes, but through it I heard scraping sounds and heavy breathing and the impact of flesh against flesh, and the indescribable sounds of two bodies struggling together. Instinctively I stepped inside the room, closed the door behind me, and felt along the wall for the electric button.

And at that instant a vivid flash of lightning lit up the room and I caught a glimpse of two men interlocked and swaying and I heard O’Leary’s hoarse whisper.

“Don’t—turn on—the lights! Don’t—” the rest was lost.

===Chinese===
接下来我记得的，是自己已经站在18号病房（Room 18）的门口，手指搭在门把手上，喘得气都不匀，一颗心简直提到了嗓子眼。

推开这扇门，会看见什么？

我长长地、发着颤地吸了一口气，推开门，向前走了几步。

扑面而来的是一片浓黑，但在黑暗里，我听见刮擦的声响、粗重的喘息、肉体撞上肉体的闷响，还有两个身体扭作一团时那种难以形容的动静。我本能地跨进病房，随手掩上身后的门，沿着墙壁摸索电灯按钮。

就在那一瞬间，一道刺目的闪电照亮了病房，我瞥见两个男人绞缠在一起、摇摇晃晃，又听见奥利里嘶哑的低语。

“别——开——灯！别——”后半句听不见了。

===Original===
I stood there as if frozen to the spot, longing to take a hand in things and not daring to do so. Then all at once someone said breathlessly:

“O’Leary!”

“Yes.”

“Hell.”

The men seemed to fall apart.

“All right, then! Here it is!” The words were whispered in a panting voice that I did not recognize.

===Chinese===
我站在那里，像被钉在了原地，一心想插上一手，却又不敢。忽然，有人气喘吁吁地说：

“奥利里！”

“是我。”

“见鬼。”

那两个人似乎分开了。

“那好，就这么办！就在这儿！”这几句话是用一种喘着气、我听不出来的嗓音低声说的。

===Original===
Then I felt rather than saw that the slighter of the two figures tiptoed to the window next to the bed, peered through the dashing of rain outside for a moment, and then tiptoed as cautiously back.

“Into that corner! There, back of the screen! Miss Keate?”

“Yes.”

“Over here, quick!”

I stumbled a little as I passed the foot of the bed, found a hand outstretched in the darkness to guide me, and in a flash was in the darkest corner of the room, behind the burlap screen.

“Be quiet!” warned O’Leary sternly.

===Chinese===
随后，与其说是看见，不如说是感觉到：两个身影中较瘦削的那个踮着脚走到床边的窗口，隔着外面泼洒的雨张望了片刻，又小心翼翼地踮着脚退了回来。

“进那个角落去！那儿，屏风后面！基特小姐？”

“在。”

“到这边来，快！”

经过床脚时我打了个趔趄，黑暗中碰到一只伸过来引路的手，转眼间已站在病房里最暗的角落、粗麻布屏风的后面。

“别出声！”奥利里严厉地警告。

===Original===
Beside me, breathing quickly, was that other man; as I shrank back a little I came in contact with something cold, touched it tentatively with my fingers and drew back, chilled. It was square and hard and pressing into the coat of the man at my side. It must be held in O’Leary’s hand.

And I was standing within an inch of the thing. I must have made a sudden movement for O’Leary whispered sharply again: “*Hush!*”

As if petrified, the three of us stood behind that burlap screen. There was not a sound in the room. As my eyes became adjusted to the darkness I found that the window near the bed was faintly visible through the crack in the screen and I glued my gaze to that crack.

Once the man at my side stirred a little and then quieted abruptly, and I had no doubt that that menacing revolver was thrust closer into his ribs.

===Chinese===
我身边就是那另一个男人，呼吸急促；我稍稍向后缩了缩，碰到一件冰凉的东西，指尖试探着碰了碰，寒意沁骨，又缩了回来。那东西四四方方、坚硬，正抵着我身旁那人的外衣。它准是握在奥利里手里。

而我站的地方，离那东西不过一寸。我一定是突然动了一下，因为奥利里又厉声低喝：“*嘘！*”

我们三个像石化了一般，站在那道粗麻布屏风后面。病房里没有一丝声息。待眼睛渐渐适应了黑暗，我发现透过屏风的缝隙，能隐约望见床边那扇窗，便把目光死死粘在那道缝上。

有一回，我身旁那人稍稍动了动，随即猛地安静下来，我毫不怀疑，那把气势汹汹的左轮手枪又往他肋下顶得更紧了。

===Original===
Just as I felt that my lungs were bursting I became aware that there was a shadow, deeper than the surrounding shadows, there at the window. I blinked and peered closer. Yes, I was sure. Silently, with amazing lack of sound it crept from the window sill into the room, paused for a second and then, so silently that it did not seem to be anything human, it glided across the room and out of my little angle of vision.
