Then I was aware that O’Leary was gone and simultaneously I heard a sound like the creaking of a bed spring and O’Leary’s voice, cold and hard as that vicious revolver.

“Stand where you are! Hands up! Turn on the light, Miss Keate. Hands up! I’ve got you!”

===Chinese===
正当我觉出肺都要憋裂了，我察觉窗口那里有一团影子，比四周的黑暗更深。我眨眨眼，凑近了细看。没错，我敢肯定。它无声无息、静得出奇，从窗台爬进病房，停了一秒，随即又那样悄没声息——静得不像是个活人——掠过病房，滑出了我那一小角视野。

接着我发觉奥利里不在了，与此同时，我听见一声像是床簧受压的吱呀，随后是奥利里的声音，冰冷坚硬，一如那把凶悍的左轮手枪。

“站住别动！举起手来！开灯，基特小姐。举起手来！你们跑不掉了！”

===Original===
Turn on the light!

Cross that room to the door? No, here was the light above the bed! Where was the cord! Ah! My fingers grasped it, pulled convulsively and light flooded the room.

There was a muffled exclamation from the closet door. A man standing there flung his hands over his head. O’Leary was standing on the high, narrow bed, his revolver covering the room. The man behind the screen was still motionless.

“All right, O’Brien,” said O’Leary very quietly, without moving his head.

“All right,” echoed a voice at the window. There was O’Brien’s head at the window and along the sill gleamed the barrel of another revolver, and then another as a stalwart policeman loomed up beside O’Brien.

My head cleared and my eyes stopped blinking in the sudden light.

===Chinese===
开灯！

要横穿病房去门口吗？不，床上方就有灯！灯绳在哪儿！啊！手指抓住了，猛地一拉，灯光涌满了整间病房。

壁橱门那边传来一声压低的惊呼。站在那儿的一个男人猛地把双手甩过头顶。奥利里站在那张又高又窄的床上，左轮手枪罩住整间病房。屏风后面那人仍旧一动不动。

“好了，奥布赖恩。”奥利里头也没动，十分平静地说。

“好了。”窗口有个声音应道。奥布赖恩的脑袋正探在窗口，窗台上亮着另一支左轮手枪的枪管，跟着又多出一支——一名魁梧的警察在奥布赖恩身旁现出了身形。

我的脑子清醒过来，在骤然的亮光里，眼睛也不再直眨。

===Original===
The man at the closet door was Dr. Fred Hajek. His face was putty-coloured. His small eyes gleamed like a frightened animal’s. His raincoat dripped moisture in a little puddle on the floor.

“Got him covered, O’Brien?” said O’Leary cheerfully.

“Right!” said O’Brien.

O’Leary leaped lightly from the bed, strode over to the burlap screen, and pulled it back.

Jim Gainsay stood there, his cap pulled low over his eyes, his lean jaw set. One hand was thrust into the pocket of his coat, and the other grasped a small, square box. At the sight of the box I gasped something and pointed.

“The—the radium!”

===Chinese===
站在壁橱门边的男人，是弗雷德·海耶克医生。他的脸是油灰般的颜色，一双小眼睛闪着受惊野兽似的光。他的雨衣不住地淌水，在地板上积成一小汪。

“盯住他了，奥布赖恩？”奥利里轻快地问。

“盯住了！”奥布赖恩说。

奥利里轻捷地从床上跳下来，大步走到粗麻布屏风前，一把拉开。

站在那里的竟是吉姆·盖恩赛，帽檐压得很低遮着眼睛，瘦削的下颌绷得紧紧的。一只手插在外衣口袋里，另一只手攥着一只小小的方盒。一看见那盒子，我倒抽一口气，指着它，含混地喊了一声。

“那——那是镭！”

===Original===
“It’s you, is it?” said O’Leary in a strange voice.

Hajek made a sudden movement; O’Leary whirled.

“Stop that!” his voice cracked like a whip. Hajek, with a furious glance at the men in the window, subsided.

O’Leary turned again, walked to the middle of the room and paused, looking from one man to the other with a curious expression in his eyes.

“Well,” he said. “I’ve got you both.”

Gainsay started to speak and stopped as the nose of one of the revolvers shifted restlessly.

===Chinese===
“原来是你，是吧？”奥利里用一种怪异的嗓音说。

海耶克猛地一动；奥利里霍地转过身去。

“不许动！”他的声音如皮鞭般炸响。海耶克朝窗口那几个人狠狠瞪了一眼，蔫了下去。

奥利里又转回身，走到病房中央停住，眼睛里带着一种说不清的神情，看看这个男人，又看看那个。

“好了，”他说。“你们两个，都在我手里了。”

盖恩赛正要开口，一支左轮手枪的枪口不安地晃动了一下，他便住了口。

===Original===
“Put down your hands if you want to, Hajek,” said O’Leary easily. “Or—wait a moment.”

He crossed to him, ran his hands quickly over Hajek’s pockets, unheeding the fury in those little eyes, extracted a small revolver and tossed it on the bed and smiled.

“There you are, Doctor,” he said politely. “You may lower your hands, now.”

There was a slight commotion at the window.

“Here’s somebody, Mr. O’Leary,” said someone. “He was in the shrubbery and you said not to let anybody get away.”

O’Leary peered into the little group at the window, then his eyes lightened.

===Chinese===
“把手放下来也行，海耶克，要是你愿意的话。”奥利里随口说。“要不——且慢。”

他走到海耶克跟前，迅速拍遍他全身的口袋，全不理会那双小眼睛里的怒火，摸出一支小号左轮手枪，往床上一抛，笑了。

“这就对了，医生，”他客客气气地说。“现在，您可以把手放下来了。”

窗口起了一阵轻微的骚动。

“这儿有个人，奥利里先生，”有人说。“他刚才躲在灌木丛里，您说过不能放走任何人。”

奥利里朝窗口那一小群人细细一看，眼睛随即一亮。

===Original===
“Oh, it’s you, Dr. Balman. You came at just the right time. I think we have bagged our birds. Can you come through the window, Doctor?”

It was Dr. Balman, sure enough, water running off his shoulders and shining in the light as he crawled through the window assisted by the policeman.

Once inside the room Dr. Balman looked slowly about him.

“What is this? What have you found, O’Leary?” His puzzled gaze found the box in Gainsay’s hand. He started. “Why—why is that the radium?”

“It may interest you to know, Dr. Balman, that we have caught the murderer and thief.”

“What!” cried Dr. Balman. His eyes travelled slowly around the room and his voice broke a little as he cried: “Not—not Fred Hajek?”

===Chinese===
“噢，是您哪，巴尔曼医生。您来得正是时候。我想咱们的鸟儿都落网了。您能从窗户进来吗，医生？”

果然是巴尔曼医生，肩头的水直往下淌，在灯光里发亮；他在那警察的帮衬下，从窗口爬了进来。

一进病房，巴尔曼医生便缓缓地环顾四周。

“这是怎么回事？你们查出了什么，奥利里？”他困惑的目光落到了盖恩赛手里的盒子上。他一怔。“咦——那难道就是镭？”

“说出来您也许有兴趣，巴尔曼医生：凶手和窃贼，我们都已经抓到了。”

“什么！”巴尔曼医生喊道。他的目光缓缓扫过整间病房，喊出声时嗓音都有些发破：“不——不会是弗雷德·海耶克吧？”

===Original===
O’Leary’s keenly exultant eyes softened a little.

“Wait,” he said. “There is another in the room.”

Taking a key from his pocket, he crossed lightly to the closed door of the further closet, unlocked it and swung it open. I took a step forward and cried out involuntarily. Instantly I recognized my own purple hat, sodden and drenched, and then, cramped in that small space, a woman’s huddled figure. It was Corole!

As we stared she glared back at us for a moment. Then she rose slowly, struggling with cramped muscles. Her eyes, narrow with hate, were fixed on Lance O’Leary.

“I’ve been there for hours,” she said in a strange voice that was hoarse and strained with fury. She stamped her feet to start circulation and flexed her arms slowly. Then she pulled my hat from her head, tossed it contemptuously out of the way and ran her brown hands through her tossed, yellow hair. “You are going to suffer for this,” she said. “How dare you force me into that closet, lock the door and leave me!” She took a tigerish step or two toward O’Leary, her nails gleaming suggestively.

“Not so fast, my lady,” interposed O’Brien, who had slipped silently through the window. Corole shifted her malignant gaze, regarded O’Brien for a moment, then slowly and malevolently swept the room.

===Chinese===
奥利里那双锋芒毕露、大获全胜的眼睛柔和了几分。

“等等，”他说。“屋里还有一位。”

他从口袋里掏出一把钥匙，轻步走向另一头那口壁橱关着的橱门，开了锁，把门拉开。我抢上一步，不由自主地叫出声来。我当即认出了自己那顶湿得透透的紫色帽子，接着，蜷在那方寸之地里的，是一个缩成一团的女人身影。是科罗尔！

我们盯着她，她也回瞪了我们片刻。然后她缓缓站起身，同僵麻的筋肉抗争着。她那双因仇恨而眯细的眼睛，死死盯住兰斯·奥利里。

“我在那里头待了几个钟头了。”她说，嗓音怪异，被狂怒憋得又嘶又紧。她跺了跺脚活活血，又慢慢屈伸着双臂。然后她从头上扯下我的帽子，不屑地一甩丢开，把两只棕色的手插进自己蓬乱的黄头发里。“这笔账，你们是要偿的，”她说。“你们怎么敢把我硬塞进那口壁橱，锁上门，就把我撂在里头！”她像一头母虎似的朝奥利里逼进一两步，指甲闪着意味深长的光。

“别急着来，我的太太，”奥布赖恩插话道，他已悄然从窗口翻了进来。科罗尔挪开那恶毒的目光，打量了奥布赖恩片刻，又缓缓地、满怀恶意地扫视全屋。

===Original===
“So you are here, too?” she said to me. “And Dr. Balman. And Jim. Quite a family party.”

“You are right,” agreed O’Leary smoothly. “Quite a family party. In fact, we need only one more to make our circle complete. Miss Keate, will you please summon Miss Day?”

My heart leaped again as I heard the name, and I heard Jim Gainsay mutter something that was quickly silenced. I opened the door and slid into the corridor; there was no need to call Maida, for there she was, standing opposite the dark door above which still gleamed that ominous red light. She was very white but said nothing as I beckoned her inside the room.

At our entrance O’Leary became active. He motioned to the available chairs.

“Sit down, Miss Day—Miss Keate. Dr. Balman, there is a place on the bed. We may as well make ourselves comfortable for I have a story to tell.”

I suppose my eyes went in some anxiety to the precious box in Jim Gainsay’s hand that was the cause of it all, for O’Leary smiled a bit grimly.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你也在这儿？”她对我说。“还有巴尔曼医生。还有吉姆。好一场阖家团聚。”

“您说得对，”奥利里圆滑地附和道。“好一场阖家团聚。其实，只消再添一位，我们这个圈子就圆满了。基特小姐，烦请您去请戴小姐来，好吗？”

听到这个名字，我的心又是一跳，我还听见吉姆·盖恩赛咕哝了句什么，很快就被止住了声。我打开门，溜进走廊；根本用不着去叫梅达，她就在那儿，站在那扇黑黢黢的病房门对面，门上方那盏不祥的红灯依然亮着。她脸色煞白，但在我招手请她进病房时一言不发。

我们一进来，奥利里便张罗起来。他朝空着的椅子指了指。

“请坐，戴小姐——基特小姐。巴尔曼医生，床上有个位置。我们不妨坐得舒服些，因为我有个故事要讲。”

想必是我的目光带着几分不安，落到了吉姆·盖恩赛手里那只招致这一切的宝贝盒子上，因为奥利里略带冷意地笑了笑。

===Original===
“Don’t be alarmed, Miss Keate. The radium is not in that box; I took it immediately to—a safe place. The box over there was only a bait.”

With a disgusted exclamation Jim Gainsay dropped the box and folded his arms. His eyes sought Maida’s but she did not return his gaze.

“Well, Dr. Hajek,” said O’Leary. “It is too bad it has turned out this way. I thought better of you.”

Dr. Hajek lifted his lip in something very like a snarl but said nothing. Corole made a sudden movement which she checked under O’Leary’s regard.

“Are you sure it was Dr. Hajek? Tell me about it, O’Leary.” The ring of authority was manifest in Dr. Balman’s weary tones.

===Chinese===
“不必惊慌，基特小姐。镭不在那只盒子里；我早就把它送到——一个安全的地方去了。那边那只盒子，不过是个诱饵。”

吉姆·盖恩赛厌恶地哼了一声，扔下盒子，抱起双臂。他的眼睛去寻梅达的眼睛，她却没有回望。

“唉，海耶克医生，”奥利里说。“事情落到这个地步，真是糟透了。我原把你看得更好些。”

海耶克医生掀起嘴唇，那样子活像一声咆哮，但什么也没说。科罗尔猛地一动，又在奥利里的注视下收住了。

“您能肯定是他？是海耶克医生吗？讲给我听听，奥利里。”巴尔曼医生疲惫的语调里，分明带着权威的口吻。

===Original===
“In my own way,” promised O’Leary with an apologetic glance toward Dr. Balman. “In the first place, the superstition which so unpleasantly impressed you, Miss Keate, has been fulfilled again.” He paused dramatically, and from somewhere in the room came a sharp sigh of suspense. “The murderer of Jackson *was* near by when you saw blood flowing from that small wound. But he was in—that closet.” He pointed. The silence breathed a question that none of us dared speak.

“Yes,” said O’Leary, answering the unspoken inquiry. “Yes. It was Dr. Letheny.”

“Dr. Letheny!” cried Jim Gainsay.

“Not—not Dr. Letheny,” faltered Dr. Balman.

===Chinese===
“按我自己的讲法。”奥利里朝巴尔曼医生投去一个抱歉的眼神，如此应承。“首先，基特小姐，那个让您听着极不舒服的迷信，又一次应验了。”他戏剧性地停了停，病房里不知哪儿响起一声悬着心的短叹。“您看见血从那小小的伤口里流出来的时候，杀死杰克逊先生的人，*就*在近旁。只不过他当时在——那口壁橱里。”他指了指。沉默吐出了一个我们谁也不敢说出口的问题。

“是的，”奥利里回答那没人问出口的疑问。“是的。是莱瑟尼医生。”

“莱瑟尼医生！”吉姆·盖恩赛叫道。

“不——不会是莱瑟尼医生。”巴尔曼医生讷讷地说。

===Original===
“It was Dr. Letheny,” repeated O’Leary quietly.

“I knew it!” cried Corole. “I knew it!”

No one looked at her. Our eyes were without exception fastened upon O’Leary’s face.

===Chinese===
“就是莱瑟尼医生。”奥利里平静地重复道。

“我就知道！”科罗尔喊道。“我早知道！”

没有人看她。我们的目光无一例外，全都钉在奥利里的脸上。

===Original===
“How do you know?” I said at last.

O’Leary glanced about the room in indecision, then he shrugged.

“As well here as anywhere,” he said. “How did I know that it was Letheny? Why did not Higgins rouse the place? Because he saw the head doctor in this room. Why was there need to hunt for the radium? Because that man who hid the stuff was dead; Dr. Letheny, disturbed about the ugly business, afraid of being caught with it in his possession, hid the thing in the loud speaker, thinking no one saw him. And only Higgins knew where it was, and Higgins, terrified at what he had seen, was afraid to tell for he knew that someone—*someone* had come upon Letheny and killed him and Higgins hoped to escape the same fate. And since there were—others desiring the radium, a hunt was made for it. A search that was finally successful.” His clear gray eyes went from Corole to Hajek.

===Chinese===
“您是怎么知道的？”我终于开口。

奥利里迟疑不决地朝病房四下里看了看，然后耸了耸肩。

“在哪儿讲都一样。”他说。“我怎么知道是莱瑟尼？希金斯为什么没有惊动全院？因为他看见主治医生进了这间病房。为什么还要搜寻那镭？因为藏镭的人已经死了；莱瑟尼医生为这桩丑事心神不宁，又怕东西在身上、落个人赃俱获，便把那东西藏进了扬声器，以为没人瞧见。而只有希金斯知道它在哪儿；希金斯被自己亲眼所见吓坏了，不敢声张，因为他知道有个人——有*个人*撞见了莱瑟尼、杀了他，而希金斯指望逃过同样的下场。再说，既然还有——别的人也觊觎那镭，搜寻便展开了。一场终于大功告成的搜寻。”他那双清澈的灰眼睛，从科罗尔身上移到海耶克身上。

===Original===
“But just as Dr. Letheny was about to leave the room another man came upon the scene, determined to take the radium for himself. Then—I don’t know exactly what happened but the two men struggled and in the struggle Dr. Letheny’s head struck with such force that it killed him—*this*”—he crossed the room to the massive, square-cornered lavatory. “I am sure of that,” went on O’Leary, “for I examined it before a thing in the room had been touched. The other man, frightened perhaps, knowing that he was in desperate danger of being charged with murder, dragged Dr. Letheny’s body into that closet, locked the door and got rid of the key, hoping to postpone the discovery of Dr. Letheny’s death for as long as possible and thus cover his own tracks. But first he found that the radium was not to be found and knew that Letheny must have hidden it somewhere in the room. He did not dare search for it then, he would have to return. He retreated by the way he had come, through the window, there, and—and crawled through the window of his own room in the hospital in time to answer Miss Keate who, by that time, was pounding on the door.”

His eyes went to Dr. Hajek, whose face was quite ghastly.

O’Leary forestalled the words on Dr. Hajek’s lips.

“Not now,” he said sternly. “You will have plenty of time to talk—later.”

===Chinese===
“可就在莱瑟尼医生要离开病房的时候，另一个男人上了场，打定主意要把镭据为己有。接着——具体发生了什么我说不准，但两个男人扭打起来，而在扭打中，莱瑟尼医生的头撞了那么狠的一下，竟至丧命——撞的是*这个*”——他穿过病房，走到那座敦实方正、棱角坚硬的盥洗池跟前。“这一点我有把握，”奥利里接着说，“因为屋里一件东西都还没人动过的时候，我就先查验过它了。另一个人，也许是吓坏了，自知大有被控谋杀的危险，便把莱瑟尼医生的尸体拖进那口壁橱，锁上门，又处理掉钥匙，指望尽量推迟莱瑟尼医生之死被发现的时候，好借此掩盖自己的行踪。但在那之前，他先发现镭不见了，便知道莱瑟尼准是把它藏在了屋里什么地方。他当时不敢搜，还得再来一趟。他顺着来路退了出去，就是从那扇窗，然后——然后爬过医院里他自己房间的窗户，正好来得及应基特小姐——到那时候，她已经在擂他的房门了。”

他的目光落到海耶克医生身上，那张脸已经惨白得不像样。

奥利里抢在海耶克医生开口之前。

“现在不必，”他严厉地说。“往后——有的是时间让你说。”

===Original===
“Then—then you feel sure it was Dr. Letheny who killed Jackson?” asked Dr. Balman incredulously.

“Positive,” said O’Leary. “As further proof, the revolver that belongs to Miss Letheny bears Dr. Letheny’s finger prints. Why should he bring a revolver to a hospital if his errand was entirely peaceful? He wanted the radium, he needed the money—I honestly believe that the man wanted the money for research.” There was a shade of pity in O’Leary’s voice. “And as to the mechanics of the situation, Dr. Letheny must have made up his mind quite suddenly to secure the radium for his own use; he came to St. Ann’s—I wonder what his feelings were when he examined the patient whom he was soon to rob, I do not think the murder was intentional—then, presumably he left. Outside the hospital he accidentally came upon Miss Day and detained her for some time—er—seizing her sleeve as she attempted to return to the wing, and in so doing detached her cuff link. Is that right, Miss Day?”

Without a word Maida nodded assent but her deep, blue eyes shot a glance of gratitude toward the young detective.

“Then, determining to carry out his hastily formed plan for stealing the radium, he watched his chance and while Miss Day was busy in the kitchen and Miss Keate was detained for some fifteen minutes in—Room 11?”

“Room 11,” I said.

===Chinese===
“那么——那么您确信，杀死杰克逊先生的是莱瑟尼医生？”巴尔曼医生将信将疑地问。

“确信无疑。”奥利里说。“再举一个证据：属于莱瑟尼小姐的那把左轮手枪上，留着莱瑟尼医生的指纹。他若是全然心怀善意地上医院来，何必带一把左轮手枪？他想要那镭，他需要钱——我真心相信，这人要钱是为了做研究。”奥利里的声音里透出一丝怜悯。“至于这件事的来龙去脉：莱瑟尼医生想必是相当突然地打定主意，要把那镭据为己用；他来了圣安医院——不知他给自己不久便要下手劫夺的病人做检查时，心里是什么滋味，我认为那桩谋杀并非蓄意——然后，他大概离开了。在医院外面，他偶然撞见戴小姐，把她耽搁了一阵——呃——在她想回南翼去时抓住她的袖子，这一抓，扯落了她的袖扣。是这样吗，戴小姐？”

梅达一言不发地点头表示认可，但她那双深邃的蓝眼睛，朝年轻的警探投去感激的一瞥。

“随后，他决意实行那个仓促间定下的盗镭计划，窥伺时机，趁戴小姐在配膳室里忙、基特小姐被绊住约莫十五分钟的当口——在11号病房（Room 11）？”

“11号病房。”我说。

===Original===
“—he must have slipped along the corridor into the drug room and helped himself to morphine tablets and hypodermic needle and hurried back, unseen, to Room 18. Jackson very likely never knew what happened, but Dr. Letheny was safe because, in the first place, Jackson was not surprised at the presence of his doctor and would have had no occasion to object to a hypodermic injection, and furthermore, on waking from a drugged sleep, impressions immediately preceding that sleep are vague and confused and could scarcely be given as evidence. I do not believe that Dr. Letheny intended to make the dose fatal; I believe he only intended that Jackson should know nothing of the radium being removed, but in his natural excitement Dr. Letheny either misjudged the dose he was giving or the resistive powers of his patient, with the result that we know. Dr. Letheny tossed the needle through the open window, where it was later found. In the main, I believe I am right; there may be slight discrepancies. One can’t be absolutely sure when both—er—participants are dead.”

There was a moment of tense silence. Then Corole spoke.

“So it was Louis,” she said in a tone of ugly satisfaction. “I knew it. I knew it all along for I watched—” She checked herself.

O’Leary turned sharply.

===Chinese===
“——他准是悄悄溜过走廊，进了药品间，自取了吗啡药片和皮下注射针，又趁着无人看见，匆匆赶回18号病房（Room 18）。杰克逊先生多半根本不知道出了什么事，而莱瑟尼医生是安全的：首先，杰克逊见到自己的医生在场并不意外，没有理由拒绝一针皮下注射；再者，从药物所致的沉睡中醒来时，入睡之前的印象模糊混乱，几乎没法当作证词。我不认为莱瑟尼医生有意把剂量下成致命的；我相信他只是想让杰克逊对镭被取走一事毫无所觉，可是在那份自然而起的激动里，莱瑟尼医生要么估错了他下的剂量，要么估错了病人的抵抗力，结果就成了我们知道的这个结局。莱瑟尼医生把针从敞开的窗口扔了出去，后来就是在那儿找到的。大体上，我相信我没有说错；细节上或许略有出入。两位——呃——当事人都不在了，谁也没法百分之百地肯定。”

一阵紧张的沉默。然后科罗尔开了口。

“原来真是路易斯。”她说，语气里是一种丑恶的快意。“我就知道。我从头到尾都知道，因为我当时看见——”她猛地收住话头。

奥利里霍地转过身。

===Original===
“Just a moment,” he said coldly. “Your skirts are not entirely clear. There is Higgins’s death yet to explain, and the theft of the radium.”

“I knew nothing about Higgins’s death,” cried Corole.

“Go on, Mr. O’Leary,” begged Dr. Balman. Under the light his face looked drawn and aged.

===Chinese===
“且慢，”他冷冷地说。“您身上的干系还没有洗干净。希金斯的死还没有解释，镭的失窃也是。”

“希金斯的死，我一无所知。”科罗尔喊道。

“讲下去吧，奥利里先生。”巴尔曼医生恳求道。灯光下，他的脸显得又枯瘦又苍老。

===Original===
“From that night on the struggle has been for the discovery and possession of the radium. It was thought, by those who knew of Dr. Letheny’s participation in the affair, to be still in Room 18. Hajek was determined to find it, even going so far as to steal the key to the south door in order to effect an entrance at any time.”

Again Dr. Hajek made an inarticulate murmur which O’Leary silenced.

“Becoming impatient at his continued failure to locate the radium, Miss Letheny herself, who was in—er—in cahoots with Hajek, took a hand in the matter. Knowing what had happened in the room and being by nature extremely superstitious, she was intensely frightened when upon entering Room 18 in the middle of the night in order to make a search for the radium herself, she saw a sheeted figure on the bed. She, too, failed to find the radium.”

“You are perfectly right about that,” said Corole brazenly. “But you are wrong about—”

===Chinese===
“从那一夜起，各方角力的就是要找出并占有那镭。知道莱瑟尼医生卷入此案的人，都以为镭还在18号病房（Room 18）里。海耶克铁了心要找到它，甚至不惜偷走南门的钥匙，好随时都能进来。”

海耶克医生又含混地咕哝了一声，被奥利里止住。

“眼见他迟迟找不到镭，莱瑟尼小姐本人——她跟海耶克——呃——通同一气——便亲自插手了。她知道那间病房里出过事，又天性极度迷信，半夜进18号病房想亲手搜镭，一看见床上一个盖着床单的人影，吓得不轻。她也没能找到镭。”

“这一点您说得半点不错，”科罗尔厚着脸皮说。“可有的事您就错了——”

===Original===
“Then one night Hajek grew desperate; he wanted the radium and Corole—that is, Miss Letheny—was reproaching him for his continued failure to find the radium. He recalled the circumstance of the electric-light connection having been damaged by lightning on the night of June seventh, decided that that condition was a valuable help and, repeated, would aid him in making a thorough and prolonged search in Room 18. So he went to the basement, disconnected the electric current, let himself out the grade door, ran around the corner of the hospital, entered the south wing by the unlocked south door, for the windows were bolted, and was into Room 18 in about a minute and a half after he pulled the light switch. Either from reflection or because he had exhausted all the other available hiding places, he went at once to the loud speaker, which by an odd circumstance was the original speaker that was in Room 18 the night of the seventh. But Higgins, in the basement, saw him and followed him. Higgins came upon him in Room 18. As I say, Hajek had at last found the radium and at the knowledge of someone witnessing his theft he shot wildly in the dark, the bullet killing Higgins instantly. Likely Higgins had said something, indicated in some way that he knew what Hajek was about and what he had taken from that loud speaker. I don’t know how it happened that Higgins got up courage enough to follow and threaten Hajek with exposure, but he evidently did. Hajek, frightened at the consequence of his deed, simply acted from primitive impulse; if he were caught on his way from the hospital the possession of the radium would be a distinctly incriminating fact, no matter how he tried to explain it away. He had only a few seconds in which to act, and he followed Dr. Letheny’s example, hiding the radium in the first place that came to hand which was—a flower pot. He scooped out the dirt, thrust the box into the aperture and the soil in his pocket and hurried from the wing, around the hospital, in the basement door and to his room—where we found him later. He had barely time to get to his room unobserved.”

“I didn’t! You are lying! I didn’t!” cried Dr. Hajek, his face livid and those glaring eyes going from one to the other of us. “I tell you I didn’t!”

At a motion from O’Leary, O’Brien stepped closer to Hajek, thrusting the revolver he held close to Hajek’s ribs.

“But—but the mud on the window casing,” I began, bewildered. “If he used the grade door and came up by the basement—”

===Chinese===
“后来有一天夜里，海耶克铤而走险了；他想要那镭，而科罗尔——也就是莱瑟尼小姐——一直在责怪他迟迟找不到。他想起六月七日夜里电灯线路曾被雷击损坏的情形，断定那种状况是个宝贵的助力，若能重演一回，便能帮他把18号病房（Room 18）搜个通透、搜个够。于是他下到地下室，切断电流，从地坪门出去，绕过医院的墙角，由没上锁的南门进了南翼——窗户都上了插销——拉断电闸之后约莫一分半钟，他人已经进了18号病房。也不知是心里想到了这一层，还是其余能藏东西的地方都已搜遍，他径直奔那台扬声器去了——说来也怪，那恰是七日夜里原本放在18号病房里的那台扬声器。可希金斯在地下室看见了他，跟了上来。希金斯在18号病房撞见了他。正如我方才所说，海耶克终于找到了镭，一发觉有人亲眼目睹他的偷窃，便在黑暗中朝那边胡乱开了一枪，子弹当场结果了希金斯。希金斯多半说了句什么，以某种方式示意自己知道海耶克在干什么、知道他从那台扬声器里取走了什么。希金斯怎会鼓起足够的胆子跟踪海耶克、拿揭发来要挟他，我不知道，但他显然是这么做了。海耶克被自己所作所为的后果吓破了胆，全凭原始的冲动行事：倘若在离开医院的半路上被逮住，身上带着镭便是明明白白的罪证，随他怎么辩解也洗刷不掉。他只有几秒钟的工夫好行事，便效法莱瑟尼医生，把镭藏进了手边第一处地方——一只花盆。他掏出盆土，把盒子塞进那个洞口，泥土揣进衣袋，匆匆离开南翼，绕过医院，进了地下室的门，奔回自己房里——后来我们就是在那儿找到他的。他险些就来不及不被察觉地赶回房中。”

“我没有！你撒谎！我没有！”海耶克医生喊道，脸皮青得吓人，那双怒目从我们一张脸扫到另一张脸。“我告诉你，不是我干的！”

奥利里一示意，奥布赖恩便朝海耶克逼近一步，把手中的左轮手枪抵近他的肋下。

“可是——可是窗框上的泥呢，”我迷惑地开口。“要是他走的是地坪门、从地下室上来的——”

===Original===
O’Leary interrupted me.

“Miss Day happened on the radium in the pot of lobelias; it was in the corridor where Hajek had placed it in his hurry, knowing that Room 18 would be thoroughly searched. Miss Keate in the meantime—I think we need not go into that. Anyway it came thus to my hands for a moment or two before Hajek knocked me senseless and took the radium. He had managed, from his room off the general office, to hear Miss Keate’s announcement and must have watched until she gave it to me—”

“I’ll admit to that,” cried Dr. Hajek. “But not to that oth—”

“There, there!” O’Brien poked him suggestively and Hajek stopped talking.

===Chinese===
奥利里打断了我。

“是戴小姐碰巧在那盆半边莲里发现了镭；花盆当时在走廊里，是海耶克慌忙之中搁在那儿的，因为知道18号病房（Room 18）会被彻头彻尾地搜查一遍。至于基特小姐这其间——我想我们就不必细说了。总之，镭就这样到了我手里，可不过一两分钟的工夫，海耶克便把我打得不省人事，把镭夺了去。他在总办公室旁边自己的房间里，设法听到了基特小姐的通报，想必一直守望着，直到她把镭交到我手上——”

“这个我认！”海耶克医生喊道。“可那件事我不——”

“得了，得了！”奥布赖恩意味深长地捅了他一下，海耶克便住了嘴。

===Original===
“But,” began Dr. Balman uncertainly, “I never dreamed it was Dr. Hajek. Why he was right there with me when we found you, O’Leary, there by the stairs. He seemed as astonished as I was.” Dr. Balman reached unsteadily for his handkerchief and passed it over his forehead. “This is terrible, O’Leary, terrible.” His voice shook. “Do you realize that you are accusing a doctor of St. Ann’s of unspeakable crimes? That you are—”

“Truth is truth.” There was a queer, icy look in O’Leary’s gray eyes. “If a doctor of St. Ann’s is guilty, he is as guilty as any other man would be.”

“Oh, yes. Yes, I suppose so,” agreed Dr. Balman, reluctantly. “But it is no less—terrible.” He shuddered visibly.

I found my tongue.

===Chinese===
“可是，”巴尔曼医生迟疑不定地开了口，“我做梦也没想到是海耶克医生。我们发现您的时候，奥利里，他就跟我一起在楼梯口那儿。他看上去跟我一样吃惊。”巴尔曼医生颤巍巍地伸手去掏手帕，在额头上抹了一把。“这太可怕了，奥利里，太可怕了。”他的声音发着抖。“您可意识到，您是在指控圣安医院的一位医生，犯下说不出口的罪行？您是在——”

“真相就是真相。”奥利里的灰眼睛里有一种古怪的、冷冰冰的神色。“圣安医院的医生若是犯了罪，他跟任何别的人一样有罪。”

“噢，是的。是的，我想是这样。”巴尔曼医生勉强附和。“可它照样——可怕。”他明显地打了个寒噤。

我总算找回了自己的舌头。

===Original===
“Then what part has Mr. Gainsay in all this?”

O’Leary eyed me curiously before replying. Then he turned to Jim Gainsay.

“Gainsay,” he said slowly, “is a young man who is going to get into serious trouble sometime through not minding his own business. He is incurably inquisitive and has been quite sure that he and he alone could solve this mystery.” There was a gleam of mirth back of those clear, gray eyes.

Jim straightened up, felt absently in his pocket and drew out a pipe, which he held without lighting, the policeman at the window watching him with an impassive countenance.

Jim sighed.

“I *am* a fool,” he admitted abruptly. “But, Lord, it didn’t seem to me that you were getting anywhere. I had to take a hand in it. I thought the first thing to do was to find the radium.”

===Chinese===
“那么盖恩赛先生在这件事里，又是个什么角色？”

奥利里好奇地打量了我一阵，才作回答。然后他转向吉姆·盖恩赛。

“盖恩赛，”他缓缓地说，“是个总有一天会因为爱管闲事而闯下大祸的年轻人。他好奇成癖，无药可救，还一直深信，这桩谜案唯独他一个人解得开。”那双清澈的灰眼睛背后，闪过一丝笑意。

吉姆直起身，心不在焉地往口袋里摸了摸，掏出一支烟斗，握在手里却没有点；窗口的警察面无表情地望着他。

吉姆叹了口气。

“我*确实*是个傻瓜，”他戛然承认。“可是，天哪，我看你们简直寸步未进。我不得不插上一手。我想，头一件该做的事，就是把镭找出来。”

===Original===
Corole’s slitted eyes flashed green fire.

“You nearly got it, too,” she said viciously. “But I got away from you.”

“Where did you hide it when Hajek turned it over to you immediately after stealing it from me?” asked O’Leary mildly.

Corole’s face was sullen but she replied, taking, I think, a certain pleasure in being the centre of the stage for a moment.

“I dug a hole under one of the trees out there,” she motioned with a long, brown hand, on which the topaz shone, toward the orchard. “I left it there all day yesterday—I mean, day before yesterday.” She glanced at the window, which was beginning to show a dim, gray light. “And then that night I got away from you,” she looked at O’Brien—“and you”—at Jim, this time—“and got it out and brought it here in my jewel case. I thought it would be safe in this room and Sarah was so excited”—she cast a malicious glance toward me—“she never noticed that I came out without my jewel case when she so thoughtfully took me to her room and *locked me in*! How did you find it?”

O’Leary did not reply.

===Chinese===
科罗尔眯细的双眼迸出绿火。

“你也差一点儿就得手了，”她恶狠狠地说。“可我还是从你手里脱了身。”

“海耶克从我手里偷走镭之后，立刻就转交给了您，您把它藏在哪儿了？”奥利里温温和和地问。

科罗尔的脸色阴沉沉的，但还是答了话——我想，她对这片刻的众目所归，倒也自有一番享受。

“我在外头一棵树底下挖了个洞，”她用一只修长的棕色手朝果园那边指了指，手上的托帕石熠熠生光。“我把它在那儿搁了一整天，昨天——不，我是说，前天。”她瞥了一眼窗户，窗上已开始透出朦胧的灰光。“到了那天夜里，我甩开了你，”她看向奥布赖恩——“还有你”——这一回是看吉姆——“把它刨了出来，装在我的首饰盒里带到了这儿。我原以为它藏在这间病房里最安全，再说萨拉当时激动成那样”——她朝我投来恶毒的一瞥——“她那么体贴地把我带进她的房间，还*把我锁在里头*，竟全然没有留意，我出来的时候没带着首饰盒！你们是怎么找到的？”

奥利里没有回答。

===Original===
“When Miss Letheny returned for the radium about eleven o’clock tonight I—er—detained her.” O’Leary glanced toward the closet from which she had emerged. “I am interested to hear that you admit to having the radium in your possession.”

“What can you do about it?” flashed Corole insolently. “And you are all wrong about Dr. Hajek. I know that he did not shoot Higgins and I know that he did not kill Louis for he was with me both times—”

“That will do, Miss Letheny. Or rather, Mrs. Hajek.”

Corole started. Her brown hands clutched at the wall back of her.

===Chinese===
“今晚十一点钟光景，莱瑟尼小姐回来取镭，我——呃——把她扣下了。”奥利里朝她先前现身的那口壁橱瞥了一眼。“听您承认镭曾落在您手里，我倒很感兴趣。”

“那你们又能拿我怎么样？”科罗尔傲慢地顶了回去。“再说海耶克医生的事，你们全都错了。我知道他没有枪杀希金斯，也知道他没有杀路易斯，因为那两回，他都是跟我在一起的——”

“够了，莱瑟尼小姐。或者该说，海耶克太太。”

科罗尔浑身一震。她那双棕色的手抓住了身后的墙壁。

===Original===
“How did you know that?”

O’Brien cleared his throat self-consciously and at the sound Corole whirled to face him.

“I suppose you were following us this afternoon,” she said vindictively.

===Chinese===
“这个，你们是怎么知道的？”

奥布赖恩不自在地清了清嗓子，一听见这声音，科罗尔霍地转身冲着他。

“我猜，今天下午盯我们梢的就是你。”她怨毒地说。

===Original===
“They were married this afternoon,” said O’Leary. “Owing to a conversation overheard by one of us”—I daresay it was my turn to look self-conscious—“we have reason to think that possibly the bride was a bit reluctant, but however that was, they were actually married at the courthouse with Mr. O’Brien—near at hand. Your own desire to perjure yourself, Mrs. Hajek, will not be of any help in the matter, for your husband cannot be cleared.”

A strange silence fell; the torrents of rain seemed to be lessening slightly and I heard a roll of thunder away off in the distance.

I was engaged in going over and over to myself O’Leary’s explanations; it did not seem to me that he had covered everything, and I was about to inquire into certain matters when O’Leary spoke again.

“Is everything clear to you, Dr. Balman?” he asked deferentially.

Dr. Balman hesitated.

“I don’t know,” he said with a puzzled and worried air. “I really don’t know. This is”—he paused to pass his hand across his eyes, rubbing the bruise on his cheekbone a little as if it itched—“this is a terrible responsibility, Mr. O’Leary.”

===Chinese===
“他们是今天下午结的婚。”奥利里说。“由于我们中间有人碰巧听见了一段谈话”——我敢说，这回轮到我不自在起来——“我们有理由认为，新娘子当时也许有几分不大情愿；可不管怎么说，他们确实在法院成了婚，奥布赖恩先生——就在近旁。海耶克太太，您那点想作伪证的心思，在这件事上帮不了半点忙，因为您丈夫是洗脱不了的。”

一阵奇异的沉默落了下来；倾盆的雨势似乎小了些，我听见远处滚过一阵雷声。

我心里正一遍又一遍地盘算着奥利里的解释；依我看，他并没有把一切都说尽，我正要就某些事追问，奥利里又开了口。

“您都清楚了吗，巴尔曼医生？”他毕恭毕敬地问。

巴尔曼医生迟疑了。

“我不知道，”他说，一副又困惑又忧心的样子。“我真的不知道。这实在是”——他停下来，抬手在眼睛上抹了一把，又稍稍揉了揉颧骨上那块淤伤，仿佛那儿发痒——“这实在是可怕的责任，奥利里先生。”

===Original===
O’Leary nodded.

“But you are head of St. Ann’s,” said O’Leary. “And while the case belongs to the state to prosecute, still I should like to feel that you, as head of the institution of St. Ann’s—are satisfied with our findings.”

“It doesn’t seem—it doesn’t seem possible,” said Dr. Balman.

O’Leary looked obviously irritated, but said with restrained impatience:

“Is there anything that I have overlooked, Dr. Balman?”

“No. No, I suppose not,” Dr. Balman replied uncertainly.

===Chinese===
奥利里点了点头。

“可您是圣安医院的院长，”奥利里说。“这桩案子固然要交由州里起诉，但我仍然愿意觉得，您作为圣安医院这所机构的院长——对我们的结论是满意的。”

“这不像——这不像可能的事。”巴尔曼医生说。

奥利里显然露出恼意，但仍按捺着性子说：

“可有什么我疏漏的地方吗，巴尔曼医生？”

“没有。没有，我想是没有。”巴尔曼医生迟疑地答道。

===Original===
“Perhaps I have not made myself perfectly clear,” said O’Leary, still patiently. “Let’s begin at the beginning again, Dr. Balman, and piece things out in their logical order. I want to be sure that it is all clear to you.”

“No, no! That will not be necessary.”

“Yes,” insisted O’Leary. “You being head of St. Ann’s, Doctor, should be given every scrap of information in my power to give.”

“No, no!” said Dr. Balman. “It is very painful to me. And anyway, I think I understand. Dr. Hajek got into Room 18, just after Dr. Letheny had hidden the radium. Isn’t that it?”

O’Leary nodded and there was a quickly subdued growl of dissent from Dr. Hajek.

===Chinese===
“也许是我没有讲得十分明白，”奥利里依旧耐心地说。“那我们就从头再讲一遍吧，巴尔曼医生，按逻辑的次序把事情一件件拼起来。我想要确信，您把样样都弄清楚了。”

“不，不！那就不必了。”

“要的。”奥利里坚持道。“医生，您是圣安医院的院长，凡是我能给出的信息，哪怕一星半点，都理当让您知道。”

“不，不！”巴尔曼医生说。“这叫我太痛苦了。再说，我想我是明白了。海耶克医生进了18号病房（Room 18），就在莱瑟尼医生藏好镭之后不久。是这么回事吧？”

奥利里点了点头，海耶克医生发出一声很快被压下去的异议低吼。

===Original===
“The two men struggled then, and Dr. Letheny was killed in the struggle?”

Again O’Leary nodded.

“Yes, I think I understand. Still it doesn’t seem possible.” Dr. Balman regarded Dr. Hajek doubtfully.

“No,” said Lance O’Leary slowly. “It does seem strange that Miss Keate should hear nothing of it.”

“I believe she did hear something of it,” said Dr. Balman, his distressed countenance turning to me. I made some gesture of assent.

“Yes,” said O’Leary. “For don’t you remember that she came down to the end of the corridor—” He left his sentence hanging in the air, and as he spoke he moved his hand slightly and I was faintly surprised to see little beads of sweat glistening on the back of it, though the night was cool. His face was quiet and composed as usual.

===Chinese===
“然后那两个男人扭打起来，莱瑟尼医生就在扭打中丧了命？”

奥利里又点了点头。

“是的，我想我明白了。可这还是不像真的。”巴尔曼医生将信将疑地望着海耶克医生。

“是啊，”兰斯·奥利里缓缓地说。“基特小姐竟然丝毫没有听见，这确实奇怪。”

“我相信她多少听见了些。”巴尔曼医生说，那张苦恼的脸转向了我。我做了个表示同意的手势。

“是啊，”奥利里说。“因为您不记得了吗，她当时一路走到了走廊尽头——”他的话悬在了半空。说话间，他的手轻轻动了一下，我隐约吃惊地看见，尽管夜气清凉，那手背上竟沁着一层细细的汗珠。他的脸一如往常，安静而镇定。

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” said Dr. Balman. “I remember now. Strange she saw or heard nothing of all this when she opened the door of Room 18 and stood there for a moment.”

Queer how silent the room was. No one seemed to breathe.

Then Lance O’Leary’s voice broke the silence; it was tight and strange and shook a little.

“*Only the murderer could know that!*” He shot a glance at O’Brien. “Quick!” The last word was like the sharp lash of a whip.

I was never sure just what happened then. There was a scream as Corole flung herself upon Dr. Hajek. There was another struggle going on somewhere else. Figures blurred in rapid motion—there were outcries—I found myself clutching at Maida—Jim Gainsay’s tall figure flashed before our eyes.

===Chinese===
“噢，对了，”巴尔曼医生说。“我现在想起来了。真奇怪，她打开18号病房（Room 18）的门、在那儿站了片刻的时候，竟没有看见、也没有听见这一切。”

说来古怪，病房里竟静成这样。仿佛没有一个人还在呼吸。

然后，兰斯·奥利里的声音划破了寂静；那声音绷得紧紧的，怪异，还微微发颤。

“*这只有凶手本人才知道！*”他向奥布赖恩疾射一瞥。“快！”最后一个字，像鞭子凌厉的一击。

接下来究竟发生了什么，我始终没能弄确实。只听见科罗尔扑向海耶克医生时的一声尖叫。另一场扭打又在别处进行着。人影翻飞，模糊一片——惊叫声四起——我发觉自己死死抓住了梅达——吉姆·盖恩赛高大的身影从我们眼前一闪而过。

===Original===
Then O’Leary’s tense voice commanded the situation.

“Right, O’Brien!” he said sharply.

Then the room seemed to clear; things resumed their normal dimensions.

I stared and rubbed my eyes and stared again.

Then my knees weakened under me and I think I screamed.

The handcuffs glittered coldly on Dr. Balman’s wrists.

===Chinese===
随后，奥利里紧绷的声音掌控了局面。

“好，奥布赖恩！”他厉声道。

然后病房似乎清明了起来；万物恢复了原本的形貌。

我瞪眼望着，揉了揉眼睛，又瞪眼望去。

然后我双膝一软，我想，我尖叫出了声。

手铐在巴尔曼医生的双腕上，闪着冷光。

## XVIII O’Leary Revises His Story / 奥利里改口

===Original===
It was fitting that the thing should end as it had begun, in Room 18.

I have only a dazed and chaotic memory of them taking Dr. Balman away. Of Corole and Fred Hajek going under guard. Of myself sitting numbly in Room 18, with Maida beside me gripping my hand, until O’Leary returned. Jim accompanied him.

I think it was seeing Jim sit down on the white bed with his coat still wet with rain, and noting the marks of muddy shoes and wet coats on the once white counterpane, that aroused me to a sense of reality.

It was dawn by that time. The electric light was paling and growing sickly under the gray streaks of daybreak at the windows, and I recall a vague little feeling of amazement when the thin rays of washed-out sunlight began to find their way into the room. Sunlight after a week of rain!

===Chinese===
这件事由18号病房（Room 18）开始，又在18号病房里收场，倒也算是得其所哉。

我只留下一段恍惚而纷乱的记忆：他们如何把巴尔曼医生带走；科罗尔和弗雷德·海耶克如何被押着离开。还有我自己如何麻木地坐在18号病房里，梅达在身旁紧握着我的手，直到奥利里回来。吉姆陪着他一道。

我想，正是看见吉姆在那张白床上坐下——外套还浸着雨水的湿气——又看见那张原本雪白的床罩上留下的泥鞋印和湿外套的印痕，我才被唤回了现实的感觉中来。

那时已是黎明。在窗外灰白的曙色里，电灯光渐渐转淡，显得病恹恹的；当一缕缕褪了色似的稀薄阳光开始照进房间时，我记得自己心里泛起一阵隐约的惊奇。下了一个星期的雨，居然出了太阳！

===Original===
O’Leary, entering the room, had closed the door and crossed to the radiator and sunk wearily down upon it. He appeared worn and enormously tired, with no shred of the jubilance I should have expected.

“Well,” my voice quavered a little as I spoke. “Well﻿—you have succeeded.”

“Yes. I’ve got my man.”

“You don’t seem to be rejoicing about it.”

===Chinese===
奥利里进屋时随手关上了门，走到暖气片跟前，疲惫地一坐了下去。他显得憔悴不堪、累极了，全然没有我本以为会见到的那种欢欣鼓舞的影子。

“唉，”我说话时嗓音有点发颤。“唉——您成功了。”

“是的。我要抓的人抓到了。”

“可您看上去并不为此感到高兴。”

===Original===
“I am not,” said Lance O’Leary flatly. “That is, don’t misunderstand me﻿—I am glad that I have done my duty. But I am sorry to see a man, a brilliant scientist, a scholar, a useful surgeon﻿—go wrong. Dr. Balman has actually given his life﻿—mistakenly of course, for his science. He wanted the radium, he needed the money it would bring. For the rest he was, as much as anything, a victim of circumstance. It is a sad thing﻿—yet just.”

“Dr. Balman was a wicked man,” I said. Odd how we were speaking of him in the past tense.

“Yes,” agreed O’Leary. “But Dr. Letheny was equally culpable. Strange how a man can devote his life to a woman or﻿—a career. Well,” he broke off, shrugging, “there’s no use philosophizing. Don’t mind my low spirits. I should feel much lower if I had failed.”

“I didn’t know what you were doing until Dr. Balman said: ‘When she opened the door.’ Then I began to have a premonition, for I recalled the request you made at the first inquest.”

===Chinese===
“我是不高兴，”兰斯·奥利里干巴巴地说。“也就是说，别误会我——我尽到了职责，自然欣慰。可眼看一个人，一个卓越的科学家、一个学者、一个有用的外科医生——走上邪路，我心里难过。巴尔曼医生实际上已经赔上了自己的性命——当然是错赔了——为了他的科学。他想要那镭，他需要镭能换来的钱。至于其余种种，他在很大程度上也是身不由己的环境牺牲品。这是件可悲的事——然而也是件公道的事。”

“巴尔曼医生是个恶人。”我说。说来奇怪，我们谈起他来，用的竟已是过去时态。

“是啊，”奥利里附和道。“可莱瑟尼医生同样难辞其咎。一个人竟能把一生献给一个女人，或者——献给一番事业，真是不可思议。好了，”他耸耸肩，打住话头，“空发议论没有用处。别在意我情绪低落。倘若我失了手，还会低落得多呢。”

“直到巴尔曼医生说出『她开门的时候』那句话，我才明白您在做什么。随后我就开始有了预感，因为我想起了您在第一次验尸审讯上提的那个请求。”

===Original===
“What was that?” asked Jim.

“I asked Miss Keate not to mention the fact that when she came down the corridor after hearing the sound that she thought was a door closing and was actually﻿—”

“Don’t!” I interrupted with a shudder.

“﻿—was actually something else, she came to the door of Eighteen and opened it and stood there for a few seconds listening.”

===Chinese===
“什么请求？”吉姆问。

“我请求基特小姐不要提及这样一桩事实：她听见一声响动，只当是哪扇门在关上，便循声走下走廊，而那声音实际上是——”

“别说了！”我打了个寒噤，打断了他。

“——实际上是别的东西；她走到18号病房门口，推开门，在那儿站了几秒钟，侧耳倾听。”

===Original===
“Why? Did she hear something? Or see anything?”

“No. But I reasoned that the guilty man must have still been in Room 18. It had not been a moment since the sound of the blow that﻿—the blow that killed Dr. Letheny, and I knew that the man who did it could not have dragged Dr. Letheny’s body to the closet, locked the door, and made his escape before Miss Keate got to the door of Eighteen. Hence I knew that only the guilty man *knew what she had done*.”

“Did you plan that far ahead? Did you know you could work him to admit that knowledge?” cried Jim in honest amazement.

===Chinese===
“为什么？她听见了什么？还是看见了什么？”

“没有。但我推断，那罪人当时必定还在18号病房（Room 18）里。那致命一击——杀死莱瑟尼医生的那一击——的声响落下还不到片刻工夫，而我知道，下手的人绝不可能在基特小姐赶到18号病房门口之前，把莱瑟尼医生的尸身拖进壁橱、锁上门、再脱身逃走。由此我明白，只有那个罪人*知道她做过什么*。”

“您事先就盘算得这么远？您就知道能引他自己招出这份知情？”吉姆嚷道，那副惊诧全然发自肺腑。

===Original===
O’Leary shook his head, smiling ruefully.

“No. I am but human. But I plan to take every chance. Hoard every possible bit of evidence. That was small but conclusive.”

“Is that the only proof you have against Dr. Balman?” asked Jim.

“No. I have others. But I wasn’t quite satisfied. You see, I let Balman know that the radium was in this room and would be guarded all day but not at night because I intended to remove it then. Miss Keate helped me there. Dr. Balman was on the other side of the door in the garage this morning﻿—or rather yesterday morning,” explained O’Leary to me. “Of course, I had actually removed the radium at once and substituted a dummy box. I expected Balman would try to secure it in the dark of night and hoped to catch him red-handed. But instead﻿—you, Gainsay, nearly spoiled the works for me. You have been a good deal of trouble, one way and another,” he interpolated, with a glance that held nothing humorous. “However, I know the reason for your﻿—er﻿—meddling.” O’Leary smiled openly at Maida; it was that extraordinary winning smile that he reserved for certain occasions and Maida smiled, too. “I can’t say that I blame you for that. Though if you had advised her to tell of the cuff link business in the first place﻿—”

===Chinese===
奥利里摇摇头，苦笑着。

“没有。我不过是凡人。但我打定主意抓住每一个机会。囤积每一丁点可能的证据。那件事虽小，却是决定性的。”

“这是您手里不利于巴尔曼医生的唯一证据吗？”吉姆问。

“不。还有别的。但我还不算十分满意。是这样：我让巴尔曼知道镭就在这间病房里，白天里会有人严加看守，夜里却不设防，因为我打算就在夜里把它转移走。这一点上基特小姐帮了我。今天早晨——不如说昨天早晨——巴尔曼医生就在车库那扇门的另一侧听着，”奥利里向我解释道。“当然，我其实早已把镭取走，换上了一只假盒子。我料定巴尔曼会趁黑夜里来攫取它，指望当场人赃并获。谁知——盖恩赛，你倒差点儿坏了我的事。你前前后后添了不少乱，”他插进一句，瞥来的目光里没有一丝玩笑的意思。“不过，你那——呃——多管闲事的缘由，我是知道的。”奥利里坦然朝梅达一笑；正是他留着在某些场合才使出的那种格外动人的微笑，梅达也笑了。“这一层我不能怪你。只是，你当初要是劝她把青金石袖扣那件事头一个说出来——”

===Original===
“That cuff link!” murmured Maida with contrition. “I am sorry. I should have explained it immediately. But it﻿—would have meant such publicity. It was so disagreeable.” She flushed pinkly and Jim’s heart was in his steady eyes fastened upon her.

“But what else do you have against Dr. Balman?” I inquired hastily, for once uninterested in matters of the tender sentiment.

“The story that I told here tonight is, in the main part, true if you simply change the name of Hajek to Balman. For the points against Balman﻿—” he checked off the items on his fingers. “First, finger prints on that revolver of Corole’s. Dr. Letheny’s and those of Dr. Balman were very clear. It was some time before I could get a good print of Balman’s, though I had those of everyone else connected with the case. Then there was the fact that he *asked* to have this low window bolted and the request came simultaneously with the disappearance of the key to the south door; having provided himself with a mode of entrance he was anxious to keep others out of the room that held the radium. Then there was the matter of the ether that you smelled, Miss Keate, the ether that Higgins said was somewhere about the coat that was left there just outside the window of Eighteen, and that you found on a handkerchief in the pocket of that yellow slicker. I found on investigation that the only person seen to leave St. Ann’s that Friday evening just at dinner time was Dr. Balman and that he wore a yellow slicker. That was not conclusive, for he might have borrowed it as Miss Keate did. But yesterday morning I found in the side pocket of his car a small empty bottle and a sponge that still had a lingering trace of that very clinging odour.”

“But why ether?” said Jim.

===Chinese===
“那枚袖扣！”梅达喃喃道，满心悔愧。“对不起。我本该立刻说明白的。可那样——势必闹得满城风雨。实在是太不堪了。”她双颊飞红；吉姆定定地望着她，一颗心全在那双沉静的眼睛里。

“可您手里还有什么不利于巴尔曼医生的？”我急忙问道，破天荒地对这类柔情蜜意的事提不起兴致来。

“我今夜在这里讲的那套说法，只要把海耶克的名字换成巴尔曼，大体上都是真的。至于不利于巴尔曼的种种——”他扳着手指一项项地数。“第一，科罗尔那把左轮手枪上的指纹。莱瑟尼医生的指纹和巴尔曼医生的指纹都非常清晰。与本案有牵连的其他所有人的指纹我都弄到了，唯独巴尔曼的，隔了好一阵子才取到一枚完好的。其次，那扇低窗的插销是他*主动*要求上的，而这请求恰好与南门钥匙的失踪同时发生；他既给自己备下了一条进路，自然急于把旁人挡在那间藏着镭的病房之外。再次，就是您闻到的那股乙醚，基特小姐——希金斯说，乙醚味就在那件撂在18号病房窗外的外套上，您又在那件黄色油布雨衣口袋里的手帕上闻到过它。我调查下来，那个星期五傍晚、正是晚饭时分，被人看见离开圣安医院的只有巴尔曼医生一人，而且他穿着一件黄色油布雨衣。这算不上决定性的，因为他也可能像基特小姐那样是借来的。可昨天早晨，我在他汽车的侧袋里找到一只小空瓶和一块海绵，上面还残留着那股缠绵不去的气味。”

“可用乙醚是为了什么呢？”吉姆说。

===Original===
“He had evidently intended to anesthetize Mr. Jackson, steal the radium while the patient was unconscious and get away. But when after waiting about the grounds for some time until he thought the coast was clear he finally got into Room 18, he found another man in the room, the radium gone and his patient dead. The next thing for me to do was to break what appeared to be an alibi. I did so when I found that there was a freight elevator at the back of the apartment house in which Balman lived. He knew how to operate it and must have taken precautions to leave by way of the freight elevator and the basement so that the night man in the front of the house never dreamed that Balman had gone out again. He returned the same way and must have got there just in time to answer the telephone. It was his car that left along the lower road just as the storm broke, Miss Keate.”

“He must have driven like mad,” I speculated.

“Think what he left behind him,” said O’Leary grimly. “He knew, too, that at any moment the alarm might come and his presence would be needed at the hospital. He must be in his rooms when the call came.”

“All put together those things are almost﻿—positive proof,” said Jim.

===Chinese===
他显然是打算把杰克逊先生麻醉过去，趁病人不省人事时偷走镭，然后脱身。可他在院子周围盘桓了好一阵，自以为风头已过，终于进了18号病房（Room 18），却发现屋里另有一个男人，镭已不翼而飞，他的病人也死了。接下来我要做的，是击破那看似成立的不在场证明。等我查明巴尔曼所住的公寓楼背面有一部货运电梯，这一步便成了。他会摆弄那东西，必定是预先做了布置，从货运电梯和地下室进出，好叫守在楼前值夜的人做梦也想不到巴尔曼又出去过。他原路返回，想必赶回去得正是时候，正好接了那通电话。暴风雨骤起时沿着下面那条路开走的车，正是他的车，基特小姐。”

“他准是把车开得跟发了疯似的。”我揣测道。

“想想他在身后撂下了什么吧，”奥利里冷冷地说。“他也知道，警报随时可能拉响，医院随时会用得着他。电话打来时，他必须守在自己的房间里。”

“这些加在一起，几乎就是——铁证了。”吉姆说。

===Original===
“Almost,” agreed O’Leary. “But I wanted to trap him into a final admission. To catch him in the act of making off with the radium. And there I failed. When you turned on the light here tonight, Miss Keate, and I saw only Hajek and Gainsay, I was sure that I had failed. But when Dr. Balman came on the scene I began to see my way clear. I caused Hajek considerable anguish of soul but he deserved it. What on earth did you come blundering around for, Gainsay?”

Jim looked uncomfortable.

“Why, you see, O’Leary, I saw Corole come into the south door of St. Ann’s last night and watched through the pane of glass. I had nearly caught her and I was convinced that she had the radium in her jewel case. I could barely see in the hospital corridor through that door, but I saw that she left the jewel case in Room 18. So I figured that she likely thought Room 18 a safe place in which to leave the stuff. Thinking it over during the day, I came to the conclusion that it would be a fine thing to recover the radium myself.” He laughed rather shamefacedly. “I﻿—didn’t think much of you, O’Leary. And I was worried about Miss Day, too. And well﻿—I just made an ass of myself generally.”

“You did,” said O’Leary. “You did. You had better stick to bridges after this, Mr. Gainsay.”

===Chinese===
“几乎，”奥利里承认。“但我要的是诱他作出最后的招认。要当场抓住他携镭而逃。这一层我失手了。今夜您在这儿开亮灯的时候，基特小姐，我看见的只有海耶克和盖恩赛两人，我当真以为自己栽了。可等巴尔曼医生一露面，我就看清了路数。我让海耶克的精神受了不少折磨，可那是他罪有应得。你到底晕头晕脑地闯进来做什么，盖恩赛？”

吉姆显得很是不自在。

“呃，您瞧，奥利里，昨夜我看见科罗尔走进圣安医院的南门，便隔着那块玻璃望进去。我差一点就逮住她了，而且我认定镭就在她的首饰盒里。隔着那扇门，医院走廊里的情形我勉强能望见一点，我看见她把首饰盒留在了18号病房（Room 18）里。于是我琢磨，她多半是觉得18号病房是个藏那东西的稳妥地方。白天我翻来覆去地想，得出个结论：这镭要是由我亲手夺回来，岂不美哉。”他笑起来，颇有些讪讪的。“我——原本没把您瞧在眼里，奥利里。我又替戴小姐担心。再说，唉——总而言之，我是把自己活活弄成了一头蠢驴。”

“确实如此，”奥利里说。“一点不假。往后你还是守着你的桥吧，盖恩赛先生。”

===Original===
“You are right,” said Jim heartily. “But I can’t say that I regret having been here. And I still hope that I have not failed﻿—at one thing I have undertaken.” His eyes were on Maida and she turned entirely crimson and O’Leary laughed boyishly.

I sighed; time enough for romance when this thing was all clear to me.

“Mr. O’Leary,” I said, “can you prove all this?”

He sobered instantly.

===Chinese===
“您说得对，”吉姆恳切地说。“不过，来了这一趟，我不能说有什么可悔的。而且我仍然希望，我着手去做的一件事——并没有落空。”他的目光落在梅达身上，她整个人涨得通红，奥利里则孩子气地大笑起来。

我叹了口气；等这桩案子在我心里水落石出，再谈儿女情长也不迟。

“奥利里先生，”我说，“这一切您都能证实吗？”

他立刻正色起来。

===Original===
“The only thing that is supposition﻿—or rather based solely upon reason, is Dr. Letheny’s part in the business, and even there, we know that only certain events could have taken place. As for the rest of it, change Hajek’s name to Balman’s and﻿—there it is.”

And I may as well say here and now that Dr. Balman confessed to the whole thing, and the only point on which O’Leary was mistaken was this: it was Dr. Hajek who took the key from its place above the chart desk on that Sunday night when Maida and I were so frightened. He had slipped out his window with the key, but when he heard us coming he fled from Room 18, around St. Ann’s to his room, through the window again, through the corridor from the main part of the hospital to the south wing, tossed the key on the desk and hurried back to his own room.

“Then that arraignment of Hajek was entirely fictitious?” asked Jim.

“Not entirely. He was actually out of the hospital and in the orchard the night Higgins was shot and at the sound of the commotion he hurried back to his room. But he had gone to meet Corole; they were, of course, determined to get the radium and were conspiring together at every opportunity. And on the night of the seventh he and Corole were in the orchard. Their story of seeing a man crawl through the window of Eighteen and waiting to catch him when he emerged is not true, for only Higgins knew of Balman’s entrance and he did not know who the man was. But Corole and Hajek knew enough of Dr. Letheny’s entrance into St. Ann’s to make them sure that, when he was found dead, the radium must still be in the room. For they had listened at the window of Room 18﻿—don’t forget the gold sequin. Oh, yes. Hajek and Corole were determined to get that radium and it *was* Hajek who knocked me senseless there in the hall. That much of my story was true. I saw that the only way to get Balman was to put him off his guard. I was not sure that I could﻿—pull it off; I was afraid my very voice would betray me, that I’d be too eager, too insistent, clumsy, blundering. The least thing would have warned Dr. Balman. I had to get him to talking of it, and he, not yet having descended so low as to want to send someone else to prison for his deed, was willing to temporize, ask questions, attempt to think of something that would clear Dr. Hajek, without at the same time incriminating himself. He was trying to think fast of such possibilities.” O’Leary smoothed back his hair and straightened his impeccable tie. “Those few moments were a strain.”

===Chinese===
“唯一出于推测的——或者说唯一仅凭推理断定的——是莱瑟尼医生在这件事里的角色；而即便是那一部分，我们也知道当时只可能发生过那几桩事实。至于其余的，把海耶克的名字换成巴尔曼的——就是这么回事。”

我不妨就在这里说明白：巴尔曼医生后来对整件事供认不讳，而奥利里唯一弄错的一点是：那个星期日夜里，梅达和我吓得魂不附体的时候，从病历台上方原处取走钥匙的，是海耶克医生。他带着钥匙从自己房间的窗口溜了出去，可听见我们来了，便从18号病房仓皇逃开，绕过圣安医院回到自己房里，又从窗口爬进去，穿过从医院主体通向南翼的走廊，把钥匙往台上一扔，匆匆回自己房间去了。

“那么，对海耶克的那番控罪，全属子虚乌有？”吉姆问。

并非纯属虚构。希金斯挨枪的那夜，他确实在医院外面，就在果园里，一听见那边的骚动便赶紧溜回了自己房里。但他本是去会科罗尔的；他们两个当然都铁了心要弄到那镭，一有机会便凑在一处密谋。七日那夜，他和科罗尔也在果园里。他们说什么看见一个男人从18号病房（Room 18）的窗口爬进去、便守着等他出来好逮住他——这套话不是真的，因为知道巴尔曼进来的只有希金斯一人，而他并不知道那男人是谁。不过，关于莱瑟尼医生潜入圣安医院一事，科罗尔和海耶克知道得已足够多，足以让他们确信：他一死，镭必定还留在那间病房里。因为他们曾在18号病房窗外偷听过——别忘了那枚金色亮片。哦，是的，海耶克和科罗尔是打定主意要弄到那镭的，在走廊里把我打昏过去的，也*确实*是海耶克。我那套说法的这一部分是真的。我看得出，要拿下巴尔曼，唯一的法子是让他放松戒备。我没有把握自己能——把它做成；我怕连自己的嗓音都会出卖我，怕自己显得太急切、太逼人，笨手笨脚，漏洞百出。哪怕一丁点小事都会让巴尔曼医生起疑。我必须引他开口谈这件事，而他呢，还没堕落到想让自己的罪行由别人顶罪坐牢的地步，便愿意敷衍周旋、旁敲侧击，试着想出个既能开脱海耶克医生、又不牵连自己的法子来。他正飞快地转着这类念头呢。”奥利里抿了抿头发，扶正他那一丝不苟的领带。“那几分钟，真是熬人。”

===Original===
“Suppose he had just kept silent, had said nothing at all?” I suggested curiously.

“Oh, I knew he would talk. He had a guilty conscience, you know. It wouldn’t have been human to refuse to talk. He knew his own guilt and hence would try to appear innocent. He was bewildered, too, and was never a practical, quick-thinking man. It was a chance but not a risk.”

There was a long silence. Away down the hall I heard the faint, muffled sound of the breakfast bell. With that I roused myself and thought for the first time of the wing. I rose, picked up my ruined hat, and at the door stopped to look back on that room.

===Chinese===
“假如他偏偏保持沉默，一个字也不说呢？”我好奇地问道。

“噢，我知道他会开口的。他心里有鬼，您明白。换了谁都不可能不开口。他自知有罪，因此必定要想装得清白。他当时又惊又乱，本来也不是个务实、脑子转得快的人。那是碰运气，却谈不上冒险。”

接着是长长的沉默。走廊深处远远传来早餐铃隐隐的、闷闷的响声。这一声把我唤醒，我头一回想起了自己主管的这一翼。我站起身，拾起那顶毁得不成样子的帽子，走到门口又停住，回望那间病房。

===Original===
Room 18! What it had held! What it had witnessed!

O’Leary followed me from the room, Maida and Jim, too. Once in the corridor I found that the small, red signal light was still gleaming dully. I returned to Room 18, pulled the light cord, mechanically straightened the bed, and closed the window.

Maida and Jim had disappeared when I returned to the corridor. O’Leary was standing at the south door, looking through the glass with an amused twinkle in his clear gray eyes. Following his glance I saw Maida’s white uniform and Jim’s tweed coat vanishing along the once more sunny orchard path.

“Young idiots,” I murmured. “And before breakfast, too.”

===Chinese===
18号病房（Room 18）！它曾装下过什么！它又曾目睹过什么！

奥利里跟着我走出病房，梅达和吉姆也是。一进走廊，我发现那盏小小的红色信号灯还幽幽地亮着。我折回18号病房，拉了灯绳，机械地抚平床铺，又关上了窗子。

我回到走廊时，梅达和吉姆已经不见了。奥利里站在南门旁，隔着玻璃往外望，清澈的灰眼睛里闪着一丝莞尔。顺着他的目光望去，我看见梅达的白色制服和吉姆的斜纹呢外套，正沿着那条重又洒满阳光的果园小径渐渐远去。

“一对年轻的傻瓜，”我低声说。“还是赶在早饭前呢。”

===Original===
The path recalled to me the Letheny cottage at its other end.

“What about Corole and Hajek?” I asked.

“They were after the radium,” said O’Leary hesitantly. “But after all, I think the best thing to do is to get rid of Hajek and let them leave.”

“This means reorganization, new doctors, new methods﻿—everything.”

===Chinese===
那条小径让我想起它另一头的莱瑟尼小屋。

“科罗尔和海耶克怎么办？”我问。

“他们图的是那镭，”奥利里迟疑地说。“不过说到底，我看最好的办法还是把海耶克除名了事，放他们走。”

“这就意味着一番改组：新医生、新章程——一切都要换新。”

===Original===
O’Leary nodded.

“How true it is,” he said thoughtfully, “that even in one of the noblest professions there are scoundrels.”

“But the proportion is much smaller,” I said loyally. “You’ll find a hundred defaulting bankers for one doctor who is untrue to his trust.”

He smiled at the warmth of my defence.

===Chinese===
奥利里点点头。

“这话真是不错，”他若有所思地说，“纵然在最高贵的职业里头，也有恶棍。”

“可比例小得多，”我忠心耿耿地说。“每有一个辜负所托的医生，您就能找出一百个挪用公款的银行家。”

他见我辩护得这般起劲，不由微笑。

===Original===
“You are recovering yourself, Miss Keate. That last remark was quite in your normal manner.”

And at that Olma Flynn tugged my sleeve.

“Will you OK the charts, Miss Keate?” Her eyes were round with curiosity and she cast a speculative glance toward Room 18.

Well, that is about all.

===Chinese===
“您缓过神儿来了，基特小姐。方才那句话，完全是您平日的口气。”

正说着，奥尔玛·弗琳扯了扯我的袖子。

“基特小姐，请您核签病历单好吗？”她一双眼睛好奇得溜圆，还朝18号病房（Room 18）那边投去一个揣度的眼色。

唔，事情的大概就是这样。

===Original===
The staff doctors met that morning, and the board of directors, and were most generous in their assistance. It was not long before we were fully equipped with resident doctors and reorganized.

Our new head doctor has a wife of Anglo-Saxon ancestry who has filled the cottage with chintz-covered furniture and muslin curtains and likes to head committees. She has a nosy disposition and we don’t get along well. We have two new internes, too; fresh-cheeked boys whom Miss Dotty pets something scandalous.

Dr. Balman developed an infection in the bruise on his cheek and lived only a short while. Corole and Hajek disappeared and have not been heard of since, though lately a report came to me that a woman much resembling Corole and dressed very beautifully had been seen at a European pleasure resort where she made large sums of money gambling. I judged that Corole was falling into soft spots as usual, unhampered by a conscience.

Maida and Jim left for Russia very soon after the events that I have herein recorded took place. I hear from them every so often, long letters full of news and snapshots.

===Chinese===
那天上午，全体编制医生开了会，董事会也开了会，两下里都慨然相助。没过多久，我们便配齐了住院医生，完成了改组。

我们新来的主任医生娶了一位盎格鲁-撒克逊血统的太太；她把小屋里摆满了印花棉布包面的家具，挂上平纹细布窗帘，又爱在各种委员会里当头儿。她生性好打听别人的事，我们处得不大来。我们还来了两名新的实习医生；都是脸颊红扑扑的男孩子，多蒂小姐宠他们宠得简直不成体统。

巴尔曼医生面颊的挫伤染了感染，没活多久。科罗尔和海耶克销声匿迹，从此音信全无；不过近来有个消息传到我这里，说在一处欧洲游乐胜地，有人见过一个极像科罗尔的女人，衣着华美，赌钱赢了大笔大笔的钱。我料想，科罗尔照旧一路落在软和去处，良心是不会碍她什么事的。

我在此处记述的这些事情发生后不久，梅达和吉姆就动身去了俄国。我时常收到他们的音信，长长的信里满是新鲜事和快照。

===Original===
I see Lance O’Leary once in a while, too, and indeed, have given him some slight help on a case or two.

But for the most part I am still at St. Ann’s, going about my business as usual, save that I miss a pair of steel-blue eyes.

And I avoid the closed, mysterious door of Room 18.

===Chinese===
兰斯·奥利里我偶尔也见得着，而且说真的，还在一两桩案子上给过他一点儿小小的帮助。

只是大部分时候，我仍旧守在圣安医院，照旧做我的分内事——只除了，我惦念着一双钢蓝色的眼睛。

而18号病房（Room 18）那扇紧闭的、神秘的门，我总是绕着走。
